1-8-7   Consolidation Of Offices
   
1-8-8   Books And Records
   
1-8-9   Fees And Monies
   
1-8-10   Interest In Contracts Or Business
   
1-8-11   Conservators Of The Peace
   
1-8-12   Leaves Of Absence
   
1-8-13   Pension Systems
   
1-8-14   Other Rules And Regulations
§ 1-8-1 SCOPE.
   The provisions of this chapter shall apply alike to all officers and
employees of the village, regardless of the time of creation of the office or
position or the time of the appointment of the officer or employee.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-20)
§ 1-8-2 QUALIFICATIONS.
   A.   Qualified Elector; Residency. A person is not eligible for an elective
or appointive village office (except as otherwise provided in this code) unless
that person is a qualified elector of the village and has resided in the
village at least one year preceding his or her election or appointment.
However, the residency requirement does not apply to municipal engineers,
health officers, attorneys, or other officers who require technical training or
knowledge, to appointed village treasurers, to appointed village clerks, or to
appointed village collectors (unless the village has designated by ordinance
that the village clerk shall also hold the office of collector).
(1987 Code, § 1-2-21) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   B.   Delinquent Taxes; Felony Conviction. A person is not eligible for an
elective or appointive village office if that person is in arrears in the
payment of a tax or other indebtedness due the village or has been convicted in
any court located in the United States for any infamous crime, bribery, perjury
or other felony.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-21)
§ 1-8-3 APPOINTMENT AND TERM.
   At the first meeting of May in each year, or as soon thereafter as possible,
there shall be appointed by the mayor, with the advice and consent of the
village board, all appointive village officers who shall hold their several
offices for the term of one municipal year and until their successors are
appointed and qualified.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-20) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 1-8-4 OATH OF OFFICE.
   Before entering upon the duties of their respective offices, all municipal
officers, whether elected or appointed, shall take and subscribe to the
following oath:
 
I, ________________, do solemnly swear that I will support the constitution of
the United States and the constitution of the State of Illinois and that I will
faithfully discharge the duties of the office of ______________________,
according to the best of my ability.
 
(1987 Code, § 1-2-20)
§ 1-8-5 BONDS.
   A.   Bond Required. Every officer and employee shall, if required by the
village board, upon entering upon the duties of his or her office, give a bond
in such amount and with such sureties as may be determined by the board,
conditioned upon the faithful performance of the duties of his or her office or
position.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-20)
   B.   Amounts. Bonds of village officers required under 65 ILCS 5/3.1-10-30
shall be executed in the following penal sums:
 
Each police officer $2,000
Mayor               $50,000
Village clerk       $50,000
Village treasurer   $500,000
 
(1987 Code, § 1-2-22) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   C.   Premium Payment By Village. The surety bonds required by law shall be
paid by the village.
   D.   Surety. The village board shall not receive or approve any bond or
security whereon the name of the village board, any one of the trustees, or any
elected or appointed officer of the village is bondsperson or security. If, by
mistake, a bond containing the name of any such officer is approved by the
village board, or if any bondsperson, after becoming such, is elected or
appointed to any village office, this subsection D. shall not act as a release
of any such obligation incurred.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-22)
§ 1-8-6 COMPENSATION.
   A.   Increase Or Decrease.
      1.   Elected Officials. No salary or compensation of any elected village
officer who is elected for a definite term of office shall be increased or
diminished so as to take effect during such elected officer’s term.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-25) (Amd. 2008 Code)
      2.   Appointed Officials. No salary or compensation of any appointed
official who is appointed for a definite term of office shall be decreased
during such term, but his or her salary or compensation may be increased.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-25)
   B.   Expenses. Any village officer or employee is entitled to reimbursement
for any reasonable actual expenses incurred by him or her in the performance of
his or her duties.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-29)
§ 1-8-7 CONSOLIDATION OF OFFICES.
   A.   The village board may, from time to time by law, impose upon any
officer filling any office created by the ordinances of the village or this
code any such other or further duties as shall be consistent with the laws of
the state, and may consolidate any two or more of the offices and impose the
duties thereof upon any other officer and may make any such regulations
respecting such offices as shall be consistent with the laws of the state.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-23) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   B.   In case the village board consolidates any offices created thereby, the
person performing the duties of the offices so consolidated shall not be
entitled on account thereof to receive any salary or compensation which he or
she would not have been entitled to receive if such consolidation had not taken
place.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-23)
§ 1-8-8 BOOKS AND RECORDS.
   A.   Delivery To Successor.
      1.   Every officer shall, upon going out of office, deliver to his or her
successor all books, papers, furniture and other things appertaining to such
office and which are the property of the village.
      2.   Within five days after notification and request, any person who has
been an officer of the village is required to deliver to his or her successor
in office all property, books and effects in his or her possession belong to
the village or pertaining to the office he or she has held. Upon his or her
refusal to do so, he or she shall be liable for all damages caused thereby, and
shall, upon conviction, be penalized according to the provisions of § 
1-4-1.
   B.   Inspection. Every officer shall at all times, when required, submit the
books and papers of his or her office to the inspection of the mayor or any
committee or member of the board.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-20)
§ 1-8-9 FEES AND MONIES.
   A.   Report Of Fees And Monies Received. All officers of the village
entitled to receive fees or monies on behalf of the village shall keep an
accurate record thereof and make a report thereof, under oath, to the village
board prior to the regular meeting of each month. In the report, they shall
specify from whom such fees or monies were received, for what services and when
received.
   B.   Disposition. All fees and monies received shall be paid over to the
village treasurer.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-20) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 1-8-10 INTEREST IN CONTRACTS OR BUSINESS.
   No village officer shall be interested directly or indirectly in any village
contract, work, business or other matter to the extent prohibited by 65 ILCS 5/
3.1-55-10.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-31) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 1-8-11 CONSERVATORS OF THE PEACE.
   After receiving a certificate attesting to the successful completion of a
training course administered by the state law enforcement training standards
board, the mayor, trustees and police officers are designated as conservators
of the peace, with power to make arrests and such other powers as provided by
65 ILCS 5/3.1-15-25.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-20) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 1-8-12 LEAVES OF ABSENCE.
   Any employee desiring to be temporarily absent from the village shall apply
to the mayor for a leave of absence which may, in the discretion of the mayor,
be granted in writing for any time and, when granted, shall be filed with the
clerk.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-20) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 1-8-13 PENSION SYSTEMS.
   A.   Participation In State Municipal Retirement Fund.
      1.   Village Participation. The village does hereby elect to participate
in the state municipal retirement fund.
      2.   Special Tax. The village includes in its levy and appropriation
ordinance provisions for the levying of a special tax to pay the village cost
of participating in such retirement fund and appropriate therefrom funds to pay
the cost of participation.
   B.   Social Security. It is hereby declared to be the policy of the village
to extend to the eligible employees the benefits of the federal old age and
survivors insurance system as authorized by the Federal Social Security Act,
being Pub. L. No. 74-271 et seq., and amendments thereto.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-32)
§ 1-8-14 OTHER RULES AND REGULATIONS.
   Every officer of the village shall perform such other duties and be subject
to such other rules and regulations as the board may provide by law.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-20)
ARTICLE A: VILLAGE CLERK
Section
   
1-8A-1   Election And Term
   
1-8A-2   Duties
   
1-8A-3   Compensation
§ 1-8A-1 ELECTION AND TERM.
   The village clerk shall be elected at the same election as the mayor for a
four-year term, and shall serve until his or her successor is elected and has
qualified.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-53)
§ 1-8A-2 DUTIES.
   A.   Attend Meetings; Records Kept.
      1.   The village clerk shall attend all meetings of the village board and
shall keep in a suitable book to be styled “the journal of the village board” a
full and faithful record of its proceedings.
      2.   The village clerk shall record and properly index in a book kept for
that purpose all ordinances passed by the village board, and at the foot of the
record of each ordinance so recorded, he or she shall make a memorandum of the
date of the passage and, when published, a memorandum of the publication of
such ordinance.
      3.   The village clerk shall also record in proper books for the purpose
all official bonds, and note upon each bond so recorded when the same was
entered of record and the book and pages where recorded.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-54)
   B.   Preservation Of Records; Corporate Seal; Successor.
      1.   The clerk shall file conveniently, safely keep, and faithfully
preserve all original ordinances, bonds, books, papers or other documents that
may come into his or her hands and belong to his or her office. He or she shall
also have the custody of and in like manner file and keep all books, papers,
maps, drawings and documents belonging to the village and not in actual use by
any other officer of the village.
      2.   The clerk shall be the custodian of the village seal, and when
superseded in office, shall deliver everything pertaining thereto to his or her
successor.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-56)
   C.   Delivery Of Documents To Officers. The clerk shall deliver to the
several committees of the village board and to the officers of the village all
petitions, communications, reports and resolutions, orders, claims and other
papers referred to those committees or officers by the board on demand
therefor. He or she shall also, without delay, deliver to the mayor all
ordinances or resolutions, orders and claims in his or her charge which may be
required to be approved or otherwise acted upon by the mayor.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-55)
   D.   Preparation Of Documents, Commissions And Licenses. The clerk shall
prepare all commissions, licenses, permits and other official documents
required to be issued by him or her under this code and shall attest the same
with the corporate seal, and he or she shall, in like manner, attest all deeds
for the sale of real estate owned and conveyed by the village.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-57)
   E.   Report Of Licenses. The clerk shall report to the village board at its
regular meeting each month, and more often if the board so requires, the data
contained in his or her license register with respect to licenses issued during
the previous month.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-58)
   F.   Administer Oaths. In addition to the mayor, the clerk shall have the
power to administer oaths or affirmations for all lawful purposes.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-59)
   G.   Outstanding Bonds. The clerk shall keep in his or her office, in a book
or books kept expressly for that purpose, a correct list of all the outstanding
bonds of the village, showing the number and amount of each, for and to whom
the bonds are issued; and when the village bonds are issued, or purchased, or
paid, or canceled, the book or books shall show the fact (and in his or her
annual report, the clerk shall describe particularly the bonds sold during the
year and the terms of sale, with each and every item of expense thereof).
(1987 Code, § 1-2-60)
   H.   Other Duties. In addition to the foregoing duties, the clerk shall
perform all such other duties pertaining to his or her office as are or may be
imposed upon him or her by law or resolution or ordinance of the village board.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-64)
Cross-reference:
   Zoning Regulations, see chapter 10 of this title
§ 1-8A-3 COMPENSATION.
   Effective on and from and after the date of commencement of the newly
elected village clerk’s term
which occurs on the first regular or special meeting held in May 2009, and for
each village clerk’s term thereafter, the village clerk shall receive as salary
the sum of $125 per month for each scheduled regular board of trustees meeting,
plus $55 for each scheduled special board of trustees meeting. No amounts shall
be paid to or received by the village clerk for attendance at any adjourned
board of trustees meeting.
(Per correspondence dated 9-11-2008)
Cross-reference:
   Effect of absences from meetings on compensation, see §
1-6-2 D.
ARTICLE B: FINANCE DEPARTMENT; VILLAGE TREASURER
Section
   
1-8B-1   Department Established
   
1-8B-2   Finance Committee
   
1-8B-3   Village Treasurer
   
1-8B-3-1   Appointment And Term
   
1-8B-3-2   Duties
   
1-8B-3-3   Deposit Of Funds
   
1-8B-3-4   Investments
§ 1-8B-1 DEPARTMENT ESTABLISHED.
   There is hereby established a department of the municipal government of the
village which shall be known as the finance department. The finance department
shall embrace the committee on finance of the village board and the village
treasurer.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-71)
§ 1-8B-2 FINANCE COMMITTEE.
   The standing committee on finance shall exercise a general supervision over
the affairs of the finance department. The committee shall ascertain the
condition and needs thereof; shall, from time to time, report the same to the
mayor and village board so that a full understanding thereof shall be had; and
generally, shall do all the acts necessary to promote the efficiency of the
department.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-72)
§ 1-8B-3 VILLAGE TREASURER.
§ 1-8B-3-1 APPOINTMENT AND TERM.
   The village treasurer shall be appointed by the mayor for a one-year term,
with the advice and consent of the village board. He or she shall serve until
his or her successor is appointed and has qualified.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-73) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 1-8B-3-2 DUTIES.
   A.   Monies; Accounts; Payments.
      1.   The village treasurer shall receive all monies belonging to the
village and shall keep the treasurer’s books and accounts which shall always be
subject to the inspection of any member of the corporate authorities.
      2.   The village treasurer shall keep a separate account of each fund or
appropriation and the debits and credits belonging to the fund or
appropriation.
      3.   The village treasurer shall give every person paying money into the
treasury a receipt, specifying the date of payment and upon what account paid.
The treasurer shall file copies of the receipts with the village clerk, with
the treasurer’s monthly reports. If the treasurer has possession of money
properly appropriated to the payment of any warrant lawfully drawn upon the
treasury, the treasurer shall pay the money specified in the warrant to the
person designated by the warrant.
   B.   Separation Of Funds. The village treasurer shall keep all monies
belonging to the village and in the treasurer’s custody separate and distinct
from the treasurer’s own money and shall not use, either directly or
indirectly, the village money or warrants for the personal use and benefit of
the treasurer or of any other person. Any violation of this subsection B. shall
subject the treasurer to immediate removal from office by the corporate
authorities, who may declare the treasurer’s office vacant.
   C.   Special Assessment Funds. All monies received on a special assessment
shall be held by the village treasurer as a special fund to be applied to the
payment of the improvement for which the assessment was made, and the monies
shall be used for no other purpose, except to reimburse the village for monies
expended for the improvement.
   D.   Reports.
      1.   Receipts And Expenditures. The village treasurer shall report to the
corporate authorities, as often as they require, a full and detailed account of
all receipts and expenditures of the village, as shown by the treasurer’s
books, up to the time of the report.
      2.   Monthly Reports; Warrants. At the end of every month, and more often
if required by the corporate authorities, the village treasurer shall render an
account, under oath, to the corporate authorities, showing the state of the
treasury at the date of the account and the balance of money in the treasury.
The treasurer shall accompany the account with a statement of all monies
received into the treasury and on what account, together with all warrants
redeemed and paid by the treasurer. On the day the treasurer renders an
account, these warrants, with all vouchers held by the treasurer, shall be
delivered to the village clerk and filed, together with the account, in the
clerk’s office. All paid warrants shall be marked “paid.” The treasurer shall
keep a register of all warrants, which shall describe each warrant, showing its
date, amount and number, the fund from which paid, the name of the person to
whom paid, and when paid.
      3.   Annual Report.
         a.   (1)   Within six months after the end of each fiscal year, the
village treasurer shall annually prepare and file with the village clerk an
account of monies received and expenditures incurred during the preceding
fiscal year as specified in this subsection D.3.a. The treasurer shall show in
the account:
               (A)   All monies received by the village, indicating the total
amounts, in the aggregate, received in each account of the village, with a
general statement concerning the source of receipts. In this subsection D.3.a.
(1)(A), the term ACCOUNT does not mean each individual taxpayer, householder,
licensee, utility user or other persons whose payments to the village are
credited to a general account.
               (B)   Except as provided in subsection D.3.a.(1)(C) below, all
monies paid out by the village where the total amount paid during the fiscal
year exceeds $2,500 in the aggregate, giving the name of each person to whom
monies were paid and the total paid to each person.
               (C)   All monies paid out by the village as compensation for
personal services, giving the name of each person to whom monies were paid and
the total amount paid to each person from each account; except, that the
treasurer may elect to report the compensation for personal services of all
personnel by name, listing each employee in one of the following categories:
 
Under $25,000.00
$25,000.00 to $49,999.99
$50,000.00 to $74,999.99
$75,000.00 to $99,999.99
$100,000.00 to $124,999.99
$125,000.00 and over
 
               (D)   A summary statement of operations for all funds and
account groups of the village, as excerpted from the annual financial report as
filed with the appropriate state agency.
            (2)   Upon receipt of the account from the village treasurer, the
village clerk shall publish the account at least once in one or more newspapers
having a general circulation within the village.
         b.   Within six months after the end of each fiscal year, the village
treasurer, as provided in subsection D.3.a. above, shall file with the county
collector of taxes a copy of the annual account that is required to be filed
with and published by the village clerk, as provided in subsection D.3.a.
above, together with an affidavit of the village clerk stating that the copy is
a true and correct copy of the annual account filed with the clerk, that it was
published as required by subsection D.3.a. above, the date of the filing and
publication, and the newspaper in which it was published.
(2008 Code)
§ 1-8B-3-3 DEPOSIT OF FUNDS.
   A.   The treasurer is hereby required to keep all funds and monies in his or
her custody belonging to the village in such places of deposit as have been
designated by subsection B. below.
   B.   When requested by the village treasurer, the corporate authorities
shall designate one or more banks or savings and loan associations in which may
be kept the funds and monies of the village in the custody of the treasurer.
When a bank or savings and loan association has been designated as a
depository, it shall continue as such depository until ten days have elapsed
after a new depository is designated and has qualified by furnishing the
statements of resources and liabilities as required by law. When a new
depository is designated, the corporate authorities shall notify the sureties
of the village treasurer of that fact in writing at least five days before the
transfer of funds. The village treasurer shall be discharged from
responsibility for all funds or monies that the treasurer deposits in a
designated bank or savings and loan association while the funds and monies are
so deposited.
   C.   No bank or savings and loan association shall receive public funds as
permitted by this section unless it has complied with the requirements of the
Public Funds Investment Act § 6, being 30 ILCS 235/0.01 et seq.
   D.   The village treasurer may require any bank or savings and loan
association to deposit with said treasurer securities or mortgages that have a
market value at least equal to the amount of the funds or monies of the village
deposited with the bank or savings and loan association that exceeds the
insurance limitation provided by the federal deposit insurance corporation or
the federal savings and loan insurance corporation.
   E.   The village treasurer may enter into agreements of any definite or
indefinite term regarding the deposit, redeposit, investment, reinvestment or
withdrawal of municipal funds.
   F.   Notwithstanding any other provision of this section or any other law,
each official custodian of municipal funds, including, without limitation, the
village treasurer or finance director, or each person properly designated as
the official custodian for municipal funds, including, without limitation, each
person properly designated as official custodian of funds held by an
intergovernmental risk management entity, self-insurance pool, waste management
agency, or other intergovernmental entity composed solely of participating
municipalities, is permitted to:
      1.   Combine monies from more than one fund of a single municipality,
risk management entity, self-insurance pool or other intergovernmental entity
composed solely of participating municipalities for the purpose of investing
such monies;
      2.   Join with any other official custodians or treasurers of municipal,
intergovernmental risk management entity, self-insurance pool, waste management
agency or other intergovernmental entity composed solely of participating
municipalities for the purpose of jointly investing the funds of which the
official custodians or treasurers have custody; and
      3.   Enter into agreements of any definite or indefinite term regarding
the redeposit, investment or withdrawal of municipal, risk management entity,
self-insurance agency, waste management agency or other intergovernmental
entity funds.
(2008 Code)
Statutory reference:
   Similar provisions, see 30 ILCS 235/6
§ 1-8B-3-4 INVESTMENTS.
   The village treasurer is hereby authorized to invest surplus funds or
reserve funds of the village in the following types of investments:
   A.   General obligation securities of the United States of America or of the
state;
   B.   Certificates of deposit and time deposits in any bank where such
investments are insured by the Federal Deposit Insurance Company;
   C.   Short-term discount obligations of the Federal National Mortgage
Association;
   D.   Money market accounts; and
   E.   Unless and until otherwise designated by the village corporate
authorities, the following banks are hereby designated as places of deposit
where the village treasurer is required to keep all funds and monies in his or
her custody belonging to the village: Citizens Bank of Edinburg.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-85) (Amd. 2008 Code)
ARTICLE C: VILLAGE ATTORNEY
Section
   
1-8C-1   Appointment And Term
   
1-8C-2   Duties
   
1-8C-3   Compensation
§ 1-8C-1 APPOINTMENT AND TERM.
   The village attorney shall be appointed by the mayor, by and with the
approval of the village board, for a term of one year, unless sooner removed
for cause, and until his or her successor shall have been appointed and
qualified.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-89)
§ 1-8C-2 DUTIES.
   The attorney shall have full charge of the law affairs of the village, and
shall be known as the village attorney of the village of Edinburg.
(1987 Code, §§ 1-2-88, 1-2-89)
   A.   Prosecute And Defend. Upon request of the mayor or board and by
agreement with the village attorney, the village attorney shall prosecute or
defend on behalf of the village all cases in which the interests of the village
or any officer thereof are involved, and the village clerk shall furnish him or
her with certified copies of any ordinance, bond or paper in his or her keeping
necessary to be filed or used in any suit or proceeding.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-90) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   B.   Preparation Of Documents. The village attorney shall, when requested,
advise the village board or any officer in all matters of law in which the
interests of the village are involved, and he or she shall draw such
ordinances, resolutions, bonds, forms and contracts or other documents or
instruments, or examine and pass upon the same as may be requested of him or
her by the mayor, the village board or any committee thereof.
   C.   Legal Advisor. When requested by the mayor and the village board, the
village attorney shall act as the legal advisor for the zoning board of appeals
and the plan commission, and he or she shall perform the legal services
required of said commissions.
   D.   Dismissal Of Suits. The village attorney shall not be required to
prosecute any suit or action arising under the ordinances of the village when,
upon investigation of the same, he or she shall become satisfied that the
complaint was instituted maliciously, vexatiously or without just cause, and he
or she shall dismiss or discontinue any such suit or proceeding upon such terms
as he or she may deem just or equitable.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-90)
§ 1-8C-3 COMPENSATION.
   The village attorney shall be paid on an hourly basis for services rendered
at such rate as may be acceptable, from time to time, to the village attorney
and the village board of trustees.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-89)
ARTICLE D: GENERAL SUPERINTENDENT
Section
   
1-8D-1   Consolidation Of Offices
   
1-8D-2   Appointment And Term
   
1-8D-3   Duties
   
1-8D-4   Compensation
§ 1-8D-1 CONSOLIDATION OF OFFICES.
   The offices of superintendent of gas, superintendent of water,
superintendent of sewer, and street superintendent are hereby consolidated and
handled by a general superintendent.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-92)
§ 1-8D-2 APPOINTMENT AND TERM.
   A general superintendent shall be appointed by the mayor, by and with the
consent of the village board, and shall hold office subject to the pleasure of
the mayor and village board.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-92)
§ 1-8D-3 DUTIES.
   The general superintendent shall, subject to the direction of the mayor or
village board, have the general direction, supervision and control over the
gas, water, sewer and street departments and of the other village employees,
except members of the police department, village treasurer, or clerical office
staff. In addition thereto, the general superintendent shall have all the
powers and be responsible for all the duties as are identified in this code as
belonging to the superintendent of gas, superintendent of water, superintendent
of sewer, and street superintendent, respectively. Any reference to such
superintendents in this code shall hereafter mean to refer to the general
superintendent.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-92) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 1-8D-4 COMPENSATION.
   The general superintendent shall receive such salary as may be determined by
the village board from
time to time.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-92)
ARTICLE E: VILLAGE ADVISORY ENGINEER
Section
   
1-8E-1   Office Created; Appointment And Term
   
1-8E-2   Duties
   
1-8E-3   Compensation
§ 1-8E-1 OFFICE CREATED; APPOINTMENT AND TERM.
   There is hereby created the office of village advisory engineer who may be
appointed for a term during the municipal year at the direction of the village
president and shall serve until his or her successor is appointed and
qualified. If appointed, the village advisory engineer shall be appointed by
the village president, by and with the advice and consent of the village board
of trustees.
(Ord. 700, passed 6-14-2004)
§ 1-8E-2 DUTIES.
   When requested by the village president or the village board of trustees,
from time to time, the village advisory engineer shall perform engineering
services and consultation and advice to the village pertaining to village
business or affairs or projects.
(Ord. 700, passed 6-14-2004)
§ 1-8E-3 COMPENSATION.
   The village advisory engineer shall be paid on an hourly basis for services
rendered at such rate as may be acceptable, from time to time, to the village
advisory engineer and the village board of trustees. (Ord. 700, passed 6-14-
2004)
CHAPTER 9: ETHICS
Section
   
1-9-1   State Regulations Adopted
   
1-9-2   Definitions
   
1-9-3   Interpretation
   
1-9-4   Prohibited Acts
   
1-9-5   Penalties
   
1-9-6   Amendments
   
1-9-7   Unconstitutionality Of Provisions
§ 1-9-1 STATE REGULATIONS ADOPTED.
   The regulations of 5 ILCS 430/5-15 and 5 ILCS 430/10-10 through 430/10-40 of
the state officials and employees ethics act, being 5 ILCS 430/1-1 et seq., are
hereby adopted by reference and made applicable to the officers and employees
of the village to the extent required by 5 ILCS 430/70-5.
(Ord. 696, passed 5-10-2004) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 1-9-2 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purposes of this chapter, the terms OFFICER and EMPLOYEE shall be
defined as set forth in 5 ILCS 430/70-5(c).
(Ord. 696, passed 5-10-2004)
§ 1-9-3 INTERPRETATION.
   This chapter does not repeal or otherwise amend or modify any existing
ordinances or policies which regulate the conduct of village officers and
employees. To the extent that any such existing ordinances or policies are less
restrictive than this chapter, however, the provisions of this chapter shall
prevail in accordance with the provisions of 5 ILCS 430/70-5(a).
(Ord. 696, passed 5-10-2004)
§ 1-9-4 PROHIBITED ACTS.
   A.   Gifts.
      1.   The solicitation or acceptance of gifts prohibited to be solicited
or accepted under such statutes by an officer or any employee of the village is
hereby prohibited.
      2.   The offering or making of gifts prohibited to be offered or made to
an officer or employee of the village under such statutes is hereby prohibited.
   B.   Political Activities. The participation in political activities
prohibited under such statutes by any officer or employee of the village is
hereby prohibited.
(Ord. 696, passed 5-10-2004) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 1-9-5 PENALTIES.
   The penalties for violations of this chapter shall be the same as those
penalties set forth in 5 ILCS 430/50-5 for similar violations of such statutes.
(Ord. 696, passed 5-10-2004) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 1-9-6 AMENDMENTS.
   Any amendment to such statutes that becomes effective after the effective
date hereof shall be incorporated into this chapter by reference and shall be
applicable to the solicitation, acceptance, offering and making of gifts and to
prohibited political activities. However, any amendment that makes its
provisions optional for adoption by municipalities shall not be incorporated
into this chapter by reference without formal action by the corporate
authorities of the village.
(Ord. 696, passed 5-10-2004) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 1-9-7 UNCONSTITUTIONALITY OF PROVISIONS.
   A.   If the state supreme court declares such statutes unconstitutional in
their entirety, then this chapter shall be repealed as of the date that the
state supreme court’s decision becomes final and not subject to any further
appeals or rehearings. This chapter shall be deemed repealed without further
action by the corporate authorities of the village if such statutes are found
unconstitutional by the state supreme court.
   B.   If the state supreme court declares part of such statutes
unconstitutional but upholds the constitutionality of the remainder of such
statutes or does not address the remainder of such statutes, then the remainder
of such statutes as adopted by this chapter shall remain in full force and
effect; however, that part of this chapter relating to the part of such
statutes found unconstitutional shall be deemed repealed without further action
by the corporate authorities of the village.
(Ord. 696, passed 5-10-2004) (Amd. 2008 Code)
CHAPTER 10: VILLAGE RECORDS
Section
   
1-10-1   Location Of Records
   
1-10-2   Disclosure Of Public Records
§ 1-10-1 LOCATION OF RECORDS.
   All official records, including the village seal, shall be kept in the
village hall.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-30)
Cross-reference:
   Preservation Of Records; Corporate Seal; Successor, see §
1-8A-2 B.
§ 1-10-2 DISCLOSURE OF PUBLIC RECORDS.
   A.   Designated Official. The village clerk is hereby designated as the
appropriate official to keep centralized records, files and information
concerning the freedom of information act, being 5 ILCS 140/1 et seq., and to
coordinate activities responsive thereto among the various departments of the
village.
(1987 Code, § 1-4-1) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   B.   Fees.
      1.   Schedule Of Fees.
         a.   The schedule of fees to be charged for copies and certification
of public documents pursuant to the freedom of information act is as follows:
 
Certifications $0.50 per certified sheet
Duplications   $0.25 per page
Village code   $25
 
         b.    If a voluminous request is for electronic records and those
records are not in a portable document format (PDF), the public body may charge
up to $20 for not more than two megabytes of data, up to $40 for more than two,
but not more than four, megabytes of data, and up to $100 for more than four
megabytes of data. If a voluminous request is for electronic records and those
records are in a portable document format, the public body may charge up to $20
for not more than 80 megabytes of data, up to $40 for more than 80 megabytes,
but not more than 160 megabytes, of data, and up to $100 for more than 160
megabytes of data. If the responsive electronic records are in both a portable
document format and not in a portable document format, the public body may
separate the fees and charge the requester under both fee scales.
(5 ILCS 140/6)
      2.   Payment Of Fees. The village clerk, each department head, and any
person designated by the village clerk or a department head to respond to a
request for public records may, upon determination that the probable cost of
copying requested records would exceed $10, require payment of the estimated
cost by the requestor prior to actually copying such records. If the actual
cost of copying said records according to the fee schedule provided in this
subsection exceeds or is less than the estimated cost, the requestor will be
required to pay or be refunded the difference accordingly at the time the
copies are delivered to the requestor.
(1987 Code, § 1-4-2)
   C.   Place Of Examination. The village clerk, each department head, and each
person designated by each department head to respond to requests for public
records in such department is authorized to designate to a requestor of records
an area within which the requested records may be examined. It shall be
unlawful for any person requesting such records to remove any of them from such
designated area. (1987 Code, § 1-4-3)
   D.   Prohibited Acts.
      1.   Destruction; Defacement; Theft. It shall be unlawful for any
requestor of records to destroy or deface said records or to take them with
intent to permanently deprive the village of their possession. (1987 Code, § 1-
4-4)
      2.   False Certification. It shall be unlawful for any person requesting
examination or copies of public records to certify falsely that such request
for inspection or copying is not for the purpose of furthering a personal or
commercial enterprise.
(1987 Code, § 1-4-5) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   E.   Appeals. Any person denied access to inspect or copy any public record
may appeal such denial by sending a written notice of appeal to the mayor, and
such appeal shall thereafter proceed in accordance with 5 ILCS 140/11.5.
(1987 Code, § 1-4-6) (Amd. 2008 Code)
TITLE 2: FINANCE AND TAXATION
   Chapter
      
1.    GENERAL FINANCE PROVISIONS
      
2.    GENERAL TAX RATES
      
3.    TAX RIGHTS AND RESPONSIBILITIES
CHAPTER 1: GENERAL FINANCE PROVISIONS
Section
   
2-1-1   Claims
   
2-1-2   Contracts And Purchases
   
2-1-3   Rates And Charges For Use Of Village Labor And Equipment
§ 2-1-1 CLAIMS.
   All claims against the village for goods purchased, damages or originating
in any other way, except for claims for salaries and other allowances that are
fixed by ordinance, must be presented on or before the fifth day of each month
to the village treasurer. All such claims must be in writing, and items shall
be specified. This does not prohibit the village board from passing on any
claims not previously presented to the village treasurer if, in the opinion of
the board, justice to the claimant requires it.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-26) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 2-1-2 CONTRACTS AND PURCHASES.
   A.   Compliance With State Law. All contracts for purchases by the village
shall be in compliance with state statutes.
   B.   Contracts Over $25,000. Except as otherwise provided by statute, any
work or other public improvement which is not to be paid for in whole or in
part by special assessment or special taxation, when the expense thereof will
exceed $25,000, shall be constructed either:
      1.   By a contract let to the lowest responsible bidder after advertising
for bids, in the manner as determined by majority motion vote of the village
board from time to time; except, that any such contract may be entered into by
the proper officers without advertising for bids if authorized by a vote of
two-thirds of all trustees then holding office; or
      2.   If authorized by a vote of two-thirds of all trustees then holding
office, the superintendent shall superintend and cause to be carried out the
construction of the work or other public improvement and shall employ
exclusively for the performance of all manual labor thereon, laborers and
artisans whom the village shall pay by the day or hour; and all material of the
value of $25,000 and upward used in the construction of the work or the public
improvement shall be purchased by contract let to the lowest responsible bidder
after advertising for bids, unless advertising for bids is waived by a two-
thirds vote of all trustees then holding office. However, nothing contained in
this section shall apply to any contract between the village and the federal
government or any agency thereof.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-24) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   C.   Public Works Construction Contracts. The following requirements shall
apply to all public works contracts between the village and contractors
involving construction work on public improvements for the village.
      1.   Bid Bonds. Bid bonds shall be required in all cases and in the form
as the village board of trustees may determine from time to time. The amount of
the bid security shall equal 10% of the amount of the contractor’s bid
proposal. The bid security shall take the form of a cash deposit with the
village, a certified check payable to the village, or a bid bond guaranteed by
a reputable surety insurance company.
      2.   Performance And Payment Bonds.
         a.   Performance and payment bonds shall be required in all public
work contracts of any kind costing over $150,000 until January 1, 2029, and
then any in kind costing over $50,000 thereafter January 1, 2029. The village
board of trustees, by majority vote, may from time to time require performance
and payment bonds for any public work contract or contracts of any kind costing
under $150,000 until January 1, 2029, and then any in kind costing over $50,000
thereafter January 1, 2029. In all cases, the performance and payment bond
shall be in the form as the village board of trustees by majority vote may
determine from time to time.
         b.   For contracts under $100,000 and not involving the expenditure of
motor fuel tax funds, federal aid funds, or other funds received from the
state, the contractor may provide a non-diminishing irrevocable bank letter of
credit from a reputable financial institution in lieu of such bond provided
such letter of credit complies with all provisions required for bonds under 30
ILCS 550/1 and under this §
1-10-1-1. The amount of such letter of credit shall be in an amount equal to
%110 of the contractor’s contract amount.
         c.   In all other cases, the performance and payment bond shall be in
an amount equal to 100% of the contractor’s contract amount and shall be
secured (guaranteed) by a reputable surety insurance company or by a cash
deposit equal to the bond amount. In lieu of a cash deposit, a contractor may
use as security a non-diminishing irrevocable letter of credit issued by a
bank, savings and loan association, surety, or insurance company in an amount
equal to 110% of the amount of the contractor’s contract amount.
(Ord. 812, passed 8-11-2014)
§ 2-1-3 RATES AND CHARGES FOR USE OF VILLAGE LABOR AND EQUIPMENT.
   A.   Scope. The hourly rates set forth in this section shall be charged to
and paid for any and all work performed by any of the village employees for any
village resident or for any owner or owners of property located within or
without the village corporate limits.
   B.   Formula. The hourly rate for labor and/or use of village machinery or
equipment shall be computed and determined at the time of such work according
to the following formula: Add the total of the gross hourly wage, social
security and state municipal retirement fund contribution costs of all full-
time village employees (excluding only the village president, board of
trustees, village clerk, village treasurer, office staff, police department
employees, village attorney and village engineer), then divide such sum by the
total of such full-time village employees, and the quotient (being the average)
shall be multiplied by four, and the product shall be the hourly rate to be so
charged and paid.
   C.   Exemptions. Notwithstanding any of the provisions of this section, the
foregoing rates and charges shall not be applicable for any work performed for
the benefit of any village-owned property.
(1987 Code, § 1-2-33)
CHAPTER 2: GENERAL TAX RATES
Section
   
2-2-1   Corporate Tax
   
2-2-2   Police Tax
§ 2-2-1 CORPORATE TAX.
   The maximum rate for general corporate purposes of the village is hereby
established at 0.25%.
(1987 Code, § 36-1-1)
§ 2-2-2 POLICE TAX.
   The maximum rate for police protection purposes of the village is hereby
established at 0.075%.
(1987 Code, § 36-1-2)
CHAPTER 3: TAX RIGHTS AND RESPONSIBILITIES

2-3-5   Delinquent Notices, Payments, Remittances Or Other Filings

2-3-7   Certain Credits And Refunds
   
2-3-8   Audit Procedure
   
2-3-9   Appeals
   
2-3-10   Payment Required; Interest And Penalties
   
2-3-11   Installment Contracts

2-3-13   Voluntary Disclosure
   
2-3-14   Publication Of Tax Ordinances; Copies
   
2-3-15   Review Of Liens
§ 2-3-1 TITLE.
   This chapter shall be known as and may be cited as the “Locally Imposed And
Administered Tax Rights And Responsibility Ordinance.”
(1987 Code, § 2-1-1)
§ 2-3-2 SCOPE; APPLICATION OF PROVISIONS.
   A.   Scope. The provisions of this chapter shall apply to the village
procedures in connection with all of the village locally imposed and
administered taxes.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-2)
   B.   Application Of Provisions; Conflicting Provisions. This chapter shall
be liberally construed and administered to supplement all of the village tax
ordinances. To the extent that any tax ordinance is in conflict with or
inconsistent with this chapter, this chapter shall be controlling.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-18)
§ 2-3-3 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions apply unless the
context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   ACT. The local government taxpayers’ Bill of Rights Act.
   CORPORATE AUTHORITIES. The village president and board of trustees.
   HEARING OFFICER. An administrative individual appointed by the village
president, with the advice and consent of the village board of trustees, to
conduct hearings and to issue final determinations regarding the collection of
all locally imposed and administered taxes.
   LOCAL TAX ADMINISTRATOR. The village treasurer is charged with the
administration and collection of the locally imposed and administered taxes,
including his or her staff, employees or agents, to the extent they are
authorized by the village treasurer to act in the village treasurer’s stead.
The LOCAL TAX ADMINISTRATOR shall have the authority to implement the terms of
this chapter to give full effect to this chapter. The exercise of such
authority by the LOCAL TAX ADMINISTRATOR shall not be inconsistent with this
chapter and the Act.
   LOCALLY IMPOSED AND ADMINISTERED TAX or TAX. Each tax imposed by the village
that is collected or administered by the village and not as an agency or
department of the state. The term does not include any taxes imposed upon real
property under the Property Tax Code, being 35 ILCS 200/1-1 et seq., or fees
collected by the village other than infrastructure maintenance fees.
   NOTICE. Each audit notice, collection notice or other similar notice or
communication in connection with each of the village’s locally imposed and
administered taxes.
   TAX ORDINANCE. Each ordinance adopted by the village that imposes any
locally imposed and administered tax.
   TAXPAYER. Any person required to pay any locally imposed and administered
tax and generally includes the person upon whom the legal incidence of such tax
is placed and, with respect to consumer taxes, includes the business or entity
required to collect and pay the locally imposed and administered tax to the
village.
   VILLAGE. The village of Edinburg, Illinois.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-3) (Amd. 2008 Code)
Statutory reference:
   Similar provisions, see 35 ILCS 200/1-1 et seq. and 50 ILCS 45/1 et seq.
§ 2-3-4 NOTICE REQUIREMENTS.
   Unless otherwise provided, whenever notice is required to be given, the
notice is to be in writing and mailed not less than seven calendar days prior
to the day fixed for any applicable hearing, audit or other scheduled act of
the local tax administrator. The notice shall be sent by the local tax
administrator by registered or certified mail.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-4) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 2-3-5 DELINQUENT NOTICES, PAYMENTS, REMITTANCES OR OTHER FILINGS.
   Any notice, payment, remittance or other filing required to be made to the
village pursuant to any tax ordinance shall be considered late unless it is
physically received by the village on or before the due date; or received in an
envelope or other container displaying a valid, readable United States postmark
dated on or before the due date, properly addressed to the village, with
adequate postage prepaid.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-5)
§ 2-3-6 APPLICATION OF TAX PAYMENTS.
   Any payment or remittance received for a tax period shall be applied in the
following order:
   A.   First, to the tax due for the applicable period;
   B.   Second, to the interest due for the applicable period; and
   C.   Third, to the penalty for the applicable period.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-6)
§ 2-3-7 CERTAIN CREDITS AND REFUNDS.
   A.   Written Protest Required. The village shall not refund or credit any
taxes voluntarily paid or remitted without written protest at the time of
payment or remittance in the event that a locally imposed and administered tax
is declared invalidly enacted or unconstitutional by a court of competent
jurisdiction. However, a taxpayer shall not be deemed to have paid or remitted
the tax voluntarily if the taxpayer lacked knowledge of the facts upon which to
protest the taxes at the time of payment or remittance or if the taxpayer paid
or remitted the taxes under duress.
   B.   Statute Of Limitations. The statute of limitations on a claim for
credit or refund shall be four years after the end of the calendar year in
which payment or remittance in error was made. The village shall not grant a
credit or refund of locally imposed and administered taxes, interest or
penalties to a person who has not paid or remitted the amounts directly to the
village.
   C.   Procedure. The procedure for claiming a credit or refund of locally
imposed and administered taxes, interest or penalties paid or remitted in error
shall be as follows:
(1987 Code, § 2-1-7) (Amd. 2008 Code)
      1.   The taxpayer shall submit to the local tax administrator, in
writing, a claim for credit or refund, together with a statement specifying:
         a.   The name of the locally imposed and administered tax subject to
the claim;
         b.   The tax period for the locally imposed and administered tax
subject to the claim;
(1987 Code, § 2-1-7)
         c.   The date of the tax payment or remittance subject to the claim
and the canceled check or receipt for the payment;
(1987 Code, § 2-1-7) (Amd. 2008 Code)
         d.   The taxpayer’s recalculation, accompanied by an amended or
revised tax return, in connection with the claim; and
         e.   A request for either a refund or a credit in connection with the
claim to be applied to the amount of tax, interest and penalties overpaid, and
as applicable, related interest on the amount overpaid; provided, however, that
there shall be no refund and only a credit given in the event the taxpayer owes
any monies to the village.
      2.   Within 14 days of the receipt by the local tax administrator of any
claim for a refund or credit, the local tax administrator shall either:
         a.   Grant the claim; or
         b.   Deny the claim, in whole or in part, together with a statement as
to the reason for the denial or the partial grant and denial.
      3.   In the event the local tax administrator grants, in whole or in
part, a claim for refund or credit, the amount of the grant for refund or
credit shall bear interest at the rate of 3% per annum, based on a year of 365
days and the number of days elapsed, from the date of the overpayment to the
date of mailing of a refund check or the grant of a credit.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-7)
§ 2-3-8 AUDIT PROCEDURE.
   Any request for proposed audit pursuant to any local administered tax shall
comply with the notice requirements of this chapter.
   A.   Information Required. Each notice of audit shall contain the following
information:
      1.   The tax;
(1987 Code, § 2-1-8)
      2.   The time period to be audited; and
      3.   A detailed description of the documentation or books and records to
be made available for the auditor.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-8) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   B.   Time For Conducting Audit.
      1.   Any audit shall be conducted during normal business hours, and if
the date and time selected by the local tax administrator is not agreeable to
the taxpayer, another date and time may be requested by the taxpayer within 30
days after the originally designated audit and during normal business hours.
      2.   The taxpayer may request an extension of time to have an audit
conducted. The audit shall be conducted not less than seven days nor more than
30 days from the date the notice is given, unless the taxpayer and the local
tax administrator agreed to some other convenient time. In the event the
taxpayer is unable to comply with the audit on the date in question, the
taxpayer may request another date within the 30 days, approved in writing, that
is convenient to the taxpayer and the local tax administrator.
   C.   Taxpayer’s Books And Records.
      1.   Every taxpayer shall keep accurate books and records of the
taxpayer’s business or activities, including original source documents and
books of entry denoting the transactions which had given rise or may have given
rise to any tax liability, exemption or deduction. All books shall be kept in
the English language and shall be subject to and available for inspection by
the village.
      2.   It is the duty and responsibility of every taxpayer to make
available its books and records for inspection by the village. If the taxpayer
or tax collector fails to provide the documents necessary for audit within the
time provided, the local tax administrator may issue a tax determination and
assessment based on the tax administrator’s determination of the best estimate
of the taxpayer’s tax liability.
   D.   Overpayments. If an audit determines there has been an overpayment of a
locally imposed and administered tax as a result of the audit, written notice
of the amount of overpayment shall be given to the taxpayer within 30 days of
the village’s determination of the amount of overpayment.
   E.   Payment To Improper Entity. In the event a tax payment was submitted to
the incorrect local governmental entity, the local tax administrator shall
notify the local governmental entity imposing such tax.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-8)
§ 2-3-9 APPEALS.
   A.   Contents Of Notice Regarding Taxes Due. The local tax administrator
shall send written notice to a taxpayer upon the local tax administrator’s
issuance of a protestable notice of tax due, a bill, a claim denial, or a
notice of claim reduction regarding any tax. The notice shall include the
following information:
      1.   The reason for the assessment;
      2.   The amount of the tax liability proposed;
      3.   The procedure for appealing the assessment; and
      4.   The obligations of the village during the audit, appeal, refund and
collection process.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-9)
   B.   Request For Hearing. A taxpayer who receives written notice from the
local tax administrator of a determination of tax due or assessment may file
with the local tax administrator a written protest and petition for hearing,
setting forth the basis of the taxpayer’s request for a hearing. The written
protest and petition for hearing must be filed with the local tax administrator
within 45 days after receipt of the written notice of the tax determination and
assessment or notice of tax liability.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-9) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   C.   Hearing Scheduled; Notice. If a timely written notice and petition for
hearing is filed, the local tax administrator shall fix the time and place for
hearing and shall give written notice to the taxpayer. The hearing shall be
scheduled for a date within 14 days of receipt of the written protest and
petition for hearing, unless the taxpayer requests a later date convenient to
all parties.
   D.   Time Limit For Filing Hearing Request. If a written protest and
petition for hearing is not filed within the 45-day period, the tax
determination, audit or assessment shall become a final bill due and owing
without further notice.
   E.   Reopening, Extension Of Hearing Request. Upon the showing of reasonable
cause by the taxpayer and the full payment of the contested tax liability along
with interest accrued as of the due date of the tax, the local tax
administrator may reopen or extend the time for filing a written protest and
petition for hearing. In no event shall the time for filing a written protest
and petition for hearing be reopened or extended for more than 90 days after
the expiration of the 45-day period.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-9)
   F.   Conduct Of Hearing; Decision.
      1.   Whenever a taxpayer or a tax collector has filed a timely written
protest and petition for hearing under this section, the local tax
administrator shall conduct a hearing regarding any appeal. The taxpayer may
request that a hearing officer conduct the hearing rather than the local tax
administrator.
      2.   No continuances shall be granted except in cases where a continuance
is absolutely necessary to protect the rights of the taxpayer. Lack of
preparation shall not be grounds for a continuance. Any continuance granted
shall not exceed 14 days.
      3.   At the hearing, the local tax administrator/hearing officer shall
preside and shall hear testimony and accept any evidence relevant to the tax
determination, audit or assessment. The strict rules of evidence applicable to
judicial proceedings shall not apply.
      4.   At the conclusion of the hearing, the local tax administrator/
hearing officer shall make a written determination on the basis of the evidence
presented at the hearing. The taxpayer or tax collector shall be provided with
a copy of the written decision.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-10)
§ 2-3-10 PAYMENT REQUIRED; INTEREST AND PENALTIES.
   A.   Time For Payment. In the event a determination has been made that a tax
is due and owing, through audit, assessment or other bill sent, the tax must be
paid or remitted within the time frame otherwise indicated.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-11) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   B.   Interest. The village hereby provides for the amount of interest to be
assessed on a late payment, underpayment, or nonpayment of the tax, to be 12%
per annum, based on a year of 365 days and the number of days elapsed.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-11)
   C.   Late Filing And Payment Penalties. If a tax return is not filed within
the time and manner provided by the controlling tax ordinance, a late filing
penalty of 5% of the amount of tax required to be shown as due on a return
shall be imposed; and a late payment penalty of 5% of the tax due shall be
imposed. If no return is filed within the time or manner provided by the
controlling tax ordinance and prior to the village issuing a notice of tax
delinquency or notice of tax liability, then a failure to file penalty shall be
assessed equal to 25% of the total tax due for the applicable reporting period
for which the return was required to be filed. A late filing or payment penalty
shall not apply if a failure to file penalty is imposed by the village.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-11) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   D.   Abatement Of Penalties. The local tax administrator shall have the
authority to waive or abate any late filing penalty, late payment penalty or
failure to file penalty if the local tax administrator shall determine
reasonable cause exists for delay or failure to make a filing.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-12)
§ 2-3-11 INSTALLMENT CONTRACTS.
   The village may enter into an installment contract with the taxpayer for the
payment or remittance of taxes under the controlling tax ordinance. The local
tax administrator may not cancel any installment contract so entered unless the
taxpayer fails to pay or remit any amount due on time. Upon written notice
by the local tax administrator that the payment or remittance is 30 days
delinquent, the taxpayer shall have 14 days to cure any delinquency. If the
taxpayer fails to cure the delinquency within the 14-day period or fails to
demonstrate good faith in restructuring the installment contract with the local
administrator, the installment contract shall be canceled without further
notice to the taxpayer.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-13) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 2-3-12 STATUTE OF LIMITATIONS.
   A.   The village, through the local tax administrator, shall review all tax
returns in a prompt and timely manner and inform taxpayers of any amounts due
and owing. The taxpayer shall have 45 days after receiving notice of the
reviewed tax returns to make any request for refund or provide any tax still
due and owing.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-14) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   B.   No determination of tax due and owing may be issued more than four
years after the end of the calendar year for which the return for the
applicable period was filed or for the calendar year in which the return for
the applicable period was due, whichever occurs later.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-14)
   C.   If any tax return is not filed or if, during any four-year period for
which a notice of tax determination or assessment may be issued by the village,
the tax paid or remitted was less than 75% of the tax due for that period, the
statute of limitations shall be six years’ maximum after the end of the
calendar year in which the return for the applicable period was due, or end of
the calendar year in which the return for the applicable period was filed,
whichever occurs later.
   D.   No statute of limitations shall apply if a fraudulent tax return was
filed by the taxpayer.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-14) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 2-3-13 VOLUNTARY DISCLOSURE.
   For any locally imposed and administered tax for which a taxpayer has not
received a written notice of an audit, investigation, or assessment from the
local tax administrator, a taxpayer is entitled to file an application with the
local tax administrator for a voluntary disclosure of the tax due. A taxpayer
filing a voluntary disclosure application must agree to pay the amount of tax
due, along with interest of 1% per month, for all periods prior to the filing
of the application but not more than four years before the date of filing the
application. A taxpayer filing a valid voluntary disclosure application may not
be liable for any additional tax, interest or penalty for any period before the
date the application was filed. However, if the taxpayer incorrectly determined
and underpaid the amount of tax due, the taxpayer is liable for the underpaid
tax along with applicable interest on the underpaid tax, unless the
underpayment was the result of fraud on the part of the taxpayer, in which
case, the application shall be deemed invalid and void. The payment of tax and
interest must be made by no later than 90 days after the filing of the
voluntary disclosure application or the date agreed to by the local tax
administrator, whichever is longer. However, any additional amounts owed as a
result of an underpayment of tax and interest previously paid or remitted under
this section must be paid or remitted within 90 days after a final
determination and the exhaustion of all appeals of the additional amount owed,
or the date agreed to by the local tax administrator, whichever is longer.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-15) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 2-3-14 PUBLICATION OF TAX ORDINANCES; COPIES.
   Any locally administered tax ordinance shall be published via normal or
standard publishing requirements. The posting of a tax ordinance on the
internet shall satisfy the publication requirements. Copies of all tax
ordinances shall be made available to the public upon request at the village
clerk’s office.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-16)
§ 2-3-15 REVIEW OF LIENS.
   The local tax administrator shall establish an internal review procedure
regarding any liens filed against any taxpayers for unpaid taxes. Upon a
determination by the local tax administrator that the lien is valid, the lien
shall remain in full force and effect. If the lien is determined to be
improper, the local tax administrator shall:
   A.   Timely remove the lien at the village’s expense;
   B.   Correct the taxpayer’s credit record; and
   C.   Correct any public disclosure of the improperly imposed lien.
(1987 Code, § 2-1-17)

CHAPTER 1: GENERAL BUSINESS REGULATIONS
Section
   
3-1-1   Nuisance, Unsafe, Unhealthful Businesses
   
3-1-2   License To Be Posted
§ 3-1-1 NUISANCE, UNSAFE, UNHEALTHFUL BUSINESSES.
   A.   No business or establishment, whether or not licensed, shall be so
conducted or operated as to constitute a nuisance in fact, and no building,
vehicle, structure, yard, lot, premises or part thereof shall be used, kept,
maintained or operated in connection with any business or establishment so as
to occasion any nuisance, or so as to be dangerous to life or detrimental to
health.
   B.   No building or structure utilized, constructed or maintained in
connection with any business or occupation shall evidence an unsanitary, unsafe
or dangerous condition.
   C.   No substance, matter or thing of any kind whatever, which would be
dangerous or detrimental to health, shall be allowed to exist in connection
with any business or occupation, or be used in any work or labor performed in
the village.
(1987 Code, § 7-1-1)
Cross-reference:
   Offensive businesses, see §
4-1-1 D.
§ 3-1-2 LICENSE TO BE POSTED.
   It shall be the duty of every person conducting a licensed business in the
village to keep his or her license posted at all times in a prominent place on
the premises used for such business.
(1987 Code, § 7-1-3)
CHAPTER 2: ALCOHOLIC BEVERAGES
Section
   
3-2-1   Definitions
   
3-2-2   License And Compliance Required
   
3-2-3   Ineligibility For License
   
3-2-4   Authority To Grant Licenses; Applications
   
3-2-5   Term Of License
   
3-2-6   Advance Payment Of Fee; Proration; Refunds; Disposition
   
3-2-7   Contents Of License
   
3-2-8   Classification Of Licenses; Fees
   
3-2-9   Number Of Licenses
   
3-2-10   Display Of License
   
3-2-11   Record Of Licenses
   
3-2-12   License Holders In Annexed Land
   
3-2-13   Discontinuance Of Business; Damaged Or Destroyed Business
