§ 3-2-1 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions apply unless the
context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   ALCOHOL. The product of distillation of any fermented liquid, whether
rectified or diluted, whatever may be the origin thereof, and includes
synthetic ethyl alcohol. The term does not include denatured alcohol or wood
alcohol.
   ALCOHOLIC LIQUOR. Includes alcohol, spirits, wine and beer, and every liquid
or solid, patented or not, containing alcohol, spirits, wine or beer, and
capable of being consumed as a beverage by human beings. The provisions of this
chapter shall not apply to alcohol used in the manufacture of denatured alcohol
produced in accordance with acts of congress and regulations promulgated
thereunder, nor to any liquid or solid containing 0.5% or less of alcohol by
volume.
   BEER. A beverage obtained by the alcoholic fermentation of an infusion or
concoction of barley or other grain, malt and hops in water, and includes,
among other things, beer, ale, stout, lager beer, porter and the like.
   CLOSE. To shut up so as to prevent entrance or access by any person; and the
entire suspension of business.
   CLUB. A corporation organized under the laws of the state and not for
pecuniary profit, solely for the promotion of some common object other than the
sale or consumption of alcoholic liquors kept, used and maintained by its
members, through the payment of annual dues, and owning, hiring or leasing a
building or space in a building of such extent and character as may be suitable
and adequate for the reasonable and comfortable use and accommodation of its
members and their quests, and provided with suitable and adequate kitchen and
dining space and equipment and maintaining a sufficient number of servants and
employees for cooking, preparing and serving food and meals for its members and
their guests; provided, that such CLUB files with the mayor, at the time of its
application for a license under this chapter, two copies of a list of names and
residences of its members, and similarly files within ten days of the election
of any additional member, his or her name and address; and provided further,
that its affairs and management are conducted by a board of directors,
executive committee or similar body chosen by the members at their annual
meeting, and that no member or any officer, agent or employee of the club is
paid, or directly or indirectly receives, in the form of salary or other
compensation, any profits from the distribution or sale of alcoholic liquor to
the club or its members or its guests introduced by members beyond the amount
of such salary as may be fixed and voted at any annual meeting by the members
or by the board of directors or other governing body out of the general revenue
of the CLUB.
   MAYOR. The local liquor control commissioner as provided in 235 ILCS 5/4-2.
   PACKAGE LIQUOR STORE. Any public place where packaged liquors are offered
for sale in the original, unopened container for consumption away from the
premises.
   PUBLIC PLACE. Any premises enclosed or unenclosed or partly enclosed or
partly unenclosed wherein any service or goods, chattels or merchandise is
offered for sale to the public or any such premises used as a clubhouse, club
room or meeting place. The terms PUBLIC PLACE and PUBLIC PREMISES shall be
interchangeable for the purposes of this chapter.
   RESIDENT. One who has his or her residence in the village.
   RESTAURANT. Any public place kept, used, maintained, advertised, and held
out to the public as a place where meals are served, and where meals actually
are served and regularly served, without sleeping accommodations, such space
being provided with adequate and sanitary kitchen and dining room equipment and
capacity and having employed therein a sufficient number and kind of employees
to prepare, cook and serve suitable food for its guests.
   RETAILER. A person who sells or offers for sale alcoholic liquor for use or
consumption and not for resale in any form.
   SALE. Any transfer, exchange or barter in any manner or by any means
whatsoever for a consideration, and includes and means all sales made by any
person, whether principal, proprietor, agent, servant or employee.
   SELL AT RETAIL and SALE AT RETAIL. Any sales for use or consumption and not
for resale in any form.
   SPIRITS. Any beverage which contains alcohol obtained by distillation, mixed
with water or other substance in solution, and includes brandy, rum, whiskey,
gin or other spirituous liquors and such liquors when rectified, blended or
otherwise mixed with alcohol or other substances.
   TO SELL. Includes to keep or expose for sale and to keep with intent to
sell.
   WINE. Any alcoholic beverage obtained by the fermentation of the natural
contents of fruits, or vegetables, containing sugar, including such beverages
when fortified by the addition of ALCOHOL or SPIRITS as defined in this
section.
(1987 Code, § 21-1-1) (Amd. 2008 Code)
Cross-reference:
   Mayor To Serve As Liquor Control Commissioner, see §
1-7-3 L.
§ 3-2-2 LICENSE AND COMPLIANCE REQUIRED.
   No person shall sell, keep or offer for sale at retail, or conduct any place
for the sale at retail of alcoholic liquor within the limits and territory of
the village without having a license to do so, issued by the mayor of the
village in the manner hereinafter provided, and a valid license for such
purpose issued by the state liquor control commission. Each building, location
and premises within the aforesaid territory of the village at or upon which
alcoholic liquor is to be sold or kept or offered for sale at retail must
comply with applicable laws.
(1987 Code, § 21-2-1)
§ 3-2-3 INELIGIBILITY FOR LICENSE.
   A.   Persons Ineligible.
      1.   Except as otherwise provided in subsection A.2. below and in 235
ILCS 5/3-12, no retail license shall be issued by the mayor to:
         a.   A person who is not a resident of the village;
         b.   A person who is not of good character and reputation in the
community in which he or she resides;
         c.   A person who is not a citizen of the United States;
         d.   A person who has been convicted of a felony under any federal or
state law, unless the mayor determines that such person has been sufficiently
rehabilitated to warrant the public trust after considering matters set forth
in such person’s application and the mayor’s investigation. The burden of proof
of sufficient rehabilitation shall be on the applicant;
         e.   A person who has been convicted of being the keeper or is keeping
a house of ill fame;
         f.   A person who has been convicted of pandering or other crime or
misdemeanor opposed to decency and morality;
         g.   A person whose license issued under this chapter has been revoked
for cause;
         h.   A person who, at the time of application for renewal of any
license issued hereunder, would not be eligible for such license upon a first
application;
         i.   A copartnership, if any general partnership thereof, or any
limited partnership thereof, owning more than 5% of the aggregate limited
partner interest in such copartnership would not be eligible to receive a
license hereunder for any reason;
         j.   A corporation or limited liability company, if any member,
officer, manager or director thereof, or any stockholder or stockholders owning
in the aggregate more than 5% of the stock of such corporation, would not be
eligible to receive a license hereunder for any reason other than citizenship
and residence within the village;
         k.   A corporation or limited liability company unless it is
incorporated or organized in the state, or unless it is a foreign corporation
or foreign limited liability company which is qualified under the Business
Corporation Act of 1983, being 805 ILCS 5/1.01 et seq., or the limited
liability company act to transact business in Illinois. The mayor shall permit
and accept from an applicant for a license under this chapter proof prepared
from the secretary of state’s website that the corporation or limited liability
company is in good standing and is qualified under the Business Corporation Act
of 1983 or the limited liability company act to transact business in the state;
         l.   A person whose place of business is conducted by a manager or
agent unless the manager or agent possesses the same qualifications required by
the licensee;
         m.   A person who has been convicted of a violation of any federal or
state law concerning the manufacture, possession or sale of alcoholic liquor,
subsequent to July 29, 2005, or has forfeited his or her bond to appear in
court to answer charges for any such violation;
         n.   A person who does not beneficially own the premises for which a
license is sought, or does not have a lease thereon for the full period for
which the license is to be issued;
         o.   Any law enforcing public official, including members of local
liquor control commissions, any mayor, alderman, or member of the city council
or commission, any president of the village board of trustees, any member of a
village board of trustees, or any president or member of a county board; and no
such official shall have a direct interest in the manufacture, sale, or
distribution of alcoholic liquor; except, that a license may be granted to such
official in relation to premises that are not located within the territory
subject to the jurisdiction of that official if the issuance of such license is
approved by the state liquor control commission; and except, that a license may
be granted to any member of the village board of trustees if the sale of
alcoholic liquor pursuant to the license is incidental to the selling of food;
the issuance of the license is approved by the state commission; the issuance
of the license is in accordance with all other code provisions; and such member
granted a license does not vote on alcoholic liquor issues pending before the
board. Notwithstanding any provision of this subsection A.1.o. to the contrary,
an alderman or member of a city council or commission, a member of a village
board of trustees other than the president of the village board of trustees, or
a member of a county board other than the president of a county board may have
a direct interest in the manufacture, sale, or distribution of alcoholic liquor
as long as he or she is not a law enforcing public official, a mayor, a village
board president, or president of a county board. To prevent any conflict of
interest, the elected official with the direct interest in the manufacture,
sale, or distribution of alcoholic liquor cannot participate in any meetings,
hearings, or decisions on matters impacting the manufacture, sale, or
distribution of alcoholic liquor;
         p.   A person who is not a beneficial owner of the business to be
operated by the licensee;
         q.   A person who has been convicted of a gambling offense as
proscribed by any of 720 ILCS 5/28-1(a)(3) through (a)(11), or as proscribed by
720 ILCS 5/28-1.1 or 5/28-3, or as proscribed by a statute replaced by any of
the aforesaid statutory provisions;
         r.   A person or entity to whom a federal wagering stamp has been
issued by the federal government, unless the person or entity is eligible to be
issued a license under the Raffle and Poker Runs Act, being 230 ILCS 15/0.01 et
seq., or the Illinois Pull Tabs and Jars Game Act, being 230 ILCS 20/1 et seq.;
and/or
         s.   A person who intends to sell alcoholic liquors for use or
consumption on his or her licensed retail premises who does not have liquor
liability insurance coverage for that premises in an amount that is at least
equal to the maximum liability amounts set out in 235 ILCS 5/6-21(a).
      2.   A criminal conviction of a corporation is not grounds for the
denial, suspension, or revocation of a license applied for or held by the
corporation if the criminal conviction was not the result of a violation of any
federal or state law concerning the manufacture, possession or sale of
alcoholic liquor, the offense that led to the conviction did not result in any
financial gain to the corporation, and the corporation has terminated its
relationship with each director, officer, employee, or controlling shareholder
whose actions directly contributed to the conviction of the corporation. The
mayor shall determine if all provisions of this subsection A.2. have been met
before any action on the corporation’s license is initiated.
(1987 Code, § 21-2-3) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   B.   Businesses Ineligible.
      1.   Stores Selling School Supplies And Lunches. No license shall be
issued to any person for the sale at retail of any alcoholic liquor at any
store or other place of business where the majority of customers are minors of
school age or where the principal business transacted consists of school books,
school supplies, food, lunches or drinks for minors.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-4)
      2.   Gasoline Service Stations; Convenience Stores. No liquor license
shall be issued to any person, firm, or corporation for the sale at retail of
any alcoholic liquor, beer or wine at any gasoline service station or similar
place of business or at any convenience store where gasoline or diesel fuel or
gasohol is also sold for use of motor vehicles.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-5)
Statutory reference:
   Similar provisions, see 805 ILCS 5/1.01 et seq., 805 ILCS 180/1-1 et seq.,
230 ILCS 15/0.01 et seq. and 230 ILCS 20/1 et seq.
§ 3-2-4 AUTHORITY TO GRANT LICENSES; APPLICATIONS.
   A.   The mayor is authorized to grant and issue licenses to individuals,
firms, and corporations to sell at retail and to keep and offer for sale at
retail alcoholic liquors within the limits and territory of the village upon
the conditions and in the manner provided by this chapter and by the act of the
general assembly of Illinois, and not otherwise. Such license shall be in
writing, signed by the mayor and attested by the village clerk, with the seal
of his office affixed thereto.
(1987 Code, § 21-2-2)
   B.   1.   Prior to issuance of a license, the applicant must sign and submit
to the mayor an application, in triplicate, in writing, and under oath, setting
forth the information and documents required in the application. The form of
such application shall be approved by the board from time to time, and the
approved application form shall be on file in the village office from time to
time.
      2.   In case of a copartnership, corporation, or limited liability
company, the information and statements required in the application shall be
furnished as to each partner, member, officer, manager, director and
stockholder thereof, with the location of the principal office of the
copartnership, corporation or limited liability company.
(1987 Code, § 21-2-2) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   C.   The application for a license shall be filed with the village clerk.
(1987 Code, § 21-2-4)
§ 3-2-5 TERM OF LICENSE.
   A.   Retail liquor licenses issued under this chapter shall be valid for a
period not to exceed six months upon the payment of the license fee as
hereinafter set forth, unless sooner revoked or suspended. The six-month
periods shall be from January 1 of each year to June 30 of that year and from
July 1 of each year to December 31 of that year.
(1987 Code, § 21-2-4)
   B.   A license issued under this chapter shall be purely a personal
privilege, good for not to exceed six months after issued, unless sooner
revoked as in this chapter authorized and provided and shall not constitute a
right.
(1987 Code, § 21-2-6)
§ 3-2-6 ADVANCE PAYMENT OF FEE; PRORATION; REFUNDS; DISPOSITION.
   The license fee shall be payable in advance by the applicant for a license
at the time the application for a license is submitted to the local liquor
control commissioner as herein provided. In the event the license is granted
for less than six months, the license fee shall be prorated. In the event the
license is denied, the license fee shall be returned to the applicant. The fees
shall be deposited with the village treasurer who shall deposit the fees in the
village general fund.
(1987 Code, § 21-2-4)
§ 3-2-7 CONTENTS OF LICENSE.
   Each license shall state thereon the names of the licensees and the address
and description of the premises for which it is granted and the date of its
issuance and expiration.
(1987 Code, § 21-2-4)
§ 3-2-8 CLASSIFICATION OF LICENSES; FEES.
   Licenses to be issued under this chapter shall be of four classes: class A,
class B, class C and class D.
   A.   Class A License. Class A licenses shall authorize the retail sale of
alcoholic liquor on the premises specified in the license either in original
containers or for consumption on the premises. The annual fee for such license
shall be $400, payable semiannually in advance.
   B.   Class B License. Class B licenses shall authorize the retail sale of
beer (regardless of alcoholic content) and wine (regardless of alcoholic
content) and other alcoholic liquors (with an alcohol content of less than 6%)
on the premises described in the license, in original containers, for
consumption off the premises. The annual fee for such license shall be $400,
payable semiannually in advance.
   C.   Class C License. The corporate authorities, in their sole discretion,
may issue class C licenses from time to time for special events. Such licenses
shall permit the retail sale of alcoholic liquor or spirits and/or wine and/or
beer for consumption on, about or off the premises where sold, all as may be
determined by the majority vote of the corporate authorities. The terms of, fee
for, and duration of license, and such other conditions the corporate
authorities may impose on such license, shall be as determined by the majority
vote of the corporate authorities.
   D.   Class D License. Class D licenses shall be issued only to not for
profit corporations and shall authorize the retail sale of beer and/or wine
coolers containing not more than 6% of alcohol by volume, either in original
containers or for consumption on or about the premises described in the
license. The annual fee for such license shall be $200, payable semiannually in
advance.
(1987 Code, § 21-2-5)
§ 3-2-9 NUMBER OF LICENSES.
   A.   Class A License. The total number of all class A licenses issued and in
force at any one time in any year shall not exceed four until the total
population of the village exceeds 2,000; and thereafter, not more than one
class A license shall be issued and in force at any time in any year for each
500 or major fraction of 500 that exceeds 2,000 of such village population.
   B.   Class B License. The total number of all class B licenses issued and in
force at any one time in any year shall not exceed one until the total
population of the village exceeds 1,500, and thereafter, not more than one
class B license shall be issued and in force at any one time in any year for
each 1,500 or major fraction of 1,500 of such population.
(1987 Code, § 21-2-5) (Ord. 856, passed 9-10-2018)
§ 3-2-10 DISPLAY OF LICENSE.
   Every licensee under this chapter shall cause his or her license to be
framed and hung in plain view in a conspicuous place on the licensed premises.
(1987 Code, § 21-2-8)
§ 3-2-11 RECORD OF LICENSES.
   The mayor shall keep a complete record of all licenses issued by him or her
and shall supply the village clerk a copy of the same. Upon issuance or
revocation of a license, the mayor shall give notice to the clerk within 48
hours.
(1987 Code, § 21-2-9)
§ 3-2-12 LICENSE HOLDERS IN ANNEXED LAND.
   The restrictions contained in this chapter shall in no way affect taverns
and other businesses holding retail liquor licenses, duly licensed by the
county, which are located in the territory annexed by the village. Licenses may
be issued to them or renewed by the duly constituted authorities upon
annexation; provided, that, thereafter, all of the restrictions and
contingencies contained herein shall apply.
(1987 Code, § 21-2-7)
§ 3-2-13 DISCONTINUANCE OF BUSINESS; DAMAGED OR DESTROYED BUSINESS.
   No license shall be held in existence by the mere payment of fees by the
licensee for a longer period than 90 days without a tavern or liquor business
for the same being in complete and full operation. However, if a tavern or
liquor business has been destroyed or damaged by fire or act of nature and
cannot be rebuilt or repaired within the 90-day period, then, in that event,
the mayor shall extend the period of time for which a liquor license may be
held by the payment of fees without the tavern or liquor business being in full
and complete operation for an additional 90 days. If either of the above stated
periods of time passes without the particular tavern or liquor business
returning to complete and full operation, the license for that particular
business shall expire and not be subject to renewal, unless all other
requirements of this chapter shall have been met.
(1987 Code, § 21-2-7) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 3-2-14 DAYS AND HOURS OF OPERATION.
   A.   Established. It shall be unlawful for any holder of a retail liquor
license and/or his, her or its agents or employees to keep open or permit to be
kept open any licensed premises between and during the following described
hours or days, and the holder of a retail liquor license and/or his, her or its
agents or employees shall not sell, give, deliver or allow to be consumed on
the licensed premises any alcoholic liquor between and during the following
described hours and days:
      1.   From 2:00 a.m. to 6:00 a.m. Monday through Saturday; and
      2.   From 2:00 a.m. to 12:00 p.m. on Sunday.
   B.   New Year’s Day Exceptions. All persons presently possessing a valid
class A license to sell liquor in taverns within the limits of the village
shall be permitted to remain open and to sell alcoholic liquors pursuant to the
license from 12:00 a.m. December 31 until 3:00 a.m. on New Year’s Day, January
1, of each year.
   C.   Penalties. Any holder of a retail liquor license or his or her agent or
employee who violates the provisions of this section in regulating the legal
hours of operation shall, upon conviction of the first offense, be fined not
less than $250 nor more than $750; and for the second offense, be fined not
less than $250 nor more than $750; and suffer the revocation of such retail
liquor license.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-1) (Amd. 2008 Code) (Ord. 784, passed 11-14-2011)
§ 3-2-15 LOCATION RESTRICTIONS.
   A.   Churches, Schools And Hospitals.
      1.   No license shall be issued for the sale at retail of any alcoholic
liquor within 100 feet of any church, school, other than an institution of
higher learning, hospital, home for aged or indigent persons or for veterans,
their spouses or children or any military or naval station; provided, that this
prohibition shall not apply to hotels offering restaurant service, regularly
organized clubs, or to restaurants, food shops or other places where sale of
alcoholic liquors is not the principal business carried on; nor to the renewal
of a license for the sale at retail of alcoholic liquor on premises within 100
feet of any church or school where the church or school has been established
within such 100 feet since the issuance of the original license. In the case of
a church, the distance of 100 feet shall be measured to the nearest part of any
building used for worship services or educational programs and not to property
boundaries.
      2.   Nothing in this subsection shall prohibit the issuance of a license
to a church or private school to sell at retail alcoholic liquor if any such
sales are limited to periods when groups are assembled on the premises solely
for the promotion of some common object other than the sale or consumption of
alcoholic liquors.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-2) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   B.   Zoning. It shall be unlawful to establish a retail liquor business
within the village in violation of the zoning restrictions.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-8)
   C.   Change Of Location. A retail liquor dealer’s license shall permit the
sale of alcoholic liquor only on the premises described in the application and
license. Such location may be changed only upon the written permit to make such
change issued by the mayor. No change of location shall be permitted unless the
proposed new location is a proper one for the retail sale of alcoholic liquor
under the laws of the state and this chapter.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-3)
§ 3-2-16 HEALTH STANDARDS; SANITARY CONDITIONS.
   A.   Health Permit. Every licensee shall have, at all times, a valid
operating permit from the county health department which regulates health
standards, if necessary.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-15)
   B.   Premises. All premises used for the retail sale of alcoholic liquor or
for the storage of such liquor for such sale shall be kept in a clean and
sanitary condition and shall be kept in full compliance with the codes
regulating the condition of the premises used for the storage or sale of food
for human consumption.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-13)
   C.   Employees. It shall be unlawful to employ in any premises used for the
retail sale of alcoholic liquor any person who is afflicted with or who is a
carrier of any contagious, infectious or sexually transmitted disease; and it
shall be unlawful for any person who is afflicted with or a carrier of any such
disease to work in or about any premises or to engage in any way in the
handling, preparation or distribution of such liquor.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-14) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 3-2-17 SALES TO CERTAIN PERSONS PROHIBITED.
   No licensee, nor any officer, associate, member, representative, agent or
employee of such licensee, shall sell, give or deliver alcoholic liquor to any
person under the age of 21 years or to any intoxicated person.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-19) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 3-2-18 PERSONS UNDER TWENTY ONE YEARS OF AGE.
   A.   Sales, Providing To Prohibited. No person, after purchasing or
otherwise obtaining alcoholic liquor, shall sell, give or deliver such
alcoholic liquor to another person under the age of 21 years, except in the
performance of a religious ceremony or service.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-19)
   B.   Purchase, Possession Or Consumption Prohibited. No person shall
purchase, accept delivery of or have possession of alcoholic liquor in
violation of this chapter. The consumption of alcoholic liquor by any person
under the age of 21 years is forbidden.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-22)
   C.   Presence On Licensed Premises.
      1.   It shall be unlawful for any person under the age of 21 years to
enter any licensed premises or tavern unless accompanied by a parent or legal
guardian.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-12) (Amd. 2008 Code)
      2.   It shall be unlawful for a licensee or any officer, associate,
member, representative, agent, or employee of a licensee to permit any person
under the age of 21 years to enter and remain in that portion of a licensed
premises that sells, gives or delivers alcoholic liquor for consumption on the
premises, unless such person is accompanied by his or her parent or legal
guardian who is of the age of 21 years or more. No prohibition under this
subsection C.2., however, shall apply to any licensed premises, such as,
without limitation, a restaurant or food shop, where selling, giving, or
delivering alcoholic liquor is not the principal business of the licensee at
those premises.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-24) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   D.   Identification.
      1.   Required. If a licensee or his or her agents or employees believe or
have reason to believe that a sale or delivery of any alcoholic liquor is
prohibited because of the age of the prospective recipient, he or she shall,
before making such sale or delivery, demand presentation of some form of
positive identification, containing proof of age, issued by a public officer in
the performance of his or her official duties. Proof that the defendant/
licensee, or his or her employees or agents demanded, was shown, and reasonably
relied upon such written evidence in any transaction forbidden by this chapter
is competent for or in any proceedings for the suspension or revocation of any
license based thereon.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-21)
      2.   Forged Identification, Improper Use Prohibited. No person shall
transfer, alter or deface an identification card; use the identification card
of another; carry or use a false or forged identification card; or obtain an
identification card by means of false information.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-22)
   E.   Employment. It shall be unlawful for any person under the age of 21
years to sell, draw, pour, mix, give or deliver any alcoholic liquor in any
licensed retail premises.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-20) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   F.   Warning Posted. In every licensed business where alcoholic liquor is
sold, there shall be displayed at all times in a prominent place a printed card
which shall be supplied by the village clerk and which shall read as follows:
 
WARNING TO PERSONS UNDER AGE 21 YEARS
YOU ARE SUBJECT TO A FINE UP TO $750 UNDER THE ORDINANCES OF THIS MUNICIPALITY
IF YOU PURCHASE ALCOHOLIC LIQUOR, OR MISREPRESENT YOUR AGE FOR THE PURPOSE OF
PURCHASING OR OBTAINING ALCOHOLIC LIQUOR.
 
(1987 Code, § 21-3-24) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   G.   Exemptions. The possession and dispensing or consumption by a person
under 21 years of age of alcoholic liquor in the performance of a religious
service or ceremony, or the consumption by a person under 21 years of age under
the direct supervision and approval of the parent or parents or those persons
standing in loco parentis of such person under 21 years of age in the privacy
of a home is not prohibited by this chapter.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-25) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 3-2-19 LEWD ENTERTAINMENT.
   No licensee, his or her agent, servant or employee, shall permit or allow
any lewd or lascivious act or any topless and/or bottomless employee and/or
employees ( TOPLESS being defined as naked and substantially without clothing
or covering of the body from the waist to the neckline, and BOTTOMLESS being
defined as naked and substantially without clothing or covering of the body
from the waist downward), or entertainment to be performed within the licensed
premises by an entertainer employed therein, or by any employee or guest; nor
shall any licensee, his or her agent, servant or employee, permit or allow any
employee or guest, or any other person whomever, to solicit or encourage the
purchasing of any alcoholic liquor or beverage of any description, or the
giving of any gratuity or gift by any patron or guest to or for the benefit of
such employee or guest. The following kinds of conduct on premises in the
village licensed to sell liquor are prohibited:
   A.   The performance of acts or simulated acts of sexual intercourse,
masturbation, sodomy, bestiality, oral copulation, flagellation or any sexual
acts;
   B.   The act of simulated exhibition, touching, caressing, or fondling of
the breasts, buttocks, pubic hair, anus, vulva or genitals;
   C.   The actual or simulated displaying of the breasts, buttocks, pubic
hair, anus, vulva or genitals;
   D.   The permitting by a licensee of any person to remain in or upon the
licensed premises who exposes to view any portion of his or her breasts,
buttocks, genitals, vulva or anus; and
   E.   The displaying of films or pictures depicting acts, a live performance
of which is prohibited by the regulations of this section.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-11)
§ 3-2-20 TRANSPORTING ALCOHOLIC BEVERAGES.
   A.   Except as provided in subsection C. below and in 235 ILCS 5/6-33, no
driver may transport, carry, possess or have any alcoholic liquor within the
passenger area of any motor vehicle upon a street in the village except in the
original container and with the seal unbroken.
   B.   Except as provided in subsection C. below and in 235 ILCS 5/6-33, no
passenger may carry, possess or have any alcoholic liquor within any passenger
area of any motor vehicle upon a street in the village except in the original
container and with the seal unbroken.
   C.   This section shall not apply to the passengers in a limousine when it
is being used for purposes for which a limousine is ordinarily used, the
passengers on a chartered bus when it is being used for purposes for which
chartered buses are ordinarily used or on a MOTOR HOME OR MINI-MOTOR HOME as
defined in 625 ILCS 5/1-145.01. However, the driver of any such vehicle is
prohibited from consuming or having any alcoholic liquor in or about the
driver’s area. Any evidence of alcoholic consumption by the driver shall be
prima facie evidence of such driver’s failure to obey this section. For the
purposes of this section, a LIMOUSINE is a motor vehicle of the first division
with the passenger compartment enclosed by a partition or dividing window used
in the for hire transportation of passengers and operated by an individual in
possession of a valid state driver’s license of the appropriate classification
pursuant to 625 ILCS 5/6-104. The exemption applicable to chartered buses under
this subsection does not apply to any chartered bus being used for school
purposes.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-7) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 3-2-21 DRINKING IN PUBLIC AND ON PRIVATE PROPERTY; OPEN CONTAINERS.
   A.   It shall be unlawful to drink any alcoholic liquor on any public
street, alley or sidewalk or on any other public place in the village.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-6) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   B.   1. No licensee shall permit any person to leave the licensee’s licensed
premises with open liquor.
      2.   No person shall leave any licensed premises with open liquor.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-23)
   C.   It shall be unlawful for any person to do or commit any of the
following acts within the village:
      1.   Drink any alcoholic liquors on any public street, alley, sidewalk or
other public way without special permission granted by the mayor;
      2.   Drink any alcoholic liquors in any public park, except with the
permission of the mayor; and/or
(1987 Code, § 21-3-10)
      3.   Drink any alcoholic liquors on any private property without
permission of the owner or occupant thereof.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-10) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 3-2-22 ADDITIONAL PROHIBITED ACTS.
   A.   Misbranding Alcoholic Liquor. Any person who shall knowingly possess,
sell or in any way dispose of any alcoholic liquor under any other than the
proper name or brand known to the trade as designating the kind and quality of
the contents of the package or other containers of said alcoholic liquor, or
who shall cause any such act to be done, shall forfeit to the state or village
said alcoholic liquor and the packages and containers, and shall be subject to
the punishment and penalties provided for violation of this chapter.
(1987 Code, § 21-4-5) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   B.   Peddling Alcoholic Beverages. It shall be unlawful to peddle alcoholic
liquor in the village. (1987 Code, § 21-3-16)
   C.   Gambling. It shall be unlawful to keep, place, maintain, or operate any
gambling device or instrument in and upon the premises used or occupied as a
place where alcoholic liquor is sold or given away.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-17)
   D.   Disorderly Conduct. It shall be unlawful for any licensee, or any
officer, associate, member, representative, agent or employee of a licensee, to
permit or allow or suffer any disorder, drunkenness, quarreling, fighting,
unlawful games, or riotous or disorderly conduct in or upon the licensed
premises. (1987 Code, § 21-3-18) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 3-2-23 EXAMINATIONS AND INSPECTIONS.
   A.   Examinations. The mayor shall have the right to examine or cause to be
examined, under oath, any applicant for a local license or for renewal thereof,
or any licensee upon whom notice of revocation or suspension has been served
and to examine or cause to be examined the books and records of any such
applicant or licensee; to hear testimony and take proof for his information in
the performance of his or her duties; and for such purpose, to issue subpoenas,
which shall be effective in any part of the state. For the purpose of obtaining
any of the information desired by the mayor under this subsection A., he or she
may authorize his or her agent to act on his or her behalf.
(1987 Code, § 21-2-2)
   B.   Entry Powers For Inspections. It shall be unlawful to refuse to grant
admittance to the premises for which a license has been issued at any time upon
the verbal request of the mayor, chief of police, any police officer, the
zoning enforcing officer, or any member of the village board for the purpose of
making an inspection of such premises, or any part thereof.
(1987 Code, § 21-3-26) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 3-2-24 PERMITTING VIOLATIONS.
   If the owner of the licensed premises or any person from whom the licensee
derives the right to possession of such premises, or the agent, employee or
representative of such owner or person, shall knowingly permit the licensee to
use said licensed premises in violation of the terms of this chapter, said
owner, agent, employee, representative or other person shall be deemed guilty
of a violation of this chapter to the same extent as said licensee and be
subject to the same punishment.
(1987 Code, § 21-4-1) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 3-2-25 LIABILITY OF LICENSEE FOR VIOLATIONS.
   Every act or omission of whatsoever nature constituting a violation of any
of the provisions of this chapter by any officer, director, member, associate,
stockholder, representative, manager, agent or employee of any licensee shall
be deemed and held to be the act of such employer or licensee, and said
employer or licensee shall be punishable in the same manner as if said act or
omission had been done or omitted by him or her personally.
(1987 Code, § 21-4-2) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 3-2-26 ENFORCEMENT POWERS OF LIQUOR CONTROL COMMISSIONER.
   The local liquor control commissioner shall have the following powers,
functions and duties with respect to licenses granted under this chapter, in
addition to those powers, functions and duties provided by statute.
   A.   In addition to and not limited by the specific penalties set out for
violations of specific sections of this chapter, the local liquor control
commissioner may suspend for not more than 30 days or revoke for cause any
liquor license issued under this chapter for any violation of any provisions of
this chapter; or for any violation of the Liquor Control Act of 1934, being 235
ILCS 5/1-1 et seq.; or for violation of any existing rules or regulations
established by the local liquor control commissioner or the state commission
which is not inconsistent with law; and in addition thereto, may levy a fine in
accordance with § 3-2-27.
   B.   The local liquor control commissioner may suspend or revoke any liquor
license if the licensee makes any false statement or misrepresentation in the
application for a license.
   C.   The local liquor control commissioner may enter or authorize any law
enforcing officer to enter at any time upon any premises licensed hereunder to
determine whether any of the provisions of this chapter, or the aforementioned
Liquor Control Act of 1934, or any applicable rule or regulation established by
the state commission or the local liquor control commissioner have been or are
being violated, and at such time, to examine the premises of the licensee in
connection therewith.
   D.   1.   Any citizen of the village shall have the right to file a
complaint with the local liquor control commissioner, stating that any licensee
under this chapter has been or is violating the provisions of this chapter, or
the aforementioned Liquor Control Act of 1934, or any applicable rule or
regulation established by the state commission or the local liquor control
commissioner. Such complaint shall be in writing and shall be signed and sworn
to by the citizen complaining.
      2.   Any five residents of the village shall have the right to file a
complaint with the local liquor control commissioner, stating that any retailer
licensee under this chapter has been or is violating the provisions of this
chapter, or the aforementioned Liquor Control Act of 1934, or any applicable
rule or regulation established by the state commission or the local liquor
control commissioner. Such complaint shall be in writing and shall be signed
and sworn to by the resident complaining.
(1987 Code, § 21-4-7) (Amd. 2008 Code)
      3.   The complaint shall state the particular provision, rule or
regulation believed to have been violated and the facts, in detail, upon which
such belief is based. If the local liquor control commissioner is satisfied
that the complaint substantially charges a violation, and that from the facts
alleged, there is reasonable cause for such belief, he or she shall set the
matter for hearing, and shall serve notice upon the licensee of the time and
place of such hearing and of the particular charges in the complaint.
(1987 Code, § 21-4-7)
§ 3-2-27 REVOCATION OR SUSPENSION OF LICENSE; NOTICE; HEARING; APPEAL.
   A.   The local liquor control commissioner may revoke or suspend any license
issued by him or her if he or she determines that the licensee has violated any
of the provisions of the state Liquor Control Act, or of this chapter, or any
applicable rule or regulation established by the local liquor control
commissioner or the state commission which is not inconsistent with law. Upon
notification by the Illinois department of revenue, the state commission, in
accordance with 235 ILCS 5/3-12, may refuse the issuance or renewal of a
license, fine a licensee, or suspend or revoke any license issued by the state
commission if the licensee or license applicant has violated the provisions of
the Retailer’s Occupation Tax Act § 3, being 35 ILCS 120/1 et seq. In addition
to the suspension, the local liquor control commissioner may levy a fine on the
licensee for such violations. The fine imposed shall not exceed $1,000 for a
first violation within a 12-month period, $1,500 for a second violation within
a 12-month period, and $2,500 for a third or subsequent violation within a 12-
month period. Each day on which a violation continues shall constitute a
separate violation. Not more than $15,000 in fines under this section may be
imposed against any licensee during the period of his license. Proceeds from
such fines shall be paid into the general corporate fund of the village
treasury.
   B.   However, no such license shall be so revoked or suspended and no
licensee shall be fined except after a public hearing by the local liquor
control commissioner with a three-day written notice to the licensee affording
the licensee an opportunity to appear and defend. All such hearings shall be
open to the public, and the local liquor control commissioner shall reduce all
evidence to writing and shall maintain an official record of the proceedings.
If the local liquor control commissioner has reason to believe that any
continued operation of a particular licensed premises will immediately threaten
the welfare of the community, he or she may, upon the issuance of a written
order stating the reason for such conclusion and without notice or hearing,
order the licensed premises closed for not more than seven days, giving the
licensee an opportunity to be heard during that period; except, that if such
licensee shall also be engaged in the conduct of another business or businesses
on the licensed premises, such order shall not be applicable to such other
business or businesses.
   C.   The local liquor control commissioner shall, within five days after
such hearing, if he or she determines after such hearing that the license
should be revoked or suspended or that the licensee should be fined, state the
reason or reasons for such determination in a written order, and either the
amount of the fine, the period of suspension, or that the license has been
revoked, and shall serve a copy of such order within the five days upon the
licensee.
   D.   The service of any notice of a hearing regarding the suspension or
revocation of a liquor license may be made by leaving a copy of such notice at
the place of business of such licensee with some person over the age of 15
years, and informing such person of the contents of such notice. The return of
the county sheriff as to the manner of service shall be sufficient.
   E.   On the day set for the hearing, if such licensee does not appear at the
time and place designated in such notice, the local liquor control commissioner
may proceed to hear said case the same as if the licensee was present and to
render a decision on the merits of the case.
(1987 Code, § 21-4-8) (Amd. 2008 Code)
Statutory reference:
   Similar provisions, see 35 ILCS 120/3
§ 3-2-28 FORFEITURE OF LICENSE FEES.
   Whenever any liquor license has been suspended or revoked, all license fees
paid thereon shall be forfeited, and it shall thereafter be unlawful and shall
constitute a further violation of this chapter for said licensee to continue to
operate under such suspended or revoked license.
(1987 Code, § 21-4-3) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 3-2-29 VIOLATIONS; PREMISES DECLARED A NUISANCE.
   Every lot, parcel or tract of land, and every building, structure, tent,
railroad car, boat, wagon, vehicle, establishment or place whatsoever, together
with all furniture, fixtures, ornaments and machinery located thereon, wherein
there shall be conducted any unlawful sale of any alcoholic liquor, or whereon
or wherein there shall be kept, stored, concealed or allowed any alcoholic
liquor intended for illegal sale or to be sold, disposed of or in any other
manner used in violation of any of the provisions of this chapter is hereby
declared to be a public nuisance and shall be abated as provided by the laws of
the state for the abatement of public nuisances.
(1987 Code, § 21-4-6)
§ 3-2-30 PENALTY.
   In addition to any other penalties and/or fines otherwise provided herein,
any person, firm or corporation violating any provision of this chapter shall,
upon conviction thereof, be fined as provided in §
1-4-1.
(1987 Code, § 21-4-9) (Amd. 2008 Code)
CHAPTER 3: COIN-OPERATED AMUSEMENT DEVICES
Section
   
3-3-1   Definitions
   
3-3-2   License Required; Sticker Displayed
   
3-3-3   Application For License
   
3-3-4   License Fee
   
3-3-5   Change Of Location; Nontransferability Of License
   
3-3-6   Display Of License
   
3-3-7   Placement Of Devices
   
3-3-8   Days And Hours Of Operation; Exemptions
   
3-3-9   Prohibited Acts And Conditions
   
3-3-10   Inspections
§ 3-3-1 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions apply unless the
context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   COIN-OPERATED AMUSEMENT DEVICE. Any amusement machine or device operated by
means of the insertion of a coin, token or currency for the purpose of
amusement or skill and for the playing of which a fee is charged. The term
includes, but is not limited to, electronic video games, pinball machines, pool
or billiard tables or other similar games. The term does not include vending
machines in which are not incorporated gaming or amusement features, and the
term does not include jukeboxes. The term COIN-OPERATED AMUSEMENT DEVICE or
DEVICE or MACHINE or LICENSED DEVICE shall not include any VIDEO GAMING
TERMINAL in any LICENSED ESTABLISHMENT or in any LICENSED FRATERNAL
ESTABLISHMENT or in any LICENSED VETERANS ESTABLISHMENT or in any LICENSED
TRUCK STOP ESTABLISHMENT as those terms are defined or are incorporated by
reference in chapter 7 of title 3 of the this code.
   OPERATOR. Any person, firm, corporation, partnership, association or club
who sets up for the operation by another, or leases, or distributes for the
purpose of operation by another, any devices herein defined, whether such
setting up for operation, leasing or distributing be for a fixed charge or
rental, or on the basis of a division of the income derived from such device,
or otherwise.
   PROPRIETOR. Any person, firm, corporation, partnership, association or a
club, who, as the owner, lessee, or proprietor, has under his, her or its
control any establishment, place or premises in or at which such device is
placed or kept for use or play, or on exhibition for the purpose of such use or
play.
(1987 Code, § 7-4-1) (Ord. 790, passed 7-9-2012)
§ 3-3-2 LICENSE REQUIRED; STICKER DISPLAYED.
   No person, firm or corporation shall engage in the business of an operator
of coin-operated amusement devices or pool or billiard tables within the
corporate limits of the village without first having obtained the proper
license therefor. The license shall display a sticker provided by the village
indicating the number of coin-operated amusement devices or pool or billiard
tables permitted under the license.
(1987 Code, § 7-4-2)
§ 3-3-3 APPLICATION FOR LICENSE.
   Application for license shall be verified by oath or affidavit and shall
contain the following information:
   A.   The name, age and address of the applicant in the case of an
individual; and in the case of a copartnership, of the persons entitled to
share in the profits thereof; and in the case of a corporation, the date of
incorporation, the objects for which it was organized, the names and addresses
of the officers and directors, and if a majority in interest of the stock of
such corporation is owned by one person or his or her nominees, the name and
address of such person;
   B.   The citizenship of the applicant, his or her place of birth, or if a
naturalized citizen, the time and place of his or her naturalization;
   C.   The address of the place where the applicant proposes to operate;
   D.   A statement whether the applicant has made a similar application for
similar license on premises other than those described in the application and
the disposition of such application;
   E.   A statement that the applicant has never been convicted of a felony and
is not disqualified to receive the license under this section; and
   F.   The number of coin-operated amusement devices or pool or billiard
tables to be located on the business premises.
(1987 Code, § 7-4-3)
§ 3-3-4 LICENSE FEE.
   The annual fee, effective January 1, 1986, for such license shall be $25 per
year for each coin-operated amusement device or pool or billiard table set up
for operation, leased or distributed to a proprietor.
(1987 Code, § 7-4-5)
§ 3-3-5 CHANGE OF LOCATION; NONTRANSFERABILITY OF LICENSE.
   The location of a license may be changed only upon the written permission of
the mayor. Any license issued hereunder shall be nonassignable and
nontransferable.
(1987 Code, § 7-4-6)
§ 3-3-6 DISPLAY OF LICENSE.
   Every licensee shall frame and hang his or her license in a conspicuous
place in the licensed premises.
(1987 Code, § 7-4-8)
§ 3-3-7 PLACEMENT OF DEVICES.
   All licensed devices shall, at all times, be kept and placed in plain view
of any person or persons who may frequent or be in any place of business where
such devices are kept or used.
(1987 Code, § 7-4-7)
§ 3-3-8 DAYS AND HOURS OF OPERATION; EXEMPTIONS.
   No establishment operating under a license issued under this chapter shall
be open for use of any such devices between the hours of 12:00 a.m. and 6:00
a.m. on any day or between 12:00 a.m. Saturday and 12:00 p.m. the following
Sunday. All holders of liquor licenses are exempt from the hours in this
section.
(1987 Code, § 7-4-10)
§ 3-3-9 PROHIBITED ACTS AND CONDITIONS.
   A.   Gambling. Nothing in this chapter shall be construed to authorize,
permit or license any gambling device of any nature whatsoever.
   B.   Prizes. It shall be unlawful for any person receiving a license
pursuant to this chapter to give or award a cash prize or equivalent to any
person playing any of the tables, devices or machines enumerated herein under
tournament, league or any other individual or competitive play.
(1987 Code, § 7-4-7)
   C.   Minors On Premises Of Establishments Selling Liquor. It shall be
unlawful for any person, persons, firm or corporation having a license from the
village to keep a billiard or pool table or any other table or pin or bowling
alley or any coin-operated amusement device or any other device mentioned or
referred to in § 3-3-1 in connection with any business in which alcoholic
liquor is sold or served, to suffer or permit any person under the age of 21
years to play any games upon any such table, alley or device or to frequent,
remain or be harbored in or about such place of business unless such person or
persons under the age of 21 years are accompanied by a parent or guardian. A
violation of this subsection C. shall result in a penalty fine as provided in §
1-4-1  for each offense.
(1987 Code, § 7-4-4) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 3-3-10 INSPECTIONS.
   The chief of police has the power to and shall inspect any place, building
or premises in which any licensed device or devices are operated or set up for
operation at such times and intervals as he or she may deem necessary for the
proper enforcement of this chapter.
(1987 Code, § 7-4-9)
CHAPTER 4: RAFFLES
Section
   
3-4-1   Definitions
   
3-4-2   License And Compliance Required
   
3-4-3   Qualifications Of Licensee; Ineligibility For License
   
3-4-4   Application For License; Fee; Issuance Or Denial; Revocation Or
Suspension
   
3-4-5   Contents Of License
   
3-4-6   Limitations On Raffles
   
3-4-7   Raffles Manager; Bond
   
3-4-8   Records And Reports
   
3-4-9   Penalty
§ 3-4-1 DEFINITIONS.
   The village adopts and incorporates herein definitions of NET PROCEEDS and
RAFFLE as set forth in the Raffle and Poker Runs Act, 230 ILCS 15/0.01 et seq.
(1987 Code, § 7-5-1)
§ 3-4-2 LICENSE AND COMPLIANCE REQUIRED.
   A.   No person, firm or organization shall conduct raffles or chances within
the corporate limits of the village, except in accordance with this chapter and
only after an organization herein defined has first obtained a license issued
in accordance with this chapter.
(1987 Code, § 7-5-2)
   B.   All licenses issued are subject to all terms and provisions as set
forth in the Raffle and Poker Runs Act, 230 ILCS 15/0.01 et seq.
(1987 Code, § 7-5-4) (Amd. 2008 Code)
 
§ 3-4-3 QUALIFICATIONS OF LICENSEE; INELIGIBILITY FOR LICENSE.
   A.   Licenses shall be issued only to qualified bona fide religious,
charitable, labor, business, fraternal, educational or veterans organizations
that operate without profit to their members, and which have been in existence
continuously for a period of five years immediately before making application
for a license, and which have had, during that entire five-year period, a bona
fide membership engaged in carrying out their objects; or to a nonprofit
fundraising organization that the village board of trustees determines is
organized for the sole purpose of providing financial assistance to an
identified individual or group of individuals suffering extreme financial
hardship as the result of an illness, disability, accident or disaster. The
village hereby adopts and incorporates the definitions of such organizations as
set forth in the Raffle and Poker Runs Act, 230 ILCS 15/0.01 et seq.
(1987 Code, § 7-5-2)
   B.   The following persons and/or groups are ineligible for any license to
conduct a raffle:
      1.   Any person who has been convicted of a felony;
      2.   Any person who is, or has been, a professional gambler or gambling
promoter;
      3.   Any person who is not of good moral character;
      4.   Any firm or corporation in which a person defined in subsections
B.1. through B.3. above has proprietary, equitable or credit interest or in
which such a person is active or employed;
      5.   Any organization in which a person defined in subsections B.1.
through B.3. above is an officer, director or employee, whether compensated or
not; and
      6.   Any organization in which a person defined in subsections B.1.
through B.3. above is to participate in the management or operation of a raffle
as defined in this chapter.
(1987 Code, § 7-5-5)
§ 3-4-4 APPLICATION FOR LICENSE; FEE; ISSUANCE OR DENIAL; REVOCATION OR
SUSPENSION.
   Written application for a raffle license shall be completed and signed in
the form which may be approved by the village board of trustees from time to
time. A fee of $1 shall be paid by the licensee prior to issuance of such
license. This license shall be valid for one raffle, or for a specified number
of raffles, to be conducted during a specified period not to exceed one year,
and may be suspended or revoked by the village board of trustees for any
violation of the Raffle and Poker Runs Act or this chapter. The village board
of trustees shall act on a license application within 30 days from the date of
the receipt of said application. The village clerk is hereby authorized to
issue such a license if the village president finds such application is in
accordance with this chapter and such application is approved by the village
president. The village clerk shall present a report to the village board of
trustees each month listing all licenses issued hereunder for the preceding
month, if any. Such license may be refused to an organization that has
previously violated any provision of this chapter after having been granted a
license under this chapter.
(1987 Code, § 7-5-3)
§ 3-4-5 CONTENTS OF LICENSE.
   The application and license shall specify the area or areas within the
village in which raffle chances will be sold or issued and the time period
during which raffle chances will be sold or issued, and shall state the time of
determination of winning chances and the location or locations at which the
winning chances will be determined.
(1987 Code, § 7-5-4)
§ 3-4-6 LIMITATIONS ON RAFFLES.
   A.   Prizes, Merchandise And Chances.
      1.   The aggregate retail value of all prizes or merchandise awarded by a
licensee in a single raffle shall not exceed $50,000.
      2.   The maximum retail value of each prize awarded by a licensee in a
single raffle shall not exceed $50,000.
(1987 Code, § 7-5-4)
      3.   The maximum price which may be charged for each chance issued or
sold shall not exceed $500.
(1987 Code, § 7-5-4) (Amd. 2008 Code)
      4.   The maximum number of days during which chances may be issued or
sold shall not exceed 365.
      5.   All licenses issued hereunder shall only authorize the sale of
raffle chances within the corporate limits of the village.
(1987 Code, § 7-5-4)
   B.   Conduct Of Raffle. The conducting of raffles is subject to the
following restrictions.
      1.   The entire net proceeds of any raffle must be exclusively devoted to
the lawful purposes of the organization permitted to conduct the game.
      2.   No person except a bona fide member of the sponsoring organization
may participate in the management or operation of the raffle.
      3.   No person may receive any remuneration or profit for participating
in the management or operation of the raffle.
      4.   A licensee may rent a premises on which to determine the winning
chance or chances in a raffle only from an organization which is also licensed
under this chapter.
      5.   Raffle chances may be sold or issued only within the area specified
on the license, and winning chances may be determined only at those locations
specified on the license.
      6.   A person under the age of 18 years may participate in the conducting
of raffles or chances only with the permission of his or her parent or
guardian. A person under the age of 18 years may be within the area where
winning chances are being determined only when accompanied by his or her parent
or guardian.
      7.   If a lessor rents premises where a winning chance or chances are
determined, the lessor shall not be liable to prosecution for violation of this
chapter if the person who uses the premises for the determining of winning
chances does not hold a license issued by the village under the provisions of
this chapter.
(1987 Code, § 7-5-6)
§ 3-4-7 RAFFLES MANAGER; BOND.
   A.   All operations of and the conduct of raffles shall be under the
supervision of a single raffles manager designated by the licensed
organization. The manager shall give a fidelity bond in the sum of $1,000, or
in the sum of the aggregate retail value of all prizes and merchandise awarded
by a licensee in the raffle, whichever is greater, in favor of the
organization, conditioned upon his or her honesty in the performance of his or
her duties.
   B.    Terms of the bond shall provide that notice shall be given in writing
to the village board of trustees not less than 30 days prior to any
cancellation. The village board of trustees may waive this bond requirement,
including a waiver provision in the license issued to the organization;
provided, that such license containing such waiver provision shall be granted
only by unanimous vote of the members of the licensed organization.
(1987 Code, § 7-5-7)
§ 3-4-8 RECORDS AND REPORTS.
   A.   Records Kept.
      1.   Each organization licensed to conduct raffles and chances shall keep
records of its gross receipts, expenses and net proceeds for each single
gathering or occasion at which winning chances are determined. All deductions
from gross receipts for each single gathering or occasion shall be documented
with receipts or other records indicating the amount, a description of the
purchased item or service or other reason for the deduction and the recipient.
The distribution of net proceeds shall be itemized as to payee, purpose, amount
and date of payment.
      2.   Gross receipts from the operation of raffles programs shall be
segregated from other revenues of the organization, including bingo gross
receipts if bingo games are also conducted by the same nonprofit organization
pursuant to license therefor issued by the state department of revenue, and
placed in a separate account. Each organization shall have separate records of
its raffles. The person who accounts for gross receipts, expenses and net
proceeds from the operation shall not be the same person who accounts for other
revenue of the organization.
(1987 Code, § 7-5-8)
   B.   Reports Required. Each organization licensed to conduct raffles shall
report promptly after the conclusion of each raffle to its membership and,
within 30 days, to the village board of trustees, its gross receipts, expenses,
net proceeds from raffles, and the distribution of net proceeds itemized as
required in this section.
(1987 Code, § 7-5-8) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   C.   Preservation Of Records. Records required by this section shall be
preserved for three years, and organizations shall make available their records
relating to the operation of raffles for public inspection at reasonable times
and places.
(1987 Code, § 7-5-8)
§ 3-4-9 PENALTY.
   Any person who violates any of the provisions of this chapter shall, upon
conviction thereof, for each offense, be punished as provided in §
1-4-1 of this code.
(1987 Code, § 7-5-9)
CHAPTER 5: PEDDLERS, TRANSIENT MERCHANTS AND ITINERANT VENDORS
Section
   
3-5-1   Definitions
   
3-5-2   License, Certificate And Compliance Required; Items Restricted

3-5-8   Receipts For Purchase Of New And Unused Property

3-5-11   Failure To Obtain License; Inventory Held
   
3-5-12   Penalty
§ 3-5-1 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions apply unless the
context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   FLEA MARKET. Any location, other than a permanent retail store, at which
space is rented or otherwise made available to others for conduct of business
as a transient merchant or itinerant vendor.
   ITINERANT VENDOR. Any person who transports tangible personal property for
retail sale within the village and who does not maintain in the village an
established office, distribution house, sales house, warehouse, service center,
or residence from which such business is conducted, it shall be prima facie
evidence that a person is an ITINERANT VENDOR if the person does not transact
business from a fixed location or if the person does not own, or lease for a
term of at least six months, the property from which business is conducted.
However, the term ITINERANT VENDOR shall not include any of the following
persons:
      1.   Any person who delivers tangible personal property within the
village who is fulfilling an order for such property which was solicited or
placed by mail, phone, fax, e-mail or other similar communication means; and
      2.   A peddler.
   NEW AND UNUSED PROPERTY. Tangible property that is acquired in the ordinary
course of business by the unused property merchant directly from the producer,
manufacturer, wholesaler or retailer, that has not been used since its
production or manufacture or is in its original and unopened package when
originally produced or manufactured.
   PEDDLER. Any person who is engaged in the retail or wholesale sale of goods,
wares, merchandise, services, or real or personal property of any kind, nature,
or extent whatsoever, or who is engaged in soliciting funds or contributions
and who, for the purpose of conducting any such business, travels on foot or in
any vehicle from house to house or from place to place within the village or on
village owned property. However, the term PEDDLER shall not include any of the
following persons:
      1.   Farmers, fruit growers and gardeners who sell the produce of their
farm, orchard or vineyard;
      2.   Persons who make sales presentations pursuant to and after making
previously scheduled appointment(s) or arrangement(s) with the owner or legal
occupants of the premises;
      3.   Sellers or deliverers of newspapers;
      4.   Persons who, without receiving any compensation for their services,
sell or solicit for any civic, patriotic, fraternal, educational, religious or
benevolent organization, provided such organization is recognized as a tax
exempt organization under the federal internal revenue service laws, rules and
regulations;
      5.   Any political candidate and/or persons who solicit support for
ideas, votes or financial contributions for any political candidate.
      6.   Persons who are not engaged in the sale of any goods, wares, or
merchandise or solicit funds or financial contributions but solely convey or
solicit ideas to or from any owner or occupant of property; and
      7.   Any person who conveys religious messages or ideas or doorstep
proselytizing to the owner(s) or occupant(s) of any property.
   PERSON. Any individual, corporation, partnership, trust, firm, association,
or other entity.
   TRANSIENT MERCHANT. Any person who is engaged temporarily in the retail sale
of goods, wares or merchandise in the village and who, for the purpose of
conducting such business, occupies any building, room, vehicle, structure of
any kind or vacant lot. It shall be prima facie evidence that a person is a
TRANSIENT MERCHANT if the person does not transact business from a fixed
location or if the person does not own, or lease for a term of at least six
months, the property from which business is conducted. However, the term
TRANSIENT MERCHANT shall not include any of the following persons:
      1.   Any person selling goods, wares or merchandise which is raised,
produced or manufactured by him or her;
      2.   Any person selling vegetables, fruits, or perishable farm products
at an established village market;
      3.   Any person operating a stand or booth on or adjacent to property
owned by him or her or upon which he or she resides;
      4.   Any person operating a stand or booth at a trade show, exposition,
convention or similar event; and
      5.   Any person selling any goods, wares or merchandise at a special
event sponsored by any recognized not for profit charitable, civic, patriotic,
fraternal, educational or religious organization.
   VILLAGE. The village of Edinburg, in Christian County, Illinois, a municipal
corporation.
(1987 Code, § 7-2-1)
§ 3-5-2 LICENSE, CERTIFICATE AND COMPLIANCE REQUIRED; ITEMS RESTRICTED.
   It is unlawful for any person, either as principal or agent, to conduct
business as a peddler, transient merchant or itinerant vendor within the
village without first complying with the requirements of the Retailers’
Occupation Tax Act § 2(a), being 35 ILCS 120/2(a), by obtaining a certificate
of registration and without first having obtained the appropriate license from
the village police department under this chapter. No license shall be issued to
any peddler, transient merchant, or itinerant vendor to engage in business
involving any meats, fish or poultry of any kind, nature or extent whatsoever
unless such peddler, transient merchant or itinerant vendor possesses the
necessary and current licenses or permits from all federal, state, or local
regulatory agencies having jurisdiction over the handling, sale or delivery of
such items. It is unlawful for any peddler, transient merchant or itinerant
vendor to engage in any business involving any meats, fish or poultry of any
kind, nature or extent whatsoever within the village without the necessary
licenses or permits from all such federal, state or local regulatory agencies.
(1987 Code, § 7-2-2)
§ 3-5-3 APPLICATIONS FOR LICENSES.
   A.   Peddlers. Any person who wishes to obtain a license as a peddler shall
file a written license application with the village police department on a form
approved by the village chief of police from time to time, and such application
shall set forth the following information, and the applicant shall provide the
following documents:
      1.   The applicant’s name, permanent business address (which must include
a street address), and phone number, and residence address and phone number of
the principal (and if the applicant is a corporation, the residence addresses
and phone numbers of its officers); the names, addresses and telephone numbers
of all persons who will engage in the business pursuant to the license to be
granted;
      2.   The nature of the business the applicant intends to conduct and a
description of the goods, wares, merchandise, services and property intended to
be sold or solicited under the issued license;
      3.   The specific dates, days of the week, and times during which such
business is desired to be conducted under the issued license. No one peddler
license shall be issued for any longer period than 25 days within a 30-
consecutive-day period. Any subsequent written application(s) by the same
peddler for another peddler license must be submitted with the requisite fees
and be in compliance with this subsection A.;
      4.   The current name, business address, and telephone numbers of the
applicant’s employer or contractor, if any;
      5.   For all peddlers who intend to sell tangible personal property, a
copy of the applicant’s certificate of registration under the Retailers’
Occupation Tax Act, being 35 ILCS 120/1 et seq., and the applicant’s state
business registration number issued by the state department of revenue, and all
other evidence to the satisfaction of the village police department that sales
of tangible personal property made within the village will be reported as
taxable sales within the village to the state department of revenue;
      6.   Current photo identification of the applicant and for each person
who will be engaged in the business under the license to be granted; and
      7.   The license plate number, model, make, year and color of any vehicle
that will be used while engaged in business.
(1987 Code, § 7-2-3)
   B.   Transient Merchants And Itinerant Vendors. Any person who wishes to
obtain a license as a transient merchant or itinerant vendor shall file a
written license application with the village police department on a form
approved the village chief of police from time to time, and such application
shall set forth the following information, and the applicant shall provide the
following documents:
      1.   The applicant’s name, permanent business address (which must include
a street address), and phone number, and residence address and phone number of
the principal (and if the applicant is a corporation, the residence addresses
and phone numbers of its officers);
      2.   The location in the village at which the applicant intends to do
business and the specific dates, days of the week, and times during which such
business will be conducted. In the case of a transient merchant, the
application shall also include the specific location and address in the village
from which the business of a transient merchant will be conducted during the
transient merchant license period applied for and shall identify the legal
names of all owners of such location from which the business of a transient
merchant will be conducted. Such applicant shall further submit a written lease
or other written evidence of the applicant’s authorization to conduct the
business of a transient merchant upon such property during all such times
applied for;
      3.   The nature of the business the applicant intends to conduct;
      4.   For all transient merchants or itinerant vendors who intend to sell
tangible personal property within the village, a copy of the applicant’s
certificate of registration under the Retailers’ Occupation Tax Act, being 35
ILCS 120/1 et seq., and the applicant’s state business registration number
issued by the state department of revenue, and all other evidence to the
satisfaction of the village police department that sales of tangible personal
property made within the village will be reported as taxable sales within the
village to the state department of revenue;
      5.   A complete inventory of the goods the applicant intends to offer for
sale;
      6.   A list of all licenses to conduct business as a transient merchant
or an itinerant vendor obtained by the applicant in the state in the 12 months
preceding the date of filing of the application;
      7.   Current photo identification of the applicant and for each person
who will be engaged in the business under the license to be granted; and
      8.   The license plate number, model, make, year and color of any vehicle
that will be used while engaged in business.
(1987 Code, § 7-2-4)
   C.   Forms. The forms are as follows.
