CHAPTER 1: POLICE DEPARTMENT
Section
   
5-1-1   Established
   
5-1-2   Chief Of Police
   
5-1-3   Additional Police Officers
   
5-1-4   Qualifications
   
5-1-5   Compensation
   
5-1-6   Duties Of Police Officers
   
5-1-7   Legal Processes
   
5-1-8   Serve As Witnesses; Fees Prohibited
   
5-1-9   Aid To Fire Department
   
5-1-10   Removal From Department
   
5-1-11   Mutual Aid Contract
   
5-1-12   Special Police
   
5-1-13   Assisting Police Required
   
5-1-14   Obstructing Police, Aiding In Escape Prohibited
   
5-1-15   Rules And Regulations
§ 5-1-1 ESTABLISHED.
   There is hereby established a department of the municipal government of the
village which shall be known as the police department. The police department
shall be under the control and supervision of the mayor and board of trustees.
(1987 Code, § 30-2-1)
§ 5-1-2 CHIEF OF POLICE.
   A.   Office Established; Appointment. There is hereby established the office
of the chief of police. The chief shall be appointed by the mayor, with the
advice and consent of the village board.
(1987 Code, § 30-2-2)
   B.   Duties. The chief of police shall:
      1.   Keep records and make reports concerning the activities of his or
her department as may be required;
      2.   Be responsible for the performance of the police department and all
its functions, and all persons who are members of the department shall serve
subject to the orders of the chief of police;
(1987 Code, § 30-2-3)
      3.   Attend all meetings of the village board, execute all orders of the
board and close the board chamber upon the adjournment of that body. He or she
shall also execute all warrants or other legal processes required to be
executed by him or her under any ordinance of the village or laws of the state;
and
(1987 Code, § 30-2-7)
      4.   Be the custodian of all lost and abandoned or stolen property in the
village.
(1987 Code, § 30-2-17)
§ 5-1-3 ADDITIONAL POLICE OFFICERS.
   A.   General. The mayor and board of trustees may determine, from time to
time, to add a full-time or part-time police officer or full-time or part-time
police officers to the police department. If a decision to add a police officer
or police officers is made, then such police officer or police officers shall
be appointed by the mayor, by and with the advice and consent of the village
board, and they shall severally hold office for such time, not to exceed one
year, as may be designated at the time of their appointment, and shall each,
before entering into the performance of their duties, take the oath or
affirmation required by law.
   B.   Duties. A part-time police officer shall have all the responsibilities
of a full-time police officer and such duties as may be established under §
5-1-6 , but the number of hours a part-time police officer may work within a
calendar year is restricted. Part-time police officers shall not be assigned to
supervise or direct full-time police officers. Full-time and part-time police
officers shall be trained in accordance with the Illinois Police Training Act,
being 50 ILCS 705/1 et seq., and the rules and requirements of the ILETSB.
   C.   Hiring Standards And Qualifications. Any person employed as a full-time
or as a part-time police officer must meet the following standards and
qualifications:
      1.   Be of good moral character, of temperate habits, of sound health,
and physically and mentally able to perform assigned duties;
      2.   Be at least 21 years of age;
      3.   Pass a medical examination;
      4.   Possess a high school diploma or GED certificate;
      5.   Possess a valid state driver’s license;
      6.   Possess no prior felony convictions;
      7.   Any individual who has served in the United States military must
have been honorably discharged; and
      8.   Must meet the additional qualifications as set forth in §
5-1-4.
   D.   Discipline. Full-time and part-time officers shall be under the
disciplinary jurisdiction of the chief of police and the village board. Full-
time and part-time police officers serve at the discretion of the village
board, shall not have any property rights in said employment and may be removed
by the village board at any time with or without cause. Full-time and part-time
police officers shall comply with all applicable rules and orders issued by the
chief of police and/or by the village board
(Ord. 787, passed 3-12-2012)
§ 5-1-4 QUALIFICATIONS.
   All members of the police department shall be both when appointed and while
serving as an village police officer are subject to the following residential
requirements:
   A.   Part-time village police officers may reside outside the corporate
limits of the village both at the time of his, her, or their appointment and
while serving as a part-time police officer;
   B.   The appointed full-time chief of police shall be a qualified elector
and resident of the village both when appointed and while serving as the chief
of police; provided, however, any hereafter appointed full-time chief of police
shall have 90 days from the date of his or her initial appointment to become a
qualified elector and resident of the village; and provided, further, that such
90-day period may be extended, from time to time, for an additional reasonable
specified time period by an affirmative vote of the village’s corporate
authorities for good cause shown as may be determined in the sole discretion of
the village’s corporate authorities; and
   C.   Except for the chief of police, all other full-time police officers
must reside within a ten-mile radius of the corporate limits of the village
within 90 days from the date of his or her initial appointment.
(Ord. 824, passed 10-12-2015)
§ 5-1-5 COMPENSATION.
   Members of the police department shall receive such compensation as may be
provided by the village board pursuant to motion or resolution from time to
time.
(1987 Code, § 30-2-6)
§ 5-1-6 DUTIES OF POLICE OFFICERS.
   A.   All police officers shall devote his, her, or their entire time to the
performance of the duties of his, her, or their office, and are hereby charged
with the preservation of the peace, order and safety of the village, and with
the duty of protecting the rights of persons and property, and of enforcing all
laws and all orders of the village board of trustees. Notwithstanding the
above, the village board of trustees may from time to time by affirmative
majority motion allow the police chief to work as a part-time employee of the
county sheriff’s office in the capacity of a part-time county deputy sheriff,
provided that:
      1.   The police chief’s duties and responsibilities and full-time work
schedule for the village are not diminished or adversely effected;
      2.   The part-time work schedule as a deputy sheriff is approved from
time to time by the village president;
      3.   The county sheriff’s office provides to the village president from
time to time a certificate of liability and worker’s compensation insurance in
such amounts satisfactory to the village president that evidences that the
police chief is afforded liability and worker’s compensation insurance
protection and benefits concerning his or her duties, responsibilities and work
as a part-time employee of the county sheriff’s office;
      4.   During the course of such part-time work, the police chief shall not
wear any uniform or insignia or equipment or display any badge or police
identification that signifies that he or she is also a police officer;
      5.   The police chief complies with such other terms and conditions
determined from time to time concerning the initial approval for, and/or the
continued approval for, any such part-time work as a county deputy sheriff; and
      6.   The village board of trustees can rescind any such approval for such
part-time work from time to time for any or no reason whatsoever.
   B.   All police officers shall take notice of all nuisances, obstructions
and defects on the highways or other public places, and shall cause the same to
be abated or removed, or immediate notice thereof given to the proper officer
whose duty it may be to take action in relation thereto.
   C.   All members of the police department shall perform such other duties as
may be established in writing by the mayor and village board from time to time.
(1987 Code, § 30-2-7) (Ord. 859, passed 2-11-2019)
§ 5-1-7 LEGAL PROCESSES.
   All police shall have the power and authority to execute village warrants or
other like legal processes without the corporate limits of the village and
within such distance therefrom as authorized by law, in all cases when any
ordinances of the village board made pursuant to law shall prescribe a penalty
for the violation of any of its provisions by persons residing, acting or doing
business within the limits of the village.
(1987 Code, § 30-2-10)
§ 5-1-8 SERVE AS WITNESSES; FEES PROHIBITED.
   Any member of the police department shall appear as witness whenever this is
necessary in a prosecution for a violation of this code or of any state or
federal law. No such member shall retain any witness fee for service as witness
in any action or suit to which the village is a party, and fees paid for such
services shall be turned over to the chief of police, who shall deposit the
same with the village treasurer.
(1987 Code, § 30-2-15)
§ 5-1-9 AID TO FIRE DEPARTMENT.
   Every police officer shall aid the fire department by giving the alarm in
case of fire, and in clearing the streets or grounds in the immediate vicinity
of any fire so that the firefighters shall not be hindered or obstructed in the
performance of his, her or their duties.
(1987 Code, § 30-2-13)
§ 5-1-10 REMOVAL FROM DEPARTMENT.
   Any member of the police department who shall neglect or refuse to perform
any duty required of him or her by this code or the rules and regulations of
the department, or who shall, in the discharge of his or her official duties,
be guilty of any fraud, favoritism, extortion, oppression or willful wrong or
injustice, shall be subject to removal from office.
(1987 Code, § 30-2-12)
§ 5-1-11 MUTUAL AID CONTRACT.
   The police department, with the approval of the mayor and village board, may
enter into an agreement to provide police protection to neighboring
municipalities.
(1987 Code, § 30-2-8)
§ 5-1-12 SPECIAL POLICE.
   The mayor may, on special occasions when, in his or her judgment, public
peace and order of the village shall require, appoint and commission any number
of special police as may be necessary and shall fix, in order of their
appointment, the time during which each shall serve; and all such special
police, during such time, shall possess the powers and exercise the duties of
regular police officers; provided, that their appointment, if for more than ten
days, shall be subject to the consent of the village board in the manner that
other appointments to office by the mayor are subject.
(1987 Code, § 30-2-9)
§ 5-1-13 ASSISTING POLICE REQUIRED.
   Every police officer of the village may, at any time, call upon any able
bodied person above the age of 18 years to aid him or her in the arresting or
retaking or holding in custody of any person guilty of or having committed any
unlawful act or charged therewith, or to aid such officer in preventing the
commission of any unlawful act.
(1987 Code, § 30-2-11) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 5-1-14 OBSTRUCTING POLICE, AIDING IN ESCAPE PROHIBITED.
   A.   It shall be unlawful for any person in the village to resist or
obstruct any member of the police force in the discharge of his or her duty, or
to endeavor to, in any manner, assist any person in the custody of any member
of the police force to escape or to attempt to escape from such custody, or to
attempt to rescue any such person in custody.
   B.   Any person in violation of this section shall, upon conviction thereof,
be fined as provided in §
1-4-1 for each separate offense, and a separate offense shall be deemed
committed on each day during or on which a violation or failure occurs or
continues.
(1987 Code, § 30-2-14) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 5-1-15 RULES AND REGULATIONS.
   The chief of police may make or prescribe such rules and regulations for the
conduct and guidance of the members of the police department as he or she shall
deem advisable, and such rules, when approved by the mayor and village board,
shall be binding upon such members.
(1987 Code, § 30-2-16)
CHAPTER 2: EMERGENCY MANAGEMENT
ARTICLE A: EMERGENCY SERVICES AND DISASTER AGENCY (ESDA)
Section
   
5-2A-1   Established
   
5-2A-2   Coordinator
   
5-2A-3   Functions
   
5-2A-4   Service As Mobile Support Team
   
5-2A-5   Agreements With Other Political Subdivisions
   
5-2A-6   Emergency Action
   
5-2A-7   Compensation
   
5-2A-8   Reimbursement By State
   
5-2A-9   Purchases And Expenditures
   
5-2A-10   Oath Of Office
   
5-2A-11   Office
   
5-2A-12   Appropriation; Tax Levy
§ 5-2A-1 ESTABLISHED.
   There is hereby created the village ESDA to prevent, minimize, repair and
alleviate injury or damage resulting from disaster caused by enemy attack,
sabotage or other hostile action, or from natural or human-made disaster, in
accordance with the Illinois Emergency Management Agency Act, being 20 ILCS
3305/1 et seq., and any amendments thereto. The ESDA shall consist of the
coordinator and such additional members as may be selected by the coordinator.
(1987 Code, § 30-3-1) (Amd. 2008 Code)
Statutory reference:
   Similar provisions, see 20 ILCS 3305/1 et seq.
§ 5-2A-2 COORDINATOR.
   A.   Appointment; Term. The coordinator of the village ESDA shall be
appointed by the mayor, with the advice and consent of the board of trustees,
and shall serve until removed by the same.
(1987 Code, § 30-3-2) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   B.   Powers And Duties. The coordinator shall have direct responsibility for
the organization, administration, training and operation of the ESDA, subject
to the direction and control of the mayor, as provided by statute.
(1987 Code, § 30-3-2)
§ 5-2A-3 FUNCTIONS.
   The ESDA shall coordinate and may perform such ESDA functions within the
village as are prescribed in and by the state emergency operations plan, and
programs, orders, rules and regulations as may be promulgated by the village,
by the Illinois Emergency Management Agency Act, being 20 ILCS 3305/1 et seq.,
and by village ordinance, and in addition, shall conduct such functions outside
the village corporate limits as may be required pursuant to any mutual aid
agreement with any other political subdivision, municipality, or quasi-
municipality entered into as provided in the Illinois Emergency Management
Agency Act, and any amendments thereto.
(1987 Code, § 30-3-3) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 5-2A-4 SERVICE AS MOBILE SUPPORT TEAM.
   A.   Members Designated. All or any members of the ESDA organization may be
designated as members of a mobile support team created by the director of the
state EMA, as provided by law.
(1987 Code, § 30-3-4) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   B.   Team Leader. The leader of such mobile support team shall be designated
by the coordinator of the ESDA organization.
   C.   Compensation; Powers And Duties. Any member of a mobile support team
who is a village employee or officer while serving on call to duty by the
governor, or the state director, shall receive the compensation and have the
powers, duties, rights and immunities incident to such employment or office.
Any such member who is not a paid officer or employee of the village while so
serving shall receive from the state reasonable compensation as provided by
law.
(1987 Code, § 30-3-4)
§ 5-2A-5 AGREEMENTS WITH OTHER POLITICAL SUBDIVISIONS.
   The coordinator of ESDA may negotiate mutual aid agreements with other
villages or political subdivisions of the state, but no such agreement shall be
effective until it has been approved by the mayor.
(1987 Code, § 30-3-5)
§ 5-2A-6 EMERGENCY ACTION.
   If the governor proclaims that a disaster emergency exists in the event of
actual enemy attack upon the United States or the occurrence within the state
resulting from enemy sabotage or other hostile action, or from human-made or
natural disaster, it shall be the duty of the ESDA to cooperate fully with the
state EMA and with the governor in the exercise of emergency powers as provided
by law.
(1987 Code, § 30-3-6) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 5-2A-7 COMPENSATION.
   Members of the ESDA who are paid employees or officers of the village, if
called for training by the state director of EMA, shall receive for the time
spent in such training the same rate of pay as is attached to the position
held; members who are not such village employees or officers shall receive for
such training such compensation as may be established by the mayor.
(1987 Code, § 30-3-7) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 5-2A-8 REIMBURSEMENT BY STATE.
   The village treasurer may receive and allocate to the appropriate fund, any
reimbursement by the state to the village for expenses incident to training
members of the ESDA as prescribed by the state director of EMA, compensation
for services and expenses of members of a mobile support team while serving
outside the village in response to a call by the governor or state director of
EMA, as provided by law, and any other reimbursement made by the state incident
to ESDA activities as provided by law.
(1987 Code, § 30-3-8) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 5-2A-9 PURCHASES AND EXPENDITURES.
   A.   The mayor may, on recommendation of the village coordinator of ESDA,
authorize any purchase of contracts necessary to place the village in a
position to combat effectively any disaster resulting from the explosion of any
nuclear or other bomb or missile, and to protect the public health and safety,
protect property and provide emergency assistance to victims in the case of
such disaster, or from human-made or natural disaster.
(1987 Code, § 30-3-9)
   B.   In the event of enemy caused or other disaster, the village coordinator
of ESDA is authorized on behalf of the village to procure such services,
supplies, equipment or materials as may be necessary for such purposes, in view
of the exigency without regard to the statutory procedures or formalities
normally prescribed by law pertaining to village contracts or obligations, as
authorized by the Illinois Emergency Management Agency Act, being 20 ILCS 3305/
1 et seq., and amendments thereto; provided, that if the mayor and village
board of trustees meet at such time, the coordinator shall act subject to the
directions and restrictions imposed by that body.
(1987 Code, § 30-3-9) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 5-2A-10 OATH OF OFFICE.
   Every person appointed to serve in any capacity in the village ESDA
organization shall, before entering upon his or her duties, subscribe to the
following oath, which shall be filed with the coordinator:
 
I, _______________, do solemnly swear (or affirm) that I will support and
defend and bear true faith and allegiance to the Constitution of the United
States of America, and the Constitution of the State of Illinois and the
territory, institutions and facilities thereof, both public and private,
against all enemies, foreign and domestic; that I take this obligation freely,
without any mental reservation or purpose of evasion; that I will well and
faithfully discharge the duties upon which I am about to enter. And I do
further swear (or affirm) that I do not advocate, nor am I, nor have I been a
member of any political party or organization that advocates the overthrow of
the government of the United States or of this State by force or violence; and
that during such time as I am affiliated with the ESDA organization, I will not
advocate, nor become a member of any political party or organization that
advocates the overthrow of the government of the United States or of this State
by force or violence.
 
(1987 Code, § 30-3-10)
§ 5-2A-11 OFFICE.
   The mayor is authorized to designate space in the village building, or
elsewhere as may be provided for by the mayor, for the village ESDA as its
office.
(1987 Code, § 30-3-11)
§ 5-2A-12 APPROPRIATION; TAX LEVY.
   The village corporate authorities may make an appropriation for ESDA
purposes in the manner provided by law, and may levy in addition, for ESDA
purposes only, a tax not to exceed $0.05 per $100 of the assessed value of all
taxable property in addition to all other taxes, as provided by 65 ILCS 5/8-3-
16; however, that amount collectable under such levy shall in no event exceed
$0.25 per capita.
(1987 Code, § 30-3-12) (Amd. 2008 Code)
ARTICLE B: CIVIL EMERGENCIES
Section
   
5-2B-1   Definitions
   
5-2B-2   Declaration Of Emergency
   
5-2B-3   Curfew
   
5-2B-4   Emergency Orders
   
5-2B-5   Duration Of Proclamation; Renewal; Extension
   
5-2B-6   Notice Requirements
§ 5-2B-1 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions apply unless the
context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   CIVIL EMERGENCY.
      1.   A riot or unlawful assembly characterized by the use of actual force
or violence or any power to execute by three or more persons acting together
without authority of law; or
      2.   Any natural disaster or human-made calamity, including flood,
conflagration, cyclone, tornado, earthquake or explosion within the corporate
limits of the village, resulting in the death or injury of persons or the
destruction of property to such an extent that extraordinary measures must be
taken to protect the public health, safety and welfare.
   CURFEW. A prohibition against any person or persons walking, running,
loitering, standing or motoring upon any alley, street, highway, public
property or vacant premises within the corporate limits of the village,
excepting officials of any governmental unit and persons officially designated
to duty with reference to the civil emergency.
(1987 Code, § 30-1-1)
§ 5-2B-2 DECLARATION OF EMERGENCY.
   Whenever an emergency, as defined in §
5-2B-1 exists, the mayor shall declare the existence by means of a written
declaration, setting forth the facts which constitute the emergency.
(1987 Code, § 30-1-2)
§ 5-2B-3 CURFEW.
   After proclamation of a civil emergency by the mayor, he or she may order a
general curfew applicable to such geographical areas of the village or to the
village as a whole as he or she deems advisable and applicable during such
hours of the day or night as he or she deems necessary in the interest of the
public safety and welfare.
(1987 Code, § 30-1-3)
§ 5-2B-4 EMERGENCY ORDERS.
   After the proclamation of a civil emergency, the mayor may also, in the
interest of public safety and welfare, make any or all of the following orders:
   A.   Order the closing of all retail liquor stores, including taverns and
private clubs or portions thereof wherein the consumption of intoxicating
liquor and beer is permitted;
   B.   Order the discontinuance of the sale of alcoholic liquor by any
wholesaler or retailer;
   C.   Order the discontinuance of selling, distributing or giving away of
gasoline or other liquid flammable or combustible products in any container
other than a gasoline tank properly affixed to a motor vehicle;
   D.   Order the discontinuance of selling, distributing, dispensing or giving
away of any firearms or ammunition of any character whatsoever; and
   E.   Issue such other orders as are imminently necessary for the protection
of life and property.
(1987 Code, § 30-1-4)
§ 5-2B-5 DURATION OF PROCLAMATION; RENEWAL; EXTENSION.
   The proclamation herein authorized shall be effective for a period of 48
hours unless sooner terminated by a proclamation of the mayor indicating that
the civil emergency no longer exists. The mayor shall have the power to
reproclaim the existence of a civil emergency at the end of each 48-hour period
during the time the civil emergency exists.
(1987 Code, § 30-1-5)
§ 5-2B-6 NOTICE REQUIREMENTS.
   Upon issuing the proclamation herein authorized, the mayor shall notify the
news media situated within the village and shall cause three copies of the
proclamation declaring the existence of the emergency to be posted at the
following places within the village:
   A.   Village hall;
   B.   Post office; and
   C.   County courthouse.
(1987 Code, § 30-1-6)
CHAPTER 3: ANIMAL CONTROL
Section
   
5-3-1   Definitions
   
5-3-2   Restraint Of Dog Required/Running At Large
   
5-3-3   Cruelty To Animals
   
5-3-4   Nuisance Dogs And Cats
   
5-3-5   Damage To Property
   
5-3-6   Dangerous Dogs And Vicious Dogs
   
5-3-7   Diseased Animals
   
5-3-8   Number Of Dogs And Cats
   
5-3-9   Animal Houses And Fences
   
5-3-10   Rabies Control
   
5-3-10-1   Dogs
   
5-3-10-2   Cats
   
5-3-11   Impoundment And Redemption Of Dogs Or Cats
   
5-3-12   Livestock
   
5-3-13   Methods Of Enforcement
   
5-3-14   Interference With Enforcement Official Prohibited
   
5-3-15   Penalties
§ 5-3-1 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions apply unless the
context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   CAT. Felis catus. Any feline, regardless of age or sex.
   CHIEF OF POLICE. The village’s chief of police.
   DANGEROUS DOG.
      1.   Any individual dog anywhere, other than upon the property of the
owner or custodian of the dog and unmuzzled, unleashed or unattended by its
owner or custodian that behaves in a manner that a reasonable person would
believe poses a serious and unjustified imminent threat of serious physical
injury or death to a person or a companion animal; or
      2.   A dog that, without justification, bites a person and does not cause
serious physical injury.
   DEPARTMENT OF AGRICULTURE. The department of agriculture of the state.
   DOG. All members of the family Canidae.
   INOCULATION AGAINST RABIES. The injection of a dog or cat with an anti-
rabies vaccine approved by a licensed veterinarian or anti-rabies clinic for
use in the prevention of rabies.
   KEEP OR HARBOR/KEEPING OR HARBORING. Habitually permitting to remain or to
be lodged or to be fed within the house, store, yard, enclosure or other place.
   LEASH. A cord, rope, strap or chain, which shall be securely fastened to the
collar or harness of a dog or other animal and shall be of sufficient strength
to keep such dog or other animal under control.
   LIVESTOCK. Any one or more cows, horses, sheep, swine, goats, chickens,
ducks, turkeys, rabbits, geese and/or bees.
   MINOR. Any person under the age of 18 years.
   OWN, OWNING, KEEPS. Unless otherwise indicated in the text, shall be deemed
to mean and include own, keep, harbor or have charge, custody or control of a
dog or dogs or cat or cats.
   OWNER or KEEPER. A person having a right of property in a dog or cat or
other animal, or keeps or harbors a dog or cat or other animal, or who has a
dog or cat or other animal in his or her care, or who acts as its custodian, or
who knowingly permits a dog or cat or other animal to remain on or about any
premises occupied by him or her. OWNER or KEEPER does not include a feral cat
caretaker participating in a trap, spay/neuter, return or release program.
   PARENT. Stepparent, foster parent, natural parent, adoptive parent or
guardian.
   PERSON. Any individual, firm, corporation, partnership, society, or
association, or other legal entity or any other business unit.
   RESTRAINT. A dog is under RESTRAINT within the meaning of this chapter if
the dog is controlled by a leash or other recognized control methods; or the
dog is within the property limits of the owner or keeper of the dog.
   RUNNING AT LARGE or RUN AT LARGE. Any dog, not under restraint, that is off
the property limits of his or her owner or keeper.
   SERIOUS PHYSICAL INJURY. A physical injury that creates a substantial risk
of death or that causes death, serious disfigurement, protracted impairment of
health, impairment of the function of any bodily organ or plastic surgery.
   VACCINATED or VACCINATION. The inoculation of a dog or cat with a vaccine
approved by a licensed veterinarian or anti-rabies clinic for use in the
prevention of rabies.
   VICIOUS DOG. A dog that, without justification, attacks a person and causes
a serious physical injury or death or any individual dog that has been found to
be a DANGEROUS DOG upon two separate occasions.
   VILLAGE. The village of Edinburg, Christian County, Illinois, a municipal
corporation, and its corporate limits as they exist from time to time.
(Ord. 772, passed 8-9-2010)
§ 5-3-2 RESTRAINT OF DOG REQUIRED/RUNNING AT LARGE.
   A.   The owner or keeper of a dog shall keep such dog under restraint at all
times.
   B.   It shall be unlawful for any owner or keeper of a dog to permit such
dog to be running at large. For purposes hereof, the owner or keeper of a dog
who fails to keep such dog under restraint is deemed to have permitted such dog
to run at large.
   C.   In addition to any other obligations, fines and penalties as provided
in this chapter, any owner of a non-vicious or non-dangerous dog who is
convicted under this chapter for allowing such dog to run at large on three
separate occasions shall not be allowed to own or keep such dog within the
village and must permanently remove such dog from the village, unless such dog
owner installs and maintains an underground electric fence that covers the
entire perimeter of his or her property and is designed to prevent the escape
of such dog from outside such aboveground non-visible electric fence enclosure
while such dog is on the property of the dog owner.
(Ord. 772, passed 8-9-2010)
§ 5-3-3 CRUELTY TO ANIMALS.
   A.   It shall be unlawful for any person to cruelly treat any animal in the
village in any way.
   B.   It shall be unlawful for any person to inhumanely beat, underfeed,
overload or abandon any animal.
(Ord. 772, passed 8-9-2010)
§ 5-3-4 NUISANCE DOGS AND CATS.
   A.   Any dog or cat which may, in any manner, continually disturb the quiet
of any person or neighborhood or shall destroy or in any manner injure any
animal, plant, shrub or other property not on the premises of its owner or
keeper is hereby declared to be a public nuisance, and in addition thereto, any
such dog may be taken up and impounded and may be redeemed or disposed of in
the manner provided under this chapter.
   B.   It shall be unlawful for any owner or keeper to knowingly keep or
harbor any dog which habitually barks, howls or yelps, or any cat which
habitually cries or howls to the discomfort of the peace and quiet of the
neighborhood, or in such manner as to materially disturb or annoy persons in
the neighborhood who are of ordinary sensibilities. Such dogs and cats are
hereby declared to be a public nuisance; and in addition thereto, any such dog
or cat may be taken up and impounded and may be redeemed or disposed of in the
manner provided under this chapter.
(Ord. 772, passed 8-9-2010)
§ 5-3-5 DAMAGE TO PROPERTY.
   It shall be unlawful for any person owning or keeping a dog or cat to permit
such dog or cat to go upon any sidewalk, parkway, boulevard, or private lands
or premises without the permission of the owner of such premises and break,
bruise, tear up, crush or injure any lawn, flowerbed, plant, shrub, tree or
garden in any manner whatsoever, or to defecate thereon. This section shall not
apply to a person who is visually or physically handicapped,
(Ord. 772, passed 8-9-2010)
§ 5-3-6 DANGEROUS DOGS AND VICIOUS DOGS.
   A.   It shall be unlawful for any person to own or keep or maintain a
vicious dog within the village. The owner or keeper of a vicious dog shall
permanently remove such vicious dog from the village and it shall be unlawful
for any owner or keeper to keep or maintain such viscous dog anywhere within
the village.
   B.   After a dog has been determined to be a vicious dog within the village
by the chief of police or his or her delegate, the owner or keeper of such
vicious dog and any member of his or her household shall be thereafter
permanently prohibited from owning or keeping or maintaining any dog anywhere
within the village.
   C.   After a dog has been determined to be a dangerous dog within the
village by the chief of police or his or her delegate, it shall thereafter be
unlawful for the owner or keeper of such dangerous dog to keep or maintain such
dangerous dog anywhere within the village unless such dog is:
      1.   Kept in a fenced enclosure of at least six feet in height that is
securely enclosed and locked and designed with secure sides, top and bottom and
shall be designed to prevent such dangerous dog from escaping from such
enclosure; or
      2.   When such dangerous dog is not within such fenced enclosure, such
dangerous dog is securely muzzled and restrained with a leash not exceeding six
feet in length and shall be under the direct control and supervision of the
owner or keeper of such dangerous dog.
   D.   Upon a violation of the preceding subsection C. above, the owner or
keeper of such dangerous dog shall permanently remove such dangerous dog from
the corporate limits of the village and it shall be unlawful for any owner or
keeper to keep or maintain such dangerous dog anywhere within the corporate
limits of the village; and the owner or keeper of such dangerous dog and any
member of his or her household shall be thereafter permanently prohibited from
owning or keeping or maintaining any dog anywhere within the corporate limits
of the village.
(Ord. 772, passed 8-9-2010)
§ 5-3-7 DISEASED ANIMALS.
   A.   It shall be unlawful for an owner of a domestic animal afflicted with a
contagious disease or an infectious disease to expose such diseased animal to
any public place whereby the health of any person or any animal may be
affected.
   B.   It shall be unlawful for an owner to ship or remove such diseased
animal from the premises of the owner thereof, except under the supervision of
a state-licensed veterinarian.
   C.   The owner of such diseased animal shall retain an state-licensed
veterinarian to secure the immediate removal from the village and the
disposition of such diseased animal and the treatment of affected premises so
as to prevent the communication and spread of contagion or infection.
(Ord. 772, passed 8-9-2010)
§ 5-3-8 NUMBER OF DOGS AND CATS.
   A.   Number Of Dogs And Cats Restricted.
      1.   Number Restricted. It shall be unlawful for any person or for
persons in any household to own or keep more than a total of five dogs or five
cats within the village, with the exception, that a litter of pups or a litter
of kittens or a portion of a litter may be kept for a period of time not
exceeding five months from birth.
      2.   Exemptions. The provisions of this subsection shall not apply to any
business establishment wherein dogs or cats are regularly kept for commercial
breeding, sale, sporting purposes or boarding.
   B.   Nuisance Declared. The keeping of an unlimited number of dogs or cats
in the village detracts from and in many instances is detrimental to the
healthful and comfortable life of residents of the village. The keeping of an
unlimited number of dogs or cats is, therefore, declared to be a public
nuisance.
(Ord. 772, passed 8-9-2010)
§ 5-3-9 ANIMAL HOUSES AND FENCES.
   A.   Pens, Yards and Runs. All pens, yards, runs or other structures wherein
any animal is kept shall be of such construction so as to be easily cleaned and
kept in good repair; and shall be kept clean and free of any fecal or urine
odor or smell.
   B.   Fences. Fences which are intended as enclosures for any animal shall be
securely constructed, shall be adequate for the purpose, kept in good repair
and shall not be allowed to become unsightly.
(Ord. 772, passed 8-9-2010)
§ 5-3-10 RABIES CONTROL.
§ 5-3-10-1 DOGS.
   A.   Inoculation And Tag Required.
      1.   Each calendar year, or at such intervals as may hereafter be
promulgated by the department of agriculture, every owner or keeper of a dog
four months or more of age shall cause such dog to be inoculated against
rabies. Every dog shall have a second rabies vaccination within one year of the
first. Such owner or keeper of such dog shall cause a serially numbered tag
evidencing such inoculation to be attached to a collar or harness worn by the
dog.
      2.   The inoculation of dogs required by subsection A.1. above shall be
performed by a veterinarian duly licensed to practice his or her profession in
the state. Upon performing such inoculation, such veterinarian shall issue to
the owner or keeper a certificate showing such fact and shall also deliver to
the owner or keeper a metallic or other suitable tag to be attached to the
collar or harness of the dog, which tag shall also certify to the fact of the
inoculation against rabies.
      3.   The tag issued under the provisions of subsection A.2. above shall
be in such form as shall be determined by the department of agriculture.
   B.   Duration Of Inoculation. The inoculation performed under the provisions
of subsection A.2. above shall be effective until the expiration of the
calendar year in which the vaccination was performed or the expiration of such
period of time as may be promulgated by the department of agriculture.
   C.   Exhibition Of Certificate. At any reasonable time upon request of any
member of the police department, the owner or keeper of any unmuzzled dog shall
exhibit his or her certificate, issued under the provisions of subsection A.2.
above, showing the inoculation against rabies of any dog owned or controlled by
him or her.
   D.   Quarantine Provisions.
      1.   Dog That Has Bitten A Person. All dogs as are shown to have bitten
any person shall be quarantined by or at the direction of the chief of police
or his or her delegate(s) for a period of ten days after the date of the bite.
      2.   Quarantine shall be accomplished by impoundment during the
quarantine period. Such quarantine dog may be redeemed at the end of the ten-
day quarantine period by its owner or keeper only after complying with
subsection A.1. above and upon paying all impounding fees and costs.
      3.   Rabies Outbreak. Whenever the chief of police or his or her delegate
determines that danger from rabies exists in the village, a quarantine of all
dogs within the village may be declared. During the period of quarantine, all
dog owners or keepers shall keep their dog or dogs confined in an enclosure
from which escape is impossible or on a leash or chain of indestructible
material. In the event a dog or dogs are not confined, then they may be
impounded by the chief of police or his or her delegate for the period of the
quarantine and may be recovered only after compliance with subsection A.1.
above and upon paying all impoundment fees. The quarantine period may be
terminated by the chief of police or his or her delegate at any time after
which it is determined there is no danger from rabies, and the termination of
such quarantine period is mandatory after any six-month period during which
time no rabies has existed in the village.
§ 5-3-10-2 CATS.
   A.   Vaccination And Tag Required. Any person owning a cat shall have such
cat vaccinated against rabies by a licensed veterinarian no less than once each
year, and the owner shall cause to be securely fastened about the neck of the
cat at all times a band or strap to which shall be securely fastened a metal
rabies tag evidencing such current rabies vaccination.
   B.   Unlawful Tampering With Tag.
      1.   Removal Of Tag. It shall be unlawful for any person to remove, or
cause to be removed, the collar, harness or metallic rabies vaccination tag
from any cat.
      2.   Use Of Another Cat’s Tag. It shall be unlawful for an owner of a cat
to permit or allow such cat to wear or to be displayed on such cat a rabies
vaccination tag issued for another cat for a rabies vaccination.
   C.   Rabies Suspects.
      1.   Observation. When any person owning a cat has been notified by any
person injured, or by someone in his or her behalf, or by someone with
knowledge of said injury, that said person has been bitten or attacked by said
cat, or when any person owning a cat has been notified by any person that said
cat has been bitten by a rabid animal, the owner shall immediately place the
cat under the care and observation of a licensed veterinarian with the expense
thereof to be borne by the owner of such cat, and failure of the owner to
submit said cat within 24 hours after notice of said bite or attack to a
licensed veterinarian constitutes a violation of this section. The owner of
such cat shall cause said cat to be impounded for such care and observation for
a period of ten days.
      2.   Destruction Of Rabid Cat. If the cat is determined by the licensed
veterinarian to have rabies, then the owner shall cause such cat to be
destroyed by the licensed veterinarian and shall permit the licensed
veterinarian to dispose of the cat’s remains as required by law.
      3.   Release Of Cat. Before any such cat shall be released, the person to
whom it is released shall submit proof, in the form of a certificate issued by
a licensed veterinarian or other person authorized by law to administer rabies
inoculation, that such cat does not have rabies and has been properly
inoculated for rabies. The impounded cat may then be released by a licensed
veterinarian.
      4.   No Rabies Tag. Any cat found within the village and not displaying a
metal rabies tag which evidences a current rabies vaccination may be
immediately humanely destroyed by any member of the village police department.
   D.   Liability Of Parent. The parent of any minor claiming to be an owner or
keeper of any cat shall be deemed to be the owner of such cat and shall be
charged for all penalties, fees and fines imposed by this section.
   E.   Penalty. Any person violating any provision of this section shall be
fined, and a separate offense shall be deemed committed on each day during or
on which a violation occurs or continues.
(Ord. 772, passed 8-9-2010)
§ 5-3-11 IMPOUNDMENT AND REDEMPTION OF DOGS OR CATS.
   A.   Places Of Impoundment Designated. The village may designate, from time
to time, a suitable place or places for the impoundment of dogs or cats or may
use the pound used by the animal control officer for the county or may use any
other available dog or cat pound provided by any other governmental entity or
may use any veterinarian facility or any other animal control facility willing
to accept impounded dogs or cats.
   B.   Authority To Impound Dogs. Any member of the village police department
or its delegate(s) shall take up and impound any dangerous dog or any vicious
dog or any dog required to be quarantined under this chapter or may take up and
impound any dog found running at large or any dog not having all required tags
fastened to the dog’s collar or any dog or cat deemed a public nuisance under
this chapter.
   C.   Safety. If any dangerous dog or vicious dog found at large cannot be
safely taken up or impounded, such dog may be slain by any member of the
village police department or its delegate(s).
   D.   Citation. When dogs are found running at large or not having all
required tags fastened to the dog’s collar, any member of the village police
department may, at his or her discretion, also cite the owner or keeper of such
dog to answer charges of violation of this chapter, regardless of whether or
not such dog or cat or animal is impounded or not. Such owner or keeper so
cited shall be subject to the fines set forth in §
5-3-15 .
   E.   Redemption Of Impounded Dog; Costs.
      1.   Any dog impounded under the provisions of this chapter, except such
as may have bitten any person specified in §
5-3-10-1D.1., shall, unless sooner redeemed, be held for the period of at least
three days in order to afford opportunity to the owner or keeper thereof to
redeem the same. Any such owner or keeper desiring to redeem his or her
impounded dog shall pay all costs incurred regarding the impoundment and
boarding of such dog.
      2.   In case such dog has not been inoculated against rabies for the
current year, such owner shall also advance the fee required to have the dog
inoculated by a duly licensed veterinarian as he or she shall elect, and the
poundkeeper shall forthwith cause the dog to be duly inoculated against rabies.
No dog shall be released without having been inoculated for the current
calendar year or in accordance with the requirements of the department of
agriculture. Upon payment of the required charges, the dog shall thereupon be
released to the owner or keeper.
   F.   Destruction Of Unredeemed Dog. Any impounded dog which shall not be
redeemed within seven business days (or after the quarantine period referred to
in §
5-3-10-1 D.1. and
5-3-10-1 D.2.) may be humanely destroyed (euthanized) or otherwise disposed of
by the poundkeeper or veterinarian. The owner or keeper of an impounded dog
shall pay all impoundment fees and all veterinarian fees and costs and
euthanasia and disposition of remains fees and costs, euthanized.
(Ord. 772, passed 8-9-2010)
§ 5-3-12 LIVESTOCK.
   A.   Prohibited; Exceptions. Except as may be permitted under the
circumstances set forth in subsections B., C. and D. below, no person shall
keep within the village any livestock.
   B.   Chickens And Rabbits.
      1.   Any person may keep any chicken and/or rabbit, for nonbusiness
purposes only, within the village, subject to the following conditions:
         a.   The combined total of chickens and/or rabbits on any person’s
premises shall not exceed 30 at any one time;
         b.   Such chickens and/or rabbits shall be kept and contained in an
outside enclosed cage which shall be maintained in a clean and healthy manner;
and
         c.   No neighbor owning or occupying any property within a one square
block area of the premises where such chickens and/or rabbits are kept objects
in writing to the presence of such chickens and/or rabbits; provided, however,
if any neighbor objects in writing, and said objection is found to be
reasonable by a review committee established by the village board of trustees
from time to time, then such chickens and/or rabbits will not be allowed. The
decision of such review committee shall be final and binding.
      2.   It shall be deemed a violation of this section if any such chicken
and/or rabbit is kept contrary hereto.
   C.   Existing Horses And Cows.
      1.   Any person who was keeping any horse or cow on his or her premises
on November 23, 2007 shall be permitted to keep such horse or cow during the
remaining life of such horse or cow, subject to the following conditions:
         a.   Such horse and/or cow shall be kept and contained in an outside
enclosure on such person’s premises;
         b.   The premises where such horse and/or cow is kept shall be
maintained in a clean and healthy manner;
         c.   No additional horses and/or cows shall be brought into or kept
within the village by such person or persons; and
         d.   No neighbor owning or occupying any property within one square
block area of the premises where such horse and/or cow is kept objects in
writing to the presence of such horse and/or cow; provided, however, if any
neighbor objects in writing, and said objection is found to be reasonable by a
review committee established by the village board of trustees from time to
time, then such horse and/or cow will not be allowed. The decision of such
review committee shall be final and binding.
      2.   It shall be unlawful for any person to own or keep any such horse or
cow in violation or contrary to this section.
   D.   Special Events. Livestock may be temporarily kept within the village
for special events such as rodeos, 4-H projects, Labor Day celebrations, fairs,
and the like, but only for the duration of such special event.
(Ord. 772, passed 8-9-2010)
§ 5-3-13 METHODS OF ENFORCEMENT.
   A.   Tranquilizer Gun. Any member of the village police department or its
delegates are authorized to use an animal tranquilizer gun or animal traps or
cages in the furtherance of enforcing the provisions of this chapter.
   B.   Killing Dangerous Animal. The chief of police or his or her delegate is
authorized to kill any dangerous dog or any vicious dog or any animal or
reptile of any kind when necessary for the protection of any person.
   C.   Private Premises. Any member of the village police department or its
delegates may enter upon private premises, provided that the entry shall not be
made into any building that is a person’s residence, to apprehend a straying
dog or other animal, a dangerous dog or a vicious dog or other animal, or an
animal thought to be infected with rabies. If, after request therefor, the
owner of the dog or other animal shall refuse to deliver the dog or other
animal to the police officer or his or her delegate(s), the owner shall be in
violation of this chapter.
(Ord. 772, passed 8-9-2010)
§ 5-3-14 INTERFERENCE WITH ENFORCEMENT OFFICIAL PROHIBITED.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to resist or molest the chief of police
or his or her delegates while engaged in the discharge of his or her duties
under this chapter or in the enforcement of any of the provisions under this
chapter.
(Ord. 772, passed 8-9-2010)
§ 5-3-15 PENALTIES.
   A.   The owner or keeper of any dog or cat or other animal that has been
impounded shall pay an impoundment fee, at the rate of $50 per day, and all
veterinarian fees and costs and euthanasia and disposition of remains fees and
costs, as the case may be.
   B.   Any person violating any provision of this chapter shall, upon
conviction thereof, be fined in accordance with the following schedule:
      1.   Two hundred twenty-five dollars for the first offense;
      2.   Two hundred seventy-five dollars for the second offense;
      3.   Three hundred twenty-five dollars for the third offense; and
      4.   A sum of not less than $375, nor more than $750 for the fourth
offense and each offense thereafter.
   C.   A separate violation shall be deemed committed on each day during which
a violation occurs or continues.
   D.   Any person issued a citation pursuant to this chapter shall pay the
village treasurer all such impoundment fees and costs and veterinarian fees and
costs and euthanasia and disposition of remains fees and costs, as the case may
be, and a fine for each violation of this chapter, in accordance with the fine
schedule set forth in subsections B. and C. above. If such person fails to pay
same within 14 days of the date of said citation, then the village may
prosecute such person through a complaint for ordinance violation filed with a
court of competent jurisdiction.
   E.   The parent of any minor claiming to be an owner or keeper of any dog
shall be deemed to be the owner of such dog and shall be charged for all
penalties, fees, costs and fines imposed by this section.
   F.   In addition, and not in lieu of the forgoing obligations, fines,
penalties, any person violating any provision of this chapter shall pay the
village a prosecutor’s fee of $150 for each such violation for which the
village attorney files a court action to prosecute such violation.
(Ord. 772, passed 8-9-2010; Ord. 776, passed 3-14-2011)
