§ 5-4-1 STATE CRIMINAL CODE ADOPTED.
   The Illinois Criminal Code of 2012, being 720 ILCS 5/1-1 et seq., as now
enacted or hereafter amended, is hereby adopted by the village. The provisions
thereof shall be controlling within the corporate limits of the village;
provided, however, the penalties as provided by this code shall apply. (1987
Code, § 27-1-2) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 5-4-2 AID IN AN OFFENSE.
   It shall be unlawful for any person, in any way or manner, to aid, abet,
counsel, advise or encourage any other person in the commission of any of the
acts mentioned herein or in any manner encourage the commission of such offense
hereby defined.
(1987 Code, § 27-2-8)
§ 5-4-3 OFFENSES INVOLVING PUBLIC PEACE AND SAFETY.
§ 5-4-3-1 DISTURBING LAWFUL ASSEMBLIES.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to willfully interrupt or disturb any
funeral assembly, funeral procession, school, any assembly met for worship or
any other assembly met for a lawful purpose by any offensive behavior or by any
disorderly conduct.
(1987 Code, § 27-2-3) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 5-4-3-2 UNLAWFUL ASSEMBLIES.
   A.   Whenever 12 or more persons, any of them armed with clubs or dangerous
weapons, or 30 or more persons, armed or unarmed, are unlawfully, riotously or
tumultuously assembled in the village, it shall be the duty of each of the
village police officers to go among the persons so assembled, or as near them
as safety will permit, and in the name of the state, command them immediately
to disperse; and if they do not obey, every person refusing to disperse shall
be deemed guilty of unlawful assembly.
(1987 Code, § 27-2-4)
   B.   When persons so unlawfully assembled neglect or refuse on command to
disperse, it shall be the duty of the police to forthwith suppress the assembly
and disperse the persons composing it in such a manner as may be most
expedient.
(1987 Code, § 27-2-4) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 5-4-3-3 STORAGE OF EXPLOSIVE COMPOUNDS.
   No person shall have, keep, possess or store at or in any place within the
village, any nitroglycerine, dynamite or giant powder, or any form or
combination of any of them.
(1987 Code, § 27-2-12)
§ 5-4-3-4 THROWING MISSILES.
   No person in the village shall throw or cast any stone or any other missile
upon or at any building, tree or other public or private property, or at any
person in any street, avenue, alley or public place.
(1987 Code, § 27-2-13)
§ 5-4-4 OFFENSES INVOLVING PROPERTY.
§ 5-4-4-1 DESTRUCTION OF PUBLIC PROPERTY.
   A.   No person in the village shall deface, destroy or in any way injure any
public property, or any other apparatus of the village.
(1987 Code, § 27-2-14)
   B.   Whoever shall intentionally deface, obliterate, tear down or destroy,
in whole or in part, any ordinance, advertisement or notice posted by authority
of the village president and trustees or any member thereof shall be fined as
provided in § 1-4-1 for each offense; provided, however, this subsection will
not apply in any case after the time for which said notice, advertisement or
ordinance has, by its own terms or ordinance, to remain posted, shall be
expired.
(1987 Code, § 27-2-5) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 5-4-4-2 WEAKENING STREETS AND PROPERTY.
   No person shall undermine, in any manner, any street or any other ground or
real estate situated in the village or belonging to any private person.
(1987 Code, § 33-2-1)
§ 5-4-4-3 DAMAGE TO UTILITIES.
   It shall be unlawful to willfully, maliciously or negligently break, deface,
injure or destroy any telegraph or telephone pole, post or wire, or any
electric light post, pole or electric conductor, wire or lamp or any other
thing connected with the same or belonging thereto, or any water main, gas
main, pipe or hydrant or lamp or lamppost, or anything belonging to or
connected therewith or with any of them. (1987 Code, § 27-5-6)
§ 5-4-4-4 DAMAGE TO STREET SIGNS.
   It shall be unlawful for any person or persons, in any manner or form, to
deface, disfigure, damage or molest any of the street signs or parts thereof
located in the village.
(1987 Code, § 27-5-7)
§ 5-4-4-5 POSTING BILLS.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to paste, post, paint, print or mail any
handbill, sign, poster, advertisement or notice of any kind on any curbstone,
flagstone, or any other portion or part of any sidewalk, or upon any tree,
lamppost, utility pole, hydrant, or upon any private wall, door, or gate
without the consent, in writing, of the owner of the wall, door or gate;
provided, however, that this section shall not prevent posting by proper
village and county officials of election signs, polling place signs and other
signs or placards necessary under the law to the conduct of elections, except
they may not be attached to a tree.
(1987 Code, § 27-2-9)
Cross-reference:
   Advertisements On Poles, see §
4-1-1 M.
   Unlawful Advertising, see §
7-1-10
§ 5-4-4-6 BARBED WIRE AND ELECTRIC FENCES.
   A.   Barbed Wire Fences.
      1.   It shall be unlawful for any person to erect, construct or maintain
anywhere within the village any fence composed in whole or in part of barbed
wire, or with any similar sharp material designed to cause injury to persons,
unless such fence is at least six feet in height, and all such barbs and sharp
materials are situated at least six feet from the ground level.
      2.   It shall be unlawful for any person to erect, construct or maintain
anywhere within the village any such fence contrary to the provisions of § 10-
1-10B.3.
   B.   Electric Fences. It shall be unlawful for any person to erect,
construct or maintain anywhere within the village any aboveground electrically
charged fence or wire; provided, however, any such electrically charged fence
or wire in existence upon the effective date hereof may continue to be
maintained until such electrically charged fence or wire is removed, destroyed
or torn down, and in such event, such electrically charged fence or wire may
not be thereafter replaced, erected, constructed or maintained.
(1987 Code, § 27-2-6)
§ 5-4-4-7 CRIMINAL HOUSING MANAGEMENT.
   A person commits the offense of criminal housing management when, having
personal management or control of residential real estate, whether as a legal
or equitable owner of residential real estate or as a managing agent or
otherwise, he or she knowingly permits, by his or her gross carelessness or
neglect, the physical condition of facilities of the residential real estate to
become or remain so deteriorated that the health or safety of any inhabitant is
endangered.
(1987 Code, § 27-4-4)
§ 5-4-4-8 SAFETY PROTECTIONS REQUIRED FOR PRIVATE SWIMMING POOLS, SPAS AND HOT
TUBS.
   A.   Notwithstanding any village code provisions to the contrary, it shall
be unlawful, on and from July 11, 2020, for an owner or tenant of any private
swimming pool, spa or hot tub to maintain any private swimming pool, spa or hot
tub unless such private swimming pool, spa or hot tub is at all times
maintained or used in compliance with each of the following applicable safety
protective requirements as set forth in subsection B. below.
   B.   Safety Protective Requirements.
      1.   The private swimming pool, spa or hot tub shall be surrounded by a
safety protective barrier and at least one access gate restricting entry into a
swimming pool, spa or hot tub that complies with the 2018 International
Swimming Pool and Spa Code §§ 305.2 through 305.7 which is included within the
2024 International Building Code (which codes have been previously adopted by
the village and are incorporated herein by reference) unless:
         a.   Such spa or hot tub is equipped with a functioning lockable
safety cover that complies with ASTM F1346; or
         b.   Such swimming pool is equipped with a functioning powered safety
cover that complies with ASTM F1346.
      2.   At all times that a private swimming pool, spa or hot tub is not in
use, each aforesaid access gate shall be closed and locked.
      3.   At all times that a private spa or hot tub is not in use the
aforesaid functioning lockable safety cover must be on such spa or hot tub and
securely locked.
      4.   At all times that a private swimming pool is not in use, the
aforesaid functioning powered safety cover shall be powered shut and closed
covering the entire private swimming pool.
      5.   For purposes of this section, a PRIVATE SWIMMING POOL, SPA OR HOT
TUB shall be considered “not in use,” when the owner or tenant thereof, or a
responsible adult authorized by such owner or tenant, is not actually in such
swimming pool, spa or hot tub or is not physically outdoors and present within
20 feet of an edge of such private swimming pool, spa or hot tub.
   C.   Penalty. Any person who violates any provision of this section shall be
subject to and fined as provided in § 1-4-1 of the village code for each
offense. Each day a violation is permitted to exist shall constitute a separate
offense.
(Ord. 873, passed 6-29-2020)
§ 5-4-5 NUDITY, INDECENT OR LEWD DRESS OR BEHAVIOR.
   No person in the village shall appear in any public place in a state of
nudity, or in an indecent or lewd dress, or shall make any indecent exposure of
his or her person, or be guilty of any indecent or lewd act or behavior.
(1987 Code, § 27-3-1)
§ 5-4-6 INTERFERENCE WITH, ABUSING A POLICE OFFICER.
   No person shall, by violent, tumultuous or obstreperous conduct or carriage,
disturb any police officer in the discharge of his or her duties; and no person
shall use unseemly, profane, obscene or offensive language in the presence of a
police officer; nor shall any person assault, strike or fight with any police
officer in the discharge of his or her duties or permit such conduct in or upon
any house or premises in the village owned or possessed by him or her or under
his or her management and control.
(1987 Code, § 27-2-1)
§ 5-4-7 IMPERSONATING, INTERFERING WITH A PUBLIC OFFICIAL.
   No person in the village shall falsely represent himself or herself to be an
officer of the village or shall, without being duly authorized by the village,
exercise or attempt to exercise any of the duties, functions or powers of any
village officer; or hinder, obstruct, resist or otherwise interfere with any
village officer in the discharge of the duties of his or her office; or attempt
to prevent any such officer from arresting any person, either by force or by
giving notice to such person; or attempt to rescue from such officer any person
in his or her custody; or impersonate any of the members of the police force of
the village; or maliciously or with the intention of deceiving any person, wear
the uniform of or a uniform similar to that worn by the members of the police
department, or use any of the signs, signals or devices adopted and used by the
police department.
(1987 Code, § 27-2-2)
§ 5-4-8 DISORDERLY CONDUCT.
   A.   It shall be unlawful for any person to commit disorderly conduct, as
provided for in 720 ILCS 5/26-1.
   B.   No person shall yell, shout, hoot, whistle or sing on any village
street, highway, roadway, easement, alley, sidewalk, boulevard or right-of-way,
particularly between the hours of 11:00 p.m. and 7:00 a.m. or at any time or
place so as to annoy or disturb the quiet, comfort or repose of persons in any
office, or in any dwelling, hotel or other type of residence, or of any persons
in the vicinity.
   C.   Any person who violates the provisions of this section shall, for each
offense, be punished as provided in §
1-4-1.
(Ord. 823, passed 9-14-2015)
§ 5-4-9 FIGHTING.
   A.   It shall be unlawful for any person to knowingly start a fight or to
fight or to commit any assault or battery while in or within or at or on or
about:
      1.   Any village street, highway, roadway, easement, alley, sidewalk,
boulevard or right-of-way;
      2.   Any village property; or
      3.   Any public place within the village.
   B.   For purposes hereof, PUBLIC PLACE shall mean and include any property
or premises of any kind, nature or extent whatsoever within the village that is
available to or frequented by members of the general public either in person or
while wholly or partly in or within or at or on or about any vehicle or
property of any kind, nature or extent whatsoever.
   C.   Any person who violates the provisions of this section shall, for each
offense, be punished as provided in §
1-4-1.
(Ord. 823, passed 9-14-2015)
§ 5-4-10 CANNABIS.
   A.   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, the following definitions
apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      CANNABIS. Includes marihuana, hashish and other substances which are
identified as including any parts of the plant Cannabis sativa, whether growing
or not; the seeds thereof, the resin extracted from any part of such plant; and
any compound, manufacture, salt, derivative, mixture or preparation of such
plant, its seeds or resin, including tetrahydrocannabinol (THC) and all other
cannabinol derivatives, including its naturally occurring or synthetically
produced ingredients, whether produced directly or indirectly by extraction, or
independently by means of chemical synthesis or by a combination of extraction
and chemical synthesis; but shall not include the mature stalks of such plant,
fiber produced from such stalks, oil or cake made from the seeds of such plant,
any other compound, manufacture, salt, derivative, mixture or preparation of
such mature stalks (except the resin extracted therefrom), fiber, oil or cake,
or the sterilized seed of such plant which is incapable of germination.
(720 ILCS 550/3)
      CANNABIS PARAPHERNALIA. Equipment, products or materials intended to be
used for planting, propagating, cultivation, growing, harvesting,
manufacturing, producing, processing, preparing, testing, analyzing, packaging,
repackaging, storing, containing, concealing, ingesting or otherwise
introducing cannabis into the human body.
      ORDINARY PUBLIC VIEW. Within the sight line with normal visual range of a
person, unassisted by visual aids, from a public street or sidewalk adjacent to
real property, or from within an adjacent property.
      RESIDENT. A person who has been domiciled in the state for a period of 30
days before cultivation.
   B.   Restrictions/Forfeiture.
      1.   It is unlawful for any person under the age of 21 years to purchase,
possess, use, process, transport, grow or consume cannabis or an item of
cannabis paraphernalia except where authorized by the Illinois Compiled
Statutes, including, but not limited to, the Cannabis Control Act (720 ILCS
550/1 et seq.), the Cannabis Regulation and Tax Act (410 ILCS 705/10-5 et
seq.), the Compassionate Use of Medical Cannabis Program Act (410 ILCS 130/1 et
seq.) or the Community College Cannabis Vocational Pilot Program (410 ILCS 705/
25-1 et seq.).
      2.   It is unlawful for any person over the age of 21 years to purchase,
possess, use, process, transport, grow, produce or consume cannabis or an item
of cannabis paraphernalia in violation of any provision of the Illinois
Compiled Statutes, including, but not limited to, the Cannabis Control Act (720
ILCS 550/1 et seq.), the Cannabis Regulation and Tax Act (410 ILCS 705/10-5 et
seq.), the Compassionate Use of Medical Cannabis Program Act (410 ILCS 130/1 et
seq.), or the Community College Cannabis Vocational Pilot Program (410 ILCS
705/25-1 et seq.).
      3.   It is unlawful to cultivate cannabis for personal use unless such
cultivation is done by a state resident 21 years of age or older who is a
registered qualifying patient under the compassionate use ofMedical Cannabis
Program Act and such cannabis cultivation does not violate any of the following
restrictions, limitations and conditions.
         a.   The cultivation of cannabis plants per household shall not exceed
five plants that are more than five inches tall, without a cultivation center
or craft grower license.
         b.   Such cannabis cultivation shall take place in an enclosed, locked
space.
         c.   Only such state-registered qualifying patients may purchase
cannabis seeds from a dispensary for the purpose of home cultivation. It is
unlawful for any such state-registered qualifying patient to give or sell
cannabis seeds to any other person.
         d.   Such cannabis plants shall not be stored or placed in a location
where they are subject to ordinary public view. Such registered qualifying
patient who cultivates cannabis shall take reasonable precautions to ensure the
cannabis plants are secure from unauthorized access, including unauthorized
access by any person or persons under 21 years of age.
         e.   Such cannabis cultivation may occur only on residential property
lawfully in possession of the cultivator or with the consent of the person in
lawful possession of the property. An owner or lessor of residential property
may prohibit the cultivation of cannabis by a lessee.
         f.   A dwelling, residence, apartment, condominium unit, enclosed,
locked space or piece of property not divided into multiple dwelling units
shall not contain more than five cannabis plants at any one time.
         g.   Cannabis plants may only be tendered by such registered
qualifying patients who reside at the residence, or their authorized agent
attending to the residence for brief periods, such as when such registered
qualifying patient is temporarily away from the residence.
         h.   It is unlawful for any person to cultivate more than the
allowable number of cannabis plants, or to sell or give away cannabis plants,
cannabis, cannabis seeds or cannabis-infused products.
      4.   Cannabis and/or cannabis plants and/or cannabis paraphernalia and/or
cannabis seeds that is/are seized by police officers pursuant to an issuance of
an ordinance citation for a violation of this section shall be forfeited to the
village upon conviction or upon payment of a fine in settlement of any said
violation without further order of the court.
   C.   Penalties. Any person violating any provision of this section shall be
fined, upon conviction thereof, the sum of $250 per violation; and each day
upon which a violation occurs or continues shall be deemed to be a separate
offense. A person under 21 years of age with cannabis in his or her possession
is guilty of a civil law violation as outlined in paragraph (a) of the Cannabis
Control Act.
(Ord. 868, passed 12-9-2019)
CHAPTER 5: WEAPONS
Section
   
5-5-1   Right To Own And Possess Firearms And Ammunition
   
5-5-2   Discharge Of Firearm Or Bow And Arrow
§ 5-5-1 RIGHT TO OWN AND POSSESS FIREARMS AND AMMUNITION.
   Except as otherwise prohibited by law, every adult person, as defined by
state statute, residing within the limits of the village shall possess the
absolute right to own, possess and maintain a firearm, together with necessary
ammunition for the use of such firearm.
(1987 Code, § 27-2-10)
§ 5-5-2 DISCHARGE OF FIREARM OR BOW AND ARROW.
   It shall be unlawful to discharge any firearm, bow and arrow or air gun in
the village, or so that the bullet, arrow, missile or projectile therefrom
enters the village, without written permission from the mayor; provided, that
this section shall not be construed to prohibit any officer of the law from
discharging a firearm in the performance of his or her duty; nor any citizen
from discharging a firearm when lawfully defending his or her personal
property.
(1987 Code, § 27-2-11)
CHAPTER 6: MINORS
Section
   
5-6-1   Sales Or Gifts To Or Possession Of Tobacco By Minors
   
5-6-2   Curfew
   
5-6-3   Trick Or Treating On Designated Day
§ 5-6-1 SALES OR GIFTS TO OR POSSESSION OF TOBACCO BY MINORS.
   A.   It shall be unlawful to sell, buy for, furnish, or to give any
cigarette, cigar or tobacco in any form or any electronic cigarette to any
person under 18 years of age; provided, however, a parent or judicially
established legal guardian of such person under 18 years of age may give such
minor any such cigarette, cigar or tobacco, provided same is solely possessed,
smoked, chewed or consumed in the privacy of the residential home of such
parent or guardian.
   B.   It shall be unlawful for any person under 18 years of age to possess,
or buy, or smoke, or chew or consume any cigarette, cigar or tobacco in any
form or any electronic cigarette except in the privacy of the residential home
of such person’s parent or judicially established legal guardian.
   C.   Any person violating any of the provisions of this section shall, for
each offense, be fined and punished as provided in §
1-4-1 for each separate offense. Each day upon which a violation occurs or
continues shall be deemed a separate offense.
(Ord. 813, passed 9-8-2014)
§ 5-6-2 CURFEW.
   A.   Definitions. For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions
apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      CURFEW HOURS.
         a.   Between 12:01 a.m. and 6:00 a.m. Saturday;
         b.   Between 12:01 a.m. and 6:00 a.m. on Sunday; and
         c.   Between 11:00 p.m. on Sunday to Thursday, inclusive, and 6:00
a.m. on the following day.
      EMERGENCY. An unforeseen combination of circumstances or the resulting
state that calls for immediate action. The term includes, but is not limited
to, a fire, a natural disaster, an automobile accident or any situation
requiring immediate action to prevent serious bodily injury or loss of life.
      ESTABLISHMENT. Any privately owned place of business operated for a
profit to which the public is invited including, but not limited to, any place
of amusement or entertainment.
      GUARDIAN.
         a.   A person who, under court order, is the guardian of the person of
a minor; or
         b.   A public or private agency with whom a minor has been placed by a
court.
      MINOR. Any person under 17 years of age.
      PARENT. A person who is:
         a.   A natural parent, adoptive parent or stepparent of another
person; or
         b.   At least 18 years of age and authorized by a parent or guardian
to have the care and custody of a minor.
      PUBLIC PLACE. Any place to which the public or a substantial group of the
public has access and includes, but is not limited to, streets, highways and
the common areas of schools, hospitals, apartment houses, office buildings,
transport facilities and shops.
      REMAIN. To:
         a.   Linger or stay; or
         b.   Fail to leave premises when requested to do so by a police
officer or the owner, operator or other person in control of the premises.
      SERIOUS BODILY INJURY. Bodily injury that creates a substantial risk of
death or that causes death, serious permanent disfigurement or protracted loss
or impairment of the function of any bodily member or organ.
   B.   Offenses.
      1.   A minor between 14 years of age and 17 years of age commits an
offense if he or she remains in a public place or on the premises of any
establishment during curfew hours or during Sunday, Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday
or Thursday between 11:00 p.m. to 6:00 a.m. the following day.
      2.   A parent or guardian of a minor or other person in custody or
control of a minor commits an offense if he or she knowingly permits the minor
to remain in any public place or on the premises of any establishment during
curfew hours.
      3.   A minor less than 14 years of age commits an offense if he or she
remains in a public place or on the premises of any establishment on any day
between 9:30 p.m. to 6:00 a.m. the following day.
   C.   Defenses. It is a defense to prosecution under subsection B. above that
the minor was:
      1.   Accompanied by the minor’s parent or guardian or other person in
custody or control of the minor;
      2.   On an errand at the direction of the minor’s parent or guardian,
without any detour or stop;
      3.   In a motor vehicle involved in interstate travel;
      4.   Engaged in an employment activity or going to or returning home from
an employment activity, without any detour or stop;
      5.   Involved in an emergency;
      6.   On the sidewalk abutting the minor’s residence or abutting the
residence of a next door neighbor if the neighbor did not complain to the
police department about the minor’s presence;
      7.   Attending an official school, religious or other recreational
activity supervised by adults and sponsored by a government or governmental
agency, a civic organization or another similar entity that takes
responsibility for the minor, or going to or returning home from, without any
detour or stop, an official school, religious or other recreational activity
supervised by adults and sponsored by a government or governmental agency, a
civic organization or another similar entity that takes responsibility for the
minor;
      8.   Exercising first amendment rights protected by the United States
constitution, such as the free exercise of religion, freedom of speech and the
right of assembly; or
      9.   Married or had been married or is an emancipated minor under the
Emancipation of Minors Act, being 750 IlCS 30/1 et seq.
   D.   Enforcement. Before taking any enforcement action under this section, a
law enforcement officer shall ask the apparent offender’s age and reason for
being in the public place. The officer shall not issue a citation or make an
arrest under this section unless the officer reasonably believes that an
offense has occurred and that, based on any response and other circumstances,
no defense in subsection C. above is present.
   E.   Penalties. A person convicted of a violation of any provision of this
section shall be guilty of a petty offense and shall be fined not less than
$250 nor more than $500; except, that neither a person who has been made a ward
of the court under the Juvenile Court Act of 1987, being 705 ILCS 405/1-1 et
seq., nor that person’s legal guardian, shall be subject to any fine. In
addition to or instead of the fine imposed by this subsection, the court may
order a parent, legal guardian, or other person convicted of a violation of
subsection B. above to perform community service as determined by the court;
except, that the legal guardian of a person who has been made a ward of the
court under the Juvenile Court Act of 1987 may not be ordered to perform
community service. The dates and times established for the performance of
community service by the parent, legal guardian or other person convicted of a
violation of subsection B. above, shall not conflict with the dates and times
that the person is employed in his or her regular occupation.
(1987 Code, § 27-2-17) (Amd. 2008 Code) (Ord. 773, passed 8-9-2010)
Statutory reference:
   Similar provisions, see 750 ILCS 30/1 et seq. and 705 ILCS 405/1-1 et seq.
§ 5-6-3 TRICK OR TREATING ON DESIGNATED DAY.
   It shall be illegal for any person to engage in the Halloween practice,
commonly called “trick or treat,” by calling at the homes or dwelling places
within the village, masked or unmasked, except upon the designated day
established by the village board.
(1987 Code, § 27-2-18)
CHAPTER 7: FIREWORKS
Section
   
5-7-1   Definition
   
5-7-2   Sales And Use Prohibited Generally
   
5-7-3   Displays; Permit Requirements
   
5-7-4   Possession; Transporting
§ 5-7-1 DEFINITION.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definition applies unless the
context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   FIREWORK. Any explosive composition, or any substance or combination of
substances, or article prepared for the purpose of producing a visible or
audible effect of a temporary exhibitional nature by explosion, combustion,
deflagration or detonation and shall include blank cartridges and toy cannons
in which explosives are used; the type of balloons which require fire
underneath to propel the same; firecrackers, torpedoes, sky rockets, Roman
candles, fireworks containing any explosive compound, or any bombs or other
fireworks of like construction and any tablets or other devices containing
explosive substances, or containing combustible substances producing visual
effects; provided, however, that the term FIREWORKS shall not include snake or
glow worm pellets, smoke devices, trick noisemakers known as “party poppers,”
“booby traps,” “snappers,” “trick matches,” “cigarette loads,” and “auto
burglar alarms,” sparklers, toy pistols, toy canes, toy guns or other devices
in which paper or plastic caps containing 0.25 grain or less of explosive
compound are used, provided they are so constructed that the hand cannot come
in contact with the cap when in place for the explosion; and toy pistol, paper
or plastic caps which contain less than 0.25 grain of explosive mixture, the
sale and use of which shall be permitted at all times.
(1987 Code, § 27-2-16)
§ 5-7-2 SALES AND USE PROHIBITED GENERALLY.
   Except as hereinafter provided, it shall be unlawful for any person, firm,
copartnership or corporation to offer for sale, expose for sale, sell at retail
or use or explode any fireworks within the village limits.
(1987 Code, § 27-2-16)
§ 5-7-3 DISPLAYS; PERMIT REQUIREMENTS.
   A.   Permit Required; Exemptions. The village board may authorize the
village clerk to grant permits for the supervised public display of fireworks.
Permits may be granted hereunder to any group of three or more adult
individuals applying therefor. No permit shall be required for supervised
public displays by state or county fair associations or by the Village Lion’s
Club.
(1987 Code, § 27-2-16) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   B.   Application For Permit. Application for permit shall be made in writing
at least 15 days in advance of the date of the display, and action shall be
taken on such application at the next regular meeting of the village board.
Forms for such application and permit may be obtained from the village clerk.
(1987 Code, § 27-2-16)
   C.   Bond Required. The village board shall require a bond from the
permittee in a sum not less than $1,000, conditioned on compliance with the
provisions of the ordinances of the village and the regulations of the state
fire marshal; except, that the village shall not be required to file such bond.
(1987 Code, § 27-2-16) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   D.   Inspection; Issuance Of Permit. The permit shall be issued after
inspection of the display site by the police chief or designee to determine
that such display shall not be hazardous to property or endanger any person or
persons.
   E.   Distribution Of Copies Of Permit. One copy of such permit shall be on
file with the village clerk and one copy forwarded to the office of the state
fire marshal.
   F.   Nontransferability Of Permit. No permit granted hereunder shall be
transferable.
   G.   Supervision; Safe Conditions. Every such display shall be handled by a
competent individual designated by the police chief and shall be of such
character and so located, discharged or fired as not to be hazardous to
property or endanger any person or persons.
(1987 Code, § 27-2-16)
§ 5-7-4 POSSESSION; TRANSPORTING.
   Possession by any party holding a certificate of registration under the
state fireworks regulation act, filed July 20, 1935, or by any employee or
agent of such party or by any person transporting fireworks for such party
shall not be a violation, provided such possession is within the scope of
business of the Illinois Fireworks Regulation Act, being 425 ILCS 30/1 et seq.
(1987 Code, § 27-2-16)

§ 6-1-1 DEFINITIONS.
   The Illinois Vehicle Code, being 625 ILCS 5/1-100 et seq., entitled “Title
and Definitions,” as passed, approved and amended by the state general
assembly, is hereby adopted by the village. The provisions thereof shall be
controlling within the corporate limits of the village.
(1987 Code, § 24-1-1) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 6-1-2 APPLICATION TO ANIMALS AND BICYCLES.
   Any person riding a bicycle or an animal or driving any animal drawing a
vehicle upon any street shall be subject to the provisions of this title
applicable to the driver of a vehicle, except those provisions which can have
no application to a person riding a bicycle or driving or riding an animal.
(1987 Code, § 24-2-8)
§ 6-1-3 EXEMPTIONS FROM PROVISIONS.
   A.   Authorized Emergency Vehicles.
      1.   The provisions of this title regulating the movement or parking of
vehicles shall not apply to the driver of any authorized emergency vehicle when
responding to an emergency call, but such driver, upon approaching a red or
stop signal or any stop sign, shall slow down as necessary for safety but may
proceed cautiously past such red or stop sign or signal. At other times,
drivers of authorized emergency vehicles shall stop in obedience to a stop sign
or signal.
      2.   No driver of any authorized emergency vehicle shall assume any
special privileges under this title except when such vehicle is operated in
response to an emergency call or in the immediate pursuit of an actual or
suspected violator of the law.
   B.   Public Works Vehicles. The provisions of this title regulating the
movement and parking of vehicles shall not apply to equipment or vehicles while
actively engaged in installing, repairing or otherwise improving streets or
street pavement.
(1987 Code, § 24-2-11)
§ 6-1-4 OBEDIENCE TO POLICE; UNLAWFUL DIRECTION OF TRAFFIC.
   A.   Members of the police department and special police assigned to traffic
duty are hereby authorized to direct all traffic in accordance with the
provision of this title or in emergencies, as public safety or convenience may
require, and it shall be unlawful for any person to fail or refuse to comply
with any lawful order, signal or direction of a police officer. Any person
convicted of violating this subsection A. is guilty of a petty offense and
shall be subject to a mandatory fine of $250.
(1987 Code, § 24-2-1) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   B.   Except in cases of emergency, it shall be unlawful for any person not
authorized by law to direct or attempt to direct traffic.
(1987 Code, § 24-2-1)
§ 6-1-5 TRAFFIC AT SCENE OF FIRE.
   The fire district officer in command or any firefighter designated by him or
her may exercise the powers and authority of a police officer in directing
traffic at the scene of any fire, or where the fire district has responded to
any emergency call, for so long as the fire district equipment is on the scene
in the absence of or in assisting the police department.
(1987 Code, § 24-2-2)
§ 6-1-6 TRAFFIC CONTROL DEVICES.
   A.   Obedience Required. It shall be unlawful for the driver of any vehicle
to disobey the instructions of any traffic sign or signal placed in view by
authority of the corporate authorities or in accordance with the laws of the
state, excepting on direction of a police officer.
(1987 Code, § 24-2-4)
   B.   Unauthorized Signs, Signals Or Devices. No person shall place, maintain
or display upon or in view of any street any unauthorized sign, signal,
marking, light, reflector or device which purports to be or is an imitation of
or resembles an official traffic control device or railroad sign or signal, or
which attempts to direct the movement of traffic.
   C.   Interference With Traffic Control Devices.
      1.   No person shall place, maintain or display upon or in view of any
street any sign which hides from view or interferes with the movement of
traffic or the effectiveness of any traffic control device or any railroad sign
or signal. No tree, bush or foliage of any kind shall be so placed, maintained,
allowed to remain or be displayed upon private property in such a manner as to
hide from view or interfere with the movement of traffic or the effectiveness
of any traffic control device, sign or signal.
(1987 Code, §§ 24-2-5 and 24-2-7)
      2.   It shall be unlawful for any person to deface, injure, move or
interfere with any official traffic sign or signal.
(1987 Code, § 24-2-6)
§ 6-1-7 TOY VEHICLES.
   It shall be unlawful for any person upon skates, a coaster, skateboard, sled
or other toy vehicle to go upon any roadway other than at a crosswalk.
(1987 Code, § 24-2-9)
CHAPTER 2: DRIVING RULES
Section
   
6-2-1   One-Way Streets; Stop And Yield Intersections
   
6-2-2   Speed Restrictions
   
6-2-3   Lane Usage
   
6-2-4   Reckless, Careless, Negligent Driving
§ 6-2-1 ONE-WAY STREETS; STOP AND YIELD INTERSECTIONS.
   A.   One-Way Streets And Alleys. It shall be unlawful to operate any vehicle
on any streets or alleys designated as one-way streets or alleys in any
direction other than that so designated.
(1987 Code, § 24-3-1)
   B.   Stop Streets. The driver of a vehicle shall stop in obedience to a stop
sign at an intersection where a stop sign is erected at one or more entrances
thereto and shall proceed cautiously, yielding to the vehicles not so obligated
to stop which are within the intersection or approaching so close as to
constitute an immediate hazard, unless traffic at such intersection is
controlled by a police officer on duty, in which event, the directions of the
police officer shall be complied with.
(1987 Code, § 24-3-2)
   C.   Yield Right-Of-Way Streets. The driver of a vehicle approaching a yield
sign shall, in obedience to such sign, slow down to a speed reasonable for the
existing conditions and, if required for safety to stop, shall stop at a
clearly marked stop line, but if none, before entering the crosswalk on the
near side of the intersection, or if none, then at the point nearest the
intersecting roadway where the driver has a view of approaching traffic on the
intersecting roadway. After slowing or stopping, the driver shall yield the
right-of-way to any vehicle in the intersection or approaching on another
roadway so closely as to constitute an immediate hazard during the time such
driver is moving across or within the intersection. (1987 Code, § 24-3-3)
   D.   Signs Posted. Appropriate signs shall be posted to show all stop and
yield right-of-way streets; all one-way streets and alleys; and all stop
intersections.
(1987 Code, § 24-3-4)
Cross-reference:
   One-Way Streets, Stop Intersections and Yield Intersections, see §§
6-4-1 ,
6-4-2  and
6-4-3
§ 6-2-2 SPEED RESTRICTIONS.
   A.   Speed Limits Generally. The speed limits on various streets shall be
approved by the village board of trustees from time to time, but shall not
exceed 20 mph on any street within the village unless otherwise posted;
provided, however, the speed limit on Wildcat Way shall be 30 mph from its
intersection with Campbell Street to its intersection with North Street and
shall be 45 mph from its intersection with North Street to its intersection
with IL. Route 29, and from its intersection with Campbell Street thence south
to IL. Route 29.
   B.   School Zones. No person shall drive a motor vehicle at a speed in
excess of 20 mph while passing through a school zone or while traveling upon
any public thoroughfare on or across which children pass going to and from
school during school days when school children are present. This subsection
shall not be applicable unless appropriate signs are posted upon streets and
maintained by the village or state wherein the school zone is located.
(1987 Code, § 24-4-2)
   C.   Failure To Reduce Speed.
      1.   A vehicle shall be driven upon the streets and alleys of the village
at a speed which is reasonable and proper with regard to traffic conditions and
the use of the street and alley. The fact that the vehicle does not exceed the
applicable maximum speed limit does not relieve the driver of the duty to
decrease speed when approaching and crossing an intersection, when approaching
and going around a curve, when approaching a hillcrest, when traveling upon any
narrow or winding roadway, or when special hazard exists with respect to
pedestrians or other traffic or by reason of weather or highway conditions.
      2.   Speed must be decreased as may be necessary to avoid colliding with
any property or with any person or vehicle on or entering the highway in
compliance with legal requirements and the duty of all persons to use due care.
(1987 Code, § 24-4-2) (Amd. 2008 Code) (Ord. 845, passed 11-13-2017)
Cross-reference:
   School Zone Speed Limits, see §
6-4-5 A.
§ 6-2-3 LANE USAGE.
   Whenever any roadway within the village has been divided into two or more
clearly marked lanes for traffic, a vehicle shall be driven as nearly as
practicable entirely within a single lane and shall not be moved from such lane
until the driver has first ascertained that such movement can be made with
safety.
(1987 Code, § 24-4-2)
§ 6-2-4 RECKLESS, CARELESS, NEGLIGENT DRIVING.
   It shall be unlawful to operate any vehicle in the village in a careless,
reckless, negligent or wanton manner or carelessly so as to endanger life or
property or to knowingly drive a vehicle and use an incline in a roadway, such
as a railroad grade crossing, bridge, approach or hill, to cause the vehicle to
become airborne.
(1987 Code, § 24-5-4) (Amd. 2008 Code)
CHAPTER 3: PARKING REGULATIONS
Section
   
6-3-1   Stopping, Standing Or Parking Prohibited In Specified Places
   
6-3-2   Towing Illegally Parked Vehicles
   
6-3-3   Prima Facie Proof
   
6-3-4   Penalties
§ 6-3-1 STOPPING, STANDING OR PARKING PROHIBITED IN SPECIFIED PLACES.
   A.   Except when necessary to avoid conflict with other traffic, or in
compliance with law or the directions of a police officer or official traffic
control device, no person shall:
      1.   Stop, stand or park a vehicle:
         a.   On the roadway side of any vehicle stopped or parked at the edge
or curb of a street;
         b.   On a sidewalk;
         c.   Within an intersection;
         d.   On a crosswalk;
         e.   Between a safety zone and the adjacent curb or within 30 feet of
points on the curb immediately opposite the ends of a safety zone, unless a
different length is indicated by signs or markings;
         f.   Alongside or opposite any street excavation or obstruction when
stopping, standing or parking would obstruct traffic;
         g.   Upon any bridge or other elevated structure upon a highway or
within a highway tunnel;
         h.   On any railroad tracks;
         i.   At any place where official signs prohibit stopping;
         j.   On any controlled access highway;
         k.   In the area between roadways of a divided highway, including
crossovers; and
         l.   In a public parking area if the vehicle does not display a
current annual registration sticker or current temporary permit pending
registration.
      2.   Stand or park a vehicle, whether occupied or not, except momentarily
to pick up or discharge passengers:
         a.   In front of a public or private driveway;
         b.   Within 15 feet of a fire hydrant;
         c.   Within 20 feet of a crosswalk at an intersection;
         d.   Within 30 feet upon the approach to any flashing signal, stop
sign, yield sign or traffic control signal located at the side of a roadway;
         e.   Within 20 feet of the driveway entrance to any fire station and
on the side of a street opposite the entrance to any fire station within 75
feet of such entrance (when properly signposted); and
         f.   At any place where official signs prohibit standing.
      3.   Park a vehicle, whether occupied or not, except temporarily for the
purpose of and while actually engaged in loading or unloading property or
passengers:
         a.   Within 50 feet of the nearest rail of a railroad crossing; and
         b.   At any place where official signs prohibit parking.
   B.   No person shall move a vehicle not lawfully under his or her control
into any such prohibited area or away from a curb such distance as is unlawful.
(1987 Code, § 24-6-1) (Amd. 2008 Code)
Cross-reference:
   No Parking Zones, see §
6-4-4
§ 6-3-2 TOWING ILLEGALLY PARKED VEHICLES.
   The police department and all members thereof are hereby authorized to
remove and tow away, or have removed and towed away by commercial towing
service, any car, boat, trailer or other vehicle illegally parked in any place
where such parked vehicle creates or constitutes a traffic hazard, blocks the
use of a fire hydrant or obstructs or may obstruct the movement of any
emergency vehicle. Vehicles towed away shall be stored on any village property
or in a private garage, service station or parking lot and shall be restored to
the owner or operator thereof after payment of the expense incurred by the
village in removing and storing such vehicle(s).
(1987 Code, § 24-6-2)
§ 6-3-3 PRIMA FACIE PROOF.
   The fact that a vehicle which is illegally parked or operated is registered
in the name of a person shall be considered prima facie proof that such person
was in control of the vehicle at the time of such violation.
(1987 Code, § 24-6-4)
§ 6-3-4 PENALTIES.
   A.   Any person accused of a violation of any ordinance prohibiting parking
a vehicle in a designated area, or restricting the length of time a vehicle may
be there parked, may settle and compromise the claim against him or her for
such illegal parking by paying the village $75 for each such offense. Such
payment may be made at the village, and a receipt shall be issued for all
monies so received, and such monies shall be promptly turned over to the
treasurer to be credited to the general fund. The members of the police
department are hereby authorized to refrain from instituting a prosecution for
the alleged offense involved for at least five days.
   B.   If such $75 fee is not paid within five days of the date of the parking
ticket, then any person who violates any section of this chapter shall, upon
conviction thereof, be fined in accordance with
title 1, chapter 4 of this code.
(1987 Code, § 24-6-3)

CHAPTER 5: VEHICLE EQUIPMENT AND CONDITION
Section
   
6-5-1   Noisy Vehicles
   
6-5-2   Lights On Vehicles Of Volunteer Firefighters
   
6-5-3   Weight Restrictions
   
6-5-4   Bicycle Equipment
§ 6-5-1 NOISY VEHICLES.
   A.   Stopped Vehicle.
      1.   No operator of a motor vehicle shall, when the motor vehicle is
stopped, unreasonably accelerate the engine thereof with the gears of the
vehicle in neutral, thereby causing an unreasonable loud or excessive noise.
(1987 Code, § 24-5-1)
      2.   No operator of a motor vehicle shall, when the motor vehicle is
stopped, accelerate the engine with the gears of such vehicle in neutral, and
while so accelerating the engine, shift the gears of the vehicle into a forward
or reverse movement, thereby causing an unreasonably loud noise with the rear
wheels of the vehicle.
(1987 Code, § 24-5-2)
      3.   No operator of a motor vehicle shall accelerate the engine thereof
when shifting the gears of such vehicle in such a manner as to cause the rear
wheels of such vehicle to spin violently thereby causing an unreasonably loud
or excessive noise.
(1987 Code, § 24-5-3)
   B.   Moving Vehicles. No operator of motor vehicle shall, when operating the
vehicle, accelerate the vehicle or rapidly stop the vehicle causing an
unreasonably loud noise.
(1987 Code, § 24-5-5)
   C.   Sound Amplification System. No driver of any motor vehicle within the
village shall operate or permit the operation of any sound amplification system
which can be heard outside the vehicle from 75 or more feet when the vehicle is
being operated upon a public street, roadway, alleyway or highway within the
village, unless such system is being operated to request assistance or warn of
a hazardous situation. This subsection C. does not apply to authorized
emergency vehicles.
(1987 Code, § 24-5-6)
§ 6-5-2 LIGHTS ON VEHICLES OF VOLUNTEER FIREFIGHTERS.
   Any motor vehicle owned or usually operated by a volunteer firefighter may
be equipped with not to exceed two lamps which shall emit a blue light without
glare. One such lamp may be mounted on the rear of any such vehicle. A flashing
blue light may be used only when such firefighter is responding to a fire call.
(1987 Code, § 24-2-3) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 6-5-3 WEIGHT RESTRICTIONS.
   A.   Streets, Highways and Roads.
      1.   It shall be unlawful to operate any truck, motor vehicle, commercial
vehicle or any vehicle on any public street, highway or other public road in
the village, except on the truck routes described in §
6-4-6, when the gross weight on the surface of the street, highway or roadway
exceeds 16,000 pounds, except for the sole business purpose of making delivery
or picking up a load, in which case, such vehicle may be driven on any street
for not more than the minimum distance necessary for such purpose.
      2.   The village corporate authorities shall cause signs to be
appropriately placed.
   B.   Alleys. During the period of time from February 15 through April 30 of
each year, it shall be unlawful to operate any motor vehicle or any vehicle on
any public alley in the village when the gross weight on the surface of the
public alley exceeds 8,000 pounds; provided, however, garbage hauling trucks,
with the prior consent of the village general superintendent, may use said
alleys during said period of time; but provided, further, however, the owner of
said garbage truck and/or garbage business shall pay all material costs
incurred by the village to repair any and all damage done to any alley by use
of said garbage hauling trucks.
(1987 Code, § 24-9-2) (Ord. 869, passed 12-9-2019)
§ 6-5-4 BICYCLE EQUIPMENT.
   A.   Lights And Reflectors.
      1.   Every bicycle, when in use at nighttime, shall be equipped with a
lamp on the front which shall emit a white light visible from a distance of at
least 500 feet to the front and with a red reflector on the rear of a type
approved by the state department of transportation which shall be visible from
all distances from 100 feet to 600 feet to the rear when directly in front of
lawful lower beams of headlamps on a motor vehicle. A lamp emitting a red light
visible from a distance of 500 feet to the rear may be used in addition to the
red reflector.
(1987 Code, § 24-2-10) (Amd. 2008 Code)
      2.   No person shall sell a new bicycle or pedal for use on a bicycle
that is not equipped with a reflex reflector on each pedal or conforming to
specifications prescribed by the state, visible from the front and rear of the
bicycle during darkness from a distance of 200 feet.
      3.   No person shall sell or offer for sale a new bicycle that is not
equipped with side reflectors. Such reflectors shall be visible from each side
of the bicycle from a distance of 500 feet and shall be essentially colorless
or red to the rear of the center of the bicycle and essentially colorless or
amber to the front of the center of the bicycle. The requirements of this
subsection A.3. may be met by reflective materials which shall be at least
three-sixteenths inch wide on each side of each tire or rim to indicate as
clearly as possible the continuous circular shape and size of the tires or rims
of such bicycle and which reflective materials may be of the same color on both
the front and rear tires or rims. Such reflectors shall conform to
specifications prescribed by the state.
      4.   No person shall sell or offer for sale a new bicycle that is not
equipped with an essentially colorless front facing reflector.
   B.   Brake. Every bicycle shall be equipped with a brake which will
adequately control movement of and stop and hold such bicycle.
   C.   Sirens Prohibited. A bicycle shall not be equipped with, nor shall any
person use any siren upon a bicycle.
(1987 Code, § 24-2-10)
CHAPTER 6: SNOWMOBILES
Section
   
6-6-1   Definitions
   
6-6-2   Use Restricted
   
6-6-3   Obedience To Traffic Laws And Police
   
6-6-4   Operation On Certain Streets Only
   
6-6-5   Conditions Of Operation
§ 6-6-1 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions apply unless the
context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   HIGHWAY. The entire width between boundary lines of any highway, road,
street, avenue, alley or public driveway located within the corporate limits of
the village.
   OPERATE. To ride in or on, other than as a passenger, use or control the
operation of a snowmobile in any manner, whether or not the snowmobile is
underway.
   OPERATOR. Any person who operates or is in actual physical control of a
snowmobile.
   SNOWMOBILE. A self-propelled device designed for travel on snow or ice or
natural terrain, steered by skis or runners, and supported in part by skis,
belts or cleats.
(1987 Code, § 24-8-1)
§ 6-6-2 USE RESTRICTED.
   Except as otherwise provided in this chapter, no snowmobile shall be driven
or operated by any person on any highway within the corporate limits of the
village.
(1987 Code, § 24-8-2)
§ 6-6-3 OBEDIENCE TO TRAFFIC LAWS AND POLICE.
   Each driver or operator of any snowmobile shall strictly observe all traffic
signs and signals and all other traffic rules and regulations applicable to
motor vehicles operating within the village, and shall obey the orders and
directions of any village police officer authorized to direct or regulate
traffic.
(1987 Code, § 24-8-5) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 6-6-4 OPERATION ON CERTAIN STREETS ONLY.
   Subject to the restrictions and conditions set forth in §
6-6-5, snowmobiles may be driven or operated upon Washington Street located
within the corporate limits of the village and upon any other highway within
said corporate limits which is the most direct accessible route for such driver
and operator to travel to and from said Washington Street to and from outside
of said corporate limits; provided, however, snowmobiles may be driven anywhere
within the village in the event of any emergency where life, limb or property
is endangered.
(1987 Code, § 24-8-3)
§ 6-6-5 CONDITIONS OF OPERATION.
   It shall be unlawful to drive or operate any snowmobile upon the highways
under the following circumstances:
   A.   Registration Required. Without having the snowmobile registered as
required and pursuant to the Snowmobile Registration and Safety Act, amendments
thereto, or any rules and regulations issued thereunder;
   B.   Driver’s License. Without having a valid motor vehicle driver’s
license;
   C.   Hours. Between the hours of 10:00 p.m. and 7:00 a.m.;
   D.   Damage To Pavement. When there is insufficient or inadequate snow or
ice upon such highways for the operation of any snowmobile without doing damage
or destruction to such highways;
   E.   Equipment.
      1.   Without having the snowmobile properly equipped with the equipment
required by and pursuant to the Snowmobile Registration and Safety Act,
amendments thereto, or any rules and regulations issued thereunder; or
      2.   At any time without at least one lighted headlamp and one lighted
taillamp on the snowmobile, both said lamps exhibiting the required minimum
candlepower as required under the Snowmobile Registration and Safety Act,
amendments thereto, or any rules and regulations issued thereunder.
   F.   Speed. At a rate of speed too fast for conditions, and in any event, at
a speed exceeding 20 mph, and the fact that the speed of the snowmobile does
not exceed said maximum 20 mph speed limit does not relieve the driver or
operator from the duty to decrease speed as may be necessary to avoid colliding
with any person or vehicle or object within legal requirements and the duty of
all persons to use due care;
   G.   Pulling Other Items. While any snow or ice sled of any kind or other
apparatus or equipment is attached to and being pulled, pushed or carried by
any snowmobile;
   H.   Reckless Operation. In a careless, reckless or negligent manner;
   I.   Under Influence Of Alcohol Or Drugs. While under the influence of
intoxicating beverages or narcotic or dangerous drugs;
   J.   Weapons Possession. While carrying or having any firearm or weapon in
his or her possession; or
   K.   Noisy Conditions. In a manner so as to create loud, unnecessary or
unusual noise so as to disturb or interfere with the peace and quiet of other
persons.
(1987 Code, § 24-8-4)
Statutory reference:
   Similar provisions, see 625 ILCS 40/1-1 et seq.
CHAPTER 7: GOLF CARTS AND RECREATIONAL OFF-HIGHWAY VEHICLES
Section
   
6-7-1   Definitions
   
6-7-2   Use Restricted
   
6-7-3   Signs
   
6-7-4   Operation Of Golf Carts And Recreational Off-Highway Vehicles Permitted
   
6-7-5   Governmental Immunity
   
6-7-6   Penalty
§ 6-7-1 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions apply unless the
context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   GOLF CART. A vehicle specifically designed and intended for the purposes of
transporting one or more persons and their golf clubs or maintenance equipment
while engaged in the playing of golf, supervising the play of golf, or
maintaining the condition of the grounds on a public or private golf course.
   RECREATIONAL OFF-HIGHWAY VEHICLE. Any motorized off-highway device designed
to travel primarily off-highway, 64 inches or less in width, having a
manufacturer’s dry weight of 2,000 pounds or less, traveling on four or more
non-highway tires, designed with a nonstraddle seat and a steering wheel for
steering control, except equipment such as lawnmowers.
   ROADWAY. A street, road, highway or alleyway located within the corporate
limits of the village and under the jurisdiction of the village.
   STATE VEHICLE CODE. 625 ILCS 5/1-100 et seq., and all amendments thereto.
   VILLAGE. The village of Edinburg, Illinois, a municipal corporation.
(Ord. 855, passed 7-9-2018)
§ 6-7-2 USE RESTRICTED.
   A.   Except as otherwise specifically provided in this chapter, it is
unlawful for any person to drive or operate a golf cart or recreational off-
highway vehicle upon any roadway in the village.
   B.   It is unlawful for any person to drive or operate any all-terrain
vehicle (as defined by the Illinois Vehicle Code, 625 ILCS 5/1-100 et seq.) or
any off-highway motorcycle (as defined by the Illinois Vehicle Code, 625 ILCS
5/1-100 et seq.).
(Ord. 855, passed 7-9-2018)
§ 6-7-3 SIGNS.
   The village’s general superintendent shall cause appropriate signs to be
posted on those village roadways where golf carts and recreational off-highway
vehicles are prohibited as set forth in this chapter.
(Ord. 855, passed 7-9-2018)
§ 6-7-4 OPERATION OF GOLF CARTS AND RECREATIONAL OFF-HIGHWAY VEHICLES
PERMITTED.
   The operation of golf carts and recreational off-highway vehicles within the
village is permitted subject to the following rules and regulations.
   A.   It is unlawful for any person to drive or operate a golf cart or
recreational off-highway vehicle on any village roadway where the posted speed
limit is more than 35 mph; provided, however, a golf cart or recreational off-
highway vehicle may cross at an intersection a village roadway where the
village roadway has a posted speed limit of more than 35 mph.
   B.   It is unlawful for any person to drive or operate a golf cart or a
recreational off-highway vehicle on any village roadway unless he or she has a
valid driver’s license issued in his or her name issued by the state secretary
of state or by a foreign jurisdiction.
   C.   It is unlawful for any person driving or operating a golf cart or
recreational off-highway vehicle to make a direct crossing upon or across any
toll road, interstate highway, or controlled access highway within the village.
   D.   It is unlawful for any person driving or operating a golf cart or
recreational off-highway vehicle to make a direct crossing upon or across any
other highway under the jurisdiction of the village except at an intersection
of the highway with another public street, road or highway.
   E.   It is unlawful for any person to drive or operate a golf cart or
recreational off-highway vehicle on any village roadway unless, at a minimum,
such golf cart or recreational off-highway vehicle has the following equipment:
brakes, a steering apparatus, tires, a rearview mirror, red reflectorized
warning devices in the front and rear, a slow moving emblem (as required of
other vehicles in the Illinois Vehicle Code, 625 ILCS 5/12-709) on the rear of
the golf cart or recreational off-highway vehicle, a headlight that emits a
white light visible from a distance of 500 feet to the front, a tail lamp that
emits a red light visible from at least 100 feet from the rear, brake lights
and turn signals.
   F.   It is unlawful for any person to drive or operate a golf cart or
recreational off-highway vehicle on any village roadway without the headlight
and tail lamps lighted on the golf cart or recreational off-highway vehicle as
required by the Illinois Vehicle Code, 625 ILCS 5/12-201.
   G.   A person who drives or operates or is in actual physical control of a
golf-cart or recreational off-highway vehicle on a village roadway while under
the influence is subject to the Illinois Vehicle Code §§ 11-500 through 11-502,
being 625 ILCS 5/11-501 through 625 ILCS 5/11-502. It is unlawful for any
person to drive, operate or be in actual physical control of a golf cart or
recreational off-highway vehicle on a village roadway in violation of any one
or more of said sections of the state vehicle code.
   H.   It is unlawful for any person to drive or operate a golf cart or
recreational off-highway vehicle on any village roadway without having the
mandatory insurance coverage as required under the Illinois Vehicle Code, 625
ILCS 5/7-601 et seq.
   I.   It is unlawful for any person to drive or operate a golf cart or
recreational off-highway vehicle on any sidewalk within the village.
   J.   It is unlawful for any person to drive or operate a golf cart or
recreational off-highway vehicle on any village roadway in violation of any
traffic regulation or regulations under the state village code.
   K.   1.   It is unlawful for any person to drive or operate a golf cart or
recreational off-highway vehicle on any village roadway unless it has been
inspected and issued an annual permit or a one-time special event permit from
the village. Such annual permit shall be issued for a time period that
commences on June 1 and terminates on May 31 of the following year unless
sooner revoked by the village for non-compliance of the equipment requirements
for such golf cart or recreational off-highway vehicle.
      2.    Anyone obtaining such annual golf cart permit or annual
recreational off-highway vehicle permit shall pay the village a non-prorated
fee of $25; provided, however, for a fee of $5 a one-time special event permit
may be issued for a golf cart or recreational off-highway vehicle during any
calendar year.
      3.   The village’s chief of police and/or his or her designee shall be in
charge of such inspection. The golf cart or recreational off-highway vehicle
being inspected must meet the equipment requirements as set forth in subsection
E. above.
      4.   It is unlawful for any person to drive or operate a golf cart or
recreational off-highway vehicle on any village roadway unless such permit
(sticker form) is affixed and openly displayed on the rear of the golf cart or
recreational off-highway vehicle near the slow moving emblem.
(Ord. 855, passed 7-9-2018; Ord. 887, passed 11-14-2022)
§ 6-7-5 GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY.
   No action by the village under this chapter or under 625 ILCS 5/11-1426.1
designates the operation of a golf cart or recreational off-highway vehicle as
an intended or permitted use of property with respect to the Local Government
and Governmental Employees Tort Immunity Act § 3-102, being 745 ILCS 10/1-101
et seq.
(Ord. 855, passed 7-9-2018)
§ 6-7-6 PENALTY.
   Any person violating any of the provisions of this chapter shall, for each
offense, be fined and punished as provided in § 1-4-1 for each separate
offense. Each day upon which a violation occurs or continues shall be deemed a
separate offense.
(Ord. 855, passed 7-9-2018)

Section

7-1-5   Buildings And Building Materials
