§ 7-1-1 STREET DEPARTMENT.
   A.   Established; Composition. There is hereby established a department of
the municipal government which shall be known as the street department. It
shall embrace the street committee, the general superintendent and the
employees. The village engineer, if any, shall serve as ex officio officer.
(1987 Code, § 33-1-1)
   B.   Committee On Streets And Alleys. The village board standing committee
on streets and alleys shall exercise a general supervision over the affairs of
the street department. The committee shall ascertain the needs and conditions
thereof and shall, from time to time, report the same to the mayor and the
village board.
(1987 Code, § 33-1-2)
§ 7-1-2 ALLEYS AND GUTTERS TO BE CLEAN.
   It shall be the duty of every person owning any lot or parcel of land
adjoining any public or private alley in the village to keep the same clean and
in an inoffensive condition, and also to keep the gutter in front of the
premises owned or occupied by him or her and under his or her control free from
filth or other matter that may prevent the free flow of water therein.
(1987 Code, § 33-2-3)
§ 7-1-3 DEPOSITS ON PUBLIC WAYS AND PLACES.
   A.   Streets And Public Places.
      1.   It shall be unlawful to deposit on any street any material which may
be harmful to the pavement thereof, or any waste material, or any other
articles, such as glass, which may cause injury to any person, animal or
property.
      2.   No person shall place or cause to be placed or erected on any public
ground, or in any public street, alley or sidewalk in the village, any debris,
materials or obstruction, except as may be permitted by this code.
      3.   It shall be the duty of the police department to exercise a vigilant
supervision over such places and to notify any person found making such deposit
or responsible for same to remove the offending matter at once.
      4.   It shall be unlawful to deposit, place, blow onto or cause to be
deposited or placed or blown onto any public street any grass clippings, weed
clippings, plant clippings, leaves, yard waste or any other waste material.
(1987 Code, § 33-2-9)
   B.   Sidewalks.
      1.   It shall be unlawful to deposit on any public sidewalk any material
which may be harmful to the pavement thereof, or any waste material, or any
glass or other articles which might cause injury to persons, animals or
property.
      2.   Merchandise or other articles may be deposited on sidewalks
preparatory to delivery; provided, that the usable width is not thereby reduced
to less than four feet; and provided, that no such article shall remain on such
walk for more than one-half hour.
(1987 Code, § 33-2-8)
   C.   Fire Hydrants. No person shall deposit or cause to be deposited any
snow and/or ice on or against a fire hydrant or on any sidewalk or roadway.
(1987 Code, § 33-2-2) (Ord. 783, passed 7-11-2011)
§ 7-1-4 MERCHANDISE DISPLAY, WRITING ON PUBLIC WAYS AND PLACES.
   It shall be unlawful for any person, firm or corporation to use any street,
sidewalk or other public place as space for the display of goods or merchandise
for sale, or to write or make any signs or advertisements on any such
pavements, unless permission is granted by the village board.
(1987 Code, § 33-2-12)
§ 7-1-5 BUILDINGS AND BUILDING MATERIALS.
   A.   Encroaching Buildings. It shall be unlawful to erect or maintain any
building or structure which encroaches upon any public street or property.
(1987 Code, § 33-2-13)
   B.   Building Materials In Public Ways.
      1.   Any person engaged in erecting a building or fence or improving any
lot on any street may deposit materials thereon and contiguous thereto for such
length of time as may be necessary for the work. The obstruction shall not
extend to more than one-half of the width of the sidewalk, street or alley
adjacent to such improvement, and the gutter shall always be left free and
unobstructed.
      2.   The general superintendent may move any obstruction on any street or
sidewalk of the village, but before doing so, he or she shall notify the person
responsible therefor to remove such obstruction within a reasonable time after
being notified.
(1987 Code, § 33-2-11)
§ 7-1-6 STAIRWAY RAILINGS.
   Steps or stairways leading into any building shall not extend from the wall
of such building onto any pavement or sidewalk, and in such case, the person
making or causing to be made such passage shall erect a railing on the side of
the stairs toward the street, to prevent persons from falling into the street.
(1987 Code, § 33-2-4)
§ 7-1-7 STORMWATER DRAINS.
   It shall be unlawful to construct or permit the construction of any
stormwater drain which discharges water onto any sidewalk in the village, and
it shall be unlawful to construct or permit the maintenance of any such drain
which discharges into any public street or alley at a height greater than 18
inches above the ground or pavement.
(1987 Code, § 33-2-10)
§ 7-1-8 DAMAGE TO NEW PAVEMENTS.
   It shall be unlawful to walk upon or drive any vehicle or animal upon or
destroy any newly laid sidewalk pavement while the same is guarded by a warning
sign or barricade, or to unknowingly injure any soft, newly laid pavement.
(1987 Code, § 33-2-15)
§ 7-1-9 CLOSING STREETS.
   Whenever public safety or the improvement or repair of any street, alley or
public place requires it, the mayor may order any street, alley or public place
temporarily closed to traffic and the placing of signs indicating that the
street, alley or public place is closed by order of the mayor. Whenever such
signs are so placed, no person shall ride or drive upon or across such street,
alley or public place, or in any manner destroy, deface or remove any such
sign.
(1987 Code, § 33-2-5)
§ 7-1-10 ADVERTISEMENTS ON POLES.
   No person shall nail, tack, paste, paint, or fasten, or cause to be nailed,
tacked, painted or fastened, any sign or any other foreign substance or
material on any telephone, telegraph, electric light, police and/or fire alarm
pole or post, or any street or traffic sign located on any sidewalk, street,
alley or public grounds or injure or deface any such pole or post.
(1987 Code, § 33-2-14)
Cross-reference:
   Posting Bills, see
5-4-4-5
   Unlawful Advertising, see §
4-1-1 M.
§ 7-1-11 RIDING VEHICLES AND BICYCLES ON SIDEWALKS.
   No person shall operate any bicycle or vehicle over any sidewalk, except in
crossing the same to go into a yard or parking lot. Any person damaging any
sidewalk by operation of any vehicle shall pay all expenses for repair or
replacement of said sidewalk made by the village.
(1987 Code, § 33-2-7)
§ 7-1-12 MOVING BUILDINGS.
   No person shall remove or cause to be removed any building through or along
any street, avenue
or alley of the village without a written permit from the mayor therefor, nor
shall any person, in removing such building, unnecessarily encumber or obstruct
any street, avenue, or alley, nor permit such operation for a longer time than
may be necessary in the prompt and diligent removal thereof.
(1987 Code, § 33-2-17)
ARTICLE A: SIDEWALK CONSTRUCTION AND REPAIR
Section
   
7-1A-1   Permit Requirements; Inspections
   
7-1A-2   Installation And Repair Work
   
7-1A-3   Sidewalk Grade
   
7-1A-4   Subdivisions Exempt
   
7-1A-5   Violations; Penalties
§ 7-1A-1 PERMIT REQUIREMENTS; INSPECTIONS.
   A.   Application For Permit. Every owner or owners of real property which
fronts or is located adjacent to any street within the village who desire(s) to
have installed a public sidewalk on or adjacent to their real property or to
have any existing public sidewalk on or adjacent to their real property
repaired or replaced must first file a written application with the village for
a sidewalk permit, and such application shall be in a form as the village board
of trustees or the village president may determine from time to time.
   B.   Review Of Permit. Such written application shall be reviewed by the
village board of trustees at a village board meeting; provided, however, if §
7-1A-2 B applies, the village president is authorized to review such written
application and to grant or deny, in his or her sole discretion, a sidewalk
permit in accordance with said §
7-1A-2 B.
   C.   Inspections. The village general superintendent, if directed by the
village board or the village president, as the case may be, shall inspect the
subject real property to determine whether the subject existing public
sidewalk, if any, is in need of repair or replacement and whether such existing
sidewalk has been damaged or become deteriorated through acts of God or nature
or through the negligence the same according to the instructions of the general
superintendent and the village board.
(1987 Code, § 33-5-1)
§ 7-1A-2 INSTALLATION AND REPAIR WORK.
   A.   Except as provided in §
7-1A-1C, all sidewalk permit applications shall be determined on a first come
first serve basis. If any sidewalk permit is granted by the majority vote of
the village board of trustees, then the installation of any such new public
sidewalk and/or the repair and/or replacement of any such existing public
sidewalk shall be installed and/or repaired and/or replaced by the village and/
or by its contractor(s), designated from time to time by the village board;
provided, however, the owner or owners of the real property shall jointly and
severally be responsible for and shall pay the village a sum equal to 50% of
the cement, materials, machinery and labor total costs, as determined from time
to time by the village in its sole discretion, to perform such sidewalk work.
Such 50% amount shall be paid to the village prior to commencement of, and as a
condition precedent to, any such sidewalk work. All such sidewalk work shall be
scheduled and performed at the convenience of the village and/or its designated
contractor(s). The specifications for the cement and the length, width, depth
and location of such sidewalk(s) shall be determined from time to time by the
village board in its sole discretion. If the village board deems it necessary,
every owner of real property issued a sidewalk permit shall sign and deliver to
the village a perpetual easement over the subject real property for the
installation, location, relocation, maintenance, repair or replacement of such
sidewalk. The easement instrument shall be in such form and shall contain such
terms and provisions as the village board shall determine from time to time in
its sole discretion. The preparation cost of such easement and the recording
thereof and any required title insurance policy costs shall be paid by such
owner(s) prior to commencement of, and as a condition precedent to, any such
sidewalk work.
   B.   If such owner(s) desire to expedite such sidewalk work and state in
such application they are willing to pay 100% of such sidewalk work, then
subsection A. above shall not apply, and if the village president, in his sole
discretion, approves such written application and grants a sidewalk permit,
then the installation of any such new public sidewalk and/or the repair and/or
replacement of any such existing public sidewalk shall be installed and/or
repaired and/or replaced by the village and/or by its contractor(s) designated
from time to time by the village board; provided, however, the owner or owners
of the real property shall jointly and severally be responsible for and shall
pay the village a sum equal to 100% of the cement, materials, machinery and
labor total costs, as determined from time to time by the village in its sole
discretion, to perform such sidewalk work. Such 100% amount shall be paid to
the village prior to commencement of, and as a condition precedent to, any such
sidewalk work. All such sidewalk work shall be scheduled by the village
president which need not be on a first come first serve basis as set forth in
the subsection A. above, and all such sidewalk work shall be performed by the
village and/or its contractor(s) at their respective convenience. The
specifications for the cement and the length, width, depth and location of such
sidewalk(s) shall be determined from time to time by the village board in its
sole discretion. If the village president deems it necessary, every owner of
real property issued a sidewalk permit shall sign and deliver to the village a
perpetual easement over the subject real property for the installation,
location, relocation, maintenance, repair or replacement of such sidewalk. The
easement instrument shall be in such form and shall contain such terms and
provisions as the village board shall determine from time to time in its sole
discretion. The preparation cost of such easement and the recording thereof and
any required title insurance policy costs shall be paid by such owner(s) prior
to commencement of, and as a condition precedent to, any such sidewalk work.
(Ord. 720, passed 5-8-2006)
§ 7-1A-3 SIDEWALK GRADE.
   No sidewalk shall be built above or below the established grade of the
village, and in all cases where no grade is established, any person building a
sidewalk shall build the same according to the instructions of the general
superintendent and the village board.
(1987 Code, § 33-5-1)
§ 7-1A-4 SUBDIVISIONS EXEMPT.
   This article is not applicable to new sections.
(1987 Code, § 33-5-1)
§ 7-1A-5 VIOLATIONS; PENALTIES.
   A.   Violations.
      1.   It shall be unlawful for any person, firm, entity or corporation
(except for the village or its designee(s) as referred to in this article) to
install, construct, repair or replace any new or existing public sidewalk, in
whole or in part, within the village.
      2.   It shall be unlawful for any person, firm, entity or corporation
(except for the village or its designee(s) as referred to herein) to remove or
damage or destroy or relocate any public sidewalk, in whole or in part.
   B.   Penalties.
      1.   Notwithstanding anything herein to the contrary, any person, firm,
entity or corporation who damages and/or destroys any public sidewalk, in whole
or in part, shall pay the village, within 14 days following demand by the
village, an amount equal to 100% of all costs incurred by the village to repair
and/or replace such sidewalk(s). It shall be unlawful for any person, firm,
entity or corporation to damage or destroy any public sidewalk, in whole or in
part (excepting the village and/or its employees and/or the village’s
authorized representatives or contractors who may have been given specific
authority by the village to damage and/or destroy any public sidewalk).
      2.   Any person, firm, entity or corporation who violates any of the
provisions in this article shall be fined, upon conviction thereof, a sum not
less than $250 and not more than $750 for each offense, and a separate offense
shall be deemed committed on each day upon which such violation occurs or
continues.
(Ord. 720, passed 5-8-2006)
ARTICLE B: DRIVEWAYS AND CULVERTS
Section
   
7-1B-1   Driveways
   
7-1B-2   Culverts
§ 7-1B-1 DRIVEWAYS.
   A.   Permit Requirements. No person shall construct a driveway for vehicles
or animals across any sidewalk in the village without having first obtained a
permit therefor. Applications for such permits shall be made to the village
board of trustees and shall be accompanied by a fee of $1.
(1987 Code, §§ 33-7-1 and 33-7-2)
   B.   Surface And Grade.
      1.   No driveway shall be so constructed or graded as to leave a step,
sharp depression or other obstruction in the sidewalk. The grade shall be as
nearly as possible the same as that of the adjoining sidewalk.
      2.   It shall be unlawful to have the surface finish of any driveway
where the same crosses the sidewalk constructed of such materials as to render
it slippery and hazardous to pedestrians, or to have the grade of such portion
vary from the grade of the sidewalk or be other than level.
(1987 Code, § 33-7-3)
   C.   Construction Specifications. Driveways across sidewalks shall be
constructed in compliance with the specifications required by the village board
of trustees.
(1987 Code, § 33-7-4)
   D.   Maintenance And Repair. It shall be the duty of the person maintaining
the driveway to keep the same in good repair where it crosses the sidewalk and
tree from obstruction and openings.
(1987 Code, § 33-7-5)
§ 7-1B-2 CULVERTS.
   A.   Obstruction Of Drain Or Storm Sewer Prohibited. It shall be unlawful to
obstruct any drain or storm sewer in any public street or property.
(1987 Code, § 33-6-1)
   B.   Permit Requirements.
      1.   Permit Required. It shall be unlawful to install any culvert or
replace any culvert without first obtaining a permit from the village board of
trustees.
(1987 Code, § 33-6-2)
      2.   Application For Permit. Any person desiring a permit to install or
replace any culvert shall file an application therefor with the village board
of trustees upon a form to be provided for that purpose. The application and
the permit issued pursuant thereto shall be on the same form which shall be as
provided by the village.
(1987 Code, § 33-6-3) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   C.   Construction Requirements.
      1.   General Requirements. All culverts shall be installed or replaced
where driveways or walkways cross open ditches within the village right-of-way.
Such culvert(s) shall be of such materials, length, diameter and gauge and
shall be installed at the grade and constructed with coupling joints, as
determined by the village board of trustees from time to time in its sole
discretion.
      2.   Costs. The applicant desiring the installation or replacement of a
culvert or culverts shall pay the village for the cost of the culvert pipe(s)
and coupling joint(s), and the applicant shall deliver the culvert pipe(s) and
coupling joint(s) to the site prior to installation or replacement of such
culvert(s).
      3.   Work Done By Village. The village will install or replace the
culvert(s) and will initially place rock, of a kind, quantity, and quality as
the general superintendent shall determine, over the culvert area(s) all at a
time convenient to the village. Immediately upon the installation or
replacement of such culvert(s), the applicant shall pay the village for its
labor, equipment and rock cost to install or replace any one culvert that
exceeds 30 feet in length. The village will provide, free of charge, the labor,
equipment and initial rock to install or replace a culvert not to exceed 30
feet in length.
(1987 Code, § 33-6-4)
ARTICLE C: OVERHANGING SIGNS AND AWNINGS
Section
   
7-1C-1   Permit And Compliance Required
   
7-1C-2   Construction Requirements
   
7-1C-3   Height Above Public Ways
   
7-1C-4   Inspections
§ 7-1C-1 PERMIT AND COMPLIANCE REQUIRED.
   No person shall erect any sign, signboard, advertisement, banner or rigid
canopy over or across any street, sidewalk, alley or other public way in the
village without having first obtained a permit therefor, as herein provided.
Permits for signs, advertisements, banners, canopies or signboards shall be
issued by the mayor upon payment of the required fee, and said permit shall
designate the location of the proposed structure. All signs, signboards,
advertisements, banners and canopies shall conform to the requirements of
regulations governing the same.
(1987 Code, § 33-8-1) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 7-1C-2 CONSTRUCTION REQUIREMENTS.
   All signs and canopies extending over public sidewalks, streets, alleys or
other public places must be securely fastened and constructed so that there
will be no danger of the same being dislodged by ordinary winds or falling from
other causes.
(1987 Code, § 33-8-2)
§ 7-1C-3 HEIGHT ABOVE PUBLIC WAYS.
   The lowest part of any canopy, sign, signboard, advertisement or banner, or
of any nonrigid awning or any support thereof, which extends over any public
way shall be at least ten feet above the level of the walk or public way over
which it extends; but no such sign, signboard, advertisement or banner shall be
maintained over any public way used by vehicles if any part of its support or
if the sign, signboard, advertisement or banner is less than 15 feet above the
level of such public way.
(1987 Code, § 33-8-3) (Amd. 2008 Code)
Cross-reference:
   Structures Overhanging Sidewalks, see §
10-1-10 B.1.
§ 7-1C-4 INSPECTIONS.
   It shall be the duty of the mayor or his or her designated representative to
inspect or cause to be inspected every sign, canopy or awning which extends
over any sidewalk, street, alley or other public way. If any such sign or
canopy is found to be insecurely fastened, he or she shall report this fact to
the owner of the sign or to the owner or occupant of the premises on which it
is fastened. If the sign or canopy is not made secure within ten days after
such notice, it may be torn down on order of the mayor.
(1987 Code, § 33-8-4)
CHAPTER 2: EXCAVATIONS
Section
   
7-2-1   Permit Required
   
7-2-2   Application For Permit
   
7-2-3   Bond Required
   
7-2-4   Conditions Of Permit
   
7-2-5   Notice To JULIE; Compliance With State Law
§ 7-2-1 PERMIT REQUIRED.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to tunnel under or to make any
excavation in any street, alley or other public place or public right-of-way
within the village without first obtaining a permit from the village board or
in violation of or in variance from the terms of any such written permit.
(1987 Code, § 33-4-1)
§ 7-2-2 APPLICATION FOR PERMIT.
   Application for such permit shall be made to the village board and shall
describe the location of the intended excavation or tunnel, the size thereof,
the purpose therefor, and the name and address of the person, firm or
corporation doing the actual excavating work and the name and address of the
person for whom or for which the work is being done. The application shall
contain an agreement that the applicant will comply with the terms and
conditions of such permit and with all ordinances and laws relating to the work
to be done.
(1987 Code, § 33-4-2)
§ 7-2-3 BOND REQUIRED.
   No such permit shall be issued unless and until the applicant therefor has
filed with the village clerk a bond in an amount as determined by the village
board, conditioned to indemnify the village for any breach of any such permit
and for any loss, liability or damage, including, without limitation, damages
resulting from cave ins that may result or accrue from or because of the
making, existence or manner of guarding or constructing any such tunnel or
excavation, for a period of one year following completion of such tunnel or
excavation. Such bond shall have as surety a corporation licensed to do
business in the state as a surety company.
(1987 Code, § 33-4-3)
§ 7-2-4 CONDITIONS OF PERMIT.
   A.   As a condition of such permit, the applicant shall hold harmless and
indemnify the village, its officers, employees and agents, from any and all
losses, including reasonable attorney fees and costs, claims, liability
damages, suits or causes of action which may result or accrue, directly or
indirectly, from or because of the making, existence or manner of guarding or
constructing any such tunnel or excavation.
   B.   The village board may further condition such permit upon such other
terms and provisions as they may determine necessary or desirable.
(1987 Code, § 33-4-4)
§ 7-2-5 NOTICE TO JULIE; COMPLIANCE WITH STATE LAW.
   The permittee, prior to any tunneling or excavation or demolition work,
shall notify all applicable persons, firms, corporations and entities
associated with JULIE (the statewide one call notice system) and shall comply
with all applicable provisions of the Illinois Underground Utility Facilities
Damage Prevention Act, being 220 ILCS 50/1 et seq.
(Ord. 704, passed 4-11-2005)
Statutory reference:
   Similar provisions, see 220 ILCS 50/1 et seq.
CHAPTER 3: TREES AND SHRUBS
Section
   
7-3-1   Definitions
   
7-3-2   Permit To Plant, Trim Or Remove
   
7-3-3   Trees On Private Property
   
7-3-4   Public Utility Poles And Wires
   
7-3-5   Injury To Trees And Shrubs
   
7-3-6   Attaching Items
   
7-3-7   Depositing Cut Materials At Water Well Site
   
7-3-8   Penalty
§ 7-3-1 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions apply unless the
context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   FALLEN OR CUT TREE OR BUSH. Shall include all or any part of a tree, bush,
shrub or plant of any size, kind or nature, including, but not limited to,
limbs, leaves, trunk or roots, which has fallen due to natural causes or has
been cut down or trimmed by any person.
   PERSON. Any person, firm, corporation or entity of any kind, nature or
extent whatsoever.
   PUBLIC WAY. Any village street, avenue, roadway, right-of-way, boulevard,
sidewalk or easement area; the area between a sidewalk and a village street,
alleyway, avenue, roadway or right-of-way; the area between a person’s property
boundary line and a village street, alleyway, avenue, roadway or right-of-way;
and all other areas owned, leased, or maintained by the village or in which the
village has a proprietary interest.
   VILLAGE. The village of Edinburg, Illinois, a municipal corporation.
   VILLAGE WATER WELL SITE. Shall include all interest in any real estate owned
by the village located in the county, and upon which said real estate the
village has water wells that service the village.
(1987 Code, §§ 33-3-7 and 35A-1-1)
§ 7-3-2 PERMIT TO PLANT, TRIM OR REMOVE.
   No persons, except the village, shall plant, replant, trim, cut, prune, top,
remove, dispose of or destroy any tree, shrub, bush or other woody vegetation
located within or overhanging on any public way without first securing a permit
from the village to do so, which permit may be denied by the village in its
sole discretion from time to time; and such permit, if granted, may be subject
to such terms and conditions as the village may determine from time to time.
Such person(s) shall make written application for such permit on a form(s) as
the village may determine from time to time and file same with the village
clerk’s office.
(1987 Code, § 35A-1-2)
§ 7-3-3 TREES ON PRIVATE PROPERTY.
   A.   Overhanging Public Ways. Every owner of any tree, shrub, bush or other
woody vegetation on private property overhanging any public way shall trim,
cut, prune or top the branches so that such branches shall not obstruct light
from any public way lamp or fixture or obstruct the view of any public way
intersection and so that there shall be a clear space of 13 feet and six inches
above the surface of the public way.
(1987 Code, § 35A-1-3) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   B.   Dead, Diseased, Dangerous Trees And Shrubs.
      1.   The owners shall remove all dead, diseased or dangerous trees,
shrubs, bushes or woody vegetation, or broken or decayed limbs or branches
which constitute a menace to the safety of the public. (1987 Code, § 35A-1-3)
      2.   The village, through its village president, shall have the right to
order the removal of any dead or diseased trees on private property within the
village when such trees constitute a hazard to life and property, or harbor
insects or disease which constitute a potential threat to trees, shrubs, bushes
or other woody vegetation located within any public way within the village. It
is hereby declared that such trees are a public nuisance. The village president
shall notify, in writing, the owners of the property where such trees, shrubs,
bushes or other woody vegetation are located. Removal shall be done by said
owners, at their own expense, within 60 days after the date of the service of
notice.
(1987 Code, § 35A-1-4)
   C.   Interfering With Light And Traffic Visibility. The village shall have
the right to trim, cut, prune, or top any tree, shrub, bush or woody vegetation
on private property when it interferes with the proper spread of light along
the public way from a public way light or interferes with the visibility of any
traffic control device or sign.
   D.   Injury To Utility Lines. The owners shall trim, cut, prune, top,
remove, dispose of or destroy any tree, shrub, bush or other woody vegetation,
or part thereof, on private property which, by reason of its nature, is
injurious to village sewers, gas lines, water lines or other public
improvements, or is injurious to public electric power lines, telephone lines,
cable television lines or telecommunication lines or systems.
(1987 Code, § 35A-1-3)
§ 7-3-4 PUBLIC UTILITY POLES AND WIRES.
   Any person or company given the right to maintain the poles and wires in the
streets, alleys or other public places in the village shall, in the absence of
provision in the franchise concerning the subjects, keep such wires and poles
free from and away from any trees, bushes, shrubs or other plants in such
places so far as may be possible and shall keep all such trees, bushes, shrubs
and other plants properly trimmed, subject to the supervision of the general
superintendent, so that no injury shall be done either to the poles or wires or
the trees, bushes, shrubs and other plants by their conduct.
(1987 Code, § 33-3-6)
§ 7-3-5 INJURY TO TREES AND SHRUBS.
   It shall be unlawful to injure any tree, bush, shrub or other plant planted
in any public place; provided, however, trees may be topped in accordance with
this chapter.
(1987 Code, § 33-3-3)
§ 7-3-6 ATTACHING ITEMS.
   A.   Advertisements Or Notices. It shall be unlawful to attach any sign,
advertisement or notice to any tree, bush, shrub or other plant in any street,
parkway or other public place.
(1987 Code, § 33-3-4)
   B.   Wires Or Rope. It shall be unlawful to attach any wires or rope to any
tree, bush, shrub or other plant in any public street, parkway or other public
place without the permission of the village board.
(1987 Code, § 33-3-6)
§ 7-3-7 DEPOSITING CUT MATERIALS AT WATER WELL SITE.
   It shall be unlawful for any owner or person to deposit any fallen or cut
tree, bush, shrub or other plant upon any place at the village water well site.
The provisions of this section shall not apply to any deposits of any fallen or
cut tree, bush, shrub or plant by any village employee authorized to do so by
the village president or his or her authorized delegate.
(1987 Code, §§ 33-3-8 and 33-3-9)
§ 7-3-8 PENALTY.
   Any person who violates any of the provisions of this chapter shall, upon
conviction thereof, for each offense, be punished as provided in §
1-4-1. A separate offense shall be deemed committed on each day in which a
violation occurs or continues.
(1987 Code, § 35A-1-5)
CHAPTER 4: SPECIAL EVENTS
Section
   
7-4-1   Definitions
   
7-4-2   Permit Required And Insurance Requirements
   
7-4-3   Violations, Release, Hold Harmless And Indemnity And Liability
   
7-4-4   Penalty
§ 7-4-1 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions apply unless the
context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   PERSON. Any and all individual persons or group of persons, or public or
private or for-profit or not-for-profit or charitable firms, companies,
partnerships, corporations, limited liability companies, associations,
organizations, entities, joint ventures, or fraternal, religious, veterans,
social, service, or civic clubs, organizations, or associations, all of any
kind, nature or extent whatsoever or any and all other groups of any of the
foregoing acting or functioning or participating as a unit, and the owners,
employees, agents, representatives, members, volunteers and members of any and
all of the foregoing, jointly and severally and collectively. The term PERSON
shall not include the village or its officers, officials or employees while
acting in the course of their office or employment.
   SPECIAL EVENT. As used herein shall mean and include a for-profit or not-
for-profit or charitable event or activity of any kind, nature or extent
whatsoever that takes place on village property, in whole or in part, and which
is open or available to the general public or to members of the sponsoring
entity by free or paid admission or invitation or participation. A SPECIAL
EVENT shall also include any and all water fowl, fishing contests or events,
deer hunting, youth or children activities, or block parties conducted or
participated on village property by any and all persons. The SPECIAL EVENT
includes all times on village property in preparation for and during the
SPECIAL EVENT and the dismantling of any equipment or property associated with
the special event after the cessation of the event or activity.
   SPONSORING ENTITY. As used herein shall mean and include any and all persons
who sponsors or organizes or hosts or arranges or conducts or who is in charge
of or is involved with a special event.
   VILLAGE PROPERTY. In whole or in part, any and all village streets,
alleyways, roadways, easements, rights-of-way, public ways, boulevards,
sidewalks, walkways, pavements, curbs and gutters, lakes, waterways and real
estate and improvements thereon of any kind, nature or extent whatsoever
acquired, owned or leased by the village, in whole or in part.
(Ord. 866, passed 9-9-2019)
§ 7-4-2 PERMIT REQUIRED AND INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS.
   A.   Any sponsoring entity who desires a special event to take place on
village property shall first submit a written application signed by a duly
authorized representative of the sponsoring entity for a village permit for
such special event. The application and village permit shall be in such form as
the village clerk may select or prepare from time to time.
   B.   The village board of trustees shall consider and decide whether to
approve each such village permit for a special event from time to time, which
decision shall be at the sole discretion of the village board of trustees from
time to time.
   C.   Unless excused by the village board of trustees in its sole discretion
from time to time after the village board of trustees considers the kind,
nature and extent of the special event for which a sponsoring entity is
requesting a village permit and any associated perceived risks of liability to
the village if the special event is allowed to be conducted on village
property, the sponsoring entity at its sole expense, and as a condition of the
issuance of a village permit for a special event, shall furnish to the village
a certificate of insurance evidencing “occurrence” type insurance coverage and
not “claims made” type insurance coverage in at least the following designated
amounts (unless a lower amount is agreed by the village board of trustees from
time to time) and which shows such insurance coverage at the sponsoring
entity’s sole expense is and will and shall be maintained in force for the
duration of the special event, to-wit:
      1.   Worker’s compensation insurance coverage in the minimum statutory
limits under state law, if applicable;
      2.   General liability insurance coverage in an amount not less than
$1,000,000 per occurrence and $2,000,000 aggregate;
      3.   Motor vehicle liability insurance coverage in an amount not less
than $1,000,000 combined single limit; and
      4.   Such insurance shall include, but shall not be limited to, coverage
for personal injuries, including death, and property damages, including, but
not limited to, property damages to village property or property of others.
   D.   After considering the kind, nature and extent of the special event for
which a sponsoring entity is requesting a village permit and any associated
perceived risks of liability to the village if the special event is allowed to
be conducted on village property, the village board of trustees in its sole
discretion from time to time may further require as another condition to the
issuance of a village permit for a special event one or more or all of the
following requirements:
      1.   All said required insurance and the certificate of insurance name as
additional insured the village, a municipal corporation, and its elected and/or
appointed village president, village board of trustees, officials, officers,
employees and agents;
      2.   The certificate of insurance shall provide that the insurer must
give the village notice of cancellation and termination according to the policy
provisions. (Omitted: the certificate of insurance shall also contain the
following statement: “The insurance covered by this certificate shall not be
canceled or altered, except after at least ten days’ written notice has been
received by the village clerk.”);
      3.   The sponsoring entity, at its sole expense, shall obtain and
maintain a special event policy which will provide the aforesaid insurance
coverage and that will also include coverage for all participants in the
special event; and/or
      4.   The prior to the commencement of the special event on village
property the sponsoring entity shall secure signed releases of liability from
all persons who will participate in the special event.
(Ord. 866, passed 9-9-2019)
§ 7-4-3 VIOLATIONS, RELEASE, HOLD HARMLESS AND INDEMNITY AND LIABILITY.
   A.   It shall be unlawful for a sponsoring entity to sponsor or organize or
host or arrange or conduct or be in charge of or involved with a special event
on village property without a village permit for such special event as required
in this chapter.
   B.   If the aforesaid insurance coverage and certificate of insurance are
required as conditions of a village permit for a special event, then it shall
be unlawful for a sponsoring entity to sponsor or organize or host or arrange
or conduct or be in charge of or involved with a special event on village
property without first having secured and delivered to the village clerk the
requisite certificates of insurance evidencing the insurance coverage and
amounts and additional named insured insurance provisions as may be required by
the village board of trustees under this chapter.
   C.   Each sponsoring entity that is issued a village permit for a special
event shall be deemed to have consented to and to have released and shall hold
harmless, indemnify, protect and defend the village and its elected and/or
appointed village president, village board of trustees, officials, officers,
employees and agents from and against any and all loss, damages, costs and
expenses (including, but not limited to, the village’s attorney fees and costs)
liability, claims, demands, suits and causes of action of any kind, nature or
extent whatsoever on account of any and all personal injuries or death or
property damages of any kind, nature or extent whatsoever occurring, arising,
incident to, resulting from, or related directly or indirectly or
consequentially, from such special event.
   D.   Each sponsoring entity of a special event who damages any village
property, in whole or in part, shall be liable to the village for any and all
damages of any kind, nature or extent whatsoever to village property. Such
person shall pay the village the full amount of such damages, as determined by
the village, within ten calendar days immediately following receipt of a
village bill for such damages.
   E.   It is unlawful for any sponsoring entity of a special event to
intentionally damage any village property, in whole or in part. For purpose
hereof, a person acts intentionally when such person’s conscious objective or
purpose is to damage village property, in whole or in part.
(Ord. 866, passed 9-9-2019)
§ 7-4-4 PENALTY.
   Any sponsoring entity or person violating any of the terms and provisions of
this chapter shall be punished in accordance with and as provided in §
1-4-1.
(Ord. 866, passed 9-9-2019)

CHAPTER 1: GENERAL UTILITY PROVISIONS
Section
   
8-1-1   Contract For And Conditions Of Utilities Services
   
8-1-2   Determination Of Individual Customer
   
8-1-3   Rates And Charges
   
8-1-4   Excavations
   
8-1-5   Service Outside Village Limits
   
8-1-6   Costs Of Extensions To Systems
   
8-1-7   Nonliability Of Village For Interrupted Service
   
8-1-8   Noncompliance With Rules
§ 8-1-1 CONTRACT FOR AND CONDITIONS OF UTILITIES SERVICES.
   A.   Customer Accepts Service. The rates, rules and regulations contained in
this title shall constitute and be considered a part of the contract with every
person, company or corporation who is supplied with gas, water and sewer
service from the gas, water and sewer service, and every person, company or
corporation, hereinafter called a CUSTOMER, who accepts and uses gas, water and
sewer services shall be held to have consented to be bound thereby.
   B.   Nonliability Of Village. The department will endeavor at all times to
provide a regular and uninterrupted supply of service, but in case the supply
of service shall be interrupted, or irregular, or defective, or fail from
causes beyond its control or through ordinary negligence of employees, servants
or agents, the department shall not be liable therefor.
   C.   Unlawful Use Of Services Without Payment.
      1.   Any person using utility services from the village without paying
therefor, or who shall be found guilty of breaking the seal of any meter or
appurtenance, or shall bypass any meter shall be fined a sum as provided in
title 1, chapter 4.
(1987 Code, § 38-2-1)
      2.   All contracts for utility services must be made in the name of the
head of the household, firm or corporation, and the established spelling of
that person’s or firm’s or corporation’s name shall be used. Attempts to obtain
service by the use of other names, different spellings or by substituting other
persons or firms will be considered a subterfuge and service will be denied. If
service had been discontinued because of nonpayment of bills, or any unpaid
obligation, and service again has been obtained through subterfuge,
misrepresentation or fraud, that service will be promptly disconnected, and the
whole or such part of the advanced payment, as may be necessary to satisfy the
unpaid obligation, shall be retained by the village and credited to the
appropriate account.
(1987 Code, § 38-2-1) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   D.   Damage To Property. Any person found guilty of defacing, tampering,
injuring or destroying, or in any manner limiting the use or availability of
any meter or any property of the utility systems, or erecting signs on the
property of the department without permission shall, upon conviction for such
act, be fined as provided in title 1, chapter 4.
   E.   Failure To Receive Bill; Inability Of Village To Bill. Failure to
receive a bill shall not excuse a customer from his or her obligation to pay
within the time specified. Should the department be unable to bill a customer
for services used during any month, the following billing shall include the
charges for services used during the unbilled month. A month shall be
considered as that period of approximately 30 days.
   F.   Responsibility For Services; Request For Discontinuance. Services shall
have been deemed to have been supplied to any property connected to the utility
systems during a month unless the customer notifies the village in writing
prior to the first day of the new billing month in which the services are to be
discontinued.
(1987 Code, § 38-2-1)
   G.   Rules Part Of Contract. All of the rules and regulations concerning the
use of the facilities of the utility systems and the consumption of services
shall be adopted and the same shall become a part of the contract with every
utility consumer, and every consumer shall be considered to take water and gas
from the village subject thereto and shall be bound thereby.
(1987 Code, § 38-2-7)
Cross-reference:
   Nonliability Of Village For Interrupted Service, see §
8-1-7
§ 8-1-2 DETERMINATION OF INDIVIDUAL CUSTOMER.
   All persons or families residing in a building under one roof, be it any
apartment or homes converted into more than one dwelling place, each family or
individual resident residing therein shall be deemed an individual customer,
and such homes or apartments or dwellings shall be billed for at least one
minimum water, sewer and/or gas account, according to the number of families or
individual residents residing therein.
(1987 Code, § 38-2-4)
§ 8-1-3 RATES AND CHARGES.
   A.   Liability For Charges. The owner of any lot, parcel of land or premises
receiving utility services, the occupant of such premises and the user of the
services shall be jointly and severally liable for the payment of the services
to such lot, parcel of land or premises, and all services are rendered to the
premises by the village only on the condition that such owner, occupant and
user shall be jointly and severally liable therefor to the village.
(1987 Code, § 38-2-2)
   B.   Estimated Charges. Whenever any meter, by reason of its being out of
repair, or from any cause, fails to properly register the utilities passing
through the same, the consumer shall be charged the average charge of the
previous three months’ usage. If no record of the previous three months exists,
then it shall be the duty of the village treasurer to estimate the amount of
utilities consumed during the time the meter fails to operate, and the customer
shall be charged with such estimated amount. Bills may be estimated whenever it
is impossible to read the meters during inclement weather.
(1987 Code, § 38-2-3)
   C.   Disconnect And Reinstatement Of Discontinued Service And Fees.
      1.   Notwithstanding any provision in this code to the contrary, no gas
and/or water and/or sewer utility service shall be reinstated for any premises,
and no person or entity or customer shall be entitled to have any gas and/or
water and/or sewer utility service reinstated to any premises, until and unless
each and all delinquent utility bill(s) and any and all applicable penalties
for gas and/or water and/or sewer services for such premises have been paid in
full, and all required utility deposits have been paid, and the following
reconnection fees, as applicable, has been paid, and the following special
disconnection fee, if applicable, has been paid.
      2.   The reconnection fee for reinstatement of gas utility service shall
be charged at the rate of $25 per hour, with a minimum fee of $50. The
disconnection fee for gas utility service shall be charged at the rate of $25
per hour, with a minimum fee of $50. The reconnection fee for reinstatement of
water/sewer utility service shall be charged at the rate of $25 per hour, with
a minimum fee of $50. The disconnection fee for water/sewer utility service
shall be charged at the rate of $25 per hour, with a minimum fee of $50.
Notwithstanding the above, if after the village has terminated the gas and/or
water and/or sewer service to the premises for any delinquent utility bill, the
gas meter and/or the water meter for such premises is turned back on by someone
other than an authorized village employee or the gas and/or water and/or sewer
service for such premises is otherwise made operable by someone other than an
authorized village employee, then a $100 special disconnection fee shall be
charged and assessed the owner(s) and occupant(s) and utility customer(s) of
the premises, regardless of who actually turned such utility service back on or
made it operable.
   D.   Attorney Fees And Costs. Any PERSON(S) (as defined under §
1-3-2 ) who fail(s) to timely pay any amount owed to the village under any one
or more provisions of this chapter shall severally and jointly pay all
reasonable attorney fees and court costs and other expenses incurred by the
village in efforts, whether successful or not, to collect such amount(s), to
prosecute any ordinance violation proceeding(s) and/or to obtain and/or enforce
and/or collect any judgment and/or to foreclose any village lien.
(1987 Code, § 38-2-12)
   E.   Bad Checks Policy.
      1.   Determination Of Bad Check. Any check presented by or on behalf of a
customer to the village for payment on any village utility bill, which check is
not honored when first presented for payment by the financial institution upon
which such check is drawn shall be deemed a bad check.
      2.   Fee; Delinquent Payments. For each bad check, the customer shall,
within seven days from the date the village mails a notice to the customer, pay
the village a fee of $25, and shall further pay the village a sum equal to the
amount of such bad check in cash, or by money order, or cashier’s check. If
such payments are not made within such seven-day period, the customer’s utility
bill shall be deemed delinquent, and the customer’s village utility services
shall be thereafter terminated, without further notice to the customer, and not
reinstated until and unless such payments are made and all reinstatement fees
are paid.
      3.   Cash Payments Of Utility Bills. Each customer who has issued more
than two bad checks to the village within any consecutive 12-month period must,
for the next consecutive 12-month period, commencing with the date of the third
bad check, and as a condition for receiving any further village utility
service, pay the village in cash, or by money order, or cashier’s check for all
utility services furnished to the customer during such consecutive 12-month
time period.
   F.   Charges And Rates Amendments. The foregoing charges and rates as set
forth in this section may be amended, modified or revised from time to time by
the village president and board of trustees by ordinance, written or oral
resolution or motion as such corporate authorities may elect from time to time.
(Ord. 702, passed 10-11-2004; Ord. 738, passed 6-11-2007)
§ 8-1-4 EXCAVATIONS.
   All excavations for gas, water, sewer and other utility installations shall
be adequately guarded with barricades and lights so as to protect the public
from hazard. Streets, sidewalks and other public property disturbed in the
course of the work shall be restored in a manner satisfactory to the village.
(1987 Code, § 38-2-8)
§ 8-1-5 SERVICE OUTSIDE VILLAGE LIMITS.
   A.   No territory or premises located outside the corporate limits of the
village, which is contiguous to the village corporate limits, shall be
connected with the village municipal water and/or sewer and/or gas distribution
system(s) unless and until a valid petition for annexation of said territory or
premises in a form signed by all owners of record of such territory or
premises, and approved by the village board of trustees, is approved by the
village board of trustees in their sole discretion, and such territory or
premises is annexed to the village.
(1987 Code, § 38-2-10) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   B.   No territory or premises located outside the corporate limits of the
village, which is not contiguous to the village corporate limits, shall be
connected with the village municipal water and/or sewer and/or gas distribution
system(s) unless and until all the owners of record of such territory or
premises enter into an annexation agreement with the village, in a form
acceptable to the village board of trustees, whereby said owners and their
respective heirs, legal and/or personal representatives, executors,
administrators, successors and assigns agree to petition to annex said
territory or premises to the village, subject to village board of trustees
approval, when and if said territory or premises becomes contiguous to the
village corporate limits. Such annexation agreement shall constitute covenants
running with such territory or premises. The village shall have the right to
terminate providing any such gas and/or water and/or sewer service if any such
petition to annex to the village is not filed with the village at the time such
territory or premises becomes contiguous to the village corporate limits.
(1987 Code, § 38-2-10)
§ 8-1-6 COSTS OF EXTENSIONS TO SYSTEMS.
   A.   Water And Sewer Extensions. Within 45 days following the submittal of
an application for a water and/or sewer extension(s), signed by the applicant
(s), the village shall notify such applicant(s) of the estimated cost for
village materials and for village labor and/or use of village machinery and/or
equipment for such extension work (as computed under §
2-1-3  and as required under other provisions of this code). Only village
materials purchased from the village shall be used in such extension work. The
applicant(s) shall pay the village such estimated amount prior to such water
and/or sewer installation extension work being performed by the village. Upon
completion of such installation work by the village, such applicant(s) shall
pay, within 30 days following the mailing of a bill to such applicant(s), such
additional amount for materials, and/or materials actually expended and used
for such extension(s) work. No installation work for such water and/or sewer
extension(s) shall be performed until and unless such estimated payment is
first paid by such applicant to the village.
   B.   Gas Extensions. Within 45 days following the submittal of application
for a gas extension(s), signed by the applicant(s), the village shall notify
such applicant(s) of the estimated cost for village labor and/or use of village
machinery and/or equipment for such extension work (as computed under § 2-1-
3 and as required under other provisions of this code). The applicant(s) shall
pay the village such estimated amount and shall also furnish to the village and
pay for and have approved by the village all required materials prior to any
such gas installation extension work being performed by the village. Upon
completion of such installation work by the village, such applicant(s) shall
pay, within 30 days following the mailing of a bill to such applicant(s), such
additional amount for labor and/or use of village machinery and/or equipment
and/or materials actually expended and used for such extension(s) work. No
installation work for such gas extension(s) shall be performed until and unless
such estimated payment is first paid by the applicant and the required
materials are approved by and furnished by the village.
   C.   Use Of Village Equipment, Materials And Labor. The village estimated
cost for village materials and village labor and/or use of village machinery
and/or equipment for such extension work as referred to in subsections A. and
B. above shall only be good for six months following the village submittal of
such estimated cost; and for any such extension work to be performed six months
after the date of such submitted estimated cost, the applicant must submit a
new application for any such extension work, and the village will thereafter
submit a revised estimated cost good for another six-month period.
(Ord. 698, passed 6-14-2004)
§ 8-1-7 NONLIABILITY OF VILLAGE FOR INTERRUPTED SERVICE.
   All connections for the utility services applied for hereunder, and all
connections now attached to the present utility systems of the village, and all
use of service of the systems shall be upon the express condition that the
village will not be liable for nor shall any claim be made against it for
damages or injury caused by reason of breaking any main, service pipe,
apparatus or appurtenance connected with said utilities or for any interruption
of the supply of services by reason of the breaking of machinery or by reason
of stoppages, alterations or repairs, or by reason of any leaks or by reason of
the shut off of the gas supply of any delinquent user or violator of the
provisions of this code.
(1987 Code, § 38-2-5)
Cross-reference:
   Nonliability Of Village, see §
8-1-1 B.
§ 8-1-8 NONCOMPLIANCE WITH RULES.
   If any consumer fails to fully comply with any of the rules and regulations
in force, the village shall notify the consumer of such failure. If the
consumer does not remedy same as the rules provide and within a reasonable
time, the village shall have the right to discontinue service. Except in cases
of nonpayment, emergency, necessity, or as otherwise provided, the village will
not discontinue service for violation of any rules until five days after notice
has been given and violation has not been remedied.
(1987 Code, § 38-2-6)
