§ 8-2-1 ADMINISTRATION OF SYSTEM.
   A.   Gas Department Established; Composition. There shall be an executive
department of the village known as the gas department. The gas department shall
include the committee on gas, appointed by the mayor, and its employees. The
designated office shall be the village hall.
(1987 Code, § 38-1-1)
   B.   Committee On Gas. The committee on gas shall exercise a general
supervision over the affairs of the gas system. They shall ascertain the
condition and needs thereof; shall, from time to time, report the same to the
mayor and the village board so that a full understanding thereof shall be had;
and generally, shall do all acts necessary to promote the efficiency of the gas
department.
(1987 Code, § 38-1-2)
   C.   Duties Of General Superintendent. The duties of the general
superintendent of the village shall be as follows:
      1.   He or she shall, subject to the direction of the village board, have
the general direction and control of the gas department, and shall see that the
object and purpose thereof are carried out, and that the natural gas system is
conducted on an economical, business like basis;
      2.   He or she shall inspect all connections to the gas system at the
time they are made and report to the treasurer the date when the gas is turned
on;
      3.   He or she shall shut off the gas supply of any delinquent user or
violator of the provisions of this title;
      4.   He or she shall determine the size of each connection to be made;
      5.   He or she shall supervise the setting, taking off, and repairing of
all gas meters; and
      6.   He or she shall make all repairs occasioned by leaks or breaks in
the mains of the gas system, and shall shut off the gas from all premises where
leaks are permitted to go unrepaired.
(1987 Code, § 38-1-4)
   D.   Duties Of Village Treasurer. The village treasurer shall issue permits
for connections to the gas system whenever an application for the same has been
made as provided herein.
(1987 Code, § 38-1-4) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 8-2-2 PERMIT FOR SERVICE.
   A.   Permit And Metered Gas Required. No person shall take any gas from any
main, pipe or other part of the village natural gas transmission line and
distribution system except upon application to the village and issuance by the
village of a permit in accordance with the provisions of this chapter; and no
permit shall be issued for any gas supply or service unless the same is
measured through a meter in accordance with the provisions of this chapter.
(1987 Code, § 38-3-1)
   B.   Application For Permit; Deposit Required.
      1.   Application for gas supply or service shall be as determined from
time to time by the mayor and board of trustees. Such application shall contain
an agreement by the applicant(s) to accept and abide by all the provisions of
this code and all applicable codes, rules and regulations of the village, all
as now or hereafter enacted or amended. In the case of rental property, a total
deposit of $175 shall be paid. When gas service to the applicant is
discontinued, this deposit, minus any amount remaining due the village for gas
service, shall be refunded to the applicant without interest. No such deposit
shall be required of any applicant who is the owner of the premises to be
served by the gas supply or service. For purposes hereof, a contract for deed
purchaser shall be deemed a renter and not an owner of the premises to be
served by gas supply or service.
      2.   The village reserves the right, at any time, to increase the amount
of the required deposit in the case of a renter and to impose a deposit in the
case of an owner in any amount the village deems appropriate as a condition for
initial gas supply or service or for further gas supply or service where such
renter or owner is delinquent in his or her utility bills(s), or has previously
been delinquent in payment of his or her utility bill(s) to the village.
(1987 Code, § 38-3-2)
   C.   Issuance Or Denial Of Permit. Upon receipt of an application as
provided in subsection B. above, a permit may be issued by the village for the
installation of gas service and for the supplying of gas in accordance with the
provisions of this chapter, but no permit shall be issued to any applicant
while he or she is in default in the payment of any past due account owing to
the village for gas supply or service or installation theretofore rendered or
made.
(1987 Code, § 38-3-3)
§ 8-2-3 TAPS AND CONNECTIONS.
   A.   Authority. Taps and connections to mains or any part or portion of the
village natural gas transmission line and distribution system shall be made
only by the village and shall be made in accordance with such reasonable rules
and regulations as may be prescribed by the village. The letting of gas to and
the turning off of gas from all buildings, structures or premises and into and
from all connections and service shall be under the exclusive control of the
village, and no plumber or other person shall let gas to or from any service or
connection for any purpose whatever without permission therefor.
(1987 Code, § 38-3-10)
   B.   Application To Connect. Application to install and connect a gas
service with the village natural gas transmission line and distribution system
shall be first made in writing to the village. The forms of application shall
be determined from time to time by the village president and board of trustees
by oral motion. Such application shall contain an agreement by the applicant(s)
to accept and abide by all the provisions of this code and all applicable
codes, rules and regulations of the village or other governing authorities, all
as now or hereafter enacted, adopted or amended.
   C.   Fee And Costs.
      1.   The applicant(s) shall pay to the village the sum of $200 as a
tapping and connection charge. This tapping and connection charge shall be in
addition to any deposit charge as may be required by §
8-2-2B.
(1987 Code, § 38-3-14)
      2.   In addition thereto, the applicant(s) shall pay for all materials,
including, but not limited to, the meter, all pipes, valves, fitting regulators
and other appurtenances which are necessary for the service connection from the
village main to the point where the gas supply is to enter the outside of the
applicant’s structure which is to receive the gas supply. The village or its
contractor shall actually install the meter and the connecting gas line and
such materials from the village main to the point where the gas supply is to
enter the outside of the applicant’s structure which is to receive the gas
supply. All such materials paid for by the applicant(s) and the gas meter and
such materials furnished by the village shall become and remain the property of
the village. The installation work shall be completed in accordance with the
guidelines set forth in the National Fire Protection Association’s most recent
edition of NFPA 54: national fuel gas code, which is incorporated herein by
reference.
   D.   Nonoperating Meters. Notwithstanding the above, if the village replaces
any nonoperating gas meter, it shall be at no cost to the customer.
(1987 Code, § 38-3-14) (Amd. 2008 Code)
(Ord. 810, passed 7-14-2014)
§ 8-2-4 INSTALLATION OF PIPING; SPECIFICATIONS.
   A.   1.   The piping from the gas meter of the natural gas transmission line
and distribution system through the foundation of the building where gas is to
be used shall be rigid steel pipe. Said rigid pipe shall be run to all gas
appliances permanently affixed to the structure, housing the same, such as hot
water heaters and gas furnaces, whether of the basement, floor or wall type. In
the installation of space heaters, clothes dryers and gas ranges which may be
regarded as personal property rather than something affixed to the real estate,
or a permanent part of the building housing the same, said rigid pipe shall
run, either to and be connected with said gas appliances, or at the option of
the owner, it may be run to within three feet or less of the gas appliances and
then connected to the gas appliances by Underwriter Laboratories approved flex
line. Said rigid pipe shall be used not only through the foundation of the
building but also through any floor before Underwriter Laboratories approved
flex line is connected thereto. A cutoff valve shall be installed in the rigid
pipe as nearly as practical to each gas appliance so that gas may be
disconnected from each gas appliance separately without disconnecting the gas
from the other appliances. All customer piping and necessary venting of
appliances shall be in accordance with guidelines set forth in the National
Fire Protection Association’s most recent edition of NFPA 54: national fuel gas
code, which is incorporated herein by reference.
(1987 Code, § 38-3-17) (Amd. 2008 Code)
      2.   If, during any inspection of a property owner’s premises by the
village employee, it is determined that the gas piping in said premises is in
violation of this subsection A. and the gas pipe conditions are determined to
be relatively and reasonably safe, then the property owner(s) of said premises
shall have 30 days, after notice given by the village, to repair or replace
said gas piping within or on said premises so as to be in compliance with the
provisions of this subsection. If it is determined or expected that the gas
piping on or within a property owner’s premises is not in compliance with this
subsection, and the condition of the existing gas piping is determined unsafe,
or the property owner fails to permit a village employee to enter the property
owner’s premises to make an inspection, then the gas service to said premises
shall be immediately shut off until such inspection is permitted by such
property owner(s) and the gas piping is repaired or replaced, as necessary to
come into compliance with this subsection A.
(1987 Code, § 38-3-17)
   B.   No plastic, polyethylene, copper or brass pipe may be connected above
the ground with the village natural gas transmission and distribution system.
No plastic or polyethylene pipe, and no copper or brass pipe or stainless trac
pipe (except as allowed in subsection A. above) may be used from the meter to
the structure and/or in the structure, house or building connected with the
above system.
(1987 Code, § 38-3-18) (Amd. 2008 Code)
(Ord. 810, passed 7-14-2014)
§ 8-2-5 METERS.
   A.   Separate Meters Required. No person shall connect gas pipes or conduct
gas into any two distinct premises or tenements unless separate and distinct
meters shall be placed and located for each such premises, nor shall any gas
pipe be allowed to cross into adjoining premises.
   B.   Inaccurate, Defective Meters. If a customer using the gas from the gas
supply believes the meter servicing his or her premises is inaccurate or
defective, the customer shall have two options:
      1.   The customer may purchase and pay for a new meter through the
village; or
      2.   The customer may request the village to remove and have the gas
meter checked for accuracy. If the meter is found, upon inspection, not to be
accurate within 3%, the village shall then repair or replace the meter and
adjust the customer’s gas bill accordingly for the three months preceding such
inspection. If, upon inspection, the meter is found to be within 3% of being
accurate, then the customer shall pay the village immediately for all costs of
the inspection, and no adjustment shall be made to the consumer’s gas bills.
(1987 Code, § 38-3-11)
§ 8-2-6 OWNERSHIP AND RESPONSIBILITY.
   All meters, pipes and other equipment which are installed by the village
shall be and remain the property of the village, and consumers using the same
shall be responsible for the safekeeping thereof. If any meter, pipe or other
equipment which is installed by the village shall be damaged or destroyed by
any cause which could have been prevented by exercising ordinary care and
caution, then, and in such case, the cost of repairing or replacing the same
shall be charged to and paid by the consumer responsible therefor.
(1987 Code, § 38-3-12)
§ 8-2-7 RATES AND CHARGES.
   A.   Rates Established And Required.
      1.   Effective on and after July 8, 2002, gas supplied through meters
shall be at the rates set by the mayor and the village board as now exist or as
may be amended or revised from time to time hereafter by motion adopted by the
affirmative majority vote of the mayor and village board of trustees. (1987
Code, § 38-3-6)
      2.   No customer of the gas utility shall receive any free service of any
kind whatsoever, and further, the village shall pay the regularly established
rates and charges as provided by this section for all gas consumed or used by
the village.
(1987 Code, § 38-3-16)
   B.   Estimated Charge. If a meter fails to register at any time the quantity
of gas, the quantity shall be estimated, and the charge to be made shall be
based on the average of that month for the previous three years.
(1987 Code, § 38-3-9) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   C.   Change Of Rates. The rates for gas services prescribed in this section
may be changed by the mayor, with the advice and consent of the board of
trustees, at any time, and the village shall not be deemed to have contracted
with any consumer to maintain such rates for any period of time whatever. The
rates for gas service shall further be subject to the increases imposed from
time to time by the village gas supplier.
(1987 Code, § 38-3-13) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   D.   Payment Of Bill; Late Payment Penalty.
      1.   Gas rates shall be paid to the village on or before the sixteenth
day of the month in which due; provided, if such rates are not paid on or
before such date, a penalty of 10% shall be added thereto.
      2.   If such sixteenth day falls on Saturday, Sunday or legal holiday,
bills shall not be considered delinquent until 12:00 a.m. the next working day.
      3.   Postmarks and dates written on checks shall not constitute proof of
payment to avoid penalty.
   E.   Returned Check Fee. If any check is returned to the village for
insufficient funds, a 10% penalty, plus an insufficient funds charge, shall be
assessed against such customer for gas service without the consent of the mayor
and the village board.
(1987 Code, § 38-3-6)
   F.   Delinquent Payments; Shut Off Of Service; Lien Provisions.
      1.   The charges for gas services rendered during each calendar month
shall be billed on the first working day of the succeeding calendar month and
payable to the village on the sixteenth day of such succeeding month. In the
event of the nonpayment of any gas bill and penalties thereon on or before the
thirtieth day of the month in which the same falls due, the gas service to such
delinquent gas customer’s premises may be shut off and discontinued by the
village at any time thereafter, without further notice to such gas customer,
subject, however, to the restrictions imposed by 65 ILCS 5/11-117-12.1.
(Amd. 2008 Code)
      2.   In the event any delinquent gas bill is turned over to the village
attorney for purposes of collection or foreclosure of lien, the debtor or
debtors shall jointly and severally pay all reasonable attorney fees and any
court costs incurred by the village.
(1987 Code, § 38-3-6)
(Ord. 702, passed 10-11-2004)
Cross-reference:
   Bad Checks Policy, see §
8-1-3 E.
§ 8-2-8 INTERRUPTION OF SERVICE.
   Notwithstanding anything in this title to the contrary and in addition to
any other provision(s) in this title, the village does not guarantee that gas
service will never be interrupted or curtailed and, accordingly:
   A.   From time to time, it may be necessary or desirable that natural gas
service be temporarily interrupted for maintenance, construction, repair,
emergencies or other purposes or due to unavailability or shortage of gas
supplies to meet the demands of the village gas customers. During such periods,
the village assumes no responsibility for resulting loss of heat, products,
materials, plants, animals or other such things that might be affected by the
fact that gas service is interrupted or curtailed for a period of time.
   B.   Any village gas customer having a facility, operation, process or
activity that cannot tolerate temporary interruption or curtailment of gas
service shall provide an alternate source of on site fuel or heat for
utilization during such interruption or curtailment. The responsibility for
this alternate rests fully with such gas customer(s).
(1987 Code, § 38-3-15A)
§ 8-2-9 RIGHT OF ACCESS TO SERVICE LINES; OBSTRUCTION PROHIBITED.
   No person shall in any manner obstruct or permit or cause to be obstructed
the free access of any officer or employee of the village to any valve, meter,
shutoff box or connection with any gas main or service pipe by means of coal,
lumber, brick, building material or by any other means, shift or device
whatever, or refuse or prevent free access thereto by such officer or employee
that shall desire access thereto in the performance of his or her duties. The
officers of the village and any and every person delegated or authorized by the
village for such purpose shall have free access to all and any part of any
building, structure or premises to which gas is supplied by the village for the
purpose of examining the taps, pipes, fixtures and gas operated appliances or
gas operated apparatus of any kind therein. Any person in possession of
property unto which any such officer or employee shall desire to make entry or
to have access for the purposes herein specified, who shall refuse to permit
such entry or access, or who shall do or cause to be done any act or thing for
the purpose of preventing such entry or access, shall be punished as herein
provided.
(1987 Code, § 38-3-5) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 8-2-10 MAINTENANCE AND REPAIR OF SYSTEM; SHUTOFF AUTHORIZED.
   Each gas customer of the village shall, at his or her own expense, maintain,
at all times, in good order and repair, any and all gas furnaces, gas stoves,
gas ovens, gas water heaters, gas washers, gas dryers, gas operated toilets,
gas operated fireplaces and any and all other gas appliances or gas operated or
fired fixture or apparatus of any kind, nature or extent whatsoever located in,
upon or under the gas customer’s property to which the village gas supply or
gas distribution system is connected. The general superintendent or the village
delegate, subject to the direction of the village board, shall have the right
to shut off the gas supply to any property in which it is determined in the
sole discretion of the village that any such gas operated appliance, fixture or
apparatus is not in good order and repair or is otherwise defective and which
may or could cause injury to any person or property or be a risk to any person
or property.
(1987 Code, § 38-3-21)
§ 8-2-11 TAMPERING WITH SYSTEM PROHIBITED.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to move, replace, tamper with or
interfere in any manner with any gas main, pipe, meter or other part of the
natural gas transmission line and distribution system without a permit therefor
as provided in this chapter.
(1987 Code, § 38-3-4)
§ 8-2-12 RELOCATION OF METERS, MAINS AND PIPING.
   A.   In the event the village corporate authorities, by majority vote,
determine it is necessary to hereafter move the location of any gas meter,
main, pipe or other part of the village natural gas transmission line and
distribution system by reason of such gas main, pipe or other part of said
village gas system being located, in whole or in part, under any building,
driveway, structure, patio or other improvement of any kind, nature or extent
whatsoever, or by reason of violation of any other village ordinances, or in
the event the owner(s) of the premises where such gas meter, main, pipe or
other part of the village natural gas transmission line and distribution system
is located requests the relocation of any such gas meter, main, pipe or other
part of such system for his or her convenience, then, in either event, the
owner(s) of the premises where such gas meter, main, pipe or other part of said
system is located shall immediately pay the village for the actual cost for the
gas meter, if another one is installed and for all materials, labor and
machinery costs to relocate such gas meter, main, pipe or other part of said
system, and the village shall actually perform the work to relocate such gas
meter, main, pipe or other part of said system.
   B.   If owner(s) desire(s) to relocate such gas meter, main, pipe or other
part of said system for convenience purposes, then such owner(s) must first
submit a signed application to the village requesting such relocation, which
application shall be on a form approved by the village board of trustees from
time to time. The president and board of trustees, by majority vote, shall
determine whether to grant such request.
(1987 Code, § 38-3-20)
§ 8-2-13 CONSTRUCTION OVER MAINS.
   No person shall hereafter construct, erect or install any building,
structure, patio or other improvement of any kind, nature or extent whatsoever
which would become situated over any gas main, pipe or other part of the
village natural gas transmission line and distribution system without having
first applied for, in writing, and obtained a construction permit from the
village. The village president and board of trustees, by majority vote, may
impose any requirements in conjunction with the issuance of such construction
permit as they deem necessary to protect the village natural gas transmission
line and distribution system. The corporate authorities, by majority vote, can
determine at any time and from time to time that it is necessary in their
opinion that any gas meter, main, pipe or other part of the village natural gas
transmission line and distribution system must be relocated, and such
determination shall be binding and conclusive upon the owner(s) of the premises
where such gas meter, main, pipe or other part of said system is located.
(1987 Code, § 38-3-19)
Cross-reference:
   Restrictions On Permits, see §
10-2-2 E.2.
§ 8-2-14 AVAILABILITY OF GAS.
   If, at any time and for any reason, the volume of gas available for delivery
by the village is not adequate to supply all the demands of the village, the
village shall have the right, upon such notice as the village deems reasonable
under the circumstances, to reduce or discontinue the delivery of gas, first to
its medium commercial users, next to its small commercial users, and finally to
its residential users, and such reduction or discontinuance, as the case may
be, shall continue as long as such shortage or supply exists. Notice of
reduction or discontinuance of delivery shall be given to users on a reasonable
nondiscriminatory basis. Whenever notice is given by the village to any user to
reduce or discontinue his or her use of gas, such consumer shall comply
therewith until such consumer is notified by the village that such shortage no
longer exists. If any user fails to reduce his or her use of gas in compliance
with any notice for him or her to do so, the village may completely discontinue
supplying gas to him or her while such shortage continues. For the purposes of
this section but not as a limitation thereof, the village supply of gas shall
be deemed to be inadequate if, and to the extent that, the total demands on it
exceed the village contract demand under its contract with its supplier.
(1987 Code, § 38-3-15)
§ 8-2-15 PENALTY.
   Any person who shall violate any of the provisions of this chapter shall,
upon conviction, be fined as provided in §
1-4-1 for each offense, and each day that a violation of this chapter occurs
shall constitute a separate offense. The cutting off of the supply of gas to
any premises, or the forfeiture of gas rates paid, or the imposition of any
liability or expense herein otherwise provided for on account of any violation
of any of the provisions of this chapter shall not be held to exempt any such
person from the penalty provided by this section.
(1987 Code, § 38-3-22) (Amd. 2008 Code)

§ 8-3-1 ADMINISTRATION OF SYSTEM.
   A.   Sewer Department Established; Composition. There shall be an executive
department of the village known as the sewer department. The sewer department
shall include the committee on sewer, appointed by the mayor, and its
employees. The designated office shall be the village hall.
(1987 Code, § 38-1-10)
   B.   Committee On Sewer. The committee on sewer shall exercise a general
supervision over the affairs of the sanitary sewerage system. They shall
ascertain the condition and needs thereof; shall, from time to time, report the
same to the mayor and village board so that a full understanding thereof shall
be had; and generally, shall do all acts necessary to promote the efficiency of
the sewer department. (1987 Code, § 38-1-11)
   C.   Duties Of General Superintendent. The duties of the general
superintendent of the village shall be as follows:
      1.   He or she shall, subject to the direction of the village board, have
the general direction and control of the sewer department, and shall see that
the object and purpose thereof are carried out, and that the sewerage system is
conducted in an economical, businesslike basis;
      2.   He or she shall inspect all connections to the sewerage system at
the time they are made and report to the treasurer the date when the sewerage
connection is completed;
      3.   He or she shall determine the size of each connection to be made;
and
      4.   He or she shall make all repairs occasioned by leaks or breaks in
the mains of the sewerage system that the village is responsible for
maintaining.
(1987 Code, § 38-1-13)
   D.   Duties Of Village Treasurer. The village treasurer shall issue permits
for connections to the sanitary systems whenever an application for the same
has been made as provided herein.
(1987 Code, § 38-1-13) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 8-3-2 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions apply unless the
context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   A.   Federal Government.
      ADMINISTRATOR. The administrator of the United States environmental
protection agency.
      FEDERAL ACT. The Federal Clean Water Act, being 33 U.S.C. §§ 1251 et
seq., as amended.
(Pub. Law No. 95-217)
      FEDERAL GRANT. The United States government participation in the
financing of the construction of treatment works as provided by title II,
grants for construction of treatment works of the act and implementing
regulations.
   B.   Local Government.
      APPROVING AUTHORITY. The wastewater treatment plant operator of the
village.
      NATURAL OUTLET. Any outlet into a watercourse, pond, ditch, lake or other
body of surface or ground water.
      NPDES PERMIT. Any permit or equivalent document or requirements issued by
the administrator or, where appropriated by the director, after enactment of
the Federal Clean Water Act, § 402, being 33 U.S.C. § 1342, to regulate the
discharge of pollutants pursuant to the federal act § 402.
      PERSON. Any and all persons, natural or artificial, including any
individual, firm, company, municipal or private corporation, association,
society, institution, enterprise, governmental agency or other entity.
      VILLAGE. The village of Edinburg.
   C.   Sewer Types And Appurtenances.
      BUILDING DRAIN. The part of the lowest piping of a drainage system which
receives the discharge from soil, waste and other drainage pipes inside the
walls of the building and conveys it to the building sewer or other approved
point of discharge, beginning five feet outside the inner face of the building
wall.
      BUILDING SEWER. The extension from the building drain to the public sewer
or other place of disposal.
      COMBINED SEWER. A sewer which is designed and intended to receive
wastewater and storm, surface and ground water drainage.
      EASEMENT. An acquired legal right for the specific use of land owned by
others.
      MAY. The term herein is permissible.
      PUBLIC SEWER. A sewer provided by or subject to the jurisdiction of the
village. The term shall also include sewers within or outside the village
boundaries that serve one or more persons and ultimately discharge into the
village sanitary (or combined sewer) system, even though those sewers may not
have been constructed with village funds.
      SANITARY SEWER. A sewer that conveys sewage or industrial wastes, or a
combination of both, and into which storm, surface and ground waters or
unpolluted industrial process waters are not intentionally admitted.
      SEWER. A pipe or conduit for conveying sewage or any other waste liquids,
including storm, surface and ground water drainage.
      SEWERAGE. The system of sewers and appurtenances for the collection,
transportation and pumping of sewage.
      STORM SEWER. A sewer that carries storm, surface and ground water
drainage but excludes sewage and industrial wastes other than unpolluted
cooling water.
      STORMWATER RUNOFF. The portion of the precipitation that is drained into
the sewers.
      SHALL. The term herein is mandatory.
   D.   State Government.
      DIRECTOR. The director of the state environmental protection agency.
      STATE ACT. The state anti-pollution bond act of 1970.
      STATE GRANT. The state participation in the financing of the construction
of treatment works as provided for by the Build Illinois Bond Act, being 30
ILCS 425/1 et seq., and for making such grants as filed with the state
secretary of state.
   E.   Treatment.
      PRETREATMENT. The treatment of wastewaters from sources before
introduction into the wastewater treatment works.
      WASTEWATER TREATMENT WORKS. An arrangement of devices and structures for
treating wastewater, industrial wastes and sludge; sometimes used as synonymous
with WASTE TREATMENT PLANT or POLLUTION CONTROL PLANT.
   F.   Types Of Charges.
      BASIC USER CHARGE. The basic assessment levied on all users of the public
sewer system.
      CAPITAL IMPROVEMENT CHARGE. A charge levied on users to improve, extend
or reconstruct the sewage treatment works.
      DEBT SERVICE COST. The amount to be paid each billing period for payment
of interest, principal and coverage of (loan, bond and the like) outstanding.
      LOCAL CAPITAL COST CHARGE. Charges for costs other than the operation,
maintenance and replacement costs, i.e., debt service and capital improvement
costs.
      REPLACEMENT. Expenditures for obtaining and installing equipment,
accessories or appurtenances which are necessary during the useful life of the
treatment works to maintain the capacity and performance for which such works
were designed and constructed. The term OPERATION AND MAINTENANCE includes
REPLACEMENT.
      SEWERAGE FUND. The principal accounting designation for all revenues
received in the operation of the sewer system.
      SURCHARGE. The assessment, in addition to the basic user charge and debt
service charge, which is levied on those persons whose wastes are greater in
strength than the concentration values established in article A of this
chapter.
      USEFUL LIFE. The estimated period during which the collection system and/
or treatment works will be operated.
      USER CHARGE. A charge levied on users of treatment works for the cost of
operation, maintenance and replacement.
      WASTEWATER SERVICE CHARGE. The charge per month levied on all users of
the wastewater facilities. The SERVICE CHARGE shall be computed as outlined in
article A of this chapter and shall consist of the total of the basic user
charge and a surcharge, if applicable.
   G.   User Types.
      COMMERCIAL USER. Shall include transit lodging, retail and wholesale
establishments or places engaged in selling merchandise or rendering services.
      CONTROL MANHOLE. A structure located on a site from which industrial
wastes are discharged. Where feasible, the manhole shall have an interior drop.
The purpose of a CONTROL MANHOLE is to provide access for the village
representative to sample and/or measure discharges.
      INDUSTRIAL USERS. Shall include establishments engaged in the
manufacturing activities involving the mechanical or chemical transformation of
materials of substance into products.
      INSTITUTIONAL/GOVERNMENTAL USER. Shall include schools, churches, penal
institutions and users associated with federal, state and local governments.
      RESIDENTIAL USER. All dwelling units such as houses, mobile homes,
apartments and permanent multi-family dwellings.
      USER CLASS. The type of user, “residential,” “institutional/
governmental,” “commercial,” or “industrial,” as defined in this section.
   H.   Wastewater And Its Characteristics.
      BOD (DENOTING BIOCHEMICAL OXYGEN DEMAND). The quantity of oxygen utilized
in the biochemical oxidation of organic matter under standard laboratory
procedure in five days at 20°C, expressed in milligrams per liter.
      EFFLUENT CRITERIA. Are defined in any applicable NPDES permit.
      FLOATABLE OIL. An oil, fat or grease in a physical state such that it
will separate by gravity from wastewater by treatment in an approved
pretreatment facility. A wastewater shall be considered free of FLOATABLE OIL
if it is properly pretreated and the wastewater does not interfere with the
collection system.
      GARBAGE. Solid wastes from the domestic and commercial preparation,
cooking and dispensing of food, and from the handling, storage and sale of
food.
      INDUSTRIAL WASTE. Any solid, liquid or gaseous substance discharged,
permitted to flow or escaping from any industrial, manufacturing, commercial or
business establishment or process or from the development, recovery or
processing of any natural resource as distinct from sanitary sewage.
      MAJOR CONTRIBUTING INDUSTRY. An industrial user of the publicly owned
treatment works that:
         a.   Has a flow of 50,000 gallons or more per average workday;
         b.   Has a flow greater than 10% of the flow carried by the municipal
system receiving the waste;
         c.   Has in its waste, a toxic pollutant in toxic amounts as defined
in standards issued under the federal act § 307(a); or
         d.   Is found by the permit issuing authority, in connection with the
issuance of the NPDES permit to the publicly owned treatment works receiving
the waste, to have significant impact, either singly or in combination with
other contributing industries, on that treatment works or upon the quality of
effluent from that treatment works.
      MILLIGRAMS PER LITER. A unit of the concentration of water or wastewater
constituent. It is 0.001 g of the constituent in 1,000 ml of water. It has
replaced the unit formerly used commonly, parts per million, to which it is
approximately equivalent, in reporting the results of water and wastewater
analysis.
      PPM. Parts per million by weight.
      pH. The logarithm (base 10) of the reciprocal of the hydrogen ion
concentration expressed by one of the procedures outlined in the IEPA division
of laboratories’ “Manual of Laboratory Methods.”
      POPULATION EQUIVALENT. A term used to evaluate the impact of industrial
or other waste on a treatment works or stream. One POPULATION EQUIVALENT is 200
gallons of sewage per day, containing 0.122 pounds of BOD and 0.211 pounds of
suspended solids.
      PROPERLY SHREDDED GARBAGE. The wastes from the preparation, cooking and
dispensing of food that have been shredded to such a degree that all particles
will be carried freely under the flow conditions normally prevailing in public
sewers, with no particle greater than one-half inch in any dimension.
      SEWAGE. Is used interchangeably with WASTEWATER.
      SLUG. Any discharge of water, sewage or industrial waste which, in
concentration of any given constituent or in quantity of flow, exceeds, for any
period of duration longer than 15 minutes, more than five times the average 24-
hour concentration of flows during normal operation.
      SUSPENDED SOLIDS (SS). Solids that either float on the surface of, or are
in suspension in water, sewage or industrial waste, and which are removable by
a laboratory filtration device. Quantitative determination of SUSPENDED SOLIDS
shall be made in accordance with procedures set forth in the IEPA division of
laboratories’ “Manual of Laboratory Methods.”
      UNPOLLUTED WATER. Water quality equal to or better than the effluent
criteria in effect, or water that would not cause violation of receiving water
quality standards and would not be benefitted by discharge to the sanitary
sewers and wastewater treatment facilities provided.
      WASTEWATER. The spent water of a community. From this standpoint of
course, it may be a combination of the liquid and water carried wastes from
residences, commercial buildings, industrial plants and institutions, together
with any ground water, surface water and stormwater that may be present.
      WASTEWATER FACILITIES. The structures, equipment and processes required
to collect, carry away and treat domestic and industrial wastes and transport
effluent to a watercourse.
      WATERCOURSE. A channel in which a flow of water occurs, either
continuously or intermittently.
      WATER QUALITY STANDARDS. Are defined in the water pollution regulations
of the state.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-1) (Amd. 2008 Code)
Statutory reference:
   Similar provisions, see 30 ILCS 405/1 et seq.
§ 8-3-3 USE OF PUBLIC SEWERS REQUIRED.
   A.   Discharge Of Human And Animal Wastes. It shall be unlawful for any
person to place, deposit or permit to be deposited in any unsanitary manner, on
public or private property within the village or in any area under the
jurisdiction of the village, any human or animal excrement, garbage or other
objectionable waste.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-5)
   B.   Discharges Into Natural Outlets. It shall be unlawful to discharge to
any natural outlet within the village, or in any area under the jurisdiction of
the village, any sewage or other polluted waters, except where suitable
treatment has been provided in accordance with this chapter.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-6)
   C.   Private Systems. Except as hereinafter provided, it shall be unlawful
to construct or maintain any privy, privy vault, septic tank, cesspool or other
facility intended or used for the disposal of sewage. (1987 Code, § 38-5-7)
   D.   Connection To Sewer System Required. The owner of each house, building
or property used for human occupancy, employment, recreation or other purposes
situated within the village and abutting on any street, alley or right-of-way
in which there is now located or may in the future be located any public
sanitary (or combined) sewer of the village, is hereby required at his or her
expense to install suitable toilet facilities therein, and to connect such
facilities directly with the proper public sewer, in accordance with the
provisions of this chapter, within 90 days after date of official notice to do
so; provided, that said public sewer is within 1,000 feet of the property line.
(Amd. 2008 Code) (Ord. 698, passed 6-14-2004)
§ 8-3-4 PRIVATE SEWAGE DISPOSAL SYSTEMS.
   A.   Private Systems Permitted. Where a public sanitary sewer is not
available under the provisions of §
8-3-3 D, the building sewer shall be connected to a private sewage disposal
system complying with the provisions of this section.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-10)
   B.   Permit Requirements.
      1.   Permit Required. Before commencement of construction of a private
sewage disposal system, the owner shall first obtain a written permit signed by
the approving authority.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-11)
      2.   Application For Permit; Fee. The application for such permit shall
be made on a form furnished by the village, which the applicant shall
supplement by any plans, specifications and other information as deemed
necessary by the approving authority. A permit and inspection fee of $400 shall
be paid to the village at the time the application is filed.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-11) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   C.   Construction Specifications; Lot Area. The type, capacities, location
and layout of a private sewage disposal system shall comply with all
recommendations of the Private Sewage Disposal Licensing Act, being 225 ILCS
225/1 et seq., and with the state environmental protection agency. No permit
shall be issued for any private sewage disposal system employing subsurface
soil absorption facilities where the area of the lot is less than 30,000 square
feet.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-13)
   D.   Inspection Of Construction. A permit for a private sewage disposal
system shall not become effective until the installation is completed to the
satisfaction of the approving authority. He or she shall be allowed to inspect
the work at any stage of construction, and in any event, the applicant for the
permit shall notify the approving authority when the work is ready for final
inspection and before any underground portions are covered. The inspection
shall be made within 72 hours of the receipt of written notice by the approving
authority.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-12)
   E.   Operation And Maintenance. The owner shall operate and maintain the
private sewage disposal facilities in a sanitary manner at all times and at no
expense to the village.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-15)
   F.   Availability Of Public Sewers; Connection Required.
      1.   At such time as a public sewer becomes available to a property
serviced by a private sewage disposal system, as provided in §
8-3-3D of this chapter, a direct connection shall be made to the public sewer
in compliance with this chapter, and any septic tanks, cesspools and similar
private sewage disposal facilities shall be abandoned and filled with suitable
materials.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-14)
      2.   When a public sewer becomes available, the building sewer shall be
connected to said sewer within 90 days, and the private sewage disposal system
shall be cleaned of sludge and filled with clean, bank run gravel or dirt.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-17) (Amd. 2008 Code)
Statutory reference:
   Similar provisions, see 225 ILCS 225/1 et seq.
§ 8-3-5 BUILDING SEWERS AND CONNECTIONS.
   A.   Permit To Connect.
      1.   Permit Required. No unauthorized person shall uncover, make any
connections with, or opening into, use, alter or disturb any public sewer or
appurtenance thereto without first obtaining a written permit from the
approving authority.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-20)
      2.   Classes Of Permits; Applications. There shall be two classes of
building sewer permits for residential wastewater service; and for commercial,
institutional/governmental or industrial wastewater service. In either case,
the owner, and the tenant in the case of residential rental property, shall
make application on a special form furnished by the village. The permit
application shall be supplemented by any plans, specifications or other
information considered pertinent in the judgment of the approving authority.
      3.   Permit And Inspection And Connection Fee. A permit and inspection
and connection fee of $250 for a residential or commercial, institutional/
governmental or industrial building sewer permit shall be paid to the village
at the time the application is filed. The industry, as a condition of permit
authorization, must provide information describing its wastewater constituents,
characteristics and type of activity.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-22) (Amd. 2008 Code)
      4.   Permit Subject To System Capacity. A building sewer permit will only
be issued and a sewer connection shall only be allowed if it can be
demonstrated that the downstream sewerage facilities, including sewers, pump
stations and wastewater treatment facilities, have sufficient reserve capacity
to adequately and efficiently handle the additional anticipated waste load.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-24)
   B.   Deposit For Rental Property.
      1.   In the case of rental property, a total deposit of $100 shall be
paid. This sum shall be retained by the village to ensure payment of any
delinquent bills. When sewerage service to the applicant is discontinued, this
deposit, minus any amount remaining due the village for sewer service, shall be
refunded to the applicant without interest. No such deposit shall be required
of any applicant who is the owner of the premises to be served by the village
water supply. For purposes hereof, a contract for deed purchaser shall be
deemed a renter and not an owner of the premises to be served by the sewer
service.
      2.   The village reserves the right, at any time, to increase the amount
of the required deposit in the case of a renter and to impose a deposit in the
case of an owner in any amount the village deems appropriate as a condition for
initial sewer service or for further sewer service where such renter or owner
is delinquent in his utility bill(s) or has previously been delinquent in
payment of his or her utility bill(s) to the village.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-23)
   C.   Separate Building Sewers. A separate and independent building sewer
shall be provided for every building; except, that where one building stands at
the rear of another on an interior lot and no private sewer is available or can
be constructed to the rear building through an adjoining alley, court, yard or
driveway, the building sewer from the front building may be extended to the
rear building and the whole considered as one building sewer.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-26)
   D.   Old Building Sewers. Old building sewers may be used in connection with
new buildings only when they are found, upon examination and test by the
approving authority, to meet all requirements of this chapter.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-27)
   E.   Installation Of Sewer Line; Indemnification. Regarding the installation
of the sewer line to any premises to be serviced (except as may be otherwise
provided by annexation ordinance 594), the village shall make all installations
between the village sewer main and five feet beyond the property line;
provided, however, the village may, at the owner’s request, install such sewer
line from the owner’s property line to within ten feet of the premises to be
serviced; and provided further, in any event, the owner(s) of such premises
shall pay for all materials and shall further pay the village for such
installation at the then prevailing hourly rate as set forth in §
2-1-3 . Such owner(s) shall indemnify and hold harmless the village from any
and all losses or damages which may be occasioned, directly or indirectly, by
the installation and/or connection of such sewer line.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-25) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   F.   Design; Construction Specifications.
      1.   The size, slope, alignment, materials of construction of a building
sewer and the methods to be used in excavating, placing of the pipe, jointing,
testing and backfilling the trench, shall all conform to the requirements of
the Illinois Plumbing Code, being found in 77 Ill. Admin Code 890, or other
applicable rules and regulations of the village.
      2.   In the absence of code provisions or in amplification thereof, the
materials and procedures set forth in appropriate specifications of the
American Society of Testing Materials, Water Pollution Control Federation
Manual of Practice No. 9, and standard specifications for water and sewer main
construction in the state, shall apply.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-28)
      3.   Whenever possible, the building sewer shall be brought to the
building at an elevation three feet below the ground level at the building. In
all buildings in which any building drain is too low to permit gravity flow to
the public sewer, sanitary sewage carried by such building drain shall be
lifted by a means which is approved in accordance with subsection H.1. below
and discharged to the building sewer.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-29) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   G.   Connection To Public Sewer. The connection of the building sewer to the
public sewer shall conform to the requirements of the building and plumbing
code, or other applicable rules and regulations of the village, or the
procedures set forth in the applicable rules and regulations and the
specifications of the state plumbing code. All such connections shall be made
gastight and watertight. Any deviation from the prescribed procedures and
materials must be approved by the approving authority before installation.
   H.   Discharges Into Sewer.
      1.   All disposal into the sewer system by any person is unlawful except
those discharges in compliance with federal standards promulgated pursuant to
the federal act and more stringent state and local standards.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-21)
      2.   No person(s) shall make connection of roof downspouts, exterior
foundation drains, areaway drains or other sources of surface runoff or ground
water to a building sewer or building drain which in turn is connected directly
or indirectly to a public sanitary sewer.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-30)
   I.   Inspections. The applicant for the building sewer permit shall notify
the approving authority when the building sewer is ready for inspection and
connection to the public sewer. The connection shall be made under the
supervision of the approving authority or his or her representative.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-32)
(Ord. 698, passed 6-14-2004)
§ 8-3-6 STORM SEWERS.
   A.   Description. Stormwater sewers shall be any pipe or sewer used for the
carrying of surface drains, ground waters, or roof leaders, or stormwaters,
rainwaters, or other waters other than sanitary sewage.
   B.   Supervision. The general superintendent shall supervise all connections
made to the public storm sewer system or excavations for the purpose of
installing or repairing the same.
   C.   Permit To Connect. Before any connection is made to the public
stormwater sewers, a permit shall be applied for and approved by the
superintendent or his or her designated representative.
   D.   Use Requirements. Where a stormwater sewer is accessible in a street,
alley or easement to a building or premises abutting thereon, the surface
drains, ground waters, roof leaders or stormwaters shall be charged into the
stormwater sewer unless otherwise authorized by the village. Under no
conditions shall sanitary sewage or wastes, or any substance other than surface
waters, ground waters, roof waters or stormwaters, be permitted to flow into or
be connected to the stormwater sewer; and no sanitary sewer shall be connect to
the stormwater sewer system.
(1987 Code, § 33-5-2)
§ 8-3-7 DISCHARGES INTO SYSTEM.
   A.   Storm And Surface Water Runoff; Storm And Combined Sewers.
      1.   No person shall discharge or cause to be discharged any stormwater,
surface water, ground water, roof runoff, subsurface drainage, uncontaminated
cooling water, or unpolluted industrial process waters to any sanitary sewer.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-38)
      2.   Stormwater and all other unpolluted drainage shall be discharged to
such sewers as are specifically designated as storm sewers, or to a natural
outlet approved by the approving authority. Industrial cooling water or
unpolluted process waters may be discharged on approval of the approving
authority to a storm sewer or natural outlet.
   B.   Prohibited Discharges. No person shall discharge or cause to be
discharged any of the following described waters or wastes to any public
sewers:
      1.   Any gasoline, benzene, naphtha, fuel oil or other flammable or
explosive liquid, solid or gas;
      2.   Any waters or wastes containing toxic or poisonous solids, liquids
or gases in sufficient quantity, either singly or by interaction with other
wastes, to injure or interfere with any sewage treatment process, constitute a
hazard to humans or animals, create a public nuisance, or create any hazard in
the receiving waters of the sewage treatment plant;
      3.   Any waters or wastes having a pH lower than 5.5 or having any other
corrosive property capable of causing damage or hazard to structures, equipment
and personnel of the sewage works; and
      4.   Solid or viscous substances in quantities or of such size capable of
causing obstruction to the flow in sewers, or other interference with the
proper operation of the sewage works such as, but not limited to, ashes,
cinders, sand, mud, straw, shavings, metal, glass, rags, feathers, tar,
plastics, wood, unground garbage, whole blood, paunch manure, hair and
fleshings, entrails and paper dishes, cups, milk containers and the like,
either whole or ground by garbage grinders.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-40)
   C.   Restricted Discharges.
      1.   No person shall discharge or cause to be discharged the following
described substances, materials, waters or wastes if it appears likely, in the
opinion of the approving authority, that such wastes can harm either the sewage
treatment process or equipment of the sewers; have an adverse effect on the
receiving stream; or can otherwise endanger life, limb or public property, or
constitute a nuisance. In forming his or her opinion as to the acceptability of
these wastes, the approving authority will give consideration to such factors
as the quantities of subject wastes in relation to flows and velocities in the
sewers, materials of construction of the sewers, nature of the sewage treatment
process, capacity of the sewage treatment plant and maximum limits established
by regulatory agencies. The substances prohibited are:
         a.   Any liquid or vapor having a temperature higher than 150°F
(65°C);
         b.   Any waters or wastes containing toxic or poisonous materials; or
oils, whether emulsified or not, in excess of 100 mg/l or containing substances
which may solidify or become viscous at temperatures between 32°F and 150°F (0°
and 65°C);
         c.   Any garbage that has not been properly shredded. The installation
and operation of any garbage grinder equipped with a motor of three-fourths
horsepower (0.76 hp metric) or greater shall be subject to the review and
approval of the approving authority;
         d.   Any waters or wastes containing strong acid, iron pickling wastes
or concentrated plating solution, whether neutralized or not;
         e.   Any waters or wastes containing iron, chromium, copper, zinc or
similar objectionable or toxic substances, or wastes exerting an excessive
chlorine requirement, to such degree that any such material received in the
composite sewage at the sewage treatment works exceeds the limits established
by the approving authority for such materials;
         f.   Any waters or wastes containing phenols or other taste or odor
producing substances, in such concentrations exceeding limits which may be
established by the approving authority as necessary after treatment of the
composite sewage to meet the requirements of the state, federal or other public
agencies of jurisdiction for such discharge to the receiving waters;
         g.   Any radioactive wastes or isotopes of such half-life or
concentration as may exceed limits established by the approving authority in
compliance with applicable state or federal regulations;
         h.   Any wastes or waters having a pH in excess of 9.5;
         i.   Any mercury or any of its compounds in excess of 0.0005 mg/l as
Hg at any time, except as permitted by the approving authority in compliance
with applicable state and federal regulations;
         j.   Any cyanide in excess of 0.025 mg/l at any time, except as
permitted by the approving authority in compliance with applicable state and
federal regulations;
         k.   Materials which exert or cause:
            (1)   Unusual concentrations of inert suspended solids (such as,
but not limited to, fuller’s earth, lime slurries and lime residues) or of
dissolved solids (such as, but not limited to, sodium chloride and sodium
sulfate);
            (2)   Excessive discoloration (such as, but not limited to, dye
wastes and vegetable tanning solutions);
            (3)   Unusual BOD, chemical oxygen demand or chlorine requirements
in such quantities as to constitute a significant load on the sewage treatment
works; and
            (4)   Unusual volume of flow or concentrations of wastes
constituting SLUGS as defined in § 8-3-1.
         l.   Waters or wastes containing substances which are not amenable to
treatment or reduction by the sewage treatment processes employed, or are
amenable to treatment only to such degree that the sewage treatment plant
effluent cannot meet the requirements of agencies having jurisdiction over
discharge to the receiving waters.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-41)
      2.   a.   If any waters or wastes are discharged or are proposed to be
discharged to the public sewers, which waters contain the substances or possess
the characteristics enumerated in subsection C.1. above, and/or which are in
violation of the standards for pretreatment provided in 40 C.F.R. part 403,
June 26, 1978, and any amendments thereto, and which, in the judgment of the
approving authority, may have a deleterious effect upon the sewage works,
processes, equipment or receiving waters, or which otherwise create a hazard to
life or constitute a public nuisance, the approving authority may:
            (1)   Reject the wastes;
            (2)   Require pretreatment to an acceptable condition for
discharge;
            (3)   Require control over the quantities and rates of discharge;
and/or
            (4)   Require payment to cover the added costs of handling and
treating the wastes not covered by existing taxes or sewer charges under the
provisions of §
8-3-12.
         b.   If the approving authority permits the pretreatment or
equalization of waste flows, the design and installation of the plants and
equipment shall be subject to the review and approval of the approving
authority, and subject to the requirements of all applicable codes, ordinances
and laws.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-42)
(Ord. 698, passed 6-14-2004)
§ 8-3-8 INTERCEPTORS.
   Grease, oil, and sand interceptors shall be provided when, in the opinion of
the approving authority, they are necessary for the proper handling of liquid
wastes containing grease in excessive amounts, or any flammable wastes, sand or
other harmful ingredients; except, that such interceptors shall not be required
for private living quarters or dwelling units. All interceptors shall be of a
type and capacity approved by the approving authority, and shall be located so
as to be readily and easily accessible for cleaning and inspection.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-43)
§ 8-3-9 PRETREATMENT OR FLOW EQUALIZATION.
   Where preliminary treatment or flow equalizing facilities are provided, they
shall be maintained continuously in satisfactory and effective operation by the
owner at his or her expense.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-44)
§ 8-3-10 CONTROL MANHOLE.
   Each industry shall be required to install a control manhole, and when
required by the approving authority, the owner of any property serviced by a
building sewer carrying industrial wastes shall install a suitable control
manhole, together with such necessary meters and other appurtenances in the
building sewer to facilitate observation, sampling and measurement of the
wastes. Such manhole, when required, shall be accessibly and safely located,
and shall be constructed in accordance with plans approved by the approving
authority. The manhole shall be installed by the owner at his or her expense,
and shall be maintained by him or her so as to be safe and accessible at all
times.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-45)
§ 8-3-11 MEASUREMENTS, TESTS AND ANALYSES.
   A.   The owner of any property serviced by a building sewer carrying
industrial wastes shall provide laboratory measurements, tests and analyses of
waters and wastes to illustrate compliance with this chapter and any special
conditions for discharge established by the village or regulatory agencies
having jurisdiction over the discharge.
   B.   The number, type and frequency of laboratory analyses to be performed
by the owner shall be as stipulated by the village; but no less than once per
year, the industry must supply a complete analysis of the constituents of the
wastewater discharge to assure that compliance with the federal, state and
local standards are being met. The owner shall report the results of
measurements and laboratory analyses to the village at such times and in such a
manner as prescribed by the village. The owner shall bear the expense of all
measurements, analyses and reporting required by the village. At such times as
deemed necessary, the village reserves the right to take measurements and
samples for analysis by an outside laboratory service.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-46)
   C.   All measurements, tests and analyses of the characteristics of waters
and wastes to which reference is made in this chapter shall be determined in
accordance with the latest edition of IEPA division of laboratories’ “Manual of
Laboratory Methods,” and shall be determined at the control manhole provided,
or upon suitable samples taken at said control manhole. In the event that no
special manhole has been required, the control manhole shall be considered to
be the nearest downstream manhole in the public sewer to the point at which the
building sewer is connected. Sampling shall be carried out by customarily
accepted methods to reflect the effect of constituents upon the sewage works
and to determine the existence of hazards to life, limb and property. The
particular analysis involved will determine whether a 24-hour composite of all
outfalls of a premises is appropriate or whether a grab sample or samples
should be taken. Normally, but not always, BOD and suspended solids analyses
are obtained from 24-hour composites of all outfalls, whereas pHs are
determined from periodic grab samples.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-47)
§ 8-3-12 SPECIAL AGREEMENTS.
   No statement contained in this chapter shall be construed as preventing any
special agreement or arrangement between the village and any industrial
concern, whereby an industrial waste of unusual strength or character may be
accepted by the village for treatment, subject to payment therefor, in
accordance with this chapter, by the industrial concern, provided such payments
are in accordance with federal and state guidelines for user charge system.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-48)
§ 8-3-13 BLOCKAGE, OBSTRUCTIONS AND OTHER PROBLEMS.
   A.   Televising Sewers.
      1.   Request Required. Any person(s) who desire(s) the village to arrange
for the underground televising of any sanitary sewer or combined sewer or
building sewer to determine the source of any problem or obstruction or
blockage within such sanitary sewer or combined sewer or building sewer that
may be the cause, in whole or in part, to any sewer problems on such person’s
or persons’ private property shall submit a written request to the village for
said televising work on a form as may be approved by the village board of
trustees from time to time.
      2.   Estimated Cost. If the village board of trustees, in its sole
discretion, approves such request, then the village president or his or her
designee shall inform such person(s) of the estimated cost for such televising
work (including, but not limited to, the televising, labor of the televising
personnel and of the village labor, excavating, digging, machinery, motor
vehicles, materials and equipment costs).
      3.   Contract. If such person(s) desires the televising work to be
performed, then such person(s) shall sign a contract with the village, in a
form as may be approved by the village board of trustees from time to time.
Such contract shall provide, inter alia, that such person(s) shall pay such
estimated costs at the time of signing such contract, and such person(s)
further agree to pay any amount in excess of such estimated cost immediately
upon the written demand of the village if the actual televising work exceeds
such estimated cost; provided, however, if the village determines that the
source of the problem or obstruction or blockage is wholly within the village
sanitary sewer system or combined sewer system and not within such private
property’s sewer system and was not caused, in whole or in part, by any problem
or sewerage or blockage or obstruction originating from the building sewer
system located on private property, then the village shall pay for such
televising work and shall refund such estimated cost previously paid by such
person(s).
      4.   Building Sewer Is Source; Immediate Payment Required. If the village
determines that the source of the problem or obstruction or blockage is within
such private property’s building sewer system or was caused, in whole or in
part, by any problem or sewerage or blockage or obstruction originating from
the building sewer system located on such private property, then the owner(s)
and tenant(s) of such property, jointly and severally, shall pay the village,
immediately upon written demand of the village, an amount equal to the cost and
expense incurred by the village to repair and/or remedy any such problem or
sewerage or blockage or obstruction within the village sanitary sewer system or
combined sewer system or within the building sewer system on such private
property.
      5.   Supervision; Televising Work. During all such televising, the
general superintendent or his or her designee shall be present. The village
shall arrange for such televising work, and such televising work shall be
performed at a time or times as the general superintendent, in his or her sole
discretion, shall determine. Any contractor employed by the village to perform
any such televising work shall sign a contract, in a form as may be approved by
the village board of trustees from time to time, whereby such contractor
agrees, inter alia, to the payment terms set forth therein.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-84)
   B.   Excavations. If the village or its contractors perform any digging or
excavating work within any village easement or right-of-way on private property
incident to determining the source of or to perform the repair of or remedying
of any problem or sewerage or blockage or obstruction within the village
sanitary sewer system or combined sewer system, and the source of such problem
or sewerage or blockage or obstruction is determined by the general
superintendent or his or her designee to have originated from the building
sewer system located on such private property, then the owner(s) of such
private property and all tenants thereof, jointly and severally, shall pay the
village immediately, upon written demand by the village, an amount equal to all
costs and expenses incurred by the village for such digging or excavating or
repair or remedying work, including, but not limited to, all materials,
equipment, machinery and labor costs.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-85)
§ 8-3-14 DAMAGE TO, TAMPERING WITH SYSTEM.
   No unauthorized person shall maliciously, willfully or negligently break,
damage, destroy or tamper with any structure, appurtenance or equipment which
is a part of the sewage works. Any person violating this section shall be
subject to immediate arrest under charge of disorderly conduct and, upon
conviction thereof, shall be fined as provided in § 1-4-1 for each violation.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-50) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   
§ 8-3-15 POWERS AND AUTHORITY OF INSPECTORS.
   A.   Entry Powers; Limitations On Requested Information.
      1.   The approving authority and other duly authorized employees of the
village and the state environmental protection agency, bearing proper
credentials and identification, shall be permitted to enter all properties for
the purposes of inspection, observation, measurement, sampling and testing in
accordance with the provisions of this chapter. The approving authority or his
or her representative shall have no authority to inquire into any processes,
including metallurgical, chemical, oil refining, ceramic, paper or other
industries beyond that point having a direct bearing on the kind and source of
discharge to the sewers or waterway or facilities for waste treatment.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-52)
      2.   The approving authority and other duly authorized employees of the
village, bearing proper credentials and identification, shall be permitted to
enter all private properties through which the village holds a duly negotiated
easement for the purposes of, but not limited to, inspection, observation,
measurement, sampling, repair and maintenance of any portion of the sewage
works lying within said easement. All entry and subsequent work, if any, on
said easement, shall be done in full accordance with the terms of the duly
negotiated easement pertaining to the private property involved.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-54)
   B.   Safety Precautions. While performing the necessary work on private
properties referred to in subsection A. above, the approving authority or duly
authorized employees of the village and the state environmental protection
agency shall observe all safety rules applicable to the premises established by
the company, and the company shall be held harmless for injury or death to the
village employees, and the village shall indemnify the company against
liability claims and demands for personal injury or property damage asserted
against the company and growing out of the gauging and sampling operations,
except as such may be caused by negligence or failure of the company to
maintain conditions as required in §
8-3-11 .
(1987 Code, § 38-5-53)
§ 8-3-16 ADDITIONAL REQUIREMENTS.
   No statement contained in this chapter shall be construed to interfere with
any additional requirements that may be imposed by the board of health of the
state and/or of the county.
(Ord. 698, passed 6-14-2004)
§ 8-3-17 VIOLATIONS; PENALTIES.
   A.   Notice Of Violation; Time For Compliance. Any person found to be
violating any provision of this chapter, except §
8-3-14 , shall be served by the village with written notice stating the nature
of the violation and providing a reasonable time limit for the satisfactory
correction thereof. The offender shall, within the period of time stated in
such notice, permanently cease all violations.
   B.   Revocation Of Permit. The village may revoke any permit for sewage
disposal as a result of any violation of any provision of this chapter.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-56)
   C.   Misdemeanor Violation; Penalty. Any person who shall continue any
violation beyond the time limit provided for in subsection A. above shall be
guilty of a misdemeanor and, on conviction thereof, shall be fined as provided
in §
1-4-1  for each violation. Each day on which any such violation shall continue
shall be deemed a separate offense.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-57) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   D.   Liability For Violations. Any person violating any of the provisions of
this chapter shall become liable to the village by reason of such violation.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-58)
