§ 8-3A-1 BASIS FOR WASTEWATER SERVICE CHARGE.
   The wastewater service charge for the use of and for service supplied by the
wastewater facilities of the village shall consist of a basic user charge for
operation, maintenance and equipment replacement; the debt service charge for
debt service cost and capital improvement costs; and a surcharge for wastes
that exceed those listed in subsection A.5. below.
   A.   Basic User Charge. The basic user charge shall be based on water usage
as recorded by water meters. It shall consist of operation and maintenance
costs, plus an estimated amount for equipment replacement. The basic user
charge shall be computed as follows:
      1.   Estimate wastewater volume, pounds of SS and pounds of BOD to be
treated;
      2.   Estimate the projected annual revenue required to operate and
maintain the wastewater facilities including a replacement fund for the year
for all works categories;
      3.   Proportion the debt service, capital improvement and estimated
operation, maintenance and replacement (OM&R) costs to wastewater facility
categories by volume, suspended solids and BOD;
      4.   Compute costs per 1,000 gallons for normal sewage strength; and
      5.   Compute surcharge costs per pound in excess of normal sewage
strength for BOD and SS.
   B.   Debt Service Charge. The debt service charge is computed by
apportioning the annual debt service on a cost per 1,000 gallons’ basis.
   C.   Surcharge. A surcharge will be levied to all users whose waters exceed
the normal domestic concentrations of BOD (350 mg/l) and SS (350 mg/l). The
surcharge will be based on water usage as recorded by water meters for all
wastes which exceed the 350 mg/l and 350 mg/l concentrations for BOD and SS,
respectively. Section
8-3A-4 B specifies the procedure to compute a surcharge.
   D.   Adequacy Of Charge. The adequacy of the wastewater service charge shall
be reviewed, not less often than annually, by certified public accountants for
the village in their annual report. The wastewater service charges shall be
revised periodically to reflect a change in local capital costs or a change in
operation and maintenance costs, including replacement costs.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-60)
§ 8-3A-2 MEASUREMENT OF FLOW.
   A.   The volume of flow used for computing basic user charges and surcharges
shall be the metered water consumption read to the lowest even increment of 100
gallons.
   B.   If the person discharging wastes into the public sewers procures any
part, or all, of his or her water from sources other than the public waterworks
system, all or a part of which is discharged into the public sewers, the person
shall install and maintain, at his or her expense, water meters of a type
approved by the approving authority for the purpose of determining the volume
of water obtained from these sources.
   C.   Devices for measuring the volume of waste discharged may be required by
the approving authority if these volumes cannot otherwise be determined from
the metered water consumption records.
   D.   Metering devices for determining the volume of waste shall be
installed, owned and maintained by the person. Following approval and
installation, such meters may not be removed without the consent of the
approving authority, unless service is canceled.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-61)
§ 8-3A-3 RATES ESTABLISHED; EXEMPTIONS.
   A.   Minimum Charge; Basic User Rate. There are hereby established minimum
charges and basic user rates for the use of and for service supplied by the
wastewater facilities of the village to premises located within and outside the
corporate limits of the village.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-62)
      1.   Inside Village Limits. As to premises serviced within the corporate
limits of the village, a minimum charge of $28 per month shall be applied to
all such users whose water consumption does not exceed 2,000 gallons per month,
and a basic user rate of $7 per 1,000 gallons shall be applied to all such
users for water consumption in excess of 2,000 gallons per month.
      2.   Outside Village Limits. As to premises serviced outside the
corporate limits, a minimum charge of $28 per month shall be applied to all
such users where water consumption does not exceed 2,000 gallons per month, and
a charge of $7 per 1,000 gallons shall be applied to all such users for water
consumption that exceeds 2,000 gallons per month.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-62) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   B.   Exceptions To Monthly Minimum Charge.
      1.   Notwithstanding subsection A. above, there shall be no minimum
monthly charge for sewer service to the following premises:
         a.   During construction of new residential homes, commercial or
industrial building or structures; or
         b.   For such newly constructed residences, buildings or structures,
after completion of construction until such homes, buildings or structures are
initially used or occupied after completion of construction.
      2.   During such time the monthly minimum charge is not being assessed,
the following basic user rates, in addition to any surcharge rate under
subsection C. below, shall apply:
(1987 Code, § 38-5-62.5)
         a.   Inside Village Limits. As to premises being serviced within the
corporate limits of the village, a basic user rate of $7 per 1,000 gallons, or
part thereof, of water provided per month shall be applied to all such users
for sewer consumption; and
         b.   Outside Village Limits. As to premises being serviced outside the
corporate limits of the village, a basic user rate of $7 per 1,000 gallons, or
part thereof, of water provided per month shall be applied to all such users
for sewer consumption.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-62.5) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   C.   Surcharge Rate. The rates or surcharges for BOD and SS shall be as
follows:
 
Per pound of BOD $0.30
Per pound of SS  $0.30
 
(1987 Code, § 38-5-63)
   D.   Changes To Rates. The foregoing rates and charges may be changed by
ordinance, written or oral resolution or motion as the corporate authorities
may elect.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-66) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   E.   Effective Date Of Rates. The rates and service charges for user charges
in this section shall be effective November 1, 2007.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-82) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 8-3A-4 COMPUTATION OF RATES.
   A.   Wastewater Service Charge. The wastewater service charge shall be
computed by the following formula:
 
CW = CD + CM + (Vu-X) CU + CS
Notes To Table:
CW = Amount of waste service charge ($) per billing period
CD = Debt service charge
CM = Minimum charge for operation, maintenance and replacement (See §
8-3A-3 A)
Vu = Wastewater volume for the billing period
X = Allowable consumption in gallons for the minimum charge (See §
8-3A-3 A)
CU = Basic user rate for operation, maintenance and replacement (See §
8-3A-3 A)
CS = Amount of surcharge (See §
8-3A-3 C and subsection B. below)
 
(1987 Code, § 38-5-65)
   B.   Surcharge. The concentration of wastes used for computing surcharges
shall be established by waste sampling. Waste sampling shall be performed as
often as may be deemed necessary by the village board and shall be binding as a
basis for surcharges. The surcharge amount shall be computed by the following
formula:
 
CD = Vu (EBOD + ESS) x .008345 x SR
Notes To Table:
Vu = Wastewater volume for the billing period in 1,000 gallons.
EBOD = BOD concentration in excess of 350 mg/l (BOD = 350).
ESS = SS concentration in excess of 350 mg/l (SS = 350).
.008345 = Factor to convert mg/l to pounds per 1,000 gallons.
SR = Surcharge rate (See §
8-3A-3 C)
 
(1987 Code, § 38-5-64)
§ 8-3A-5 MONTHLY PAYMENTS; LIABILITY FOR CHARGES.
   The rates or charges for service shall be payable monthly by the owner of
the premises, the occupant thereof and the user of the service to such
premises, and the service is furnished to the premises by the village only upon
the condition that the owner of the premises, the occupant and the user of the
service are jointly and severally liable therefor to the village.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-70)
§ 8-3A-6 BILLS RENDERED; DUE DATE; LATE PAYMENT PENALTY.
   A.   Bills for sewer service shall be dated and mailed by the village
treasurer on the first village working day of the month succeeding the period
for which the service is billed. All sewer bills are due and payable on or
before the sixteenth day following the date of the bill.
   B.   Bills not fully paid within such time shall be considered delinquent. A
penalty of 10% shall be added to all bills not fully paid by the sixteenth day
after the date of the bill. Sewer bills and penalties shall be paid at the
office of the village treasurer.
   C.   If the sixteenth day falls on a Saturday, Sunday or legal holiday,
bills shall not be considered delinquent until midnight the next working day.
   D.   Postmarks and dates written on checks shall not constitute proof of
payment to avoid penalty.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-70)
§ 8-3A-7 RETURNED CHECK FEE.
   If any check is returned to the village for insufficient funds, a 10%
penalty, plus an insufficient funds charge, shall be assessed against such
customer and further checks shall not be accepted from such customer for sewer
service without the consent of the mayor and village board of trustees.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-70) (Amd. 2008 Code)
Cross-reference:
   Bad Checks Policy, see §
8-1-3 E.
§ 8-3A-8 DELINQUENT PAYMENTS; SHUT OFF OF SERVICE.
   If the charges for sewer services are not paid within 30 days after the date
of the bill for such sewer services, the sewer services to the delinquent sewer
customer’s premises shall be shut off and discontinued without further notice
to the utility customer.
(Ord. 702, passed 10-11-2004)
§ 8-3A-9 LIEN PROVISIONS.
   A.   Lien Claim And Notice. Whenever a bill for sewer service remains unpaid
for 60 days after it has been rendered, the village treasurer shall file with
the county recorder of deeds a sworn statement of lien claim. This sworn
statement shall contain a description of the premises served, sufficient for
the identification thereof, the amount of the unpaid sewer bill, when such
amount became delinquent, and a notice that the village claims a lien for this
amount as well as for all sewer charges subsequent to the period covered by the
bill. If the user whose bill is unpaid is not the owner of the premises and the
village treasurer has notice of this, notice shall be mailed to the owner of
the premises, if his or her address is known to the treasurer, whenever such
bill remains unpaid for 45 days for a monthly bill after it has been rendered.
The failure of the village treasurer to record such lien or to mail the notice,
or the failure of the owner to receive the notice shall not affect the right to
foreclose the lien for unpaid bills as mentioned herein.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-72)
   B.   Foreclosure Of Lien. Property subject to a lien for unpaid charges
shall be sold for nonpayment of the same, and the proceeds of the sale shall be
applied to pay the charges, after deducting costs, as is the case in the
foreclosure of statutory liens. Such foreclosure shall be filed in the name of
the village. The village attorney is hereby authorized and directed to
institute such proceedings in the name of the village in any court having
jurisdiction over such matters against any property for which the bill has
remained unpaid 60 days after it has been rendered.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-74)
   C.   Attorney Fees And Court Costs. In the event any delinquent sewerage
bill is turned over to the village attorney for purposes of collection or
foreclosure of lien, the debtor or debtors shall, jointly and severally, pay
all reasonable attorney fees and any court costs incurred by the village.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-76)
 
§ 8-3A-10 REVENUES.
   A.   All revenues and monies derived from the operation of the sewerage
system shall be deposited in the sewerage fund. All such revenues and monies
shall be held by the village treasurer separate and apart from his or her
private funds and separate and apart from all other funds of the village, and
all of the sum, without any deductions whatever, shall be delivered to the
village treasurer not more than ten days after receipt of the same, or at such
more frequent intervals as may, from time to time, be directed by the mayor and
village board of trustees.
   B.   The village treasurer shall receive all such revenues from the sewerage
system and all other funds and monies incident to the operation of the system
as the same may be delivered to him or her and deposit the same in the account
of the fund designated as the sewerage fund of the village. The treasurer shall
administer such fund in every respect in the manner provided by state statutes.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-75)
§ 8-3A-11 ACCOUNTING.
   A.   System Of Accounts. The village treasurer shall establish a proper
system of accounts and shall keep proper books, records and accounts in which
complete and correct entries shall be made of all transactions relative to the
sewerage system, and at regular annual intervals, he or she shall cause to be
made an audit by an independent auditing concern of the books to show the
receipts and disbursements of the sewerage system.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-77)
   B.   Annual Audit Report. In addition to the customary operating statements,
the annual audit report shall also reflect the revenues and operating expenses
of the wastewater facilities, including a replacement cost, to indicate that
sewer service charges under the waste cost recovery system and capital amounts
required to be recovered under the industrial cost recovery system do, in fact,
meet these regulations. In this regard, the financial information to be shown
in the audit report shall include the following:
      1.   Flow data showing total gallons received at the wastewater plant for
the current fiscal year;
      2.   Billing data to show total number of gallons billed per fiscal year;
      3.   Debt service for the next succeeding fiscal year;
      4.   Number of users connected to the system;
      5.   Number of nonmetered users; and
      6.   A list of users discharging nondomestic and industrial wastes and
volume of waste discharged.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-78)
§ 8-3A-12 ACCESS TO RECORDS.
   The state environmental protection agency or its authorized representative
shall have access to any books, documents, papers and records of the village
which are applicable to the village system of user charges or industrial cost
recovery for the purpose of making audit, examination, excerpts and
transcriptions thereof to ensure compliance with the terms of the special and
general conditions to any state grant.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-81)
§ 8-3A-13 APPEALS.
   The method for computation of rates and service charges established for user
charges in this article shall be made available to a user within 35 days of
receipt of a written request for such. Any disagreement over the method used or
in the computations thereof shall be remedied by the village within 35 days
after notification of a formal written appeal.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-83)
§ 8-3A-14 PENALTY.
   Any owner, occupant or user violating or failing to comply with any
provisions of this article shall, upon conviction thereof, be fined as provided
in §
1-4-1 for each offense, and a separate offense shall be deemed committed on
each day during or on which a violation or failure occurs or continues.
(1987 Code, § 38-5-80) (Amd. 2008 Code)
