§ 8-4-1 ADMINISTRATION OF SYSTEM.
   A.   Water Department Established; Composition. There shall be an executive
department of the village known as the water department. It shall include the
committee on water, appointed by the mayor, and its employees. The designated
office shall be the village hall.
(1987 Code, § 38-1-20)
   B.   Committee On Water. The committee on water shall exercise a general
supervision over the affairs of the waterworks system. They shall ascertain the
condition and needs thereof; shall, from time to time, report the same to the
mayor and village board so that a full understanding thereof shall be had; and
generally shall do all acts necessary to promote the efficiency of the water
department.
(1987 Code, § 38-1-21)
   C.   Duties Of General Superintendent. The duties of the general
superintendent of the village shall be as follows:
      1.   He or she shall, subject to the direction of the village board, have
the general direction and control of the water department, and shall see that
the object and purpose thereof are carried out, and that the waterworks system
is conducted on an economical, businesslike basis;
      2.   He or she shall inspect all connections to the waterworks system at
the time they are made and report to the treasurer the date when the water is
turned on;
      3.   He or she shall shut off the water supply of any delinquent user or
violator of the provisions of this title;
      4.   He or she shall determine the size of each connection to be made;
      5.   He or she shall supervise the setting, taking off and repairing of
all water meters;
      6.   He or she shall make all repairs occasioned by leaks or breaks in
the mains of the waterworks system, and shall shut off the water from all
premises where leaks are permitted to go unrepaired; and
      7.   He or she shall examine and test, at least annually, all fire
hydrants and street valves, and if they are found to be in an unsatisfactory
condition, he or she is to repair the same.
(1987 Code, § 38-1-23)
   D.   Duties Of Village Treasurer. The village treasurer shall issue permits
for connections to the waterworks system whenever an application for the same
has been made as provided herein.
(1987 Code,   § 38-1-23) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 8-4-2 INSTALLATION OF LINES; CONNECTION TO SYSTEM.
   A.   Installation Requirements. Regarding the installation of the water line
to any premises (except as may be otherwise provided by annexation ordinance
594), the village shall make all installations between the village water main
and five feet beyond the property line; provided, however, the village may, at
the owner’s request, install such water line from the owner’s property line to
within ten feet of the premises to be serviced; and provided further, in any
event, the owner(s) of such premises shall pay for all materials and shall
further pay the village for any such installation at the then prevailing hourly
rate as set forth in §
2-1-3 .
(Amd. 2008 Code)
   B.   Connection To System.
      1.   Permit And Notice Required. No connections with any village water
main shall be made without a permit being issued by the village and 24 hours’
notice having been given to the general superintendent.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-1)
      2.   Application And Fee. Applications for such connection shall be made
to the village treasurer, and a fee of $200, plus meter and materials, shall be
paid for each connection permit. The forms of application shall be as
determined from time to time by the mayor and village board of trustees.
      3.   Indemnification. The owner(s) shall indemnify and hold harmless the
village from any and all losses or damages which may be occasioned, directly or
indirectly, by the installation and/or connection of such water line.
      4.   Specifications.
         a.   Before any new connection is made to the water distribution
system of the village, the premises to be served must be equipped with a meter
and buffalo box or meter vault properly installed on the service pipe.
         b.   For service installation, type K copper or any approved
equivalent service pipe shall be installed from the corporation stop to the
meter; and where an outside meter vault is used, type K copper shall be
installed five feet beyond the meter vault.
         c.   All services shall be installed with a minimum ground cover of 48
inches.
         d.   Solder joints shall not be used where the pipe will be
underground.
         e.   No premises shall be connected with the village water
distribution system unless the premises has a shutoff valve. When the village
must shut off the water supply at the meter in order that the owner may make
emergency repairs, the water supply shall not be turned back on until the owner
has installed a shutoff valve and the general superintendent has inspected said
shutoff valve.
         f.   From and after January 10, 2000, all future new or replacement
water extension and/or service lines from the water main to the water meter(s)
and from the water meter(s) to the property to be serviced with water shall be
of such size as is required by the Illinois Plumbing Code.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-29)
(Ord. 698, passed 6-14-2004)
§ 8-4-3 APPLICATION FOR, TURNING ON SERVICE.
   A.   Application For Service.
      1.   Generally. Application to have water from the village water supply
turned on for service into any premises shall be made in writing to the village
treasurer. The forms of application shall be as determined from time to time by
the mayor and board of trustees. Such application shall contain an agreement by
the applicant(s) to accept and abide by all the provisions of this chapter and
all applicable codes, rules and regulations of the village as now or hereafter
enacted or amended.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-4)
      2.   Rental Property; Deposit Required.
         a.   In the case of rental property, a deposit of $100 shall be paid.
This sum shall be retained by the village to ensure payment of all bills and
may be applied by the village toward payment of any delinquent bill(s). No such
deposit shall be required of any applicant who is the owner of the premises to
be served by the village water supply. For purposes hereof, a contract for deed
purchaser shall be deemed a renter and not an owner of the premises to be
served by the water supply or service.
         b.   When water service to the applicant is discontinued, this
deposit, minus any amount remaining due the village for water service, shall be
refunded to the applicant without interest.
         c.   The village reserves the right, at any time, to increase the
amount of the required deposit in the case of a renter and to impose a deposit
in the case of any owner in any amount the village deems appropriate as a
condition for initial water supply or service or for further water supply or
service where such renter or owner is delinquent in his or her utility bill(s)
or has previously been delinquent in payment of his or her utility bill(s) to
the village.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-5)
   B.   Compliance With Plumbing Requirements.
      1.   No water service from the village water supply shall be turned on
for service in any premises in which the plumbing does not comply with this
chapter and other ordinances of the village; provided, however, water may be
turned on for service for the purpose of construction work in any unfinished
building subject to the provisions of this chapter.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-6)
      2.   All fixtures and methods of installation in any premises to be
serviced and which are constructed or improved hereafter or in any premises to
be serviced where plumbing fixtures are replaced shall comply with the
requirements of the Illinois Plumbing Code.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-7)
   C.   Unauthorized Work Prohibited. No water from the village water supply
shall be turned on for service in any premises by any person but the general
superintendent or some person authorized by him or her to perform this duty.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-8)
§ 8-4-4 METERS.
   A.   Metered Water Required.
      1.   No premises shall be occupied and no water from the village water
supply shall be turned on for service until and unless a water meter is
installed as required by this chapter.
(Ord. 698, passed 6-14-2004)
      2.   All premises using the village water supply must be equipped with an
adequate water meter approved and furnished by the village but paid for by the
owner of the premises prior to installation.
   B.   Location.
      1.   Such water meter shall be installed in a location which shall be of
easy access and shall remain the property of the village.
      2.   In the event the village corporate authorities, by majority vote,
determine it is necessary to hereafter move the location of any water meter by
reason of such water meter, pipe or other part of the village water system
being located, in whole or in part, under any building, structure, driveway,
patio or other improvements of any kind, nature or extent whatsoever, or by
reason of violation of any other village ordinances, or in the event the owner
(s) of the premises where such water meter is located requests the relocation
of any such water meter for his or her convenience, then, in either event, the
owner(s) of the premises where the water meter is located shall immediately pay
the village for the actual cost of the water meter if another one is installed,
and for all materials, parts and pipes used in such relocation, and for all
labor and machinery costs to relocate such water meter; and the village shall
actually perform the work to relocate such water meter.
      3.   If the owner(s) desires to relocate the water meter for convenience
purposes, then such owner(s) must first submit a signed application to the
village requesting such relocation, which application shall be on a form
approved by the village board of trustees from time to time. The president and
village board of trustees, by majority vote, shall determine whether to grant
such request.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-9)
   C.   Reading Meters. The general superintendent shall read or cause to be
read every water meter at such times as are necessary in order that the bill
for water service may be mailed at the proper time. (1987 Code, § 38-4-11)
   D.   Testing Meters. Upon complaint of the party using the water from the
village water supply and payment of a fee of $20, the village shall remove and
test the water meter. If after testing, the water meter is not within 3% of
being accurate, the water meter shall be repaired or replaced by the village,
and the $20 fee shall be returned to the complaining party.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-12)
§ 8-4-5 RATES AND CHARGES.
§ 8-4-5-1 RATES AND CHARGES ESTABLISHED.
   A.   The water service charge for the use of and for water service supplied
by the village’s waterworks facilities system to premises located within and
outside the corporate limits of the village shall consist of a minimum charge,
a basic usage charge, a debt service charge and a capital improvement charge.
The water service charge shall not apply to bulk tank purchasers, who shall be
charged a bulk tank water purchase charge as hereinafter set forth.
   B.   The minimum service charge and the basic usage charge is levied on all
users to recover the operation, maintenance, plus replacement (OM&R) costs and
shall be based on water usage as recorded by water meters. The basic usage
charge shall be computed as follows:
      1.   Estimate the annual volume;
      2.   Estimate the projected annual revenue required to operate and
maintain the waterworks facilities system, including a replacement fund for the
municipal year, for all works categories;
      3.   Subtract the minimum service charge; and
      4.   Compute costs per 1,000 gallons, in excess of 2,000 gallons;
   C.   The debt service charge is levied on all users to repay any debt
service for the village’s waterworks facilities system from time to time. The
debt service charge is computed as necessary by apportioning any annual debt
service as a fixed charge per user account each month. The debt service charge
shall be set aside in a separate village waterworks account and dedicated,
pledged and used solely to pay any outstanding debt or to satisfy any loan
repayment agreement with the IEPA public water supply revolving loan program
attributable to the village’s waterworks facilities system. During the time
that such loan repayment agreement remains in effect, the village shall submit
to the state environmental protection agency upon request a statement on the
status of the debt service charge account, including the projected revenues,
actual revenues fund balance, debt service obligations and other requirements
of the loan repayment agreement with the IEPA public water supply revolving
loan program. To the extent necessary, the IEPA shall have reasonable access at
the village office during reasonable business office hours to view the
village’s records regarding its waterworks facilities system.
   D.   The capital improvement charge is levied on all users to provide funds
towards the costs of capital improvements, extensions or reconstruction of the
village’s waterworks facilities system from time to time. The capital
improvement charge is computed by apportioning the annual amount to be accrued
as a fixed charge per user account per month.
   E.   The adequacy of the foregoing water service charges shall be reviewed
annually by the certified public accountant(s) that prepare the village’s
annual audit report. The foregoing water service charges shall be revised
periodically to reflect any substantial change in the anticipated capital
improvement costs or in the OM&R costs.
   F.   The volume of flow of water used for computing the minimum service
charge and the basic usage charge shall be the metered water consumption read
to the lowest even increments of gallons.
   G.   There shall be and there is hereby established a minimum service charge
for all users of water service up to and including 2,000 gallons per month.
      1.   For premises serviced within village corporate limits: $43 per
month.
      2.   For premises serviced outside village corporate limits: $53 per
month.
      3.   Notwithstanding the foregoing, the aforesaid minimum service charge
shall not apply in the following instances:
         a.   During construction of new residential homes, commercial or
industrial buildings or structures;
         b.   For newly constructed residential homes, commercial or industrial
buildings or structures after completion of construction until such homes,
buildings or structures are initially used or occupied after completion of
construction; and
         c.   During said time periods and instances the minimum service charge
for such users of water service up to and including 2,000 gallons per month
shall be as follows:
            (1)   For premises serviced within village corporate limits: $7 per
1,000 gallons, or part thereof; and
            (2)   For premises services outside village corporate limits: $11
per 1,000 gallons, or part thereof.
   H.   There shall be and there is hereby established a basic usage charge for
usage in excess of 2,000 gallons per month to be charged at the following rates
per 1,000 gallons:
      1.   For premises serviced within village corporate limits: $10 per 1,000
gallons, or part thereof; and
      2.   For premises services outside village corporate limits: $11 per
1,000 gallons, or part thereof.
   I.   There shall be and there is hereby established a debt service charge of
$10.50 per month to each user account.
   J.   There shall be and there is hereby established a capital improvement
charge of $0.00 per month to each user account.
   K.   The water service charge for each user account each month shall be
computed by the following formula:
 
CW = CC + CD + CM + ((Vu - 2,000 gallons)*CU)
Notes To Table:
CW = Dollar amount of water service charge per billing period
CC = Capital improvement charge (subsection J. above)
CD = Debt service charge (subsection I. above)
CM = Minimum service charge (subsection G. above)
Vu = Water volume per the billing period.
CU = Basic usage charge for operation, maintenance & replacement (subsection H.
above)
 
   L.   1.   For bulk tank purchasers, the charge shall be $1.25 per 100
gallons or portion thereof. Such water shall be dispensed pursuant to a coin
machine or a token machine, as the village board of trustees may determine from
time to time.
      2.   Bulk tank water purchasers or his, her or their principals, agents,
employees, successors or assigns, shall not resell or distribute such purchased
waster for less than the village’s water service charge (as computed under the
preceding subsection K. above) for water service to premises located within or
outside the village’s corporate limits, as the case may be.
      3.   The village reserves the right, from time to time, to restrict or
terminate entirely the sale of water to bulk tank water purchasers. Such
restriction or termination may be for any reason or cause whatsoever as may be
solely determined by the majority vote of the village president and board of
trustees, from time to time. Any bulk tank water purchaser who purchases water
form the village thereby submits such purchaser and his, her or their
principals, agents, employees, successors, and assigns to the jurisdiction of
the village; and each such person, firm or entity does thereby agree to and
shall comply with all applicable village’s ordinances, resolutions, rules and
regulations pertaining to water. All bulk tank water purchasers and his, her or
their principals, agents, employees, successors, and assigns shall further
comply with all cross-connection requirements as may be determined by the
village general superintendent at the time of the purchase and receipt of such
water from the village.
   M.   Any one or more or all of the water service charges, including, but not
limited to, the minimum charge, the basic usage charge, the debt service
charge, and the capital improvement charge, and the bulk water tank purchaser
charge may be amended, modified or revised from time to time by the village
president and board of trustees by ordinance, written or oral resolution or
motion as such corporate authorities may determine and adopt by at least an
affirmative majority vote from time to time.
   N.   The method of computation of water service charges established for
water service charges as set forth in this section shall be made available to a
user within 35 days of receipt of a written request for such. Any disagreement
over the method used or in the computations thereof for and as applicable to
that user shall be resolved and determined by the village board of trustees
within 35 days after notification of a formal written appeal is received by the
village treasurer.
(Ord. 765, passed 2-8-2010; Ord. 852, passed 5-14-2018)
§ 8-4-5-2 MONTHLY PAYMENTS; LIABILITY FOR CHARGES.
   The rates or charges for service shall be payable monthly. The owner of the
premises, the occupant thereof, and the user of the service shall be jointly
and severally liable to pay for the service to such premises, and the service
is furnished to the owner of the premises by the village only upon the
condition that the owner of the premises, the occupant and user of the services
are jointly and severally liable therefor to the village.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-14)
§ 8-4-5-3 BILLS RENDERED; DUE DATE; LATE PAYMENT PENALTY.
   A.   Bills for water service shall be dated and mailed by the village
treasurer on the first working day of the month succeeding the period for which
the service is billed. All water bills are due and payable on or before the
sixteenth day following the date of the bill.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-14)
   B.   Bills not fully paid within such time shall be considered delinquent. A
penalty of 10% shall be added to all bills not fully paid by the sixteenth day
after the date of the bill. Water bills and penalties shall be paid at the
office of the village treasurer.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-14) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   C.   If the sixteenth day falls on a Saturday, Sunday or legal holiday,
bills shall not be considered delinquent until 12:00 a.m. the next working day.
   D.   Postmarks and dates written on checks shall not constitute proof of
payment to avoid penalty. (1987 Code, § 38-4-14)
§ 8-4-5-4 RETURNED CHECK FEE.
   If any check is returned to the village for insufficient funds, a 10%
penalty, plus an insufficient funds charge, shall be assessed against the
customer for water service and further checks shall not be accepted from such
customer for water service without the consent of the mayor and board of
trustees.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-14) (Amd. 2008 Code)
Cross-reference:
   Bad Checks Policy, see §
8-1-3 E.
§ 8-4-5-5 DELINQUENT PAYMENTS; SHUT OFF OF SERVICE.
   If the charges for water services are not paid within 30 days after the date
of the bill for such water services, the water services to the delinquent water
customer’s premises shall be shut off and discontinued without further notice
to the utility customer.
(Ord. 702, passed 10-11-2004)
§ 8-4-5-6 LIEN PROVISIONS.
   A.   Lien Claim And Notice. Whenever a bill for water service remains unpaid
for 60 days after it has been rendered, the village treasurer shall file with
the county recorder of deeds a sworn statement of lien claim. This sworn
statement shall contain a description of the premises served, sufficient for
the identification thereof, the amount of the unpaid water bill, the date when
such amount became delinquent, and a notice that the village claims a lien for
this amount as well as for all water charges subsequent to the period covered
by the bill. If the user whose bill is unpaid is not the owner of the premises
and the village treasurer has notice of this, notice shall be mailed to the
owner of the premises, if his or her address be known to the treasurer,
whenever such bill remains unpaid for 45 days for a monthly bill after it has
been rendered. The failure of the village treasurer to record such lien or to
mail the notice, or the failure of the owner to receive the notice shall not
affect the right to foreclose the lien for unpaid bills as mentioned herein.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-16)
   B.   Foreclosure Of Lien. Property subject to a lien for unpaid charges
shall be sold for nonpayment of the same, and the proceeds of the sale shall be
applied to pay the charges, after deducting costs, as in the case in the
foreclosure statutory liens. Such foreclosure shall be filed in the name of the
village. The village attorney is hereby authorized and directed to institute
such proceedings in the name of the village in any court having jurisdiction
over such matters against any property for which the bill has remained unpaid
60 days after it has been rendered.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-17)
   C.   Attorney Fees And Court Costs. In the event any delinquent water bill
is turned over to the village attorney for purposes of collection or
foreclosure of lien, the debtor or debtors shall, jointly and severally, pay
all reasonable attorney fees and any court costs incurred by the village.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-14)
§ 8-4-5-7 REVENUES.
   A.   All revenues and monies derived from the operation of the water system
shall be deposited in the water fund. All such revenues and monies shall be
held by the village treasurer separate and apart from his or her private funds
and separate and apart from all other funds of the village, and all of said
sum, without any deductions whatever, shall be delivered to the village
treasurer not more than ten days after receipt of the same, or at such more
frequent intervals as may, from time to time, be directed by the mayor and
board of trustees.
   B.   The village treasurer shall receive all such revenues from the water
system and all other funds and monies incident to the operation of the system
as the same may be delivered to him or her and deposit the same in the account
of the fund designated as the water fund of the village. The treasurer shall
administer such fund in every respect in the manner provided by state statutes.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-18)
§ 8-4-6 SUBDIVISION FEES.
   When a subdivision is opened, the subdivider, prior to the installation of
any mains, shall pay the village the total amount of the estimated material
cost to install the mains. At the end of the project, the actual total material
cost shall be recalculated. In the event the village overestimated the material
costs, the subdivider shall be reimbursed; however, in the event the village
underestimated the material cost, the subdivider shall be billed for the
difference which shall become immediately due and payable. The village shall
furnish the necessary labor and equipment to install the mains.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-19)
§ 8-4-7 HYDRANTS.
   A.   Village Property; Use Restrictions. All hydrants shall be owned,
maintained and used only by the village except in a fire emergency situation,
in which case, the fire hydrant may be used by the village fire protection
district.
   B.   Nonliability Of Village. The village shall not be liable and will not
assume any responsibility for the condition of any fire hydrant inside or
outside the village limits, or the pressure or amount of water obtainable
therefrom, or any damages, either direct or resultant because of the condition,
pressure or amount of water available from any fire hydrant.
   C.   Hydrants Outside Village. All public fire hydrants outside of any
village limits owned by the village will be maintained in as good order as
reasonably possible, but the village will not undertake or assume any
responsibility or liability for their condition, use or abuse. Such public fire
hydrants shall be used only for the purpose of extinguishing fire.
(Ord. 698, passed 6-14-2004)
§ 8-4-8 RESALE OR DISTRIBUTION OF WATER.
   Except in case of emergency, no water shall be resold or distributed by the
recipient of the village water supply to any premises other than that for which
application has been made and the meter installed.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-20)
§ 8-4-9 DAMAGE TO, TAMPERING WITH SYSTEM.
   Unless authorized by the village to do so, it is unlawful for any person,
firm, entity or corporation to maliciously, willfully, or negligently break,
damage, destroy, uncover, deface, tamper with, alter, or injure, in whole or in
part, any structure, appurtenance or equipment which is a part of the village
waterworks or supply system or any water meter. Any person violating this
section shall, upon conviction thereof, be fined and/or sentenced in accordance
with
title 1, chapter 4. In addition thereto, such person shall pay the village
costs and expenses (determined in accordance with §
2-1-3) for all materials and labor and/or use of village machinery or equipment
to repair any such breakage, damage, destruction, covering, defacement,
tampering, alteration or injury to such structure, appurtenance or equipment.
Further, any water service to the premises shall be terminated and not
reinstated until and unless all such costs and expenses are fully paid to the
village; provided, the person violating this section is an owner or occupant of
the premises in which such structure, appurtenance or equipment services were
damaged.
(Ord. 698, passed 6-14-2004)
Cross-references:
   Damage To Utilities, see §
5-4-4-3
§ 8-4-10 DANGEROUS CONDITIONS.
   A.   Unsafe Usage. The village shall have the right to refuse water service
or to discontinue water service without notice at any time to any consumer if
the village finds any apparatus or appliances, the operation of which will be
detrimental to the water system of the village or to any or all of its
consumers. Standpipes, hydrants, gate valves and any other apparatus that cause
water damage or any danger to the water system or other customer’s plumbing
shall be immediately repaired or removed upon notice from the village, or at
its option, the village may immediately discontinue the service without notice
and without any liability for direct or resulting damages therefrom.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-23)
   B.   Electric Ground Wires. All persons are strictly forbidden to attach any
electric ground wire to any plumbing or water piping which is or may be
connected to any water service pipe, water meter or water main belonging to the
village. The village will hold the owner of the premises responsible and liable
for any damage to the property or injury to the employees of the village caused
by such ground wire. All owners and consumers shall remove any existing ground
wires immediately upon written notice from the village. If not so disconnected
after five days’ written notice, the village, through its officials, may enter
the property and remove such ground wires, and the consumer shall pay all
costs.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-24)
§ 8-4-11 WATER CONSERVATION MEASURES.
   A.   The village president is hereby authorized to proclaim from time to
time the existence or continuance of a water supply emergency whenever it
appears to the village president that the village water supply is or may become
inadequate or precariously low for all general water uses and purposes. Each
such proclamation shall declare which water restriction phase, as set forth in
the following subsection B. below, shall then be in effect. Each such
proclamation shall be published in the “Taylorville Breeze-Courier” newspaper
or in any other newspaper of general circulation in the village.
   B.   Water usage shall be restricted and prohibited in the following three
phases, with the commencement and termination of each phase being determined by
the village president from time to time. The three phases are:
      1.   Phase 1. It shall be unlawful:
         a.   To use any water to maintain lawns, landscaping, plants, shrubs,
trees, vegetable gardens, flower gardens or athletic field grass on Saturday,
Sunday, Tuesday or Thursday, but use of water to maintain vegetation
inventories at commercial gardening, landscaping or plant nurseries shall be
exempt;
         b.    To purchase or obtain or withdraw any water from the village’s
bulk water facility, station or tank;
         c.   To use water to wash, clean or detail any motor vehicles of any
kind, except for public health and safety; but commercial vehicle washing,
cleaning or detailing facilities are exempt under phase 1;
         d.   It shall be unlawful to not repair any detectable water service
line leak within 72 hours of discovery. If such leak is not repaired within
this time frame, the village may shut off water service; and
         e.   To use any water to wash or clean streets, driveways, sidewalks,
parking lots and all other paved surfaces, except for public health and safety;
and construction sites are exempt under phase 1.
      2.   Phase 2. It shall be unlawful:
         a.   To use any water to maintain lawns, landscaping, plants, shrubs,
trees, vegetable gardens, flower gardens or athletic field grass, and use of
water to maintain vegetation inventories at commercial gardening, landscaping
or plant nurseries shall be exempt under phase 2; and except that the use of
water to maintain vegetable gardens used to produce food is allowed only if the
water is poured from a bucket that is five gallons in volume or less on
Tuesdays, Thursdays and Saturdays;
         b.    To purchase or obtain or withdraw any water from the village’s
bulk water facility, station or tank;
         c.   To use water to wash or clean or detail any motor vehicles of any
kind, except for public health and safety; and commercial vehicle washing,
cleaning or detailing facilities are not exempt under phase 2;
         d.   It shall be unlawful to not repair any detectable water service
line leak within 72 hours of discovery. If such leak is not repaired within
this time frame, the village may shut off water service;
         e.   To use any water to wash or clean streets, driveways, sidewalks,
parking lots and all other paved surfaces, except for public health and safety;
and construction sites are exempt under phase 2;
         f.   To use any water to fill swimming pools, hot tubs or spas, or
ponds or water fountains, except topping off of swimming pools and hot tubs or
spas is allowed; and
         g.   To serve water at any restaurant or food serving facility unless
the customer requests it.
      3.   Phase 3. It shall be unlawful:
         a.   To use any water to maintain lawns, landscaping, plants, shrubs,
trees, vegetable gardens, flower gardens or athletic field grass, and use of
water to maintain vegetation inventories at commercial gardening, landscaping
or plant nurseries shall not be exempt under phase 3;
         b.   To purchase or obtain or withdraw any water from the village’s
bulk water facility, station or tank;
         c.   To use water to wash or clean or detail any motor vehicles of any
kind, except for public health and safety; and commercial vehicle washing,
cleaning or detailing facilities are not exempt under phase 3;
         d.   It shall be unlawful to not repair any detectable water service
line leak within 72 hours of discovery. If such leak is not repaired within
this time frame, the village may shut off water service;
         e.   To use any water to wash or clean streets, driveways, sidewalks,
parking lots and all other paved surfaces, except for public health and safety;
and construction sites are not exempt under phase 3;
         f.   To use any water to fill or top off swimming pools, hot tubs or
spas, or ponds or water fountains; and
         g.   To serve water at any restaurant or food serving facility unless
the customer requests it.
(Ord. 791, passed 8-13-2012)
§ 8-4-12 NONLIABILITY OF VILLAGE.
   The village shall not be held responsible for or in any manner liable to any
person, company, consumer or public body for any claim or damage, either direct
or resultant, because of any shortage of water supply, any shutoff of water
supply for any reason, any bursting or leakage of either the consumer’s or
village’s mains, pipes and fixtures, any pollution or impurity in water supply,
or any fire or water damage.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-27)
§ 8-4-13 PENALTY.
   Any person who shall violate any of the provisions of this chapter shall be
fined and/or sentenced in accordance with the applicable general penalty
provisions set forth in §
1-4-1.
(Ord. 698, passed 6-14-2004)
ARTICLE A: MINIMUM AND MAXIMUM WELL SETBACK ZONES
Section
   
8-4A-1   Purpose And Scope
   
8-4A-2   Definitions
   
8-4A-3   Prohibitions
   
8-4A-4   Waivers, Exceptions And Certifications Of Minimal Hazard
   
8-4A-5   Penalty
§ 8-4A-1 PURPOSE AND SCOPE.
   Pursuant to the authority conferred by 65 ILCS 5/11-125-4, and in the
interest of securing the public health, safety and welfare, to preserve the
quality and quantity of ground water resources in order to assure a safe and
adequate water supply for present and future generations, and to preserve
ground water resources currently in use and those aquifers having a potential
for future use as a public water supply, the provisions of this article shall
apply to all properties located within the minimum setback zone established
under the Environmental Protection Act § 14.2 (“act”), being 415 ILCS 5/14.2,
and this article, and the maximum setback zone established under § 14.3 of the
Act, being 415 ILCS 5/14.3, and this article.
(1987 Code, § 43-1-1)
§ 8-4A-2 DEFINITIONS.
   Except as stated in this section, and unless a different meaning of a word
or term is clear from the context, the definitions of words or terms in this
article shall be the same as those used in the Act, as said term is defined in
this section, and the Illinois Ground Water Protection Act, being 415 ILCS 55/
1.
   ACT. The Environmental Protection Act, being 415 ILCS 5/1 et seq.
   AGENCY. The state environmental protection agency.
   BOARD. The state pollution control board.
   MAXIMUM SETBACK ZONE. The area around the village water supply well
established under § 14.3 of the Act and this article.
   MINIMUM SETBACK ZONE. The area around the village water supply well
established under § 14.2 of the Act and this article.
(1987 Code, §43-1-2) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 8-4A-3 PROHIBITIONS.
   A.   1.   Except as provided in subsection A.2. below and §
8-4A-4, no person shall place a new potential primary source, new potential
secondary source or new potential route within the minimum setback zone.
(1987 Code, § 43-1-3)
      2.   This subsection A. shall not apply to new common sources of sanitary
pollution as specified pursuant to Environmental Protection Act § 17, being 415
ILCS 5/17, and the regulations adopted thereunder by the agency; however, no
such common sources may be located within the applicable minimum distance from
the village water supply well specified by such regulations.
(1987 Code, § 43-1-5)
   B.   Except as provided otherwise in §
8-4A-4, no person shall place a new potential primary source within the maximum
setback zone.
(1987 Code, § 43-1-3)
§ 8-4A-4 WAIVERS, EXCEPTIONS AND CERTIFICATIONS OF MINIMAL HAZARD.
   A.   Waivers. If, pursuant to § 14.2(b) of the Act, the owner of a new
potential primary source, new potential secondary source, or a new potential
route is granted a waiver by the agency, such owner shall be deemed to have a
waiver to the same extent from §
8-4A-3 A.1.
   B.   Exceptions.
      1.   If, pursuant to § 14.2(c) of the Act, the owner of a new potential
primary source (other than landfilling or land treating), new potential
secondary source, or new potential route is granted an exception by the board,
such owner shall be deemed to have an exception to the same extent from §
8-4A-3A.1.
      2.   If, pursuant to § 14.2(c) of the Act, the owner of a new potential
primary source (other than landfilling or land treating) is granted an
exception by the board, such owner shall be deemed to have an exception to the
same extent from §
8-4A-3B.
   C.   Certificate Of Minimal Hazard. If, pursuant to § 14.5 of the Act, being
415 ILCS 15/14.5, the owner of a new potential primary source, new potential
secondary source or new potential route is issued a certificate of minimal
hazard by the agency, such owner shall not be subject to §
8-4A-3 A.1. to the same extent that such owner is not subject to § 14.2(d) of
the Act.
(1987 Code, § 43-1-4)
§ 8-4A-5 PENALTY.
   Any person who violates this article shall, upon conviction thereof, be
fined as provided in §
1-4-1. A separate offense shall be deemed committed upon each day during on or
which a violation occurs or continues.
(1987 Code, § 43-1-6) (Amd. 2008 Code)
ARTICLE B: CROSS-CONNECTION CONTROL
Section
   
8-4B-1   Purpose
   
8-4B-2   Scope
   
8-4B-3   Definitions
   
8-4B-4   Water System Described
   
8-4B-5   Responsibility Of Owner
   
8-4B-6   Cross-Connections Restricted
   
8-4B-7   Surveys And Investigations
   
8-4B-8   Backflow Prevention
   
8-4B-9   Booster Pumps
   
8-4B-10   Violations; Penalties
§ 8-4B-1 PURPOSE.
   The purpose of this article is:
   A.   To protect the public water supply system from contamination or
pollution by isolating within the customer’s water system contaminants or
pollutants which could backflow through the system;
   B.   To promote the elimination or control of existing cross-connections,
actual or potential, between the public or consumer’s potable water system and
nonpotable water systems, plumbing fixtures and sources or systems containing
substances of unknown or questionable safety; and
   C.   To provide for the maintenance of a continuing program of cross-
connection control which will prevent the contamination or pollution of the
public and consumer’s potable water system.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-39)
§ 8-4B-2 SCOPE.
   This article shall apply to all premises served by the public potable water
supply system of the village.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-40)
§ 8-4B-3 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this article, the following definitions apply unless the
context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   AGENCY. The state environmental protection agency.
   APPROVED. Backflow prevention devices or methods approved by the Research
Foundation for Cross-Connection Control of the University of Southern
California, Association of State Sanitary Engineers, American Water Works
Association, American National Standards Institute or certified by the National
Sanitation Foundation.
   AUXILIARY WATER SYSTEM. Any water source or system on or available to the
premises other than the public water supply system and includes the water
supplied by the system. These auxiliary waters may include water from another
purveyor’s public water supply system; or water from a source such as wells,
lakes or streams or process fluids; or used water. These waters may be polluted
or contaminated or objectionable or constitute a water source or system over
which the water purveyor does not have control.
   BACKFLOW. The backflow of water or other liquids, mixtures or substances
into the distribution pipes of a potable water system from any source other
than the intended source of the potable water supply.
   BACKFLOW PREVENTION DEVICE. Any device, method or type of construction
intended to prevent backflow into a potable water system. All devices used for
backflow prevention in the state must meet the standards of the state plumbing
code and the state environmental protection agency.
   CONSUMER or CUSTOMER. The owner, official custodian or person in control of
any premises supplied by or in any manner connected to a public water system.
   CONSUMER’S WATER SYSTEM. Any water system located on the customer’s
premises. A building plumbing system is considered to be a CUSTOMER’S WATER
SYSTEM.
   CONTAMINATION. An impairment of the quality of the water by entrance of any
substance to a degree which could create a health hazard.
   CROSS-CONNECTION. Any physical connection or arrangement between two
otherwise separate piping systems, one of which contains potable water and the
other a substance of unknown or questionable safety or quality, whereby there
may be a flow from one system into the other.
   DIRECT CROSS-CONNECTION. A cross-connection formed when a water system is
physically joined to a source of unknown or unsafe substance.
   DOUBLE CHECK VALVE ASSEMBLY. An assembly composed of single, independently
acting check valves approved under ASSE standard 1015. A DOUBLE CHECK VALVE
ASSEMBLY and suitable connections for testing the watertightness of each check
valve.
   FIXED PROPER AIR GAP. The unobstructed vertical distance through the free
atmosphere between the water discharge point and the flood level rim of the
receptacle.
   HEALTH HAZARD. Any condition, device or practice in a water system or its
operation resulting from a real or potential danger to the health and well
being of consumers. The word SEVERE, as used to qualify HEALTH HAZARD, means a
hazard to the health of the user that could be expected to result in death or
significant reduction in the quality of life.
   INDIRECT CROSS-CONNECTION. A cross-connection through which an unknown
substance can be forced, drawn by vacuum or otherwise introduced into a safe
potable water system.
   INSPECTION. A plumbing inspection to examine carefully and critically all
materials, fixtures, piping and appurtenances, appliances and installations of
a plumbing system for compliance with requirements of the state plumbing code
and 77 Ill. Admin. Code 890.
   NONPOTABLE WATER. Water not safe for drinking, personal or culinary use.
   PLUMBING.
      1.   The actual installation, repair, maintenance, alteration or
extension of a plumbing system by any person.
      2.   PLUMBING includes all piping, fixtures, appurtenances and appliances
for a supply of water for all purposes, including, without limitation, lawn
sprinkler systems, from the source of a private water supply on the premises or
from the main in the street, alley or at the curb to, within and about any
building or buildings where a person or persons live, work or assemble.
      3.   PLUMBING includes all piping, from discharge of pumping units to and
including pressure tanks, in water supply systems.
      4.   PLUMBING includes all piping, fixtures, appurtenances and appliances
for a building drain and a sanitary drainage and related ventilation system of
any building or buildings where a person or persons live, work or assemble from
the point of connection of such building drain to the building sewer or private
sewage disposal system five feet beyond the foundation walls.
   POLLUTION. The presence of any foreign substance (organic, inorganic,
radiological or biological) in water that tends to degrade its quality so as to
constitute a hazard or impair the usefulness of the water.
   POTABLE WATER. Water which meets the requirements of 35 Ill. Admin. Code 654
for drinking, culinary and domestic purposes.
   POTENTIAL CROSS-CONNECTION. A fixture or appurtenance with threaded hose
connection, tapered spout, or other connection which would facilitate extension
of the water supply line beyond its legal termination point.
   PROCESS FLUID(S). Any fluid or solution which may be chemically,
biologically or otherwise contaminated or polluted in a form or concentration
such as would constitute a health, pollution or system hazard if introduced
into the public or a consumer’s potable water system. This includes, but is not
limited to:
      1.   Polluted or contaminated waters;
      2.   Process waters;
      3.   Used water originating from the public water supply system which may
have deteriorated in sanitary quality;
      4.   Cooling waters;
      5.   Questionable or contaminated natural waters taken from wells, lakes,
streams or irrigation systems;
      6.   Chemicals in solution or suspension; and
      7.   Oils, gases, acids, alkalis and other liquid and gaseous fluids used
in industrial or other processes, or for firefighting purposes.
   PUBLIC WATER SUPPLY. All mains, pipes and structures through which water is
obtained and distributed to the public, including wells and well structures,
intakes and cribs, pumping stations, treatment plants, reservoirs, storage
tanks and appurtenances, collectively or severally, actually used or intended
for use for the purpose of furnishing water for drinking or general domestic
use and which serve at least 15 service connections or which regularly serve at
least 25 persons at least 60 days per year. A PUBLIC WATER SUPPLY is either a
community water supply or a noncommunity water supply.
   REDUCED PRESSURE PRINCIPLE BACKFLOW PREVENTION DEVICE. A device containing a
minimum of two independently acting check valves, together with an
automatically operated pressure differential relief valve located between the
two check valves and approved under ASSE standard 1013. During normal flow and
at the cessation of normal flow, the pressure between these two checks shall be
less than the supply pressure. In case of leakage of either check valve, the
differential relief valve, by discharging to the atmosphere, shall operate to
maintain the pressure between the check valves at less than the supply
pressure. The unit must include tightly closed shutoff valves located at each
end of the device, and each device shall be fitted with properly located test
cocks.
   SERVICE CONNECTION. The opening, including all fittings and appurtenances,
at the water main through which water is supplied to the user.
   SURVEY. The collection of information pertaining to a customer’s piping
system regarding the location of all connections to the public water supply
system and must include the location, type and most recent inspection and
testing date of all cross-connection control devices and methods located within
that customer’s piping system. The SURVEY must be in written from, and should
not be an actual plumbing inspection.
   SYSTEM HAZARD. A condition through which an aesthetically objectionable or
degrading material not dangerous to health may enter the public water supply
system or a consumer’s potable water system.
   UNUSED WATER. Any water supplied by a public water supply system to a
consumer’s water system after it has passed through the service connection and
is no longer under the control of the water supply office custodian.
   WATER PURVEYOR. The owner or official custodian of a public water system.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-42) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 8-4B-4 WATER SYSTEM DESCRIBED.
   The water system shall be considered as made up of two parts: the public
water supply system and the consumer’s water system.
   A.   Public Water Supply System.
      1.   The public water supply system shall consist of the source
facilities and the distribution system, and shall include all those facilities
of the potable water system under the control of the general superintendent up
to the point where the consumer’s water system begins.
      2.   The source shall include all components of the facilities utilized
in the production, treatment, storage and delivery of water to the public water
supply distribution system.
      3.   The public water supply distribution system shall include the
network of conduits used to deliver water from the source to the consumer’s
water system.
   B.   Consumer’s Water System. The consumer’s water system shall include all
parts of the facilities beyond the service connection used to convey water from
the public water supply distribution system to points of use.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-43)
§ 8-4B-5 RESPONSIBILITY OF OWNER.
   A.   The owner or official custodian shall be responsible for protection of
the public water supply system from contamination due to backflow or back
siphonage of contaminants through the customer’s water service connection.
   B.   If, in the judgment of the general superintendent or his or her
authorized representative, an approved backflow prevention device is necessary
for the safety of the public water supply system, the general superintendent
shall give notice to the consumer to install such approved backflow prevention
device at each service connection to the premises.
   C.   The consumer shall immediately install such approved device or devices
at his or her own expense. Failure, refusal or inability on the part of the
consumer to install such device or devices immediately shall constitute grounds
for discontinuing water service to the premises until such device or devices
have been installed.
   D.   The consumer shall retain records required in §
8-4B-8B for a period of at least five years.
   E.   The general superintendent may require the consumer to submit a cross-
connection inspection report to the village to assist in determining whether or
not service line protection will be required. All cross-connection inspections
shall be conducted by a cross-connection control device inspector certified by
the state environmental protection agency.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-41)
§ 8-4B-6 CROSS-CONNECTIONS RESTRICTED.
   A.   Connections between potable water systems and other systems or
equipment containing water or other substances of unknown or questionable
quality are prohibited except when and where approved cross-connection control
devices or methods are installed, tested and maintained to ensure proper
operation on a continuing basis.
   B.   No physical connection shall be permitted between the potable portion
of a supply and any other water supply not of equal or better bacteriological
and chemical quality as determined by inspection and analysis by the agency.
   C.   There shall be no arrangement or connection by which an unsafe
substance may enter a supply.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-44)
§ 8-4B-7 SURVEYS AND INVESTIGATIONS.
   A.   Right To Inspect. The consumer’s premises shall be open at all
reasonable times to the approved cross-connection control device inspector for
the inspection of the presence or absence of cross-connections within the
consumer’s premises, and testing, repair and maintenance of cross-connection
control devices within the consumer’s premises.
   B.   Information Required. On request of the general superintendent or his
or her authorized representative, the consumer shall furnish information
regarding the piping system or systems or water use within the customer’s
premises. The consumer’s premises shall be open at all reasonable times to the
general superintendent for the verification of information submitted by the
consumer to the public water supply custodian regarding cross-connection
inspection results.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-45)
   C.   Surveys. It shall be the responsibility of the water consumer to
arrange periodic surveys of water use practices on his or her premises to
determine whether there are actual or potential cross-connections to his or her
water system through which contaminants or pollutants could backflow into his
or her public potable water system. All cross-connection control or other
plumbing inspections must be conducted in accordance with 225 ILCS 320/3(1).
(1987 Code, § 38-4-45) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 8-4B-8 BACKFLOW PREVENTION.
   A.   Responsibility Of Owner. It is the responsibility of the water consumer
to prevent backflow into the public water system by ensuring that:
      1.   All cross-connections are removed or approved cross-connection
control devices are installed for control of backflow and back siphonage;
      2.   Cross-connection control devices shall be installed in accordance
with the manufacturer’s instructions; and
      3.   Cross-connection control devices shall be inspected at the time of
installation and at least annually by a person approved by the agency as a
cross-connection control device inspector (CCCDI). The inspection of mechanical
devices shall include physical testing in accordance with the manufacturer’s
instructions.
   B.   Testing And Records.
      1.   Each device shall be tested at the time of installation and at least
annually or more frequently if recommended by the manufacturer.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-45)
      2.   Records submitted to the community public water supply shall be
available for inspection by agency personnel in accordance with 415 ILCS 5/4
(e).
(1987 Code, § 38-4-45) (Amd. 2008 Code)
      3.   Each device shall have a tag attached listing the date of most
recent test, name of CCCDI, and type and date of repairs.
      4.   A maintenance log shall be maintained and include:
         a.   Date of each test;
         b.   Name and approval number of person performing the test;
         c.   Test results;
         d.   Repairs or servicing required;
         e.   Repairs and date completed; and
         f.   Service performed and date completed.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-45)
   C.   Where Protection Required.
      1.   An approved backflow prevention device shall be installed on all
connections to the public water supply as described in the plumbing code, 77
Ill. Admin. Code 890, and the agency’s regulations, 35 Ill. Admin. Code 680. In
addition, an approved backflow prevention device shall be installed on each
service line to a consumer’s water system serving premises where, in the
judgment of the general superintendent, actual or potential hazards to the
public water supply system exist.
      2.   An approved backflow prevention device shall be installed on each
service line to a consumer’s water system serving premises where the following
conditions exist:
         a.   Premises having an auxiliary water supply, unless such auxiliary
supply is accepted as an additional source by the general superintendent, and
the source is approved by the state environmental protection agency;
         b.   Premises on which any substance is handled which can create an
actual or potential hazard to the public water supply system. This shall
include premises having sources or systems containing process fluids or waters
originating from the public water supply system which are no longer under the
sanitary control of the general superintendent;
         c.   Premises having internal cross-connections that, in the judgment
of the general superintendent and/or the cross-connection control device
inspector, are not correctable or have intricate plumbing arrangements which
make it impractical to determine whether or not cross-connections exist;
         d.   Premises where, because of security requirements or other
prohibitions or restrictions, it is impossible or impractical to make a
complete cross-connection survey; and
         e.   Premises having a repeated history of cross-connections being
established or reestablished.
      3.   An approved backflow prevention device shall be installed on all
connections to the public water supply as described in the plumbing code, 77
Ill. Admin. Code 890, and the agency’s regulations, 35 Ill. Admin. Code 653. In
addition, an approved backflow prevention device shall be installed on each
service line to a consumer’s water system serving, but not necessarily limited
to, the following types of facilities, unless the general superintendent
determines that no actual or potential hazard to the public water supply system
exists:
         a.   Hospitals, mortuaries, clinics and nursing homes;
         b.   Laboratories;
         c.   Piers, docks and waterfront facilities;
         d.   Sewage treatment plants, sewage pumping stations or stormwater
pumping stations;
         e.   Food or beverage processing plants;
         f.   Chemical plants;
         g.   Metal plating industries;
         h.   Petroleum processing or storage plants;
         i.   Radioactive material processing plants or nuclear reactors;
         j.   Car washes;
         k.   Pesticide or herbicide or extermination plants and trucks; and
         l.   Farm service and fertilizer plants and trucks.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-46)
   D.   Type Of Protection.
      1.   The type of protection required under subsection C. above shall
depend on the degree of hazard which exists as follows:
         a.   An approved fixed proper air gap separation shall be installed
where the public water supply system may be contaminated with substances that
could cause a severe health hazard;
         b.   An approved fixed proper air gap separation or an approved
reduced pressure principle backflow prevention assembly shall be installed
where the public water supply system may be contaminated with a substance that
could cause a system or health hazard; and
         c.   An approved fixed proper air gap separation or an approved
reduced pressure principle backflow prevention assembly or a double check valve
assembly shall be installed where the public water supply system may be
polluted with substances that could cause a pollution hazard not dangerous to
health.
      2.   The type of protection required under subsection C. above shall be
an approved fixed proper air gap separation or an approved reduced pressure
principle backflow prevention device.
      3.   Where a public water supply or an auxiliary water supply is used for
a fire protection system, reduced pressure principle backflow preventers shall
be installed on fire safety systems connected to the public water supply when:
         a.   The fire safety system contains antifreeze, fire retardant or
other chemicals;
         b.   Water is pumped into the system from another source;
         c.   Water flows by gravity from a nonpotable source;
         d.   Water can be pumped into the fire safety system from any other
source; or
         e.   There is a connection whereby another source can be introduced
into the fire safety system.
      4.   All other fire safety systems connected to the potable water supply
shall be protected by a double check valve assembly on meter service lines and
a double detector check valve assembly on unmetered service lines.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-47)
   E.   Approved Backflow Prevention Devices.
      1.   All backflow prevention devices or methods required by this article
shall be approved by the Research Foundation for Cross-Connection Control of
the University of Southern California, American Water Works Association,
American Society of Sanitary Engineering, or American National Standards
Institute or certified by the National Sanitation Foundation to be in
compliance with applicable industry specifications.
      2.   Installation of approved devices shall be made in accordance with
the manufacturer’s instructions. Maintenance as recommended by the manufacturer
of the device shall be performed. The manufacturer’s maintenance manual shall
be available on site.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-48)
   F.   Inspection And Maintenance.
      1.   It shall be the duty of the consumer at any premises on which
backflow prevention devices required by this article are installed to have
inspections, tests, maintenance and repair made in accordance with the
following schedule or more often where inspections indicate a need or are
specified in manufacturer’s instructions.
         a.   Fixed proper air gap separations shall be inspected to document
that a proper vertical distance is maintained between the discharge point of
the service line and the flood level rim of the receptacle at the time of
installation and at least annually thereafter. Corrections to improper or
bypassed air gaps shall be made within 24 hours.
         b.   Double check valve assemblies shall be inspected and tested
annually thereafter, and required service performed within five days.
         c.   Reduced pressure principle backflow prevention assemblies shall
be tested at the time of installation and at least annually or more frequently
if recommended by the manufacturer, and required service performed within five
days.
      2.   Testing shall be performed by a person who has been approved by the
agency as competent to service the device. Proof of approval shall be in
writing.
      3.   Each device shall have a tag attached listing the date of most
recent test or visual inspection, name of tester and type and date of repairs.
      4.   A maintenance log shall be maintained and include:
         a.   Date of each test or visual inspection;
         b.   Name and approval number of person performing the test or visual
inspection;
         c.   Test results;
         d.   Repairs or servicing required;
         e.   Repairs and date completed; and
         f.   Servicing performed and date completed.
      5.   Whenever backflow prevention devices required by this article are
found to be defective, they shall be repaired or replaced at the expense of the
consumer, without delay, as required by subsection F.1. above.
      6.   Backflow prevention devices shall not be bypassed, made inoperative,
removed or otherwise made ineffective without specific authorization by the
general superintendent.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-49)
§ 8-4B-9 BOOSTER PUMPS.
   A.   Where a booster pump has been installed on the service line to or
within any premises, such pump shall be equipped with a low pressure cutoff
device designed to shut off the booster pump when the pressure in the service
line on the suction side of the pump drops to 20 psi or less.
   B.   It shall be the duty of the water consumer to maintain the low pressure
cutoff device in proper working order and to certify to the general
superintendent at least once a year that the device is operable.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-50)
§ 8-4B-10 VIOLATIONS; PENALTIES.
   A.   Denial Or Discontinuance Of Service. The general superintendent shall
deny or discontinue, after reasonable notice to the occupants thereof, the
water service to any premises wherein any backflow prevention device required
by this article is not installed, tested, maintained and repaired in a manner
acceptable to the general superintendent, or if it is found that the backflow
prevention device has been removed or bypassed, or if an unprotected cross-
connection exists on the premises, or if a low pressure cutoff required by this
article is not installed and maintained in working order.
   B.   Conditions Of Restoration Of Service.
      1.   Water service to such premises shall not be restored until the
consumer has corrected or eliminated such conditions or defects in conformance
with this article and to the satisfaction of the general superintendent, and
the required reconnection fee is paid.
      2.   Water service to such premises shall not be restored until the
consumer has corrected or eliminated the conditions or defects in conformance
with this article and to the satisfaction of the general superintendent.
   C.   Nonliability Of Village. Neither the village, the general
superintendent, nor the assigns thereof shall be liable to any customers of the
village for any injury, damages or lost revenues which may result from
termination of the customer’s water supply in accordance with the terms of this
article, whether or not the termination of the water supply was with or without
notice.
   D.   Contamination Costs To Consumer. The consumer responsible for back
siphoned material or contamination through backflow, if contamination of the
potable water supply system occurs through an illegal cross-connection or an
improperly installed, maintained or repaired device, or a device which has been
bypassed, must bear the cost of cleanup of the potable water supply system.
   E.   Liability For Damages. Any person violating any of the provisions of
this article, in addition to the fine provided, shall become liable to the
village for any expense, loss or damage occasioned by the village by reason of
such violation, whether the same was caused before or after notice.
   F.   Notice Of Violation. Any person found to be violating any provision of
this article shall be served with written notice stating the violation and
providing a reasonable time limit for the satisfactory correction thereof. The
offender shall, within the period of time stated in such notice, permanently
cease all violation.
(1987 Code, § 38-4-51)
   G.   Penalty. Any person convicted of a violation of any section or
provision of this article shall be fined and sentenced in accordance with the
applicable general penalty provisions set forth in §
1-4-1 .
(Ord. 698, passed 6-14-2004)
CHAPTER 5: GARBAGE AND REFUSE COLLECTION AND DISPOSAL
Section
   
8-5-1   Businesses
   
8-5-2   Recyclables At Roll-Off Box Sites
§ 8-5-1 BUSINESSES.
   A.   Receptacles.
      1.   Required. The occupant of every building, structure or premises used
or maintained in connection with any business or occupation shall provide and
maintain in good condition and repair a sufficient number of refuse containers
for the temporary storage of all refuse accumulating between collections.
      2.   Specifications. The standard refuse container required by this
section shall be a receptacle of not less than 20 gallons’ nor more than 32
gallons’ capacity, of impervious material and sturdy construction, with a
tightfitting cover, and equipped with handles properly placed to facilitate
handling; provided, however, dumpsters are permitted if emptied once each week.
   B.   Removal Of Refuse And Garbage.
      1.   It shall be the duty of the occupant of every building, structure or
premises used or maintained in connection with any business or occupation to
cause to be removed, at his or her own cost and expense, at least once each
week, all refuse produced therein.
      2.   Every person owning or controlling any hotel, restaurant, café or
retail food establishment where more than 32 gallons of refuse are normally
produced weekly shall cause all garbage to be placed in sanitary refuse
containers and shall cause all substances deposited in such containers to be
removed daily from his or her premises and to be disposed of at his or her own
expense; provided, garbage may be deposited in dumpsters rather than removed
daily from the premises, provided the dumpsters are emptied once each week.
(1987 Code, § 7-1-2)
§ 8-5-2 RECYCLABLES AT ROLL-OFF BOX SITES.
   A.   Definitions. For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions
apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      ILLEGAL DUMP or ILLEGAL DUMPING. The replacement of any solid waste
(which is not an approved recyclable) in or around the site of the roll-off
container provided by the county for recycling activities or any recycling
container approved by the village.
      RECYCLABLE(S). Any of the following: PETE, HDPE, aluminum, bimetal cans,
tin/steel cans, newsprint, corrugated cardboard, clear glass and brown glass or
any other recyclable materials approved by the village.
      ROLL-OFF CONTAINER. The container provided by the county solid waste
management department for the collection of recyclables and the site upon which
it is placed in the village.
      SCAVENGE or SCAVENGING. To collect, obtain, possess or pick up any
recyclable item from within the roll-off container(s) or from the site of the
roll-off container(s).
      SOLID WASTE. All materials other than the “recyclables” defined in this
subsection.
(1987 Code, § 27-8-1) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   B.   Accepted Recyclables And Signs Posted. Recyclables which are accepted
for deposit shall be clearly marked on or near the roll-off container, and “no
dumping” signs shall also be posted.
(1987 Code, § 27-8-2)
   C.   Hours Of Operation. The hours of operation of roll-off box sites shall
be as determined by the village corporate authorities from time to time.
(1987 Code, § 27-8-3) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   D.   Prohibited Acts.
      1.   Scavenging. It shall be a violation for any person or persons to
scavenge any recyclable or recyclables from the roll-off containers or the site
of the roll-off containers.
      2.   Illegal Dumping. It shall be a violation for any person or persons
to illegally dump in the roll-off container or on the site of the roll-off
container.
(1987 Code, § 27-8-2)
   E.   Penalty. Any person violating any provision of this section shall be
fined as provided in §
1-4-1  for each offense, and a separate offense shall be deemed committed on
each day during or on which a violation occurs or continues.
(1987 Code, § 27-8-4) (Amd. 2008 Code)

9-1-1   Codes Adopted

§ 9-1-1 CODES ADOPTED.
   The following current codes are hereby adopted by reference thereto and the
same shall be the rules and regulations governing the construction,
reconstruction, alteration, installation, demolition, equipment use, occupancy,
location and maintenance of buildings and structures of any kind, nature or
extent whatsoever and also governing the issuance of building permits and
certificates of occupancy:
   A.   International building code (IBC) including appendix G (flood resistant
construction), 2024 edition;
   B.   International existing building code (IEBC), 2024 edition;
   C.   International residential code (IRC), 2024 edition;
   D.   State energy conservation code (IECC), 2024 edition;
   E.   2018 state accessibility code;
   F.   State 2014 plumbing code;
   G.   Fire prevention and safety rules (includes NFPA 101);
   H.   International mechanical code (IMC), 2024 edition;
   I.   NFPA 101 life safety code (NFPA 101), 2024 edition;
   J.   International property maintenance code (IPMC), 2024 edition;
   K.   National electrical code (NEC), 2024 edition.
(Ord. 910, passed 11-12-2024)
§ 9-1-2 COPIES ON FILE.
   One copy of each of the aforesaid codes has been on file with the village
clerk’s office for a period of at least 30 days prior to the effective date of
this codified ordinance; and one copy of each of the aforesaid codes shall
remain on file in the village clerk’s office and shall be made available for
public use, inspection and examination.
(Ord. 910, passed 11-12-2024)
§ 9-1-3 PENALTY.
   Any person who shall violate any provision of any of the aforesaid code or
codes set forth in §
9-2-1 or who shall fail to comply with any of the requirements thereof, or who
shall erect, construct, reconstruct, install, alter, add to, move, demolish,
use, occupy, locate or maintain any building or structure of any kind, nature
or extent whatsoever, or portion thereof, in violation of the any of the
aforesaid code or codes or in violation of a detailed statement or plan
submitted and approved thereunder, or of a permit or certificate issued
thereunder shall, upon conviction of each violation, be fined in an amount not
less than $250, nor more than $750 for each offense; and a separate offense
shall be deemed committed as to each violation for each and every day or
portion thereof during which any provision of the aforesaid code or codes is
committed or continues. For purposes hereof, the owner of a building or
structure, or portion thereof, or of the premises where anything in violation
of any of the aforesaid code or codes shall be placed or shall exist and an
architect, engineer, builder, contractor, agent, person, firm or corporation
employed or retained in connection therewith and who assisted in the commission
of such violation or violations shall also be deemed likewise guilty of such
offense or offenses.
(Ord. 910, passed 11-12-2024)
CHAPTER 2: DANGEROUS AND UNSAFE, UNCOMPLETED
AND ABANDONED BUILDINGS
Section
   
9-2-1   Definition
   
9-2-2   Nuisance Declared
   
9-2-3   Prohibition
   
9-2-4   Alternative Action
   
9-2-5   Enforcement And Injunction
§ 9-2-1 DEFINITION.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definition applies unless the
context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   DANGEROUS AND UNSAFE BUILDING.
      1.   Any building, shed, fence or other human-made structure which is
dangerous and unsafe to the public health because of its construction or
condition, or which may cause or aid in the spread of disease or cause injury
to the health of the occupants thereof or of neighboring structures.
(1987 Code, § 6-1-1) (Amd. 2008 Code)
      2.   Any building, shed, fence or other human-made structure which,
because of faulty construction, age, lack of proper repair or any other cause,
is especially liable to fire, and constitutes or creates a fire hazard.
      3.   Any building, shed, fence or other human-made structure which, by
reason of faulty construction, age, lack of proper repair or other cause, is
liable to cause injury or damage by collapsing or by a collapse or fall of any
part of such structure.
      4.   Any building, shed, fence or other human-made structure which,
because of its condition or because of lack of doors or windows, is available
to and frequented by malefactors or disorderly persons who are not lawful
occupants of such structure.
(1987 Code, § 6-1-1)
      5.   Any building, shed, fence or other human-made structure containing
garbage, debris or other hazardous, noxious or unhealthy substances or
materials.
(2008 Code)
§ 9-2-2 NUISANCE DECLARED.
   Any dangerous and unsafe building or uncompleted and abandoned building
within the village is hereby declared to be a nuisance.
(1987 Code, § 6-1-1) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 9-2-3 PROHIBITION.
   It shall be unlawful to maintain or permit the existence of any dangerous
and unsafe building or any uncompleted and abandoned building in the village,
and it shall be unlawful for the owner, occupant or person in custody of any
dangerous and unsafe building or any uncompleted and abandoned building to
permit the same to remain in a dangerous and unsafe condition or in an
uncompleted and abandoned condition, or to occupy such building or permit it to
be occupied while it is or remains in such condition.
(1987 Code, § 6-1-2) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 9-2-4 ALTERNATIVE ACTION.
   In addition to the actions authorized by other sections of this chapter, the
chief of the fire protection district, if any, or any other municipal official
whose duty it is to investigate fires, may make the investigation authorized by
425 ILCS 25/9, 25/9a, 25/9b and 25/9e. If such officer shall find that any
building or structure is so occupied or situated as to endanger persons or
property, or by reason of faulty construction, age, lack of repair, or for any
other cause, is especially liable to fire, or is liable to cause injury by
collapsing or otherwise, he or she shall order the dangerous condition removed
or remedied, and shall so notify the owner or occupant of the premises. Service
of such notice may be in person or by registered mail, and any person so
notified may appeal from the decision of such officer in the manner provided by
law.
(1987 Code, § 6-1-5) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 9-2-5 ENFORCEMENT AND INJUNCTION.
   A.   65 ILCS 5/11-31-1, as passed, approved and amended by the state general
assembly, entitled “demolition, repair, enclosure or remediation,” shall
prevail in the enforcement of this chapter, and the village shall be entitled
to exercise powers, rights and liens, and to otherwise proceed under such
statute.
   B.   The village board may proceed under the provisions of 65 ILCS 5/11-31-
2, which authorizes an injunction to require compliance with building,
structure, fire, health and safety standards.
(1987 Code, § 6-1-9) (Amd. 2008 Code)
CHAPTER 3: MOBILE HOMES AND MOBILE HOME PARKS
Section
   
9-3-1   Definitions
   
9-3-2   State Regulations Adopted
