10-1-10   Exceptions And Modifications
§ 10-1-1 TITLE.
   This comprehensive zoning title shall be known as the “Zoning Code of the
Village of Edinburg, Illinois.”
(1987 Code, § 40-1-1)
§ 10-1-2 PURPOSE.
   The zoning regulations and districts as herein set forth have been made with
the following objectives:
   A.   To the end that adequate light, pure air and safety from fire and other
dangers may be secured;
   B.   The taxable value of land and buildings throughout the village may be
conserved;
   C.   The congestion in the public streets may be lessened or avoided;
   D.   The hazards to persons and damage to property resulting from the
accumulations or runoff of stormwaters or floodwaters may be lessened or
avoided;
   E.   The public health, safety, comfort, morals and welfare may otherwise be
promoted; and
   F.   To ensure and facilitate the preservation of sites, areas and
structures of historical, architectural and aesthetic importance.
(1987 Code, § 40-1-2)
§ 10-1-3 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions apply unless the
context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   ACCESSORY USE. Any structure or use that is:
      1.   Subordinate in size or purpose to the principal structure or use
which it serves;
      2.   Necessary or contributing to the comfort and convenience of the
occupants or the principal structure or use served; and
      3.   Located on the same lot as the principal structure or use served.
   AGRICULTURAL USES, BONA FIDE. The growing of crops in the open, raising of
stock and poultry, forestry, mushroom growing, flower gardening, operation of
apiaries and aviaries, nurseries, orchards, fur farms, roadside stands, signs
and billboards relating to the sale or use of products produced thereon, and
necessary structures and farm dwellings of such uses.
   ALLEY. Any public or private way dedicated to public travel and less than 20
feet in width.
   BUILDING. Any structure constructed or used for residence, business,
industry or other public or private purposes, or accessory thereto, and
including tents, lunch wagons, dining cars, trailers, billboards, signs and
similar structures, whether stationary or movable.
   BUILDING HEIGHT. The vertical distance measured from the average grade at
the front wall of a building to the highest point of the roof, or to the mean
height level between eaves and ridge for gable, hip or gambrel roofs. Chimneys,
towers, cooling towers and similar projections (other than signs) shall not be
included in calculating BUILDING HEIGHT.
   DWELLING. A permanent building resting on and fully supported by a permanent
foundation, used primarily for human habitation, but not including facilities
for the housing of transient residents, nor to include mobile homes,
manufactured homes or modular homes.
      1.   DWELLING, MULTIPLE-FAMILY. A permanent building resting on and fully
supported by a permanent foundation, providing separate human living
accommodations for three or more families.
      2.   DWELLING, SINGLE-FAMILY. A permanent building, separate and
freestanding in itself, resting on and fully supported by a permanent
foundation, providing human living accommodations for one family.
      3.   DWELLING, TWO-FAMILY. A permanent building resting on and fully
supported by a permanent foundation, designed exclusively for human living
accommodations for two families.
   HEIGHT. The vertical measurement from the ground to the vertical apex of the
measured wireless telecommunication tower.
   HOME OCCUPATION. Any business, profession or occupation conducted for gain
or support entirely within any dwelling or on any residential premises.
   JUNKYARD. A tract of land, including any accessory structures thereon, that
is used for buying, selling, exchanging, storing, bailing, packing,
disassembling or handling waste or scrap materials. Such scrap materials
include vehicles, machinery and equipment not in operable condition (or parts
thereof), and metals, glass, paper, plastics, rags and rubber tires. A lot on
which any inoperable vehicles are stored shall not be deemed a JUNKYARD. A
JUNKYARD includes an automobile wrecking yard and upon which other salvage or
wrecking operations are conducted.
   LOT. A piece, parcel or plot of land occupied or to be occupied by one
principal building and its accessory buildings and including the open spaces
required under this title.
   MOBILE HOME OR MANUFACTURED HOME.
      1.   A structure designed for permanent habitation and so constructed as
to permit its transport on wheels, temporarily or permanently attached to its
frame, from the place of its construction to the location or subsequent
locations at which it is intended to be a permanent habitation and designed to
permit the occupancy thereof as a dwelling place for one or more persons, and
shall also include an IMMOBILIZED MOBILE HOME, as defined under §
9-3-1 .
      2.   A MOBILE HOME OR MANUFACTURED HOME shall not include a camping
trailer or recreational vehicle.
      3.   The term MOBILE HOME OR MANUFACTURED HOME shall also include
manufactured homes constructed after June 30, 1976, in accordance with the
Federal National Manufactured Home Construction and Safety Standards Act of
1974, being 42 U.S.C. §§ 5401 et seq., and any amendments or replacements
thereto.
   MODULAR HOME. A prefabricated, precut dwelling which rests on and is fully
supported by a permanent foundation and has at least 800 square feet of living
space, but not including mobile homes or manufactured homes.
   NONCONFORMING USES. A use of a building or land, lawful at the effective
date hereof, that does not conform with the permitted use provisions of this
title.
   PERMANENT FOUNDATION. A PERMANENT FOUNDATION consisting of concrete,
mortared concrete block or mortared brick extending into the ground below the
frost line (i.e., at least 36 inches) and constructed, installed and maintained
around the exterior perimeter of a building or structure or mobile home or
manufactured home.
   PERSON. Any individual, firm, corporation, partnership, association or other
entity.
   PUBLIC UTILITY. Any government-regulated company that provides essential
public services such as water, gas or electricity. However, a pubic utility
shall not include any company or entity that provides telephone, cell phone,
television, cable television, digital television, satellite television or
internet services.
   SHALL. The term herein is mandatory and not directory.
   STREET. Any public or private way dedicated to public travel 20 feet or more
in width. The word STREET shall include the words ROAD, HIGHWAY and
THOROUGHFARE.
   TELECOMMUNICATIONS. The technology which enables information to be exchanged
through the transmission or receiving of voice, video or data signals by means
of electrical or electromagnetic systems or radio waves.
   USE or USED. The purpose or activity for which land or a structure thereon
is designed, arranged, intended, occupied or maintained. The word shall be
deemed to include designed, intended or arranged to be used.
   WIRELESS TELECOMMUNICATION TOWER. A device or structure affixed to the
ground, a building or other structure, which device or structure is capable of
or intended to support equipment used to transmit and/or receive wireless
telecommunications signals of any kind, nature or extent whatsoever, including,
but not limited to, cable services through a cable system, or telecommunication
services provided through the internet, mobile service providers, devices or
remote stations or devices, or through data-driven services.
   YARD. An open space on the same lot with a principal building, open,
unoccupied and unobstructed by buildings except as otherwise provided in this
title.
      1.   YARD, FRONT. The yard extending across the entire width of the lot
between the principal building and the right-of-way line or street line which
the building faces.
      2.   YARD, REAR. The yard extending across the entire width of the lot
between the rear lot line and the nearest part of the principal building.
      3.   YARD, SIDE. The yard extending along the side lot line from the
front yard to the rear yard and lying between the side lot line and the nearest
part of the principal building.
(1987 Code, § 40-1-3) (Amd. 2008 Code) (Ord. 829, passed 4-18-2016)
§ 10-1-4 COMPLIANCE, APPLICATION AND INTERPRETATION.
   A.   Compliance With Provisions. No building or land shall hereafter be used
and no building or part thereof shall be erected, moved or altered unless for a
use expressly permitted by and in conformity with the regulations for the
district in which it is located.
(1987 Code, § 40-2-3)
   B.   Minimum Requirements. In the interpretation and application of this
title, the provisions contained herein shall be held to be minimum
requirements, adopted for the promotion of public health, morals, safety and
general welfare.
   C.   Conflicting Provisions.
      1.   Any existing village code or part thereof, to the extent it
duplicates, conflicts with or otherwise affects the whole or any part of this
title, shall be disregarded.
      2.   In the case of any conflict between this title or part thereof, and
the whole or part of any existing or future private covenants or deeds, the
most restrictive shall apply.
(1987 Code, § 40-12-1)
   D.   Validity. If any section, clause, provision or portion of this title
shall be held to be invalid or unconstitutional by any court of competent
jurisdiction, such holding shall not affect or otherwise impair any other
section, clause, provision or portion of this title which is not in or of
itself invalid or unconstitutional.
(1987 Code, § 40-12-2)
   E.   Agricultural Uses. Notwithstanding subsection A. above, none of the
regulations of this title, with the exception of the front yard requirements,
shall be applicable to bona fide agricultural uses as defined in §
10-1-3  of this chapter. This shall not be construed, however, as to eliminate
the necessity of bona fide agricultural uses from applying for and obtaining
the necessary building permit prior to construction, alteration or moving of
buildings. No fee, however, shall be required for a building permit for such
agricultural uses.
(1987 Code, § 40-2-3)
§ 10-1-5 NONCONFORMING USES.
   Any lawful use of land or structure existing at the effective date hereof,
or subsequent amendment of this title, may be continued with the following
limitations.
   A.   Expansion; Substantial Changes. Any structure containing a
nonconforming use may not be expanded nor substantially remodeled. The board of
appeals may approve any remodeling and incidental repairs which do not tend to
prolong the life of the nonconforming use.
(1987 Code, § 40-2-4)
   B.   Damage To Structure. Any structure containing a nonconforming use which
has been damaged to the extent of 50% of its current fair market value, as
estimated immediately prior to damage, shall not be repaired or reconstructed
except in conformity with this title.
(1987 Code, § 40-2-4) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   C.   Discontinuance Of Use. No nonconforming use shall be reestablished
after having been discontinued for 12 months. Vacating of premises or building
of nonoperative status shall be evidence of discontinued use.
   D.   Substitution Of Another Nonconforming Use. No nonconforming use may be
substituted for any another nonconforming use of any conforming use.
   E.   Mobile Homes And Immobilized Mobile Homes.
      1.   Notwithstanding §
10-5-2A, regarding mobile and manufactured homes, or any other provisions
contained in this title, any existing nonconforming mobile home or manufactured
home located, as of August 1, 2000, on any tract, lot, plot of real estate or
space outside of a MOBILE HOME PARK (as defined in §
9-3-1 ) shall be entitled to remain on said tract, lot, plot of real estate or
space until the first to occur of the following events:
         a.   Said mobile home or immobilized mobile home is removed from said
lot or space for any reason;
         b.   Said mobile home or immobilized mobile home is thereafter damaged
to the extent of 50% or more of its fair market value (said fair market value
as determined immediately prior to any such damage); or
         c.   Said mobile home or immobilized mobile home is hereafter sold or
conveyed by the owner(s) thereof for a total sales price of less than $10,000,
exclusive of the lot or space upon which it is situated. For purposes hereof, a
SALE includes, but is not limited to, a sale made through a written or oral
installment contract, contract for deed, cash sale/purchase agreement, lease/
purchase agreement or any other written or oral contract or agreement.
(1987 Code, § 40-2-4)
      2.   Upon the first of the foregoing events (subsection E.1. above), the
owner or owners of said mobile home or manufactured home shall immediately
remove said mobile home or manufactured home from said lot, plot of real estate
or space; and no mobile home or manufactured home shall ever thereafter be
placed or maintained on said tract, lot, plot of real estate or space unless
said lot or space becomes part of a MOBILE HOME PARK, as defined in §
9-3-1  of this code.
(1987 Code, § 40-2-4) (Amd. 2008 Code)
      3.   a.   In order to aid the enforcement of this subsection E.:
            (1)   Following a seven-day written notice to the owner(s) or
occupants of said mobile home or manufactured home, the village president and
board of trustees, or their representative(s), shall have the right, at any
reasonable time and from time to time, to go upon and within said mobile home
or manufactured home and the tract, lot, plot of real estate or said space upon
which it is located and appraise said mobile home or manufactured home and/or
said tract, lot, plot of real estate or space; and/or
            (2)   Within seven days of a written request of the village
president, the owner(s) of said mobile home or manufactured home shall provide
and produce to the village president and board of trustees all information,
records and documents evidencing any sale or conveyance of and/or the fair
market value of said mobile home or manufactured home.
         b.   If said owner(s) and/or occupant(s) refuses to allow said
appraisal or timely refuse to provide said information and/or produce said
records or documents, then said mobile home or manufactured home shall be
removed from said tract, lot, plot of real estate or space within 30 days
following a written notice to the then owner(s) and/or occupant(s) to do so.
(1987 Code, § 40-2-4)
      4.   For purposes of this subsection, any existing mobile home or
manufactured home now located within the village and outside of said mobile
home park shall only include the existing mobile homes or manufactured homes as
are listed at the respective locations on exhibit A attached to ordinance 654
and incorporated herein by reference and as further shown in the photos on
exhibit B attached to ordinance 654 and now on file in the village clerk’s
office.
(1987 Code, § 40-2-4) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 10-1-6 OFF-STREET PARKING.
   A.   Gravel, concrete or asphalt off-street automobile parking shall be
provided on any lot on which any of the following uses are hereafter
established. Such space shall be provided with vehicular access to a street or
alley. For purposes of computing gross off-street parking area required, the
ratio of 250 square feet per parking space shall be used with a minimum width
of 12 feet. The following are minimum requirements for specific uses:
Type Of Use Parking Space Required
Type Of Use                           Parking Space Required
Boarding and rooming houses           1 for each 2 rooms occupied by boarders
                                      or roomers
Dwellings                             1 for each dwelling unit
Hospitals and other institutions for  1 for each 4 beds, plus 1 for each staff
care and treatment                    and visiting doctor, plus 1 for each 3
                                      employees
Office uses                           1 for each 300 square feet of gross floor
                                      area
Retail establishments                 1 for each 400 square feet of gross floor
                                      area
Theater, auditorium, including school 1 for each 8 seats available at maximum
auditorium, church or other place of  capacity
public assembly
Tourist accommodations                1 for each room offered for tourist
                                      accommodations
Wholesale, storage, and manufacturing 1 for each 5 employees
establishments
 
(1987 Code, § 40-2-5) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   B.   If off-street parking space for nonresidential uses, as required in
subsection A. above, cannot be provided on the same lot on which the principal
use is conducted, the zoning enforcing officer may permit such space to be
provided on other off-street property, provided such space is within 400 feet
of the main entrance to such principal use. Such off-street parking space shall
thereafter be deemed to be required open space associated with the permitted
use and shall not be reduced nor encroached upon In any manner.
(1987 Code, § 40-2-5)
§ 10-1-7 OFF-STREET LOADING.
   Every building or structure used for other than residential uses and
constructed after the effective date hereof shall provide space on the property
to be used exclusively for loading and unloading of vehicles. Such space shall
be in accordance with the following schedule:
Building Gross Floor Area  Required Spaces
(Square Feet)
Building Gross Floor Area  Required Spaces
(Square Feet)
0 to 8,000                 1
8,001 to 25,000            2
25,001 to 40,000           3
40,001 to 100,000          4
100,001 to 250,000         5
250,001 to 400,000         6
Buildings over 400,000     6, plus additional space for each additional 200,000
                           square feet
 
(1987 Code, § 40-2-6) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 10-1-8 VISION AT INTERSECTIONS.
   On any corner lot in all districts, except the C-1 general retail district,
there shall be no obstruction to traffic visibility within 35 feet of the
intersection of the two street property lines of the corner lot.
(1987 Code, § 40-2-7)
§ 10-1-9 PRIVATE SWIMMING POOLS, SPAS AND HOT TUBS.
   A.   Requirements. Subject to obtaining the requisite building permit in
accordance with subsection B. below, private swimming pools and/or spas and/or
hot tubs appurtenant to a residential dwelling on the same lot shall be allowed
as permitted uses in R-1 residential districts, provided:
      1.   Such swimming pool, spa or hot tub is constructed, maintained,
operated and used in accordance with all applicable laws, statutes, rules,
regulations and requirements as are now or as may be hereafter enacted or
amended by the state or any of its agencies or subdivision;
      2.   Such swimming pool, spa or hot tub is constructed, maintained,
operated and used in accordance with all applicable provisions of the village
code as now or as may be hereafter enacted or amended, including, but not
limited to, §
9-1-1 that adopted the International Building Code (IBC), 2024 edition, which
incorporates in § 3109 thereof, the International Swimming Pool and Spa Code;
      3.   Notwithstanding anything in this section to the contrary, each
swimming pool, spa or hot tub shall be surrounded by a safety protective
barrier and at least one access gate restricting entry into a swimming pool,
spa or hot tub that complies with the International Building Code (IBC), 2024
edition, §§ 305.2 through 305.7, unless:
         a.   Such spa or hot tub is equipped with a functioning lockable
safety cover that complies with ASTM F1346; or
         b.   Such swimming pool is equipped with a functioning powered safety
cover that complies with ASTM F1346.
      4.   At all times that a swimming pool, spa or hot tub is not in use,
each aforesaid access gate shall be closed and locked;
      5.   At all times that a spa or hot tub is not in use the aforesaid
functioning lockable safety cover must be on such spa or hot tub and securely
locked;
      6.   At all times that a swimming pool is not in use, the aforesaid
functioning powered safety cover shall be powered shut and closed covering the
entire swimming pool; and
      7.   For purposes of this section, a SWIMMING POOL, SPA or HOT TUB shall
be considered “not in use,” when the owner or adult occupant of the residence,
or a responsible adult authorized by such owner or adult occupant, is not in
the swimming pool, spa or hot tub or is not outside the residence within 20
feet of an edge of the swimming pool, spa or hot tub.
   B.   Applicable To Existing And Future Swimming Pools, Spas And Hot Tubs.
      1.   Notwithstanding the issuance of any prior building permits for the
construction of any swimming pool, spa or hot tub, the provisions of subsection
A. above shall apply to all owners or tenants of swimming pools, spas or hot
tubs from and after July 11, 2020.
      2.   It shall be unlawful for any owner or tenant of any swimming pool,
spa or hot tub to construct, maintain, use or operate such swimming pool, spa
or hot tub unless such swimming pool, spa or hot tub meets and complies with
all applicable requirements for the construction, maintenance, operation and
use thereof as set forth in the preceding subsections A.1. through A.7. above.
   C.   Permit Requirements For Swimming Pools, Spas And Hot Tubs.
      1.   Building Permit Required. It shall be unlawful to commence the
excavation for, or the construction of, or the placement of, or the alteration
of any swimming pool, spa or hot tub until a building permit is first obtained
from the zoning enforcing officer for such work. Such building permit shall be
obtained in accordance with the procedures and provisions as are set forth in §
10-2-2 . For purposes of this subsection C., the terms BUILDING or BUILDINGS as
set forth in §
10-2-2  shall be deemed to include such swimming pools, spas and hot tubs.
      2.   Occupancy Permit. It shall be unlawful for any owner or tenant of
any residential dwelling to maintain, use or operate any swimming pool, spa or
hot tub unless such swimming pool, spa or hot tub meets all requirements as set
forth in subsection A. above and such owner obtains an occupancy permit from
the zoning enforcing officer.
      3.   Building And Occupancy Permit Fee. To partially defray the costs of
administering this building permit and occupancy permit procedure, a fee of $10
shall be paid by the applicant to the village treasurer at the time the
application for a building permit is submitted.
   D.   Penalty. Any person who violates any provision of this section shall be
subject to and fined as provided in §
1-4-1  for each offense. Each day a violation is permitted to exist shall
constitute a separate offense.
(Ord. 872, passed 7-13-2020)
§ 10-1-10 EXCEPTIONS AND MODIFICATIONS.
   A.   Existing Lot Of Record. When a lot which is an official lot of record
at the effective date hereof does not comply with the area, yard or other
requirements of this title, an application shall be submitted to the zoning
board of appeals for a variance from the terms of this title in accordance with
the procedure outlined in chapter 2, article A of this title. Such a lot may be
used as a building site; provided, however, that the yard and other
requirements of the district in which the variance is located are complied with
as closely as possible in the opinion of the zoning board of appeals.
(1987 Code, § 40-7-1) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   B.   Projections Into Yards.
      1.   Structures Overhanging Sidewalks. Signs, awnings, canopies and
marquees are permitted to overhang the sidewalk in the C-1 general retail
district only; provided, that overhanging signs are a minimum of ten feet above
the sidewalk at any point and that all other structures are a minimum of ten
feet above the sidewalk at any point.
(1987 Code, § 40-7-3) (Amd. 2008 Code)
      2.   Accessory Buildings. One story accessory buildings may project into
only rear yards abutting on an alley, provided such projection extends not
closer than five feet to the rear lot line.
(1987 Code, § 40-7-3)
      3.   Fences And Vegetation. Fences, trees, shrubs and other plantings may
be constructed in or project into yards; provided, that:
(1987 Code, § 40-7-3) (Amd. 2008 Code)
         a.   No fences, trees, shrubs or other plantings more than three feet
high or 30% solid shall be located within 30 feet of a street intersection;
         b.   No fence more than four feet high shall be located closer to the
front of the lot than the principal building; and
         c.   No fence more than six feet high shall be allowed on any other
part of the lot except as may be allowed in §
5-4-4-5 of this code.
(1987 Code, § 40-7-2)
   C.   Height Limits. The height limitations of this title 10 shall not apply
to church spires, belfries, cupolas and domes not intended for human occupancy;
monuments; water towers; chimneys; smokestacks; derricks; conveyors; or flag
poles.
(Ord. 829, passed 4-18-2016)
CHAPTER 2: ADMINISTRATION AND ENFORCEMENT
Section
   
10-2-1   Zoning Enforcing Officer
   
10-2-2   Building Permit
   
10-2-3   Certificate Of Occupancy
   
10-2-4   Amendments
   
10-2-5   Remedies And Penalties
§ 10-2-1 ZONING ENFORCING OFFICER.
   A.   Appointment; Duties. This title shall be administered and enforced by
the zoning enforcing officer who shall be appointed by the mayor, with the
advice and consent of the village board of trustees. (1987 Code, § 40-9-1)
   B.   Salary. The salary for the zoning enforcing officer shall be set by the
village board of trustees.
(1987 Code, § 40-9-3)
§ 10-2-2 BUILDING PERMIT.
   A.   Permit Required. It shall be unlawful to commence the excavation for or
the construction of any building, including accessory buildings; or to commence
the moving or alteration of any building, including accessory buildings; or to
commence the excavation for or the construction of any improvement to any
property, including, but without limitation, patios, decks, swimming pools
(aboveground or belowground) and driveways, until the zoning enforcing officer
has issued a building permit for such work.
(1987 Code, § 40-9-4)
   B.   Application For Permit. In applying to the zoning enforcing officer for
a building permit, the applicant shall submit a dimensioned sketch or scale
plan indicating the shape, size, height and location of all buildings to be
erected, altered or moved and of any building already on the lot. The applicant
shall also state the existing and intended use of all such buildings and supply
such other information as may be required by the zoning enforcing officer for
determining whether the provisions of this title are being observed.
(1987 Code, § 40-9-5)
   C.   Issuance Or Denial Of Permit.
      1.   If the proposed excavation or construction, as set forth in the
application, is in conformity with the provisions of this title and other
applicable regulations of the village, the zoning enforcing officer shall issue
a building permit for such excavation or construction. Under no circumstances
shall a building permit be issued by the enforcing officer if it does not
completely conform to the provisions provided in this title. All waivers,
special uses and all other variances must be approved by the zoning board of
appeals.
(1987 Code, §§ 40-9-2 and 40-9-5)
      2.   If a building permit is refused, the zoning enforcing officer shall
state such a refusal in writing, with the cause, and shall immediately forward
such notice of refusal to the applicant.
      3.   The zoning enforcing officer shall grant or deny the permit within
ten days from the date the application is submitted.
   D.   Building Permit For Mobile Homes, Manufactured Homes, Immobilized
Mobile Homes and Modular Homes. The issuance of a building permit for any
mobile home, manufactured home, immobilized mobile home or modular home shall
follow the same procedure as set forth above. However, also required shall be
proof of compliance with §
10-5-2 A. or with §
9-3-4 , as the case may be.
   E.   Restrictions On Permits.
      1.   The issuance of a building permit shall in no case be construed as
waiving any provisions of this title or any other applicable regulations. All
work for which a building permit is issued shall be performed in accordance
with the provisions of this title and any other applicable regulations.
(1987 Code, § 40-9-5)
      2.   No building or improvement shall be constructed over any gas line,
gas main or other part of the village natural gas transmission and distribution
system.
(1987 Code, § 40-9-4)
   F.   Term Of Permit. A building permit shall become void 90 days after
issuance unless substantial progress has been made by that date on the project
described therein.
   G.   Fees. To partially defray the expenses of administering this title, a
fee shall be collected by the zoning enforcing officer in accordance with the
following schedule:
      1.   Residential Uses.
 
Number Of Rooms Building Permit Fee Charge Per Room
2 or less       $8.50
3               $6.00
4               $4.75
5               $4.00
6 or more       $3.50
Notes To Table:
Kitchen, bath, separate room or garage alteration is each counted as a
separate room.
 
      2.   Commercial And Industrial Uses. Building permits for commercial and
industrial uses shall be a minimum of $5, plus an additional $0.50 for each
$500 up to $20,000, plus an additional $0.50 for each $1,000 improvement beyond
$20,000.
   H.   Posting Permit. All building permits shall be visibly posted on the
property where the work is being performed.
(1987 Code, § 40-9-5) (Ord. 850, passed 3-12-2018)
Cross-reference:
   Construction Over Mains, see §
8-2-13
§ 10-2-3 CERTIFICATE OF OCCUPANCY.
   A.   Certificate Of Occupancy Required. No land, building, structure, mobile
home, manufactured home, immobilized mobile home, or modular home or part
thereof that is hereafter located, erected or constructed, or altered in its
use or structure, shall be occupied or used until the zoning enforcing officer
shall have issued a certificate of occupancy indicating that such land,
building, structure, mobile home, manufactured home, immobilized mobile home,
or modular home or part thereof, and the proposed use thereof, are found to be
in conformity with the provisions of this title.
   B.   Inspections; Issuance Or Denial Of Certificate. Within three days after
notification that a building or premises or part thereof is ready for occupancy
or use, it shall be the duty of the zoning enforcing officer to make a final
inspection thereof and to issue a certificate of occupancy if the land,
building or part thereof and the proposed use thereof are found to conform with
the provisions of this title; or if such certificate is refused, to state such
refusal in writing, with the cause, and immediately forward such notice to the
applicant.
(1987 Code, § 40-9-6) (Ord. 850, passed 3-12-2018)
§ 10-2-4 AMENDMENTS.
   A.   Certificate Required. No land, building or part thereof hereafter
erected or altered in its use or structure shall be used until the zoning
enforcing officer shall have issued a certificate of occupancy indicating that
such land, building or part thereof, and the proposed use thereof, are found to
be in conformity with the provisions of this title.
   B.   Inspections; Issuance Or Denial Of Certificate. Within three days after
notification that a building or premises or part thereof is ready for occupancy
or use, it shall be the duty of the zoning enforcing officer to make a final
inspection thereof and to issue a certificate of occupancy if the land,
building or part thereof and the proposed use thereof are found to conform with
the provisions of this title; or if such certificate is refused, to state such
refusal in writing, with the cause, and immediately forward such notice to the
applicant.
(1987 Code, § 40-9-6)
§ 10-2-5 REMEDIES AND PENALTIES.
   A.   In case any land, building, structure, mobile home, manufactured home,
immobilized mobile home, or modular home or part of any thereof is located,
erected, constructed, reconstructed, altered, repaired, converted or
maintained, or used or occupied in violation of this title, the zoning
enforcement officer or the proper authorities of the zoning board of appeals or
the village board of trustees, in addition to and not in lieu of other
available remedies, may institute any appropriate court action or proceedings
to prevent the use or occupancy of such building, structure mobile home,
manufactured home, immobilized mobile home, or modular home or land, or part of
any thereof.
   B.   In addition to and not in lieu of any available remedies, the owner(s)
and/or occupant(s) of any mobile home, manufactured home, immobilized mobile
home that is located or maintained or used or occupied in violation of any one
or more provisions of this title shall remove such mobile home, manufactured
home, or immobilized mobile home from the corporate limits of the village
within 30 consecutive calendar days of the receipt of a written order to cause
such removal that is issued by the zoning enforcing officer. Such owner(s) and/
or occupant(s) shall each be subject to a fine of $250 for each day after said
30 days until such mobile home, manufactured home or immobilized mobile home is
removed from the corporate limits of the village.
   C.   In addition to and not in lieu of any available remedies, any person
violating any provision of this title shall be fined as provided in §
1-4-1 for each offense. Each day a violation is permitted to exist shall
constitute a separate offense.
(Ord. 850, passed 3-12-2018)
ARTICLE A: ZONING BOARD OF APPEALS; VARIATIONS AND APPEALS
Section
   
10-2A-1   Statute Authority
   
10-2A-2   Appointment And Term
   
10-2A-3   Removals And Vacancies
   
10-2A-4   Meetings
   
10-2A-5   Powers
   
10-2A-6   Variations
   
10-2A-7   Appeals Procedure
   
10-2A-8   Judicial Review
§ 10-2A-1 STATUTE AUTHORITY.
   A zoning board of appeals is hereby authorized in accordance with the
Illinois Municipal Code, being 65 ILCS 5/11-13-3.
(1987 Code, § 40-10-1)
§ 10-2A-2 APPOINTMENT AND TERM.
   The zoning board of appeals shall consist of seven members to be appointed
by the mayor, with the advice and consent of the village board of trustees.
Successors to each member so appointed shall serve a term of five years. One of
the members so appointed shall be named chair at the time of his or her
appointment.
(1987 Code, § 40-10-1)
§ 10-2A-3 REMOVALS AND VACANCIES.
   A.   Any member of the board of appeals who misses three meetings of the
board within any calendar year without a valid excuse, as solely determined by
the mayor, shall be subject to immediate removal from the board by the mayor.
The chair of the board of appeals shall immediately advise the mayor when any
member has missed three meetings of the board.
   B.   The mayor has the power to remove any member of the board of appeals
for any other cause after a public hearing.
   C.   Vacancies shall be filled for the unexpired term of the member whose
place has become vacant.
(1987 Code, § 40-10-1)
§ 10-2A-4 MEETINGS.
   A.   Meetings of the zoning board of appeals shall be held at the call of
the chair and at such other times as the board may determine. The chair, or in
his or her absence, the acting chair, may administer oaths and compel the
attendance of witnesses.
   B.   All meetings of the board shall be open to the public. The board shall
keep minutes of its proceedings showing the vote of each member upon every
question, or if absent or failing to vote, indicating that fact, and shall keep
records of its examinations and other official actions.
   C.   No hearing shall be conducted without a quorum of the board being
present, which shall consist of a majority of all the members. Any absent
member who certifies that he or she has read the transcript of the proceedings
before the board may vote upon any question before the board.
   D.   All actions by the zoning board of appeals shall be immediately filed
in the office of the board and shall be a public record.
(1987 Code, § 40-10-2)
§ 10-2A-5 POWERS.
   The board of appeals shall have the following powers:
   A.   Appeals And Zoning Map Interpretation. To hear and decide appeals where
it is alleged that there is an error in any action by the zoning enforcing
officer or other administrative official in carrying out the provisions of this
title, and for the interpretation of the zoning map; and
   B.   Variations. To hear and decide on applications for a variation in cases
where there are practical difficulties or a particular hardship in the way of
carrying out the provisions of this title.
(1987 Code, § 40-10-4)
§ 10-2A-6 VARIATIONS.
   A.   Findings Of Fact. Before any variation is granted, the board of appeals
must make findings of fact that all of the following conditions are shown to be
present:
      1.   The property in question cannot yield a reasonable return if
permitted to be used only under the conditions allowed by the regulations in
that zone;
      2.   The plight of the owner is due to unique circumstances; and
      3.   A variation, if granted, will not alter the essential character of
the locality.
   B.   Conditions Of Variation. In granting a variation, the board may attach
thereto any conditions and safeguards it deems necessary or desirable in
furthering the purposes of this title. Violation of any of these conditions or
safeguards shall be deemed a violation of this title.
   C.   Hearing. No variation shall be made by the board of appeals except in a
specific case and after a public hearing before the board of appeals of which
there shall be a notice of the time and place of the hearing, published at
least once, not more than 30 days nor less than 15 days before the hearing, in
a newspaper published in the village, or if none, then in one or more
newspapers with a general circulation within the village which is published in
the county. This notice shall contain the particular location for which the
variation is requested as well as a brief statement of what the proposed
variation consists.
   D.   Fee. In order to partially defray the expenses of public hearings
involving variations, the applicant shall pay the sum of $150 to the village at
the time of filing the appeal for the variance.
   E.   Powers Of Zoning Board Limited. Nothing herein contained shall be
construed to give or grant to the board the power or authority to permit a use
not generally permitted in the district involved.
(1987 Code, § 40-10-4)
§ 10-2A-7 APPEALS PROCEDURE.
   A.   Authority. An appeal to the board of appeals may be taken by any person
aggrieved by any order, requirement, decision or determination of any
governmental officer, department, board or bureau based in whole or in part
upon the provisions of this title.
   B.   Filing Appeal. Such appeal specifying the grounds thereof shall be
filed with the zoning enforcing officer and the zoning board of appeals within
45 days of the action appealed from. Upon appeal, the zoning enforcing officer
shall immediately transmit to the board of appeals all papers constituting the
record upon which the action appealed from was taken.
(1987 Code, § 40-10-3)
   C.   Stay Of Proceedings. An appeal stays all proceedings in furtherance of
the action appealed from unless the zoning enforcing officer certifies to the
board of appeals, after the notice of appeal has been filed with him or her,
that, by reason of facts stated in the certificate, a stay would, in his or her
opinion, cause imminent peril to life or property. In this event, the
proceedings shall not be stayed otherwise than by a restraining order which may
be granted by the board of appeals or by a circuit court on application and on
notice to the zoning enforcing officer and on due cause shown.
(1987 Code, § 40-10-3) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   D.   Hearing. The board of appeals shall fix a reasonable time for hearing
of the appeal, a notice of which shall be given to the parties and shall be
published at least once, not more than 30 days nor less than 15 days before the
hearing, in a newspaper published in the village, or if none, then in one or
more newspapers with a general circulation within the village.
   E.   Decision. The concurring vote of four members of the board of appeals
is necessary to reverse any order, requirement, determination or decision of an
administrative official or to decide in favor of the applicant, or to effect
any variation in this title.
(1987 Code, § 40-10-3)
§ 10-2A-8 JUDICIAL REVIEW.
   All final administrative decisions of the board of appeals shall be subject
to judicial review pursuant to the Administrative Review Act and the rules
adopted pursuant thereto.
(1987 Code, § 40-10-4)
Statutory reference:
   Similar provisions, see 735 ILCS 5/3-101 et seq.

§ 10-3-1 DISTRICTS ESTABLISHED.
   For the purpose of this title, all land within the village is hereby
designated on the zoning map as being in one of the following districts:
District Classification
District Classification

AG-2     Agricultural
R-1      One- and two-family residential
R-2      Multiple-family residential
C-1      General retail
C-2      Service retail
I-1      Light industrial
I-2      Heavy Industrial
 
(1987 Code, § 40-2-1) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 10-3-2 ZONING MAP PUBLISHED.
   The village board of trustees shall cause to be published no later than
March 31 of each year a map clearly showing the existing zoning divisions and
classifications for the preceding calendar year. If, in any calendar year,
there are no changes in zoning divisions or classifications, no map shall be
published for such calendar year.
(1987 Code, § 40-11-4)
§ 10-3-3 DETERMINATION OF DISTRICT BOUNDARIES.
   Unless otherwise indicated on the zoning district map(s), the boundary lines
of the districts follow lot lines, centerlines of streets, alleys or railroad
rights-of-way, or such centerlines extended, centerlines of creeks or the
corporate limit lines as existing at the effective date hereof.
(1987 Code, § 40-2-2)
§ 10-3-4 ZONING OF ANNEXED AREAS.
   All territory which may hereafter be annexed to the village shall, from the
time of annexation, be considered as being the same zoning district designated
to said territory under the zoning provisions established by the county zoning
board, until and unless otherwise designated by amendment to this title as
specified in the procedure in § 10-2-4, or as specified in the annexation
ordinance.
(1987 Code, § 40-2-3)
CHAPTER 4: AGRICULTURAL DISTRICTS
Section
   
10-4-1   Purpose
   
10-4-2   Uses Permitted
   
10-4-3   Special Uses
   
10-4-4   Lot Area And Width
   
10-4-5   Building Height

§ 10-4-1 PURPOSE.
   A.   AG-1 District. The purpose of the AG-1 district is:
      1.   To encourage the preservation of a proper setting for and pleasant
approaches to the village;
      2.   To provide an area for agricultural pursuits protected from
infringement of unguided urban development;
      3.   To create and preserve a setting for rural small estate residential
development; and
      4.   To conserve the area physically immediately surrounding the village,
an environment capable of accommodating future expansion of the village in an
orderly and efficient manner.
   B.   AG-2 District. The purpose of the AG-2 district is to protect the area
around future proposed lakes from encroachment and intermixing of land which
can be detrimental to the public health, welfare and conservation of property
values.
(1987 Code, § 40-3-1)
§ 10-4-2 USES PERMITTED.
   A.   AG-1 District. The following uses are permitted in the AG-1 district:
      1.   Agricultural uses, commercial grain storage;
      2.   Community buildings; libraries; museums; picnic grounds; religious,
educational or charitable institutions;
      3.   Home occupations, subject to the restrictions of §
10-5-2A for home occupations;
      4.   One- and two-family residential dwellings; and
      5.   Signs:
         a.   Unlighted signs notifying of the sale, rental or lease of land or
sale of farm goods on the premises on which the sign is maintained having not
over 50 square feet of sign area; and
         b.   Signs announcing meeting time and place of civic organizations.
(1987 Code, § 40-3-2)
   B.   AG-2 District. Within any AG-2 agricultural district, except for bona
fide agricultural uses, as defined in §
10-1-3 , no building or premises shall be used or arranged or designed to be
used except for one or more of the following uses which shall be subject to all
regulations and requirements permitted by this title:
      1.   Lakes (artificial);
      2.   Public parks and forest preserves;
      3.   Public picnic grounds, marinas and beaches;
      4.   Public utility substations or booster stations, but not including
power generation or gas manufacturing plants.
(1987 Code, § 40-3-3)
(Ord. 829, passed 4-18-2016)
§ 10-4-3 SPECIAL USES.
   The following uses are permitted as special uses when authorized by the
zoning board of appeals after a public hearing. Such special uses shall be
subject to the requirements the zoning board of appeals feels necessary to
further the purpose of the agricultural districts as stated in this chapter:
   A.   AG-1 District.
      1.   Cemeteries;
      2.   Commercial resort uses such as lodges, restaurants, rental cottages,
riding stables, golf courses, swimming pools, tennis courts and other such
recreational facilities;
      3.   Outdoor advertising structures, provided they are not closer than
300 feet to the nearest residential or public or semipublic property, and that
permits are issued for not longer than five years;
      4.   Uses of land including quarrying and mining of natural resources,
sanitary landfill and other types of landfill, refuse disposal and dumps; and
      5.   Public utility buildings, structures, substations, uses or land.
   B.   AG-2 District. No special uses provided.
(1987 Code, § 40-3-4) (Ord. 829, passed 4-18-2016)
§ 10-4-4 LOT AREA AND WIDTH.
   A.   AG-1 District. No dwelling in the AG-1 district shall be located on a
lot less than one acre in area nor 150 feet in width.
   B.   AG-2 District. Lot area and lot width are not specified for the AG-
2 district.
(1987 Code, § 40-3-5)
§ 10-4-5 BUILDING HEIGHT.
   A.   AG-1 District. No residential dwelling in the AG-1 district shall
exceed two stories or 30 feet in height.
   B.   AG-2 District. No height limitation is specified for the AG-2 district.
(1987 Code, § 40-3-6)
§ 10-4-6 YARDS.
   A.   All structures shall have the following minimum yard spaces:
      1.   Front Yard. Fifty feet;
      2.   Side Yards. Each not less than 15 feet; and
(1987 Code, § 40-3-7)
      3.   Rear Yard. Not less than 30 feet.
   B.   Corner lots shall provide the minimum front yard requirements on each
street side of the lot.
(1987 Code, § 40-3-7) (Amd. 2008 Code)
§ 10-4-7 OFF-STREET PARKING.
   Off-street parking shall be provided in the AG districts in accordance with
§
10-1-6.
(1987 Code, § 40-3-8)
CHAPTER 5: RESIDENTIAL DISTRICTS
Section
   
10-5-1   Purpose
   
10-5-2   Uses Permitted
   
10-5-3   Special Uses
   
10-5-4   Lot Area And Width

§ 10-5-1 PURPOSE.
   The purpose of the residential districts is to provide an area for
residential uses and those public and semipublic uses normally considered an
integral part of the residential neighborhood they serve.
(1987 Code, § 40-4-1)
§ 10-5-2 USES PERMITTED.
   A.   R-1 District.
      1.   General Uses.
         a.   Churches, schools, libraries, museums, art galleries, parks,
playgrounds, community centers, cemeteries, public services, utility buildings;
         b.   Light agriculture uses including nurseries and raising of farm
products (not to include livestock or sale of products on premises); and
         c.   Single-family and two-family residence dwellings, leasing of
rooms to not more than two families.
      2.   Accessory Uses To The Above Uses.
         a.   Home occupations; provided, however, no home occupation shall be
established or conducted except in conformity with the following regulations:
            (1)   Employees Restricted. A home occupation shall employ no more
than one individual who is not related to the family residing on the premises;
            (2)   Floor Space. The total area used for a home occupation shall
not exceed 25% of the gross floor area of the dwelling or 300 square feet,
whichever is less;
            (3)   Dwelling Alterations. In any residential district, a
principal residential building shall not be altered to accommodate a home
occupation in such a way as to materially change the residential character of
the building;
            (4)   Outdoor Storage. Outdoor (unenclosed) storage on the premises
of equipment and materials used in connection with a home occupation is
prohibited;
            (5)   Nuisances. A home occupation shall not generate any offensive
noise, vibration, smoke, dust, odors, heat, glare or electrical interference
noticeable at or beyond the lot lines; and
            (6)   Parking. One space per 150 square feet of floor area devoted
to the home occupation in addition to the parking requirements for the dwelling
shall be provided.
         b.   Doublewide mobile homes and doublewide manufactured homes as
follows:
            (1)   On and after August 1, 2000, doublewide mobile homes or
doublewide manufactured homes are permitted in an R-1 district only under the
following conditions:
               (A)   Such doublewide mobile home or doublewide manufactured
home was purchased new from the manufacturer thereof or from a dealer in the
business of selling such new doublewide mobile homes or doublewide manufactured
homes at retail within 180 consecutive calendar days prior to the application
for a village building permit or occupancy permit to place and locate such home
on any lot, tract or plot of real estate within the village;
               (B)   Such doublewide mobile home or doublewide manufactured
home was purchased for $80,000 or more; and such purchase price for such new
doublewide mobile home or doublewide manufactured home shall be evidenced by a
written paid receipt and a bona fide bill of sale or purchase contract each
bearing the notarized authorized signature of the seller of such doublewide
mobile home or doublewide immobilized home to the applicant for a village
building permit or occupancy permit;
               (C)   Such doublewide mobile home or doublewide manufactured
home is either situated not less than two inches over a permanent foundation or
rests on and is fully supported by a permanent foundation; and
               (D)   Such doublewide mobile home or doublewide manufactured
home shall be thereafter considered permanently attached to and a part of the
tract, lot, plot of real estate or space it is situated on, thereby subjecting
such doublewide mobile home or doublewide manufactured home to the village ad
valorem (real estate) annual tax levy ordinances.
            (2)   On and after August 1, 2000, except as permitted under the
above paragraph, limited conditions for doublewide mobile homes or doublewide
manufactured homes, mobile homes (singlewide or doublewide) or manufactured
homes (singlewide or doublewide) shall not be permitted in any residential,
agricultural, commercial or industrial zoning district within the village
except in a properly zoned mobile home park licensed by the village in
accordance with title 9, chapter 3 of this code and if such mobile home park is
granted a special use designation as provided in § 10-5-3 regarding mobile home
parks.
            (3)   Newly constructed modular homes of not less than 800 square
feet living space that was purchased for $80,000 or more. Such purchase price
for such modular home shall be evidenced by a written paid receipt and a bona
fide bill of sale or purchase contract each bearing the notarized authorized
signature of the seller of such modular home to the applicant for a village
building permit or occupancy permit.
         c.   Unlighted real estate signs, nonconforming business use signs and
public building or church signs or bulletin boards pertaining to the property
on which they are placed and not having over 12 square feet of sign area.
   B.   R-2 District.
      1.   Any use permitted in the R-1 district (subsection A. above);
      2.   Clubs, lodges, hospitals, sanitariums and rest homes; and
      3.   Multiple-family dwellings, tourist homes and lodging uses with
accommodations for not more than 15 persons.
(1987 Code, § 40-4-3) (Amd. 2008 Code)
(Ord. 850, passed 3-12-2018)
Cross-reference:
   Swimming pools, spas and hot tubs permitted in the R-1 district, see §
10-1-9
§ 10-5-3 SPECIAL USES.
   The following uses are permitted as special uses in the R-1 and R-
2 districts when authorized by the zoning board of appeals after a public
hearing. Such special uses shall be subject to the following requirements and
any other requirements the zoning board of appeals feels necessary to further
the purpose of the residential districts as stated in §
10-5-1.
   A.   Business and professional offices, medical and dental offices or
clinics, law, insurance and real estate offices, bank, finance, credit union,
savings and loan and financial institution offices.
   B.   Mobile home parks (as defined in
title 9, chapter 3 of this code); provided, that:
      1.   Such mobile home park fully complies with the provisions of
title 9, chapter 3 of this code, as now adopted and as may be hereafter amended
from time to time, and the required permit has first been obtained from the
village board in accordance with
title 9, chapter 3 of this code.
      2.   Such mobile home park shall be constructed, operated and maintained
in such a manner so that any mobile home or immobilized mobile home located or
to be located in said mobile home park will qualify for the issuance of an
occupancy permit according to the requirements of
title 9, chapter 3 of this code.
   C.   1.   Planned residential development under single ownership in which
incidental business and recreational facilities for the convenience of the
occupants may be furnished; provided, however, that the property shall have a
gross area of 30 acres and that permitted business uses shall be limited to
those uses allowed in the C-1 district.
      2.   They shall not occupy more than 10% of the gross land area of the
development. Such planned developments may vary the dwelling type requirements,
yard, height or area per dwelling requirements; provided, however, that the
total number of dwelling units to be allowed under normal development does not
exceed the maximum number.
   D.   Any other special use by a particular individual. Such use shall not be
changed or transferred to another individual.
(1987 Code, § 40-4-4) (Ord. 829, passed 4-18-2016)

§ 10-5-5 BUILDING HEIGHT.
   No building shall exceed two stories or 30 feet in height unless each side
yard is increased over the required minimum of five feet for every five feet or
fraction thereof of additional height over 30 feet. In no case shall the
building height exceed 50 feet.
(1987 Code, § 40-4-6)
§ 10-5-6 YARDS.
   All structures to be constructed, altered or moved in the R-1 and R-
2 districts shall provide yards of the following minimum depths:
   A.   Front Yard. Twenty-five feet;
(1987 Code, § 40-4-7)
   B.   Side Yards. Five feet for each side yard;
(1987 Code, § 40-4-7) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   C.   Rear Yard. Twenty feet;
   D.   Corner Lots. Corner lots shall provide a front yard on each street
side, however, not so as to reduce the buildable width of the lot to below 32
feet; and
   E.   Projections Into Yards. Architectural features of residential buildings
such as windowsills, cornices and/or roof overhangs shall not project into the
aforesaid required yard minimum depths.
(1987 Code, § 40-4-7)
§ 10-5-7 OFF-STREET PARKING.
   There shall be provided in the R-1 and R-2 districts adequate off-street
parking in accordance with the schedule in §
10-1-6.
(1987 Code, § 40-4-8)
§ 10-5-8 ADDITIONAL LOT SIZE REQUIREMENTS.
   For purposes of satisfying the definition of a LOT set forth in §
10-1-3  of this title, the minimum lot area and minimum lot width as set forth
in §
10-5-4 , the building height requirements in §
10-5-5 , the yard requirements set forth in §
10-5-6  and §
10-1-10 B.2., the off-street parking requirements set forth in §
10-1-6 A., entry reading “Boarding and rooming houses” and entry reading
“Dwellings,” and swimming pool requirements set forth in §
10-1-9 , a single-family dwelling or a two-family dwelling may be constructed
and/or located on two or more lots if such lots are adjoining and contiguous
with one another and all such lots have a common owner or owners. In such
event, the total dimensions of all such adjoining and contiguous lots so
utilized for such residential construction or occupancy shall be construed as
comprising one lot for the purpose of determining compliance with the
definition of a LOT and with the requirements for the minimum lot area, minimum
lot width, building height, yard requirements, off-street parking requirements
and swimming pool requirements as are required and set forth in the R-1 and R-
2 districts.
(1987 Code, § 40-4-9)
CHAPTER 6: COMMERCIAL DISTRICTS
Section
   
10-6-1   Purpose
   
10-6-2   Uses Permitted
   
10-6-3   Lot Area And Width
   
10-6-4   Building Height
   
10-6-5   Yards
   
10-6-6   Off-Street Parking And Loading
§ 10-6-1 PURPOSE.
   A.   C-1 District. The purpose of the C-1 general retail district is to
provide for a wide range of retail facilities and services of such a nature as
to be fully compatible in the proximity they must enjoy in the central business
district or in neighborhood shopping areas.
(1987 Code, § 40-5-1)
   B.   C-2 District. The purpose of the C-2 service retail district is to
provide for those retail businesses and services which require a location other
than the central business district or the neighborhood business district,
requiring either large tracts of land or a highway location.
(1987 Code, § 40-5-2)
§ 10-6-2 USES PERMITTED.
   A.   C-1 District.
      1.    General Uses.
         a.   Any use permitted in a residential district; provided, however,
the zoning enforcing officer determines that any special use complies with §
10-5-3;
         b.   Automotive and related uses: new and used car sales, service and
repair, gasoline filling stations, motorcycle and bicycle shops, cab and bus
stands and depots;
         c.   Business and professional offices, medical and dental offices or
clinics, law, insurance, and real estate offices, bank, finance, credit union,
savings and loan, and financial institution offices;
         d.   Food, drug and beverage: grocery stores, supermarkets, meat
markets, drugstores and liquor stores, bakeries in conjunction with retail
sales, restaurants, tearooms and taverns;
         e.   Major retail outlets: furniture, department, clothing, shoe and
variety stores, hardware, appliance, paint and wallpaper stores;
         f.   Service and recreation: motels and hotels, laundromats, dry
cleaning and laundry pick up station, barbershops and beauty shops, shoe repair
and tailor shops, mortuaries, newspaper publishing, printing shops with not
more than ten full-time, regular employees, storage and transfer
establishments, places of amusement and assembly, commercial recreation; and
         g.   Specialty shops: gift shops, magazine, book and stationery
outlets, florist shops, camera and photography shops, sporting goods.
      2.   Accessory Uses To The Above Uses. Business and advertising signs
pertaining to the business on the property on which the sign is located;
provided, that:
         a.   Illumination of all signs shall be diffused or indirect and shall
be arranged so as not to reflect direct rays of light into adjacent residential
districts or into the public way; and
         b.   Any sign located in the direct line of vision of any traffic
control signal shall not have flashing intermittent red, green or amber
illumination.
(1987 Code, § 40-5-3) (Amd. 2008 Code)
   B.   C-2 District.
      1.   Any use permitted in the C-1 general retail district (subsection A.
above);
      2.   Building trades and equipment: building, concrete, electrical,
masonry, sheet metal, plumbing and heating shops, building material
establishments (provided no assembly), construction, millwork, or concrete
block manufacturing is done on the premises;
      3.   Heavy service processing facilities: laundry and dry cleaning
plants, linens, towel, diaper and similar supply services, animal pounds,
kennels and veterinary establishments, frozen food lockers, seed and food
processing plants, dairies;
      4.   Vehicle drive-in and heavy vehicle service: drive-in theaters,
drive-in restaurants and refreshment stands; express, cartage, and trucking
facilities; large item machinery or bulk sales and storage, not including
outdoor unfenced storage;
      5.   Accessory uses to the above uses;
      6.   Retail and/or wholesale businesses or dealerships that include the
sale, servicing and repair of new and used agricultural, commercial and
residential equipment, vehicles and parts, and the related buildings, offices,
structures and outdoor unfenced or fenced storage of equipment, vehicles,
inventory, parts and supplies incident to conducting such business; and
      7.   Retail and/or wholesale waterworks businesses that include related
buildings, offices, structures, equipment, inventory and supplies, water meter
box fabricating facilities, and outdoor fenced storage of waterworks materials
and equipment including, but not limited to, pipes, valves, hydrants, fittings
and fabrication of waterworks related appurtenances, and all other waterworks
materials and equipment of any kind, nature or extent whatsoever.
(1987 Code, § 40-5-4) (Amd. 2008 Code)
(Ord. 829, passed 4-18-2016; Ord. 907, passed 7-8-2024)
§ 10-6-3 LOT AREA AND WIDTH.
   A.   Residential Uses. Each residential use to be accommodated in the C-
1 and C-2 commercial districts shall meet the minimum lot area and minimum lot
width requirements of the R-2 residential district.
   B.   Business Uses. Each business use to be accommodated in the C-1 and C-
2 commercial districts shall provide a minimum lot area of 2,500 square feet.
No minimum lot width is required for business uses.
(1987 Code, § 40-5-5)
§ 10-6-4 BUILDING HEIGHT.
   A.   C-1 District. In the C-1 general retail district, no building shall
exceed three stories or 45 feet.
   B.   C-2 District. In the C-2 service retail district, no building shall
exceed two stories or 30 feet.
(1987 Code, § 40-5-6)
§ 10-6-5 YARDS.
   All buildings to be constructed, altered or moved in the commercial
districts shall meet the following minimum requirements.
   A.   C-1 District.
      1.   Front Yard. No minimum yard required;
      2.   Side Yards. No minimum yard required, except lots adjoining a
residential district shall provide a side yard on that adjoining side equal to
that required in the adjoining residential district; and
      3.   Rear Yard. Twenty feet. (Where a rear lot line abuts an alley, one-
half of the width of such alley may be considered in meeting the rear yard
requirements.)
   B.   C-2 District.
      1.   Front Yard. Fifteen feet;
      2.   Side Yards. Ten feet; and
      3.   Rear Yard. Twenty feet. (Where a rear lot line abuts an alley, one-
half of the width of such alley may be considered in meeting the rear yard
requirements.)
(1987 Code, § 40-5-7)
§ 10-6-6 OFF-STREET PARKING AND LOADING.
   There shall be provided in the C-1 and C-2 commercial districts off-street
parking and loading in accordance with the schedules in §§
10-1-6 and
10-1-7.
(1987 Code, § 40-5-8)
CHAPTER 7: INDUSTRIAL DISTRICTS
Section
   
10-7-1   Purpose
   
10-7-2   Permitted And Special Uses
   
10-7-3   Lot Area And Width
   
10-7-4   Building Height
   
10-7-5   Yards
   
10-7-6   Off-Street Parking And Loading
§ 10-7-1 PURPOSE.
   A.   I-1 District. The purpose of the I-1 light industrial district is to
provide for commercial uses, storage, and those manufacturing uses not normally
creating a nuisance discernible beyond its property. (1987 Code, § 40-6-1)
   B.   I-2 District. The purpose of the I-2 heavy industrial district is to
provide for industrial uses not allowed in any other district; provided, that
within this district, uses of a hazardous nature or those producing extensive
smoke or odor shall not be located so that the general hazard or nuisance
affects a large segment of the community.
(1987 Code, § 40-6-2)
§ 10-7-2 PERMITTED AND SPECIAL USES.
   A.   I-1 District.
      1.   Any use permitted in the C-1 and C-2 commercial districts, except
residential uses;
      2.   Manufacturing: manufacturing or processing of small items including
gloves, footwear, bathing caps, boxes, cartons and cans, hardware, toys,
electric batteries, motors or generators; textile products manufacturing;
glass, plastics, cement and stone products manufacturing; furniture
manufacturing; food manufacturing or processing, including hatcheries, canning,
freezing, storage and bottling;
      3.   Other manufacturing uses of a light nature, free from any
objectionable odors, fumes, dirt, vibration or noise detectable, at the lot
line. Such uses shall not be established without an application for a permit
which shall be accompanied by a certification by a registered engineer or
architect indicating that fumes, odors, dirt, vibration or noise produced by
the industry will not be detectable at the lot line. In the event of the denial
of such permit, the applicant shall have the right of appeal to the zoning
board of appeals in accordance with
chapter 2, article A. of this title; and
      4.   Warehousing and storage: indoor and outdoor storage of goods and
materials including warehousing, pole yards, building material storage and
trucking storage.
(1987 Code, § 40-6-3)
   B.   I-2 District.
      1.   All uses not otherwise prohibited by law, except as residential
uses; provided, however, the following uses will be permitted as special uses
in the I-2 heavy industrial district only when authorized by the village board
of trustees after a public hearing and recommendation by the zoning board of
appeals:
         a.   Wireless telecommunication towers, radio, telephone, or cell
phone communication or transmission towers or antennas with a height greater
than 45 feet from the ground surface where such tower or antenna is located;
         b.   Junk yards, provided, however, no part of any junk yard which
includes any lot on which any inoperable vehicles are stored, shall be located
closer than 500 feet to the boundary of any residential district, and provided
further that all vehicles, parts and equipment shall be stored, and all salvage
and wrecking operations are conducted within a completely enclosed structure or
within an area screed by a wall, solid fence or closely-planted shrubbery at
least ten feet high and of sufficient density to block the view from adjacent
property; and
         c.   Bag cleaning, boiler and tank works; central mixing plant for
cement, mortar, plaster or paving materials; coke oven, curing, tanning and
storage of raw hides and skins; distillation of bones, coal, wood or tar; fat
rendering; forge plant; foundry or metal fabrication plant; gasoline or oil
storage above ground in excess of 500 gallons; slaughterhouse or stockyards;
smelting plant; and the manufacture of acetylene, acid, alcohol or alcoholic
beverages; ammonia, bleaching powder, chemicals, brick, pottery, terra-cotta,
or tile; candles, disinfectants, dyestuffs; fertilizers; linseed oil, paint,
oil turpentine, varnish, soap and tar products, or any other use which, in the
opinion of the zoning board of appeals, would emit detrimental or obnoxious
noise, vibrations, smoke, odors, dust or other objectionable conditions beyond
the confines of its property.
      2.   The zoning board of appeals shall recommend approval to the village
board of trustees if it is determined that the proposed use will not extend its
detrimental or obnoxious effects beyond the limits of the heavy industrial
district in which it is located. Such special uses shall be subject to any
requirements the village board of trustees feels necessary to further the
purpose of the industrial district as stated in §
10-7-1B.
(Prior Code, § 10-7-2) (Ord. 829, passed 4-18-2016)
§ 10-7-3 LOT AREA AND WIDTH.
   Each use to be established in the I-1 or the I-2 district shall provide a
minimum lot area of 5,000 square feet and minimum lot width of 50 feet.
(1987 Code, § 40-6-5)
§ 10-7-4 BUILDING HEIGHT.
   No building in the I-1 or I-2 district shall exceed 50 feet in height.
(1987 Code, § 40-6-6)
§ 10-7-5 YARDS.
   All structures to be constructed, altered or moved in the I-1 and I-
2 districts shall provide yards of the following minimum depths:
   A.   Front Yard. Twenty-five feet;
   B.   Side Yards. Ten feet, except where a side yard abuts a residential
district, in which case, a side yard of 25 feet shall be provided; and
   C.   Rear Yard. Twenty-five feet.
(1987 Code, § 40-6-7)
§ 10-7-6 OFF-STREET PARKING AND LOADING.
   There shall be provided in the I-1 and I-2 districts adequate off-street
parking and loading in accordance with the schedules in §§
10-1-6 and
10-1-7.
(1987 Code, § 40-6-8)
